# Chorus v1 — Master Roadmap (Foundation)

_Location: `docs/Features/Foundation/roadmap.md` · Last updated: **2026-08-16** (architect pass at `4369954` — Phase 6a closed, Phase 5 opened, D154–D158, F77–F78)_

---

## 1. Purpose

Chorus v1 is a local-first, BYOK Electron + Vue 3 + TypeScript desktop app (Windows-only) for running multiple AI coding agents in parallel terminal panes. The **prime contract**: a developer can launch, watch, control, and persist many concurrent agent sessions across multiple projects — restart-safe and cleanly killable — from a single window.

This roadmap refines **`docs/PLAN.md` §14** into an executable, phase-by-phase program of work. It is the single master roadmap for the whole v1 effort. It records **what** each phase delivers and **why** — not how (implementation detail lives in Task docs and Implementation Specs).

---

## 2. Source Of Truth

| Authority | Owns |
|---|---|
| `docs/PLAN.md` (master plan v2.1) | Product vision, full architecture, macro-phase intent |
| `CLAUDE.md` | Non-negotiable, locked architecture rules |
| **This roadmap** | Phase status, decision log, verified ground facts |
| Workflow skills (`/architect`, `/phase-kickoff`, `/phase-prompt`) | The operating rhythm that keeps the three above in sync (see §3) |

When these disagree, PLAN.md and CLAUDE.md win on intent and rules; this roadmap wins on _current status_ and _what happens next_.

---

## 3. Governance & Workflow

Chorus is built on a repeating rhythm. One phase at a time, in the order below.

1. **`/architect`** — after each phase lands, updates this roadmap: marks the phase complete, re-verifies ground facts against the code, revises provisional phases, appends decisions.
2. **`/phase-kickoff`** — decomposes the next phase into `Phase-N-Overview.md` plus 1–5 paired `Task-N-#.md` + `ImplementationSpec-N-#.md` files under this Foundation folder (`Tasks/` and `ImplementationSpecs/`).
3. **`/phase-prompt`** — generates a verbose, self-contained execution prompt to open a fresh conversation for the phase.
4. **Execution session** — implements the tasks, **verifies by running the real app** (not just compiling), and makes **one intentional narrated commit**.
5. **Back to `/architect`** — the loop closes; the roadmap re-syncs to reality.

Council Review (§4) can interrupt steps 2 and 4 whenever a section meets its trigger criteria.

---

## 4. Council Review (CR) Mechanism

A **multi-LLM council** reviews tough sections — several other LLM models giving independent review and deliberation.

> **⚠ CORRECTED 2026-08-16 — THIS SECTION DESCRIBED A WORLD THAT ENDED ON 2026-07-26, AND IT SAID SO IN TWO PLACES.** It read *"Matthew runs a multi-LLM council — a Cursor-based setup"*, *"**Claude cannot run the council**"*, and (below) *"until 3b lands, CR runs stay in Cursor exactly as described here."* **Phase 3b landed 2026-07-26** (`426ee81`, closing four tasks) and the council has been a native Chorus feature since. Measured at `4369954`: `councilService.ts` + `councilCore.ts` + `councilMembers.ts` + `councilDocketCore.ts` ship with their tests, **15 council IPC channels** exist (**11 `council:*`** — `pick-brief` · `start` · `cancel` · `opened` · `progress` · `summary` · `transcript` · `docket` · `findings` · `forget-run` · `verdict` — **plus 4 `council-member:*`** CRUD), and the brief-`.md` in → findings-`.md` out pipeline D27 promised is the one that runs.
>
> **⚠ WHICH BUILD RUNS THE COUNCIL IS NOT A DETAIL — IT IS THE WHOLE ANSWER TO "CAN THIS GATE FIRE TODAY".** Members and credentials live in the **database**, not the code, and the two databases on this machine disagree completely. Measured this pass, read-only, blobs never touched: the **installed** app (`%APPDATA%\chorus-app`, Chorus **0.6.0**) has **5 configured council members and 2 OpenRouter credential profiles**; the **dev** instance (`%APPDATA%\chorus`) has **0 and 0**. **A CR gate therefore runs on the INSTALLED app, and running it against a dev build would find no council at all** — which would read as "the feature is broken" rather than "the fixture is empty". See **D158**.

**Claude's four obligations are UNCHANGED, and they apply whichever council runs** — the mechanism moved in-house, the discipline did not:

1. **Flag** when a section meets CR trigger criteria — at kickoff time or mid-phase.
2. **Prepare a council brief**: the specific decision / design / diff in question, the development goals and acceptance criteria it must satisfy, and the specific questions Claude wants answered.
3. **Pause** and prompt Matthew to run the council. Do not proceed past the checkpoint.
4. **Record findings** in the §6 decision log — unanimous agreement, dissents, and action items — before continuing.

**Trigger criteria for a "tough section":**

- Hard-to-reverse architectural shapes — adapter interface, DB schema growth, event bus design.
- Security-sensitive surfaces — credential vault, secret injection/redaction, localhost hook listener, permission broker.
- Data-loss surfaces — worktree lifecycle, destructive git operations.
- Anything an execution session flags as **low-confidence**, or where **two approaches remain contested**.

Phases marked **[CR]** below carry at least one pre-identified council checkpoint.

**✅ MIGRATION COMPLETE (D27, 2026-07-20 → delivered 2026-07-26).** D27 slated this externally-run mechanism to become a **native Chorus feature** in **Phase 3b — Native Council Review** (§7): 3–5 configurable API-mode council members + a frontier arbiter over the Phase-3 vault/provider infrastructure, an in-app council pane, brief-`.md` in → findings-`.md` out in this same format. **It shipped, and the roadmap kept describing the pre-3b world for three weeks afterwards** — long enough for this document to have been read by several kickoffs while asserting the opposite of the code beneath it. **⚠ THE LESSON GENERALISES BEYOND THE COUNCIL: §5 is re-verified every pass by procedure, and §§3–4 are not, so the governance sections are the ones that go stale silently.** A pass that closes a phase should re-read the sections describing *how work is run*, not only the ones counting what it produced.

**⚠ AND WHAT DID NOT CHANGE IS THE PART WORTH PROTECTING.** A native council is easier to fire, which makes it easier to fire *carelessly*: the four obligations above — flag, brief, **pause**, record in §6 — exist because a review nobody stopped for is not a review. The one genuinely new obligation is **venue**: state which build ran it, because D158 makes that determine whether it ran at all.

>
> **⚠ TWO CLAIMS IN THIS PASS'S OWN INPUT DOCUMENT DID NOT SURVIVE MEASUREMENT, AND THAT DOCUMENT SAID THEY MIGHT NOT.** `Tasks/Architect-Pass-Prompt-6a-Close.md` instructed *"recompute every number against `main` at the moment you write it — including the numbers in this file… what you measure wins."* Two did not: **(1) F77's central claim is materially weaker than stated** — see the finding, where the correction is recorded on the row rather than here; **(2) the day-summarizer's line range is `ipc.ts:4263–4299` with the `createApiSession` call at `:4274`**, not the cited `4135–4171`. **Every other figure in that document reproduced exactly** — 97 / 87 / 20 / 19 / 8 / 2 / 2230 / 66 / D153 / F76 — which is what makes the two misses worth naming rather than quietly fixing: an input document that is right thirteen times out of fifteen is exactly the kind that gets trusted the fourteenth time.
>
> **⚠ AND THIS PASS PRODUCED A FALSE GREEN OF ITS OWN, IN THE GATE COMMAND ITSELF — G1's FAILURE MODE IN A NEW REGISTRY.** The suite was first run as `npx vitest run --reporter=basic`. The `basic` reporter **no longer exists** in vitest 4.1.10; the run died in `loadCustomReporterModule` with `ERR_LOAD_URL` having executed **zero tests** — **and exited `0`.** A caller checking the exit code, or tailing for `error TS`-style strings, would have recorded a green suite that never ran. Re-run with the default reporter: **2230 / 2230 across 66 files.** This is the third distinct false green this document has recorded (empty `node_modules` → `'tsc' is not recognized`; the perl byte-mode citation audit; now a dead reporter), and the shared shape is that **the toolchain failed in a way that does not look like a test failure.** The standing rule earns another restatement: **assert on the SUCCESS signal — a test count you can read — never on the absence of an error string.**

| **(F27, found by the Task 3-6 implementer, coordinator-confirmed 2026-07-24) Claude Code discloses a MASKED FRAGMENT of an injected key, and NO exact-value scrubber can ever catch it.** On a BYOK `claude` launch, Claude Code **2.1.218** prompts: "Detected a custom API key in your environment — ANTHROPIC_API_KEY: sk-ant-...<tail> — Do you want to use this API key?" The head/tail mask is a **≥8-character substring of the real key**, rendered on screen and appended to the ring buffer. Task 3-5's scrubber matches **exact values** (D33(a)); a mask is a *derived* form, so exact-value scrubbing **cannot match it BY CONSTRUCTION** — this is not a scrubber defect, it is a boundary of the mechanism the council chose. **This sharpens D33's council risk 1 from "an agent could echo the key" to the stronger and true statement: the agent's own auth UX is a fragment-leak channel — an agent can echo a DERIVED form of a key.** **Not a Phase 3 defect:** D44 makes `claude` subscription-only by product policy, so Chorus injects no key into it and the prompt was only reachable in a deliberate probe. **Standing constraint:** **Phase 3b and every later phase must never word a transcript/exfiltration guarantee as "agents cannot echo the key"** — the honest wording is "Chorus redacts registered exact values on ingest; it cannot redact values an agent derives." Must be quoted **verbatim** in Phase 3b's council brief wherever scrub guarantees are worded. | Claude Code 2.1.218 BYOK launch prompt; `scrubber.ts` exact-value contract | 2026-07-24 |
| **(F28, found by the Task 3-6 implementer, coordinator-confirmed 2026-07-24) `TERM` is INHERITED from whatever the host shell exports, and it changes both agent rendering and scrubber effectiveness.** The execution shell exported **`TERM=dumb`**; inherited by the child through the allow-list env, it put codex into a fallback renderer that emits text with cursor-advance escapes **between individual characters** (`-  a  p  i  0  3  -  K  7 …`). The value was fully legible **on screen** and simultaneously **invisible to substring matching**, so exact-value scrubbing was defeated without any bug in the scrubber. This is the **documented ANSI-interleaving limit observed LIVE** (D33's accepted residual, Qwen's preserved dissent), not a new defect. The harness now boots with `TERM=xterm-256color` and the interleaving disappears. **Proposal, NOT done in 3-6 (env-policy changes were out of scope):** pin `TERM` deliberately in `composeChildEnv` (or in the spawn options) rather than inheriting host-shell noise — since `TERM` is a non-secret eighth variable, doing so **amends D33's seven-var allow-list** and must be ruled on, not slipped in. Carried as a **Phase 3a kickoff question** (§7), per D50's reasoning. | `env.ts` `composeChildEnv` / `BASELINE_ENV_VARS`; live codex 0.145.0 render | 2026-07-24 |

| **(F42, measured 2026-08-01 on run `7dda3482`) `council_runs.cost_usd` UNDER-REPORTS SPEND BY ~37%, AND IT IS A SECOND DEFECT INDEPENDENT OF F39's.** Chorus reported **$0.413684483**; OpenRouter billed **$0.66**; and **Chorus's OWN stored token counts, priced at the live published rates, give $0.660306 — the bill to within 0.05%.** So the tokens are right, the prices are right, and **only the derived dollar figure is wrong**: `cost_usd` is **62.7%** of true spend, the bill **1.595×** it. **⚠ THIS FALSIFIES 3e-2's "ONE CAUSE, TWO OBSERVED DEFECTS" AS A GENERAL CLAIM.** That finding — a capped stream never receives the frame carrying `usage`, so fixing the byte cap fixed the under-reporting — **holds only for the abort case**: this run had **ZERO turns with a null usage figure** and still under-reports by 37%. The remaining defect is in how `usageUsd` is derived (`councilService.ts:1012–1043`), not in what was measured. **⚠ CONSEQUENCES: (1) every cost figure in this roadmap is LOW, AND — CORRECTED 2026-08-02 BY RUN `8087fbf5` — THE FACTOR IS NOT A CONSTANT AND MUST NOT BE USED AS ONE.** The first write-up of F42 said figures were *"low by roughly this factor"* and scaled two of them; **variant B measured 1.540 where variant A measured 1.596**, so the multiplier moves with the token mix. **Every historical figure must be RE-DERIVED per run from that run's own `council_messages` rows — never scaled by a ratio borrowed from another run.** The scaled guesses previously offered here (`c06874ad` ≈ $1.74, Phase 3e ≈ $2.85) are **withdrawn as method, not merely as arithmetic**; the runs are still under-reported and still re-derivable without re-running anything. **(2) D111 resolution (c)'s cost-confirmation gate cannot be built on this number** — a $5.00 gate would not fire until ~$8.00 of real spend, so the $2.00-vs-$5.00 debate was arguing about the wrong digit. **(3) `COUNCIL_MINT_LIMIT_USD` is UNAFFECTED and remains the real protection**, because the $10 cap is enforced by OpenRouter's pre-authorisation against the minted key rather than by Chorus's arithmetic. **⚠ MECHANISM DIAGNOSED 2026-08-02, AND IT IS A RACE RATHER THAN AN ARITHMETIC BUG — THE NUMBER WAS NEVER DERIVED FROM THE RUN'S OWN DATA AT ALL.** `cost_usd` is one scalar read from the **minted key's spend counter** (`councilService.ts:775` → `:1046–1050` → `openrouterKeys.ts:131–137`), which is OpenRouter's **eventually-consistent** per-key aggregate — a generation joins it only after accounting finalises, i.e. after the stream closes. `settle()` reads it milliseconds after the last stream ends **and the next call `DELETE`s the key, so the reading can never be revised.** **The arithmetic proof:** brute-forcing all 256 subsets of run A's per-turn charges, the closest to the recorded figure is **every turn except the last** ($0.409346, within 1.06%) — the last turn being the arbiter's **synthesis**, the run's final and most expensive request. **The real fix is smaller than recomputation: `apiSession.readUsage` already parsed the final frame's `usage` object and DISCARDED `usage.cost`**, the gateway's own charge for that generation. **⚠ AND THE COORDINATOR'S OWN PREMISE THAT `model_catalog` (v9) HOLDS PRICES IS FALSE — it has no price column** (`schema.ts:295–305`); `storage.ts` refused to cache prices deliberately, and that refusal was right, since `glm-5.2` moved from $0.67/$2.10 to $0.72/$2.25 in six days. **A local price table would have been a second, staler source of truth for the very number this finding is about.** **✅ THE COMPUTED-FROM-TOKENS METHOD IS CONFIRMED TWICE AT DIFFERENT MIXES** — A computed $0.660306 vs billed **$0.66**, B computed $1.130450 vs billed **$1.13**, both inside **0.05%**, while `cost_usd` missed by 1.596× and 1.540×. **⚠ THE FIX EXISTS BUT IS NOT MERGED** (`27dc928` on `worktree-agent-ac607b24c8ebfc41d`), and its test evidence must be re-measured in the main tree: the agent reported "2 pre-existing failures on main" which **do not reproduce** — main runs `councilCore.test.ts` **96/96 green** while its worktree fails 2, because the fixture brief is **21,500 bytes in main and 21,749 in the worktree** (a CRLF/LF delta) though `git status` calls it unmodified. **The real finding inside the false one: `parseBriefQuestions`'s fixture tests read real `.md` files and are line-ending sensitive — a test guarding D68(1) that passes or fails on how git materialised a checkout.** **⚠ STATED LIMIT: one run, one roster.** The 1.595× ratio is **not** established as a constant until variants B–D confirm it; what IS established is the direction and the cause. | `council_runs.cost_usd` vs OpenRouter dashboard vs `council_messages` | 2026-08-01 |

runs and messages **created EMPTY**, first written in Task 3b-3, the `attention_spans` precedent) — all coordinator-verified on the real dev DB through the three-dump protocol). ~~Eleven tables: `projects` / `pane_layouts` / `settings` / `schema_migrations` / `sessions` / `worktrees` / `provider_configs` / `credential_profiles` / **`dispatches`** / **`attention_spans`** / **`model_catalog`**.~~ ~~**TWELVE tables**, listed explicitly so a future pass has something to check a claim against rather than a version range to infer one from: `attention_spans` · `credential_profiles` · `dispatches` · **`launch_profiles`** · `model_catalog` · `pane_layouts` · `projects` · `provider_configs` · `schema_migrations` · `sessions` · `settings` · `worktrees`.~~ **⚠ Both counts above are SUPERSEDED by v11 and are struck rather than removed, so the drift trail this row exists to expose stays legible.** **FIFTEEN tables**, listed explicitly so a future pass has something to check a claim against rather than a version range to infer one from: `attention_spans` · **`council_members`** · **`council_messages`** · **`council_runs`** · `credential_profiles` · `dispatches` · `launch_profiles` · `model_catalog` · `pane_layouts` · `projects` · `provider_configs` · `schema_migrations` · `sessions` · `settings` · `worktrees`. **THREE indexes**, listed for the same reason: **`council_messages_run`** · `dispatches_open` · `dispatches_open_ledger`. **FKs are ENFORCED (F16)** — the driver defaults `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON` — **which is exactly why the two v7 telemetry tables and v9's `model_catalog` carry NO `REFERENCES` clause**: a dispatch and an attention span both outlive the session row they name (pane close deletes it, D16(d)), so a FK would make the next pane close throw, and a `REFERENCES provider_configs(id)` on the catalog would make the first `provider:delete` after a refresh throw for the sake of a **cache**. Each of the three handles the consequence explicitly instead: `deleteProviderConfig` purges the provider's catalog rows in its own transaction, **and the pre-existing count-and-refuse on `credential_profiles` is untouched — profiles still block a delete, a catalog never does.** **v10 takes the deliberate INVERSE ruling (D62):** `launch_profiles` carries REAL `REFERENCES` to both its route and its credential, because a launch profile is a live INSTRUCTION that becomes a lie the moment either is gone — and RESTRICT is right **precisely because** it forces a count-and-refuse to be authored in main BEFORE the statement runs. **`sessions.launch_profile_id` is the third case and takes the `dispatches` ruling** — a SOFT pointer with no `REFERENCES` (`storage.ts:279` says so), its dangling case absorbed by D61's fail-safe predicate. **⚠ v11 THEN SPLITS THE SAME RULING THREE WAYS INSIDE A SINGLE MIGRATION, which is why D62 is amended rather than merely cited (Task 3b-2, `01556a8`):** `council_members.credential_profile_id` takes the `launch_profiles` ruling — REAL `REFERENCES`, RESTRICT, because a member is a live INSTRUCTION; `council_runs` takes the `dispatches`/`model_catalog` ruling — **ZERO references on every column**, because a run is a historical FACT; and `council_messages` takes it too, because **a transcript stays true after its member is deleted** and a FK there would make deleting one member throw for every run it ever joined. Verified by `PRAGMA foreign_key_list` — **council_members 1, council_runs 0, council_messages 0** — with `PRAGMA foreign_keys = 1` re-confirmed. **Because SQLite will not cascade a soft pointer, `deleteCouncilRun(id)` purges its own `council_messages` in one transaction**, the `deleteProviderConfig` → `model_catalog` precedent. **`council_members` carries NO `base_url` and NO `provider_id`:** a member names a route BY NAMING A CREDENTIAL, reached through `credential_profiles.provider_id` → `provider_configs.base_url`, which is **D48's one home**; `launch_profiles` needs both columns only because **D33 clause 9** makes a route-with-no-credential first class, and a council member ALWAYS authenticates, so that case does not exist and two columns able to disagree would be a bug class. **⚠ THIS IS THE ROADMAP'S MOST DRIFT-PRONE ROW — three passes have now had to correct it LATE, and the cause is structural: it is the only §5 row a migration invalidates, while the live counts that move every task deliberately live in the task docs instead.** The failure is not slow drift but a SKIPPED READ: `6e12b0e` closed Phase 3a without adding any §5 re-verification, so v10 went unrecorded until the next pass found it. **Re-read this row against a live dump FIRST, at the start of every architect pass.** **⚠ AND THE FOURTH PASS DID NOT DRIFT — the first time that is true of this row.** v11 was recorded HERE, at the architect pass immediately following the migration, from a live dump read BEFORE the row was edited, rather than repaired after a later pass noticed. **That practice is the thing to repeat; the warning above is what it costs when it is skipped.** Re-verified against the shipped v10 **and v11** DDL and a live dump of the real dev DB 2026-07-26. | `src/main/services/storage.ts`, `src/main/db/schema.ts` | **2026-07-26** |

| **(F29, Task 3a-2 Step 0) CDP-injected input is INVISIBLE to the Windows idle timer, and this invalidates the obvious way to drive any idle-dependent proof.** `Input.dispatchKeyEvent` / `insertText` deliver into Chromium, not through the OS input stack, so `GetLastInputInfo` — which `powerMonitor.getSystemIdleTime()` wraps — never sees them: measured live, the counter climbed **monotonically 44 s → 86 s straight through five CDP keystrokes** and flipped `getSystemIdleState(60)` to `'idle'` mid-phase. **Any future phase proving presence, idle, attention, or away-detection must drive with REAL OS input** — the harness uses a bare `SHIFT` press+release via `keybd_event`, which travels the real input path but **inserts no character into any window**, so it is safe to fire while an arbitrary app holds focus (`_verify/3a-2/osinput.ps1`). An independent out-of-process PowerShell `GetLastInputInfo` P/Invoke **agrees with Electron's reading to within 0.89 s** across 63 aligned pairs (integer truncation + sampling skew), which is what proves Electron reports the OS counter rather than something about its own window. Two corollaries for any focus-dependent drive: the dev window is **not** foregrounded by default in a harness session (so `isFocused()`-dependent code records the blurred branch until `SetForegroundWindow` is called), and **other desktop apps steal foreground mid-run** — sample `GetForegroundWindow()` through the phase and retry rather than trusting it. | `powerMonitor`; `_verify/3a-2/step0.ps1`, `STEP0-RESULTS.md` | 2026-07-25 |
| **(F30, Task 3a-2 Step 0) `getSystemIdleState()` has NOT been observed returning `'locked'` on this machine, and the lock probe is not cheaply runnable.** Across a 150 s instrumented run only `'active'` and `'idle'` appeared, and **zero** `lock-screen` / `unlock-screen` / `suspend` / `resume` events fired. The probe was **deliberately not driven**: `LockWorkStation` is only reversible by typing Matthew's Windows password, which an agent must not handle, so in an unattended session the cost of the answer exceeds its value. **Consequence, and it is a real limit rather than a formality:** the `locked` attention class rests on the **`'lock-screen'` event alone** — a smaller claim than "event plus state cross-check", recorded as smaller in `attention.ts`'s header. Anything later that depends on lock/suspend detection (Phase 4 notifications; Phase 8's away-time) **must re-open this with a human at the keyboard** rather than inherit it as settled. | `powerMonitor.getSystemIdleState`; `_verify/3a-2/STEP0-RESULTS.md` | 2026-07-25 |

| **(F16, found in Task 2-1, coordinator-verified 2026-07-20) SQLite FOREIGN KEYS ARE ENFORCED — the earlier "FKs off" ground fact was WRONG.** better-sqlite3 **12.11.1 sets `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON` on every connection by default**; `storage.ts` never needed an explicit pragma. Verified three ways on this machine: a fresh connection's `foreign_keys` pragma reads `1`; an INSERT with a fabricated FK throws `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_FOREIGNKEY`; **and DELETING a referenced parent row also throws** (default RESTRICT). The stale belief originated with the coordinator (inferred from the absence of a pragma in `storage.ts`) and was propagated into the Phase 2 task docs, specs, and execution prompt — **all now corrected in place**. Consequences: v4's `REFERENCES` clauses are real constraints, so `worktrees` inserts must reference existing `projects`/`sessions` rows (2-2's row-before-worktree ordering is therefore MANDATORY, not stylistic); **`storage.deleteSession` THROWS while any `worktrees` row references that session, making D26 Q1's "detach rather than cascade" DB-enforced rather than convention (binds 2-3 hard)**; `sessions.worktree_id` has no `REFERENCES` (added via `ALTER TABLE`) and is unconstrained. | **(F17, found in Task 2-1) git worktree porcelain + Windows path quirks — bind 2-2/2-3/2-4.** `git worktree list --porcelain` emits **forward-slash** paths (`worktree C:/…`) while rows store `join()`-produced backslash paths, and Windows paths are case-insensitive — so **every path comparison must go through a normalization key** (`win32.normalize(p).toLowerCase()`; `worktrees.ts::pathKey` is the reference). A hand-deleted worktree gains a `prunable` attribute line; `parseWorktreePorcelain` skips unknown attributes and is CRLF-tolerant. `git worktree remove -h` advertises only `[-f]`, but the long `--force` (what the adapter emits) was verified working empirically (D4). git's dirty refusal text is `fatal: '<path>' contains modified or untracked files, use --force to delete it` — confirmed NOT to match the manager's Windows-lock retry matcher, so a dirty refusal never retries. Also: **electron-vite dev does NOT hot-restart the main process** on `src/main` edits (renderer HMR only) — every main-process check needs a real tree-kill cold boot. | `git.ts`, `worktrees.ts` + runtime | 2026-07-20 |

A crash between `git worktree add` succeeding and activation leaves the pair **asymmetric**, and the boot reconcile's `promote` writes only the worktrees row (spec §7: "reconcile never writes session rows"). Because **2-2's branch label and 2-4's diff summary both resolve from the SESSION side** (`row.worktreeId ? … : null`), such a pane renders with no branch and no diff counts despite living in a worktree — and 2-3's `session:delete` would still hit the F16 constraint via the row-side link. **Decide at 2-2 execution:** (a) resolve the worktree by `worktrees.session_id` on the read paths (recommended — smaller, no reconcile change), or (b) have promote repair the session pointer (upholds resolution (a)'s both-pointers-together invariant). | **(F19, found by coordinator review of Task 2-2, 2026-07-20) Reconcile populations 4 and 5 are UNREACHABLE when a repo has no worktrees rows — a gap in ImplementationSpec-2-1 §7, faithfully implemented.** `reconcileAll()` derives its repo list **from the rows themselves** (`getAllWorktrees()` → group by `repoRoot`), so a repo with **zero** rows produces zero groups, `listWorktrees` is never called for it, and the pure core never receives the git/filesystem evidence that would trigger `adopt` (P4) or `surface-orphan-dir` (P5). The pure core handles both correctly — it is simply never invoked. **Proven live on this machine 2026-07-20:** the `wt-39b6f2fe` worktree + its `chorus/Chorus/39b6f2fe` branch exist on disk and in `git worktree list`, this machine's `worktrees` table has 0 rows, and a cold boot logged `[worktrees] reconcile: 0 row(s) across 0 repo(s); 0 surfaced` — no adoption, no surfacing. **Consequence:** an untracked worktree under a managed root is invisible to Chorus unless that repo already has ≥1 row; the D26 Q3 population-4 rule ("found untracked worktree → adopt as detached") cannot fire from a clean table. **2-3's `worktree:list` inherits the same blind spot** (it reads `getWorktreesForProject` only). **Fix (assigned to Task 2-3):** enumerate candidate repos from the **union** of worktree rows AND the projects table (`resolveRepoRoot(project.rootPath)` per project, deduped by `pathKey`), so a repo with zero rows is still scanned. **The spec, not the implementer, is at fault** — IS-2-1 §7's sketch prescribed the row-derived enumeration verbatim. | `worktrees.ts::reconcileAll`, IS-2-1 §7 / IS-2-3 amended | 2026-07-20 |

Execution prompts should state this as a known condition rather than a suspicion. | coordinator cross-check at `94f062c` | 2026-07-20 |
| **(F31, Task 3a-3, 2026-07-25) ⚠ F20's WORKAROUND RESTORES THE DATABASE, NOT THE VAULT — `--user-data-dir` does not restore the DPAPI context, and credential blobs written under a different one are UNDECRYPTABLE.** Booting the dev app from a raw harness shell with `--user-data-dir=…\Claude_pzs8sxrjxfjjc\LocalCache\Roaming\chorus` opens the **right database** (proven: projects `985d547b…`/`f47ac10b…`, migrations v1–v7 with their real timestamps) — but **both credential blobs written on 2026-07-24 failed to decrypt**: `[vault] decrypt failed; profile marked unavailable`, for `OR milestone key` AND `Claude fake key`. **A profile created in the SAME boot decrypted fine**, so the vault mechanism is healthy and it is the CONTEXT that changed. `safeStorage.isEncryptionAvailable()` returned `true` throughout, and the user-data-dir's `Local State` file was untouched since 2026-07-18 — so the OSCrypt key file is not the variable. **The mechanism is UNPROVEN** (most likely the packaged-container process identity the 2026-07-24 session ran under versus a raw process, but that was not demonstrated and should not be repeated as fact). **Consequences:** (1) an execution session that needs a REAL credential cannot inherit one from a previous session's vault — it must be re-entered through Settings, which is a HUMAN step and belongs in the prompt's preconditions, not discovered at minute forty; (2) **D33 clause 8 behaved exactly as designed and is now proven in anger** — the rows survived, both launches were refused **by label**, and `replaceProfile` → `updateCredentialBlob` cleared `unavailable_since` on re-entry; (3) a task whose acceptance depends on live spend should verify decryptability **before** its first billable step. **⚠ AMENDED 2026-07-25 by Task 3a-4 — the "it is the CONTEXT that changed" reading is TOO STRONG, and the correction matters because it changes what a future session should plan for.** Re-probed in a single harness boot: `Claude fake key` again failed to decrypt, **but `OR milestone key` DECRYPTED FINE** — the authenticated model refresh ran on it and returned 345 models. Two blobs, same vault, same boot, opposite outcomes. **So the failure is BLOB-SPECIFIC, not a blanket cross-boot context failure**, and a harness session should probe the specific credential it needs rather than assuming every pre-existing blob is lost. Clause (1) above is correspondingly softened: re-entry through Settings is the *remedy when a given blob fails*, not a standing precondition of every session. **⚠ THE MECHANISM IS NO LONGER UNPROVEN — identified and demonstrated both directions on 2026-07-26 (Task 3a-5).** `safeStorage` does **not** DPAPI-wrap each blob directly: it encrypts with **Chromium's OSCrypt key, which lives in `<user-data-dir>/Local State`** and is itself DPAPI-protected. A scratch copy of `chorus.db` taken **without `Local State`** therefore gets a **freshly generated** key on first boot — so every blob written in an earlier session is undecryptable while a profile created in *that same boot* decrypts fine. **That asymmetry is exactly what Task 3a-3 observed and could not explain.** Proven both ways in one session: copying the database alone produced two `[vault] decrypt failed; profile marked unavailable` errors and a failed attribution key census; copying **`Local State` alongside it** made the identical blob decrypt and the census return `4 live · 0 ours`. **Practical rules: (1) copy `Local State` beside `chorus.db` for any rehearsal or scratch boot; (2) treat a credential blob as bound to the user-data DIRECTORY, not merely to the Windows user — anything that relocates or recreates that directory invalidates every stored key.** | Task 3a-3 execution, `_verify/3a-3/boot1.log` + `step0-vault-diagnose.json`; amendment: Task 3a-4, `_verify/3a-4/vault-probe.json` + `step3-refresh-auth.json`; **mechanism proven: Task 3a-5, `_verify/3a-5/boot-rehearsal.log` (without) vs `boot-rehearsal2.log` (with)** | 2026-07-25, mechanism **2026-07-26** |
| **(F32, Task 3a-4, 2026-07-25) The live OpenRouter `/models` list, measured — and a model-id charset that a plausible regex gets wrong.** `GET https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/models` with **no `Authorization` header** answers **200 in ~257 ms**; top level is `data` + `total_count` + `links`; **345 models; 535,821 bytes (0.511 MiB)**. **⚠ The complete set of non-alphanumeric characters across all 345 published ids is exactly `- . / : ~`** — and **10 of the 345 carry a leading `~`**, OpenRouter's "latest alias" convention (`~anthropic/claude-opus-latest`, `~openai/gpt-latest`, …). An ingest charset that omits `~` — as Task 3a-4's own spec did — silently drops all ten on **every** refresh of the one route the app ships, which is how a real "some rows were dropped" signal gets trained into noise. `context_length` is populated on all 345 (so the nullable column is for honesty, not for this provider); **`expiration_date` is populated on only 7 of 345**, as a bare `YYYY-MM-DD`, one of them the sentinel `2098-12-31` — real enough to store, far too sparse to build a warning threshold on. | Task 3a-4 D4 pass, `_verify/3a-4/models-live.json` + `d4-record.md` | 2026-07-25 |
| **(F33, Task 3a-4, 2026-07-25) codex 0.145.0 validates config FIELD NAMES but not every field's VALUE — and only one local path validates at all.** `codex debug models` and `codex doctor` accept an invented `-c` key silently, so neither can be used to check a config override. The discriminating path is the TUI's own config load under **`--strict-config`**, which runs **before** the tty check: an unknown field is rejected **by name** (``unknown configuration field `chorus_not_a_real_key` in -c/--config override``). Against that check, **`-c model_reasoning_effort="high"` passes** and **`-c model_reasoning_effort=123` is rejected with ``invalid type: integer `123`, expected a string`` naming the field** — which is what proves the field is real, and proves it is typed **String, not an enum**. The control matters: `-c sandbox_mode="banana"` **is** rejected, with its accepted variants listed. **Consequences:** (1) no launch can fail at config load because of an effort value, so the four-level mapping is safe to ship; (2) `codex debug models` shows the reasoning ladder is genuinely **per-model** (`max` on 3 of 8), but **what codex does at REQUEST time with an unsupported level is NOT established** — observing it costs a real completion, and it is recorded as a gap rather than guessed. | Task 3a-4 D4 pass, `_verify/3a-4/probe-*.txt` + `d4-record.md` | 2026-07-25 |
| **(F34, Task 3b-1, 2026-07-26) A REASONING model can bill the ENTIRE output cap and return an EMPTY answer — measured, not reasoned about.** `moonshotai/kimi-k3` — **the standing OpenRouter route's configured model**, i.e. the one every task in this phase reaches for by default — is a **reasoning** model. Confirmed free and unauthenticated from OpenRouter's own `GET /api/v1/models`: it declares `reasoning`, `reasoning_effort` and `include_reasoning` in `supported_parameters`, and its published description opens by calling it a 2.8T-parameter open-weight multimodal **reasoning** model. OpenRouter's docs are explicit that *"Reasoning tokens are considered output tokens and charged accordingly"*, and that `max_tokens` must be **strictly higher than the reasoning budget** or no tokens remain for the final response. **MEASURED in Task 3b-1: a probe with `max_tokens: 60` returned `usage.tokensOut = 60` — EXACTLY the cap — with ZERO `choices[].delta.content` frames and an empty answer.** **The transport was not at fault, and that is provable rather than assumed:** the same call read `usage` off the final frame, completed cleanly, and raised no refusal. The identical prompt at a 1000 cap answered in **66** output tokens. **`createApiSession` yields only `delta.content` BY DESIGN** — a council transcript should carry the answer, not the chain of thought — **so reasoning text is invisible to the consumer while still being billed in full.** **⚠ THIS SHARPENS D64(2): the run cap must clear reasoning budget + answer, not merely "max output allocation".** A cap sized to the answer buys a paid-for silence that looks exactly like a broken transport. | `_verify/3b-1/RESULTS.md`, `_verify/3b-1/D4-VERIFICATION.md`; OpenRouter `GET /api/v1/models` + its reasoning-tokens docs | 2026-07-26 |

Asked for the trailing hour during Task 3b-1's drives it returned **`gatewayTotalUsd: $0.25834` against roughly $0.01 of actual drive spend** — consistent with daily bucketing plus unrelated account activity, not with a defect. **Per-call `usage`, read from each response's own final SSE frame, is the reliable per-member source; the analytics endpoint is not.** **⚠ This binds Task 3b-3, which owns per-member cost and would otherwise reach for the wrong instrument** — D64(2) already names the `usage` block, and this is the measurement that makes that a requirement rather than a preference. **It does not impugn 3a-3:** "% of spend attributed" is an account-level honesty metric and remains correct at that grain; the error would be reusing it at a grain it was never built for. | `_verify/3b-1/cost.json`, `_verify/3b-1/RESULTS.md` | 2026-07-26 |
| **(F37, Task 3b-4, 2026-07-26) ⚠ A SURFACE RENDERING SEVERAL CONCURRENT MODEL STREAMS MUST KEY EACH DELTA BY ITS SOURCE, NOT BY RECENCY — measured at 291 rendered fragments where 8 turns belonged.** Found by the FIRST live council run and **by no test**, and no test would have found it: every unit fixture streams one member at a time. A blind round asks **every member CONCURRENTLY**, so their delta events interleave arbitrarily, and the store appended each incoming delta to the **most recently opened block** — so every switch between members opened a new one. The run rendered **291 message fragments for 8 turns.** Fixed by keying the block on **(member, phase, round)**; the identical re-run rendered **7**, and a regression test now drives interleaved deltas from two members. **⚠ This is a STANDING CONSTRAINT, not a closed bug: any future surface that renders more than one model stream at once inherits the class** — the **native chat pane D45 commits to** is the named next one, and a multi-agent transcript view or a streamed diff review would be the same shape. **The general rule: recency is not identity.** A demultiplexer keyed on *"the last thing I saw"* is correct only while exactly one producer exists — which is precisely the condition the council protocol is built to violate. | `src/renderer/src/stores/council.ts` + Task 3b-4 drive-3 runtime observation | 2026-07-26 |

Two further weaknesses reproduced exactly as 3b-3 predicted: **one member produced 6 of the 9 dissent entries** (talkative-member skew), and **two "dissents" are noise** — text mechanically pulled from a `DISAGREE:` label whose prose says nothing challenges the synthesis, because the matcher cannot distinguish a non-objection from an objection. **Where native is STRONGER, all three are structural rather than qualitative:** provenance is machine-generated and exact; the dissent section is core-generated so the arbiter cannot drop it; the detection path is labelled per question — at **$0.0012 against frontier models**. **The honest statement: the pipeline works; these models at this cap cannot yet produce a document of the external run's completeness.** | `_verify/3b-4/` (drive-4 findings, preserved out-of-repo) vs `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-3b.0-Findings.md` | 2026-07-26 |
| **(F39, post-close tuning, 2026-07-26) ⚠ ONE COUNCIL MEMBER NOW REFUSES EVERY RUN ON A TRANSPORT BYTE CAP, AND ITS SPEND IS INVISIBLE WHILE IT DOES — measured across THREE CONSECUTIVE RUNS, never once succeeding.** `moonshotai/kimi-k3` failed all three, **and ⚠ CORRECTED 2026-07-26 SHORTLY AFTER FILING — IT WAS NOT THE SAME FAILURE EACH TIME, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS THE WHOLE DIAGNOSTIC.** Re-read from the transcripts rather than from memory: run `6d234ecd` (120 s deadline) → **`The response exceeded its time limit and was stopped.`**, the same failure `z-ai/glm-5.2` took in that run; runs `f105d716` (10 min) and `87082401` (15 min) → **`The response exceeded its size limit and was stopped.`**. **So it hits BOTH bounds `apiSession.ts` carries — the wall clock at 120 s, and then `RESPONSE_CAP_BYTES = 4_000_000` once given the time to reach it** — which is D63(e)'s *"nothing bounds the stream in bytes or time"* pair doing exactly what it was built to do. The measured fact is that this model **streams for over two minutes AND emits more than 4 MB of SSE frames for ONE answer**, and F34 already established that its billed output can arrive with **zero `delta.content`**. **The original wording here said "size limit on all three" — wrong, and corrected in place rather than silently, because the time/size split is the evidence that points at the reasoning stream rather than at a large answer.** **⚠ WORSE THAN THE REFUSAL: it reports NO `usage` block at all**, so its share of the spend is invisible to Chorus and surfaces only on OpenRouter's own billing page — meaning **Chorus's reported run cost UNDER-REPORTS the truth whenever kimi participates**, and F35's "per-call `usage` is the reliable source" holds only for members that send one. **⚠ AND THE RUN SURVIVES ONLY BECAUSE OF THE FLOOR: GLM + Qwen is EXACTLY the two-member minimum of D67 Q6's structural refusal rule**, so **one more member failure aborts the whole run** — there is zero margin, and the surviving council is the smallest one the protocol permits. **THE HONEST AMBIGUITY, recorded rather than resolved: whether kimi is pathological, or whether a 4 MB cap is simply too small for a reasoning model streaming its chain of thought, is NOT ESTABLISHED** — and **the fix depends entirely on which** (raise `RESPONSE_CAP_BYTES` vs drop the member). It should be **measured**, not assumed; a cap raised on a guess is how an unbounded stream gets re-authorised. | `src/main/services/apiSession.ts` (`RESPONSE_CAP_BYTES`) + three post-close tuning runs at `edc3133`/`ad64e66` | 2026-07-26 |
| **(F40, post-close tuning, 2026-07-26) The findings document now carries TWO `## Dissents preserved` headings — and the fix must NOT be the one that looks obvious.** With a frontier arbiter the synthesis writes its **own** dissent section, and **the pure core appends one UNCONDITIONALLY, by design** — D67 Q5 ruling 5C, corrected by coordinator verification item (b) precisely so dissent preservation stops depending on a string comparison being right. So the duplication is **two correct mechanisms meeting**, not a defect in either. **The cost is cosmetic but real: a reader cannot tell which of the two is authoritative**, and the authoritative one is the machine-generated appendix the arbiter cannot edit. **⚠ THE CORE'S UNCONDITIONAL APPEND IS THE WHOLE ENFORCEABILITY ARGUMENT AND MUST NOT BE THE THING THAT GETS REMOVED** — deleting it to de-duplicate would hand dissent preservation back to the arbiter's goodwill, which is 5A, explicitly rejected as unenforceable. **The fix belongs on the CORE'S HEADING (distinguish it — provenance is already labelled per entry) or on the SYNTHESIS PROMPT (tell the arbiter the section is generated for it), never on the append itself.** | `src/main/services/councilCore.ts` + run `87082401-9e0a-4387-b234-638deed648df`'s findings document | 2026-07-26 |
| **(F41, coordinator, 2026-07-26 — surfaced while ratifying D70, and previously recorded NOWHERE) ⚠ COUNCIL TIME IS BUCKETED AS `overhead` — i.e. EXPLICITLY NOT TASK WORK — AND NOBODY EVER DECIDED THAT.** `classify()` returns `overhead` for every non-workspace view (`attentionCore.ts:120`), so **every minute spent watching a council deliberate on a real brief is counted as Chorus-configuration overhead rather than as work on the project**. **The case that it is project work is not weak:** a run is recorded against `council_runs.project_id`, it is pointed at a brief inside the repo, and its output is written back beside that brief — by any reading of the word, that is work on the project. **The case for `overhead` is also not weak:** no pane is mounted, the user is reading rather than driving an agent, and the class vocabulary has no third option that fits. **⚠ THIS IS NOT A D70 QUESTION AND DOES NOT DEPEND ON ITS OUTCOME — it is identically true under the widened enum and under the reverted one**, because both map to the same class. **WHY IT MATTERS AND WHY IT IS TIME-SENSITIVE:** attention-minutes are one of Phase 8's two non-token cost dimensions, and per **D50** actuals **cannot be backfilled** — so every day this stands is a day of council time permanently recorded as overhead, and the longer the feature is used the more of it there is. **⚠ RECORDED AS UNDECIDED RATHER THAN AS A DEFECT:** `overhead` may well be the right answer; the finding is that it **fell out of the `!== 'workspace'` rule** rather than being chosen, and no phase currently owns choosing it. **ASSIGNED to Phase 3e** (§7). | `src/main/services/attentionCore.ts:120` · `attention_spans` has `class` but no `view` (`storage.ts:198`) | 2026-07-26 |
| **(F21, found by coordinator review of Task 2-3, 2026-07-21) The `-D` branch-force escalation is gated only by the RENDERER — main permits it on a CLEAN worktree.** `worktree:remove` computes `forceBranch: req.confirmation === w.path` **outside** the dirty branch, so a request carrying a path-matching `confirmation` together with `deleteBranch: true` force-deletes an **unmerged** branch even when the worktree is clean — destroying commits. It is **unreachable through the shipped UI**: `WorktreePanel.vue` sends `confirmation` only when `needsToken` (dirty), so a clean removal always attempts plain `-d` and surfaces an unmerged refusal. **Severity: latent, not live.** But it is the one data-loss path in Phase 2 whose safety rests on renderer behavior rather than a main-side gate — inconsistent with the defense-in-depth used everywhere else in this task (main re-checks cleanliness even though the renderer already read it fresh). The implementer flagged it themselves (summary deviation 7 / concern 2) and asked for a ruling. **Ruling: their instinct is correct.** D26(j)'s "same typed-confirmation acknowledgment" was written for the dirty-removal case; overloading that one token to mean two different things (destroy uncommitted files / destroy unmerged commits) is the defect. **Fix direction (deferred, NOT Task 2-4 scope):** a distinct branch-force acknowledgment — a separate field main requires before ever passing `force: true` to `branchDelete` — so the escalation is unreachable regardless of what any renderer sends. | `main/ipc.ts` `worktree:remove`; verified against `WorktreePanel.vue:112` | 2026-07-21 |
| **(F22, ground-fact CORRECTION, coordinator 2026-07-21) The `Chorus-Second` project's `root_path` is `C:\Projects\ContactEstablished` — the PARENT directory — not `C:\Projects\ContactEstablished\Chorus-Second`.** Read directly from the real dev DB. The Task 2-2 and 2-3 execution prompts both asserted the `…\Chorus-Second` path; the directory does exist, but the project row has never pointed at it. **No live defect:** `C:\Projects\ContactEstablished` is not a git repository (`git rev-parse` there fails), so `resolveRepoRoot` returns null, the project contributes nothing to F19's union enumeration, and the "not a git repo" inline state that Task 2-2 verified against this project still renders — for a different reason than documented. **Why it now matters:** F19 made `resolveRepoRoot(project.rootPath)` run for **every** project on every boot, so this row is on a hot path it was not on before. If that parent directory ever becomes a repo (a stray `git init`), it would be scanned and its managed root would resolve to `C:\Projects\.chorus\ContactEstablished` — a different tree from Chorus's own, so still no collision, but worth knowing. Prompts must stop repeating the wrong path. | real `%APPDATA%\chorus\chorus.db` dump | 2026-07-21 |
| **(F23, found by the Task 2-4 implementer, coordinator-confirmed 2026-07-21) A palette launch REPLACES the whole layout tree — surviving sessions become invisible and die at the next boot.** `layoutStore.insertLaunchedLeaf(target, id)` falls through to `createLeaf(id)` and assigns `this.tree = { version: 1, root }` whenever `target` is null (`src/renderer/src/stores/layout.ts:55-63`). Its comment says "the single root leaf when launching from the empty state", which was correct when the empty state was its only caller — **Task 1b-3's "Launch agent…" palette command made that path reachable with a POPULATED tree** (`App.vue` passes `splitTarget.value`, which is null for a palette launch). Consequence chain: every other pane vanishes from the layout; the sessions keep running in main (PTYs are unaffected); but they are now **leafless `running` rows**, and D16 Q2's invisible-process guard heals leafless `running` rows to `exited` **before any spawn on the next boot** — so those agents are silently terminated at restart. **No on-disk data loss** (agent work lives in files/worktrees), but the session and its scrollback are gone. **Pre-existing since 1b-3; NOT introduced by Task 2-4** — the implementer hit it with a driver script and reported it. **Fix direction (Phase 3):** for a null target, append-as-split against the focused pane (or insert without discarding the tree); the empty-state case is the only one that should ever replace the root. | `stores/layout.ts` + coordinator read of `App.vue`/D16 | 2026-07-21 |
| **(D26f trigger semantics — CLARIFICATION, not a defect, coordinator 2026-07-21) A live agent that is ITSELF in a worktree does not flip the launch default to new-worktree.** `resolveRepoRoot` inside a worktree returns the **worktree's own** toplevel, so worktree-resident sessions never match the main repo root and never count toward `liveSessionsInRepo` (`main/ipc.ts` documents this as intended D22 semantics). The Task 2-4 implementer flagged that Phase-2 acceptance wording ("launching a second agent whose cwd resolves to the same repo root defaults to new isolated worktree") reads broader than the implementation. **Coordinator ruling: the IMPLEMENTATION is correct and the WORDING is loose.** The default exists to prevent two writers in one tree; a current-tree launch collides only if another session is already in the current tree. Three worktree agents plus an empty main tree is four distinct trees — safe — and the default flips to new-worktree the instant a current-tree session exists, i.e. exactly when collision becomes possible. **Action: correct the acceptance wording to "a live session IN THE MAIN TREE"; no code change.** | `main/ipc.ts` launch-context; coordinator analysis | 2026-07-21 |
| **(F15, pre-existing app-wide CSS defect, surfaced during 1b-3 — NOT from 1b-3; FIXED in Task 2-1 chore commit `624f3da`)** `src/renderer/src/assets/main.css` (lines 3–9) contains an **unlayered** `*, *::before, *::after { box-sizing: border-box; margin: 0; padding: 0 }` reset after `@import 'tailwindcss'`. Unlayered CSS beats **all** `@layer` rules, so **every Tailwind v4 margin/padding utility app-wide is generated but nullified** (verified: `.pt-24` and `.mt-2` rules exist in the sheet yet compute to 0; non-margin/padding utilities like `max-h-80`, `w-[32rem]`, colors are unaffected). The whole UI (LaunchDialog's `p-5`/`mt-3`, the palette's `pt-24`, etc.) renders **tighter than designed** and has since the reset was introduced. Fix is **one line** (drop the reset or move it into a layer — Tailwind preflight already resets); **deliberately NOT fixed in 1b-3** (out of its file list). Candidate small chore before or during Phase 2. | `src/renderer/src/assets/main.css` + 1b-3 runtime observation | 2026-07-20 |
| **(F43, Task 3h-2, 2026-08-07) ⚠ THE ATTENTION REPORTER KEEPS WRITING SPANS FOR A PROJECT THAT NO LONGER EXISTS — and the delete path deliberately does NOT close it.** `attention_spans.project_id` is a SOFT pointer with no `REFERENCES` clause (v7's own note: telemetry must survive the session it describes), and `attention:report` (`src/main/ipc.ts`) is deliberately unguarded and fire-and-forget — its comment says *"a throw here would break the renderer's fire-and-forget send"*. So a renderer holding a stale project id after a delete will keep appending rows naming it, and nothing refuses them. `deleteProject` purges the spans that exist AT THE MOMENT OF THE DELETE (verified: 339 rows removed, 0 remaining), which is all it can honestly promise. **Not fixed here, on purpose:** guarding the reporter means either giving a fire-and-forget channel a failure mode it has no way to report, or adding a project-existence read to the app's highest-frequency write. **⚠ IT BLOCKS NOTHING TODAY** — nothing reads spans by project id for a project that is gone, and the attribution surfaces are account-scoped (F35). It becomes real the first time a per-project spend or attention view is built, which is exactly when the orphan would be counted into someone's total. **✅ FIXED 2026-08-07 (Matthew asked for it directly, after the phase's three commits), AND THE TWO OPTIONS THIS ENTRY ORIGINALLY NAMED WERE BOTH WRONG.** Re-reading the code retired both: the channel *"fires only on a real edge, never on a timer"* (`renderer/src/attention/reporter.ts`), so it is **not** the app's highest-frequency write and a per-report lookup costs nothing; and nothing needed to throw, because **`projectId` is ALREADY NULLABLE on that payload with exactly the right meaning** — *"the active project, or null — nothing to attribute to"*. **⚠ AND THE UNBOUNDED HALF OF THE LEAK WAS NOT THE ONE THIS ENTRY DESCRIBED.** `readInputs()` reads `this.report?.projectId ?? storage.getActiveProjectId()`; the FALLBACK was already safe (`deleteProject` reassigns the pointer in its own transaction), but the **RETAINED REPORT** kept naming the deleted project on **every tick, for as long as the app ran**, whether or not the renderer ever sent another report. The fix is two parts, both in `attention.ts`: **(1) `onProjectDeleted(projectId)`** — `onSessionExited`'s shape one field over, called from `project:delete` **before** the transaction, which clears the credited project from the retained report and **drops the run via `markGap()`** rather than re-pointing it (the purge removed this project's spans, so `openRowId` named a row that was gone and every subsequent `extend` would have updated zero rows); **(2) `applyReport` resolves `projectId` and `councilProjectId` against the projects table** and degrades a stale one to null, never throwing, which closes the in-flight race structurally instead of relying on the renderer having reloaded first. **⚠ `sessionId` IS STILL DELIBERATELY UNCHECKED — F4 IS NOT WIDENED.** A session id going stale is ordinary and `onSessionExited` retires it; a project id going stale means the row was deleted. **⚠ AND THIS TURNED OUT TO BE UNIT-TESTABLE, WHICH THE PHASE HAD ASSUMED IT WAS NOT:** `attention.ts` imports `StorageService` as a **type**, so the import is erased and vitest CAN reach the module (unlike anything touching `storage.ts` itself), and the tracker takes storage as an injected, explicitly substitutable dep. **9 tests added in `attention.test.ts`, and their value was checked by MUTATION: with the fix removed, 6 of the 9 fail and the 3 that pass are exactly the controls asserting unchanged behaviour.** **Runtime confirmation:** a project actively being credited (its span extending in place on every tick) was deleted; 16 spans were purged; **five ticks later, zero spans named it**, while the tracker went on writing normally for the successor project. | `src/main/services/attention.ts` · `src/main/ipc.ts` (project:delete) · `src/main/services/attention.test.ts` | 2026-08-07 · **FIXED same day** |
| **(F44, Task 3h-3, 2026-08-07) ⚠ A TRANSFORM CONTRIBUTES TO SCROLLABLE OVERFLOW IN CHROMIUM, AND AN UNCLAMPED DRAG AUTO-SCROLLS FOREVER BECAUSE OF IT — measured, not reasoned about.** The rail's drag translates the dragged row with `transform: translateY(dy)` and auto-scrolls when the pointer is within 24px of an edge. With `dy` unclamped the two feed each other: auto-scroll advances `scrollTop`, `dy` grows by the scroll delta, the transformed row EXTENDS the container's scrollable area, the pointer is still inside the edge band, and the loop never terminates. **Measured with the pointer held stationary at the bottom edge of a 110px viewport over a 240px list: `scrollTop` climbed from 0 to 5,499px against a true scroll range of 130px, still rising when the pointer was released.** Fixed in the same task by clamping `dy` to the first and last rows' edges — after which the same gesture scrolls to 130 and stops. **Recorded because the symptom points at the wrong place:** it looks like an auto-scroll bug, and the auto-scroll is correct; the defect is that a transform is layout-affecting for overflow purposes in a way `position`/`top` would not be. **Any future drag in a scroll container in this app inherits it.** | `src/renderer/src/components/ProjectRail.vue` (`recompute`) | 2026-08-07 |
| **(F45, Task 3h-1, 2026-08-07 — DECLARED DEVIATION, not a defect) `project:add`'s response gained `reactivated_from`, which is one payload field beyond the "only `projectSchema` changes shape" budget the phase set itself.** The phase's purity contract says *"One payload reshape, bounded: `projectSchema` gains `status` and `color_seed`. Nothing else on the wire changes shape"* — while the SAME two documents specify, in their task bodies, that `getOrCreateProject` must return `{project, reactivatedFrom}` and that *"`project:add` [carries] `reactivated_from` so the renderer can say 'Unarchived Chorus — it was in your archive'"*. **The detailed specification was followed and the summary line was not**, because `root_path` is UNIQUE: re-adding an archived project's folder returns THAT row, and returning it silently would put a project the user retired back in their rail with nothing said. **The field is required and nullable-valued** (`null` on the ordinary paths), so a renderer cannot forget to read it. **Recorded rather than absorbed** so the tally in D125 is not read as covering a change it does not. **✅ RESOLVED 2026-08-07 (Matthew asked for it directly), AND THE FIELD WAS DEAD — WHICH WAS THE REAL DEFECT INSIDE THIS ENTRY.** `reactivated_from` was computed in storage, carried across the wire, and **read by nobody**: `store.add()` returned only the project id, so a reactivation was indistinguishable from a fresh add at every call site, and **the user was still told nothing** — the exact hazard the field was introduced to prevent. **Removing the field was rejected as the wrong reading of "fix":** it would delete a capability both authoring documents specify in detail and re-open the silent-reactivation hazard, so the field was made to earn its place instead. **Three changes.** (1) New `describeReactivation(name, from)` in `shared/projectLifecycle.ts` — the sentence lives in the shared, tested module for the reason the rest of that file does, and it is **deliberately short because App's toast clears after 2.5 s**, which is now a pinned assertion rather than a hope. (2) `store.add()` returns `{id, name, reactivatedFrom}` instead of a bare id. (3) **A REACTIVATED PROJECT NO LONGER GETS THE CREATE FLOW** — the second half of the same defect, and it was live: re-adding an archived folder dropped the user on the "name and colour this project" screen for a project they had named months ago, which also implies the old one is gone. It now lands in the workspace with the project selected and the toast explaining what happened. **⚠ AND THE COMPILER FOUND A MODELLING ERROR IN THE FIELD ITSELF THE MOMENT ANYTHING TRIED TO RENDER IT:** `reactivated_from` was typed with the full `ProjectStatus` enum, which admits `'active'` — a value that **cannot occur**, because a project that was already active reports `null`. Narrowed to a new `PROJECT_TUCKED_STATUSES` / `TuckedProjectStatus` (`'hidden' | 'archived'`), end to end from `getOrCreateProject` to the sentence. That is the same "nullable would encode a state that cannot be reached" rule D120 applied to the row, applied to this field and missed the first time. **5 tests added; runtime-verified through the REAL folder picker on both branches:** re-adding an archived project's folder rendered **`"Unarchived Chorus (main) — it was in your archive."`** and did **not** open the create screen; a hidden one rendered **`"Chorus-Second was hidden — it's back in the rail."`**; and the control — a genuinely new folder — still opened the settings screen pre-filled with the folder name and showed no toast. | `src/shared/projectLifecycle.ts` · `src/shared/ipc.ts` · `src/renderer/src/stores/project.ts` · `src/renderer/src/App.vue` | 2026-08-07 · **RESOLVED same day** |
| **(F46, Task 3h-3, 2026-08-07) `getOrCreateProject` can hand a NEW project a `sort_order` that already exists, and only `listProjects`'s tie-breaks keep the rail stable.** A new project takes `countProjects()` for both `sort_order` and `color_seed`, which is correct on an untouched rail and collides after a DELETE: deleting one of four projects leaves three rows with orders 0,1,3 and the next project created takes 3 as well. **Observed in the scratch profile: `TR-Integration` and a freshly added `Chorus` both at `sort_order = 3`.** **⚠ IT IS NOT A BUG TODAY, AND THE SCHEMA PERMITS IT ON PURPOSE** — `sort_order` carries no UNIQUE index (a reorder passes through transient duplicates inside its own transaction, and SQLite unique indexes are not deferrable), and `listProjects` orders by `sort_order, created_at, id`, so a duplicate renders deterministically and the first drag renumbers everything 0..n-1. **What it costs is predictability of the INITIAL position of a project added after a delete** — it lands beside an existing project rather than strictly last. **✅ FIXED 2026-08-07 (Matthew asked for it directly, after the phase's three commits): `getOrCreateProject` now takes its position from a new private `nextSortOrder()` — `MAX(sort_order) + 1`, coalescing an empty table's NULL to **-1** so the first project on a fresh database still takes 0 rather than 1.** **⚠ `color_seed` AND `defaultProjectColor` DELIBERATELY KEPT THE COUNT, and the asymmetry is documented at the call site so it is not "tidied" later:** neither is a position — the seed indexes a THREE-token cycle and the colour a TWELVE-swatch one, duplicates are ordinary in both, and a seed duplicate is unobservable because every project created from v13 on carries a stored `color` and `chipColorValue` consults the seed only when `color` is NULL. **Verified at runtime, all three edges, none of which vitest can reach (`storage.ts` is unimportable under the Electron ABI):** deleting the middle of four projects left positions **0,1,3 with a count of 3**, and the next project added through the real folder picker took **4, not 3**, rendering strictly last with **zero duplicate `sort_order` values**; a brand-new database gave its first project **0**; and a second gave **1**. | `src/main/services/storage.ts` (`getOrCreateProject`, `nextSortOrder`) | 2026-08-07 · **FIXED same | **(F47, Task 3h-2, 2026-08-07, PRE-EXISTING — found while surveying the delete target) The dev database holds SESSION ROWS WHOSE `project_id` NAMES NO PROJECT, which the enforced foreign key should have made impossible.** `sessions.project_id` is `NOT NULL REFERENCES projects(id)` and FKs are enforced on this database (F16), yet 19 of the 27 session rows in the 2026-08-07 snapshot point at `a43b395d-…` and `b684e96e-…`, neither of which is in `projects`. **They predate Phase 3h** — nothing before today ever deleted a projects row — so they were written or orphaned while `PRAGMA foreign_keys` was off, most plausibly during an early migration or a hand edit. **⚠ IT MATTERS BECAUSE IT WEAKENS AN ASSUMPTION SEVERAL DECISIONS REST ON:** F16's "FKs are ENFORCED" is true of writes made through the app today, and is NOT true of every row already in the table. `deleteProject`'s ordering is derived from the CONSTRAINTS rather than from the data, so it is unaffected — but a future integrity check that assumes no orphans exist would report a false alarm, and a cleanup that deletes them would remove history nobody has decided to lose. **✅ RESOLVED 2026-08-07 (Matthew chose the disposition; the decision was his because the rows are his history).** **⚠ FIRST, THE COUNT IN THIS ENTRY WAS UNDERSTATED AND THE CAUSE WAS MIS-STATED.** SQLite's own `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` reported **22** session rows, not 19 — and a **THIRD** orphaned project id (`9cda691d…`) turned up in `attention_spans`, which `foreign_key_check` **never mentions**, because that column is a soft pointer with no `REFERENCES` clause. The cause was not FK enforcement having lapsed at write time: the two projects were **re-created with new ids** in the Phase 1–2 era (19–24 July), leaving their sessions behind — `a43b395d…` was the old *Chorus* row and `b684e96e…` the old *Chorus-Second* row. **The app cannot reach this state today**: the only path that removes a projects row is `deleteProject`, which purges every child in one FK-enforced transaction, and inserts are guarded by `requireProject` plus the key itself. **THE DISPOSITION, chosen over deleting everything:** the 20 sessions of `a43b395d…` ran in `C:\Projects\ContactEstablished\Chorus`, which is **exactly** the `root_path` of the live project `985d547b…` ("Chorus (main)") — and `root_path` is UNIQUE and is what `getOrCreateProject` keys on, so by the app's own identity rule that IS the same project. They were **adopted**, not deleted, which makes the record whole rather than shorter. **⚠ FOUR OF THEM WERE HEALED FROM `'running'` TO `'exited'` FIRST, IN THE SAME TRANSACTION**, and skipping that would have been the trap: they had claimed 'running' forever (the boot reconcile is per-project and no project owned them), so adopting them as-is would have handed a live project four 'running' rows for D16's restore contract to **relaunch**. The 2 sessions of `b684e96e…` were deleted — no live project has their root (today's *Chorus-Second* is rooted one level up, F22), so no project is honestly their owner — along with the 1 orphan span. **⚠ AND THE DURABLE HALF IS A CHECK, NOT THE CLEANUP.** New `storage.countOrphanedProjectRows()` covers **every table carrying a `project_id`, soft pointers included**, and `src/main/index.ts` reports it at boot — **silent on a clean database**, which is what makes it worth having. That the soft-pointer half is not theoretical is proved by the third id above. **Verified end to end:** rehearsed on a copy first (and re-run to prove idempotence: 0 changes), the repair refuses to run at all if its assumptions do not hold; on the real database `foreign_key_check` went **22 → 0** with orphan sessions and spans both **0**; the boot check printed **`⚠ 23 row(s) name a project that does not exist: sessions=22 attention_spans=1`** against the un-repaired copy and **nothing** against the repaired one; and the real app booted clean with the rail's **chip strip and project names pixel-identical** to the pre-migration screenshot — the only visible change being *Chorus (main)* reading **21 sessions** instead of 1, which is the recovered history appearing where it belongs. Backup kept at `_verify/3h/chorus.db.pre-f47-*.bak`. | `src/main/services/storage.ts` · `src/main/index.ts` · `_verify/3h/f47-repair.js` | 2026-08-07 · **RESOLVED same day** |

| **(F58, Task 4a-4 post-landing, 2026-08-13) ⚠ THE SCROLLBACK MIRROR WORKED PERFECTLY AND A RESTORED PANE STILL LOOKED EMPTY — TWO DIFFERENT MECHANISMS, ONE OUTCOME, AND THE OBVIOUS FIX IS MEASURABLY WRONG.** With `bbf6d32` landed the bytes were on disk and being replayed, and the user saw nothing. **The two mechanisms, measured in the agents' own mirrors:** codex emits **`ESC[2J`** and does **not** use the alternate screen (**4 occurrences of `ESC[2J`, ZERO of `?1049h`/`?1047`/`?47`**), so its fresh TUI wipes the replayed screen on startup; Claude Code **does** use the alternate screen (**`?1049h` ×2**), so the replay painted onto a buffer that has no scrollback and is discarded on the switch back. **⚠ THE OBVIOUS FIX — STRIP THE ESCAPES, REPLAY PLAIN TEXT — WAS TRIED AND FAILS TWICE OVER.** (a) **The escapes ARE the layout.** Absolute cursor moves are what put text at a row and column; removing them concatenates unrelated screen regions and produces garbage — measured output included `Claude Codev2.1.229` and `C:\Projects\ContactEstablished\Chorus◉ xhigh · /effort`. (b) **A TUI MIRROR IS A REPAINT STREAM, NOT A TRANSCRIPT.** One captured mirror stripped to plain text gave **44,958 non-blank lines of which 14 were UNIQUE — 3,211× duplication**, the same codex banner painted **1,798** times; a second, smaller Claude mirror gave **21 non-blank lines / 13 unique, 100% chrome**. Then `replayEpilogue()` appends **`ESC[r` `ESC[m` `ESC[999;1H` + 40 CRLFs**: reset the scroll region, reset attributes, park the cursor at the bottom, and **scroll the painted screen up into scrollback BEFORE the fresh TUI's `ESC[2J` arrives**. `ESC[2J` clears the viewport; it never clears the scrollback. **MEASURED OUTCOME, PER AGENT, AND THEY DIFFER:** **codex** — history is **VISIBLE by scrolling up** in a restored pane, including text typed before the quit (screenshot evidence). **Claude Code** — instrumented at the pane via `attachSession`: **`seedEpiloguePresent=true`, `altEnterBeforeEpilogue=0`** (the strip worked)**, `altEnterTotal=1`** (the **new** pty sent its own `?1049h`). So the history **is** preserved on the normal buffer and is unreachable **only while the TUI holds the alternate screen** — exactly how a terminal behaves while you are inside vim — and it returns when the agent exits. **⚠ WHAT WAS RESOLVED IS VISIBILITY OF THE LAST PAINTED SCREEN, AND NOTHING MORE.** A readable **transcript** was **proven impossible from this data** by (b) above; it could only come from the agent's own `.jsonl`, which **Phase 4a's own non-goals forbid reading**, and which **Phase 7 already owns** ("scrollback search; transcript export"). A session that boot-restore **heals to `exited` never spawns**, so `attach()` returns null, no seed is read, and the pane the user opens is empty even though a complete mirror is sitting on disk beside it, unread. **Three populations land there today:** a **credentialed** session, which D33/F26 deliberately refuse to relaunch without the key being re-presented; a session whose **cwd no longer exists**; and anything **beyond `RESTORE_CAP`**. **⚠ THE FIRST OF THOSE IS THE PAINFUL ONE.** A credentialed session is precisely the one the user must relaunch **by hand**, i.e. the one where "what was this agent doing before the reboot?" is a real question rather than a curiosity — and it is the one population the feature currently cannot answer for. **Not a defect in 4a-4:** the mirror is written, capped, swept and deleted correctly for these sessions; the gap is that nothing reads it outside the spawn path, and the spawn path is guarded for good reasons that are not being reopened here. **The fix is a decision about where else a seed may be read, not a repair** — and it is not Task 4a-4's to make. | `src/main/services/sessionManager.ts` (`restore()` / `spawn()` / `attach()`) · `src/main/services/scrollbackStore.ts` (`readTail`) | 2026-08-13 · **OPEN** |
| **(F60, coordinator, 2026-08-13) `deleteProject` PURGES THE SESSION ROWS AND LEAVES THEIR SCROLLBACK MIRRORS BEHIND — bounded, swept on the next boot, and worth writing down because it falsifies a sentence people will otherwise believe.** D121's delete removes a project's session rows **in one transaction**, and that transaction knows nothing about the mirror directory; the orphaned `.log` files survive until the next boot's `pruneOrphans` sweep finds them with no row to match. **This is NOT a leak** — the sweep is unconditional, runs at every boot, and was observed working (`[scrollback] swept 1 orphan mirror file(s)`) — and it is **bounded** by the same cap every mirror is. **⚠ THE THING TO CORRECT IS THE INVARIANT, NOT THE CODE (yet): "the file dies with its row" holds at the TWO `deleteSession` sites in `ipc.ts` and NOWHERE ELSE.** Every future path that removes session rows inherits the same obligation, and the obligation is currently discharged **per call site** rather than structurally. The `-c tui.status_line=[…]` token in `CODEX_BASELINE_ARGS` renders `Context 100% left` on a **FRESH** launch — **8 occurrences captured** — and renders **NOTHING** on a resume: control `codex resume <id>` **0**, `codex -c <status_line> resume <id>` **0**, `codex resume -c <status_line> <id>` **0**. **So it is NOT a `-c` position problem and cannot be fixed by ordering.** **⚠ AND THE OTHER `-c` OVERRIDES DO APPLY ON THAT SAME RESUME, which is what makes this codex's behaviour rather than a Chorus wiring error:** `-c model=` drew codex's own warning — *"This session was recorded with model `gpt-5.6-sol` but is resuming with `gpt-5.6-codex`"* — and a full baseline + `-m` + effort resume rendered `gpt-5.6-codex high`. codex applies `-c` on resume **generally**; it does not re-apply `tui.status_line` to a **resumed TUI**. **The consequence is silent, which is the whole reason it is written down:** `contextUsageCore.parseCodexContextLeft` matches `N% context left`, a resumed session never prints that string, so the progress ring stays **blank for the entire resumed session with nothing logged** — the same silent-failure shape `codex.ts`'s existing `tui.status_line` warning was written about. **⚠ RIGHT NOW IT COSTS NOTHING, BECAUSE NOTHING RESUMES. | **(F62, coordinator, 2026-08-13) ⚠ DISCOVERY'S `cwd` COMPARE IS EXACT AND THEREFORE CASE-SENSITIVE, ON A FILESYSTEM THAT IS NOT.** `discoverSessionId` requires **exact string equality** between the rollout header's `payload.cwd` and `context.cwd`. That strictness is mandated, not incidental — by the CR-4a.0 ruling and by **D140**: a wrong pointer resumes **someone else's conversation into this pane**, an empty one costs a manual relaunch, and those two failures are not close in cost. **But Windows paths are case-insensitive:** `c:\projects\chorus` and `C:\Projects\Chorus` are **the same directory** and compare **unequal**. The behaviour is asserted deliberately — `codexDiscovery.test.ts` carries *"rejects a different case — cwd equality is exact"* — so it is a chosen position, not an oversight. **⚠ THE FAILURE MODE IS TOTAL AND INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM NORMAL OPERATION, WHICH IS THE PART WORTH WRITING DOWN.** If Chorus's stored cwd casing ever differs from the casing codex records — drive letter, a user-typed path, a worktree path composed differently — discovery misses **100% of the time and silently**: the pointer stays **NULL forever**, the pane is fresh at every launch, and that is **indistinguishable from "discovery hasn't finished yet"**. **NOT changed in 4a-2 on purpose — loosening an identity compare is a DECISION, not a fix**, and a case-insensitive compare is **wrong on POSIX**. | `src/main/adapters/codex.ts` (`discoverSessionId`) · `src/main/adapters/codexDiscovery.test.ts` · **D140** · **F57** | 2026-08-13 · **OPEN — and DOWNGRADED by measurement, not by argument.** Task 4a-3 drove discovery against a real rollout: the header's `cwd` came back **byte-identical** to the launch cwd (`C:\Projects\ContactEstablished\Chorus`), so **the casing hazard did not bite on the path Chorus actually takes** — codex records the cwd it was handed. The finding stands as a latent hazard for user-typed or differently-composed paths, but it is **no longer a candidate explanation for a discovery miss**: F64 is, and F64 is total. **Re-owned by whoever fixes F64**, since a watcher-based discovery must answer the same equality question. |
