| **(F64, Task 4a-3, 2026-08-13) ⚠ codex WRITES ITS ROLLOUT FILE ON THE FIRST USER TURN, NOT AT LAUNCH — SO A BOUNDED POST-SPAWN DISCOVERY WINDOW CANNOT WORK, AND codex SHIPS WITHOUT RESUME.** Measured on the real app, codex-cli 0.147.0: Chorus spawned codex at **13:42:43** and `~/.codex/sessions/2026/08/13/rollout-2026-08-13T13-42-43-….jsonl` was **CREATED AT 13:46:02 — 3 minutes 19 seconds later, the instant the first turn was submitted** — with **both the filename and the header `timestamp` BACKDATED to the spawn instant**. So the header, once it exists, is perfectly matchable (`cwd` compared **byte-identical**, `timestamp >= launchedAt`, and **F62's casing hazard did not bite** on this path) — **the matcher is correct and the TIMING is impossible.** The file does not exist during any window that follows the spawn, because it does not exist until the user speaks, which may be minutes later or never. Every boot logged `[discover] no rollout matched this launch` at `info`, silently and correctly. **⚠ AND WIDENING THE WINDOW IS WORSE, NOT BETTER, WHICH IS THE PART THAT MAKES THIS A DESIGN FINDING RATHER THAN A TUNING ONE:** the header bound is `startedAt >= launchedAt` and has **NO UPPER EDGE**, so a long-lived poll lets a pane claim a rollout written by a **LATER** codex launch in the same cwd — a silent cross-claim of someone else's conversation, which is exactly the failure D140 ranks as worse than having no pointer at all. **A SECOND, INDEPENDENT COST:** `discoverSessionId` walks the **whole** `~/.codex/sessions` tree and `readFileSync`s **each rollout in full** to take its first line — measured here at **320 files, 499 MB, 2.5 s cold / 0.85 s warm per scan** — so the poll count is bounded by I/O rather than by patience. `ImplementationSpec-4a-3.md` §5 states the bounds that would fix this (*"today and yesterday only"*, *"first line only"*) and **4a-2 implemented neither**. **THE LIKELY FIX IS A WATCHER, NOT A POLL:** `fs.watch` on the sessions tree bound to the SESSION'S OWN lifetime rather than to a fixed window, which catches the file whenever it appears and reads only the file that appeared. That needs its own decision about the two-panes-one-cwd ambiguity, and it is a change to `adapters/codex.ts` — a file Phase 4a kept byte-identical on purpose. **Per `Task-4a-3.md` (*"ship claude-only resume and record codex as a finding — half the feature working correctly beats all of it working sometimes"*), the orchestration ships in place, correct when it fires, silent when it does not.** | `src/main/adapters/codex.ts` (`discoverSessionId`, `listRolloutFiles`, `readSessionMeta`) · `src/main/services/sessionManager.ts` (`beginDiscovery`, `DISCOVERY_POLL_DELAYS_MS`) · `_verify/4a-3/` | 2026-08-13 · **RESOLVED 2026-08-13 by `50662e6` — codex now resumes, demonstrated end to end (`_verify/f64/`).** Three changes, and the first two only work together: **(1) the wait moved into the adapter**, which now resolves when the rollout APPEARS rather than scanning once — bounded by the session's own lifetime, since a PTY that has exited will never write one; **(2) the time match gained an UPPER edge**, turning `startedAt >= launchedAt` into a two-sided window around the launch instant, which is what makes waiting indefinitely safe rather than reckless (the measured header skew is +413/+428/+520 ms, so the window is ~20× the observation); **(3) an ORIGINATOR STAMP made it exact.** ⚠ **THE STAMP IS THE part THAT ACTUALLY FIXES THE COMMON CASE, AND IT RESTS ON AN UNDOCUMENTED SURFACE — see D144.** `RESTORE_STAGGER_MS` relaunches panes 500 ms apart, so two codex panes in one project are always inside each other's window; cwd+time cannot separate them and both would have claimed the same conversation. The cost half is fixed too: the walk is bounded to the launch day and its neighbours, headers are read by bounded prefix (~18.6 KB) rather than whole 53 MB files, and every header is cached — so the 2 s safety tick reads nothing once warm. |
| **(F65, Task 4a-3, 2026-08-13) ⚠ "CONTEXT WAS NOT RESTORED" FIRES FOR CONVERSATIONS THAT NEVER EXISTED — D143(c) BLOCKED THE SPURIOUS ACCUSATION AT THE FRONT DOOR AND IT ARRIVED THROUGH THE BACK ONE.** claude writes **no transcript until the first turn**, so a pane that is launched (or restored) and **never spoken to** has a perfectly valid persisted pointer to a conversation that has no file behind it. On the next restore the resume fails, `classifyResumeFailure` correctly returns `not-found`, and the user is told **context was not restored — for a session that had no context to lose.** Observed twice unprompted during 4a-3's own verification. **⚠ THE RECOVERY ITSELF IS CORRECT AND COSTS ONE LINE** — the pane comes back fresh and working, no toast, no rail, no closed dispatch — **so this is a HONESTY defect, not a functional one**, and it is the same class of harm D143(c) rejected the findings' action item 6 over: *"a spurious accusation of data loss is worse than the loss it describes"*. **The obvious fix is forbidden:** knowing whether the conversation exists means stat-ing or reading a vendor transcript, which is exactly the pre-flight CR-4a.0 Q4 ruled out and this phase's non-goals forbid. **So the honest options are to distinguish "resume failed and there WAS output before" from "resume failed on a conversation with no turns" using facts Chorus already owns** — the scrollback mirror's size, or D130's hook-observed turn count — **or to soften the wording. TURNS, NOT OUTPUT: a TUI paints a banner and a prompt whether or not anything happened, so bytes on screen are not evidence of a conversation. **⚠ `notify:false` SUPPRESSES THE NOTICE, NEVER THE REPAIR** — the disposition is still `recover`, the pointer still cleared, the pane still relaunched, and a test asserts the quiet and loud dispositions are identical in every respect but the telling (fanning out instead would leave a stale pointer killing the pane, which is the regression **F66** was caught being). **This does not weaken Q4's never-silent rule:** Q4 forbids being quiet about a LOSS, and here there is none — and the quiet path still logs, at `info` rather than `warn`, with its reason. Measured BOTH directions on the real app, because only one of them is the interesting one: no turns → `info`, mirror notice count **3→3**, pointer still re-minted, exit still suppressed (0 toast-listener fires); one turn on record → `warn`, count **3→4**. **⚠ TWO RESIDUALS, NAMED. (1)** Granularity is per session ROW, so a row whose OLD conversation had turns but whose CURRENT pointer is fresh is still told; narrowing it needs turns keyed by `agent_session_id`, a schema change nobody has asked for. **(2)** Turns come from the hook listener, so an app running without one goes quiet on every recovery — the safe direction, and such an app is already running without its lights. |
| **(F66, Task 4a-3, 2026-08-13) ⚠ THE ADAPTERS' FAILURE CLASSIFIERS WERE NEVER TESTED AGAINST REAL PTY BYTES, AND AGAINST REAL PTY BYTES THEY MATCHED NOTHING — THE ENTIRE Q4 RECOVERY PATH WAS DEAD CODE ON ARRIVAL.** 4a-2 shipped `classifyResumeFailure` with regexes written in ordinary prose (`/No conversation found with session ID/i`) and unit tests that feed them **ordinary prose**. What claude actually emits through a pty is **`No` `ESC[1C` `conversation` `ESC[1C` `found` `ESC[1C` `with` `ESC[1C` `session` `ESC[1C` `ID:` …** — the renderer spells each space as a **cursor-forward-one**, so the regex matched **nothing at all**. Caught only because 4a-3's runtime gate 4 was **driven on the real app** and the recovery visibly failed to fire; the `[notify] toast shown: Claude Code exited (1)` line in that run is the fan-out running for a failure that should have been suppressed. **⚠ THE CONSEQUENCE HAD IT SHIPPED IS A REGRESSION, NOT A MISSING FEATURE:** a stale pointer would leave the pane **dead** where the pre-4a-3 app always came back **fresh**. **Fixed in 4a-3 by `flattenTerminalText` in `scrollbackCore.ts` — deliberately in MAIN rather than in each adapter's regex**, so vendor-agnostic terminal decoding has one home instead of being re-hand-rolled per adapter (the D45(1) argument applied to reading instead of writing); the unit test asserts against the **captured bytes**, not an invented fixture. **THE GENERAL LESSON, WHICH IS WHY THIS IS A FINDING AND NOT JUST A FIXED BUG: any adapter contract whose input is "terminal output" needs at least one test whose input came OUT OF A TERMINAL.** A fixture written by the same person who wrote the regex proves only that they agree with themselves. | `src/main/services/scrollbackCore.ts` (`flattenTerminalText`) · `src/main/adapters/claude.ts` + `codex.ts` (`classifyResumeFailure`) · `_verify/4a-3/` | 2026-08-13 · **RESOLVED 2026-08-13 by `756066c`** — the lesson stays open |

| **(F68, Task 4-1, 2026-08-13) ⚠ ONE FULL-SUITE RUN FAILED A SINGLE TEST AND THE FAILURE WAS NEVER ATTRIBUTED — RECORDED BECAUSE THE NEXT TASK INHERITS THIS SUITE, NOT BECAUSE IT IS UNDERSTOOD.** The first `npm test` run after Task 4-1's edits reported **`1 failed \| 2007 passed`**; the failing test's name was not captured before the output scrolled, and it has **not recurred in 18 consecutive full-suite runs** (nor in 8 isolated runs of the new `agentEvents.test.ts`, nor in the two full runs that closed the task). **⚠ THE HONEST POSITION IS THAT IT CANNOT BE PINNED ON THE NEW FILE AND CANNOT BE CLEARED EITHER.** `agentEvents.test.ts` is deterministic by construction — `Date.now` is stubbed, ports are OS-assigned ephemeral (`127.0.0.1:0`), and the listener's derivation is synchronous in the same tick as its HTTP reply — but it is the only file in the suite that binds real sockets, so it is the obvious suspect and the evidence does not convict it. **What to do about it:** if a future run fails once, **capture the reporter output to a file before re-running** — the loss of the name, not the failure, is what made this unresolvable. | `src/main/services/agentEvents.test.ts` · `npm test` | 2026-08-13 · **OPEN — unreproduced in 18 subsequent full runs** |
| **(F69, Task 4-1 runtime gate, 2026-08-13) ⚠ CLAUDE CODE FIRES `Notification` *WHILE ALREADY BLOCKED* ON A PERMISSION PROMPT, WHICH MADE THE NEW `reason` FIELD DOWNGRADE ITSELF — FOUND BY RUNNING IT, INVISIBLE TO EVERY UNIT TEST.** Measured against **claude 2.1.229/2.1.232** in a real pane: a `PermissionRequest` arrived, and **~6 s later a `Notification` arrived with the pane still visibly showing *"Do you want to proceed?"***. That is the safe direction — both cases already show the same amber light, and a blocked agent under-reported is a human who never comes back. | **(F70, coordinator probe, 2026-08-14) ⚠ `codex` HAS A HOOKS SUBSYSTEM WITH A CLAUDE-COMPATIBLE EVENT VOCABULARY — SO "ONE PRODUCER, NOT FOUR" IS A STATEMENT ABOUT CHORUS'S WIRING, NOT ABOUT THE CLI'S CAPABILITY.** Probed against the **installed codex 0.147.0 binary**, prompted by Matthew asking whether reporting from codex was truly impossible. **Proven:** `codex --help` carries `--dangerously-bypass-hook-trust` (*"Run enabled hooks without requiring persisted hook trust"*); the binary carries `hook_event_name` — **the identical body field `readHookEventName` already reads** — and the event names **`Stop`, `PermissionRequest`, `Elicitation`, `SessionStart`, `SessionEnd`, `UserPromptSubmit`, `PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse`, `PermissionDenied`, `SubagentStart`, `SubagentStop`, `PreCompact`, `PostCompact`**, plus codex-specific `TurnStart`, `TurnComplete`, `TurnEnd`, `SessionConfigured`. **The three that drive `needs-you` are all present.** The config surface is `hooks.json` with `hooks.managed_dir` / `hooks.windows_managed_dir` / `hooks.command`; the shapes are `HookHandlerConfig {state, matcher, hooks}` (the same matcher-group form as Claude's settings) and `HookStateToml {enabled, trusted_hash}`; the engine is `codex_hooks::engine::command_runner`. **⚠ THE ADAPTER ALREADY HALF-KNEW THIS AND SAID SO HONESTLY:** `codex.ts:78` reads *"hooks: NULL even though the CLI has a hook-trust flag — the extension METHOD is unimplemented"*. **`hooks: null` was always a claim about what Chorus implements, and the phase documents compressed it into "three-state agents never report", which is true TODAY and false in principle.** **⚠ NOT PROVEN, AND EACH IS A REASON NOT TO ACT ON THIS YET:** (a) whether `-c hooks.managed_dir=…` works from **argv** — decisive, because **D49 forbids Chorus writing `~/.codex/config.toml`**, and argv injection is exactly the shape D130 already chose for claude (`--settings`, never the user's file); the accept/reject test was **inconclusive** because `--strict-config` validates `config.toml` only, not `-c` overrides; (b) whether a command hook can POST to the existing listener (**no HTTP handler type is evidenced** — the `url`/`headers` strings in the binary belong to the network-policy and provider subsystems, not to hooks); (c) **the trust model's friction** — `trusted_hash` implies hooks are trusted **by content hash**, so a Chorus-written hook may demand interactive approval, and shipping a flag whose own help text says DANGEROUS is not an answer; (d) whether the events fire where their names suggest. **Names in a binary are not behaviour (F66).** **✅ IMPACT ON PHASE 4 IS BOUNDED AND THIS DOES NOT REOPEN TASK 4-2.** Overview §5 already anticipated it — *"widening to a second producer is a decision, not a matcher change"*. The design absorbs a second producer cheaply: the classifier is one home, the reason map is one home, and the listener attributes by **token**, never by payload. 4-2's "three-state agents are absent" assertion stays correct and stays tested. **What must change is the PHRASING** before "codex never reports" hardens into four more documents. **`opencode` (1.18.18) is a weaker, separate case** — no hooks flag, but `plugin <module>`, a headless `serve`, `attach <url>` and an ACP server, so an event surface plausibly exists by a different mechanism. Not probed further. | `codex --help` (0.147.0) · the installed binary · `src/main/adapters/codex.ts:78` · `docs/Features/Foundation/Investigations/Codex-Hooks-Spike.md` · D49 · D130 · F66 | 2026-08-14 · **AMENDED AND ANSWERED THE SAME DAY — the spike was RUN; see `Investigations/Codex-Hooks-Spike-Result.md`.** ⚠ **TWO CLAIMS ABOVE ARE WRONG AND ARE CORRECTED RATHER THAN EDITED AWAY: `Elicitation` and `PermissionDenied` are NOT codex hook events.** Both were inferred from strings in the binary; `Elicitation` occurs there 239 times because of **MCP elicitation**, not hooks. The authoritative list is the `HookEventName` enum from `codex app-server generate-json-schema`, and it is 11 events — **`stop` and `permissionRequest` ARE among them, so the finding's substance stands.** **This is F66's lesson repeating inside the very finding that cited F66: a string in a binary is not a feature.** **Verdict: VIABLE-WITH-COST, and the recommendation is NOT to adopt now** — argv registration works and touches nothing of the user's (D49 satisfied, checksum-verified), but an **untrusted hook is SILENTLY SKIPPED** (no prompt, no error) and trust is content-hashed into the user's own `config.toml`, which Chorus must not write. **See F71/F72.** |
| **(F71, codex hooks spike, 2026-08-14) ⚠ AN UNTRUSTED `codex` HOOK IS SILENTLY SKIPPED — NO PROMPT, NO ERROR, NO LOG — SO ANY INTEGRATION BUILT ON IT FAILS INDISTINGUISHABLY FROM "NOT IMPLEMENTED".** Measured against codex 0.147.0 in a **real authenticated session**: the same argv-registered `SessionStart` hook produced **nothing** on its own, and `hook: SessionStart` / `hook: SessionStart Completed` plus its on-disk side effect **with `--dangerously-bypass-hook-trust`**. Only the flag differed, so trust is exactly what blocks it — and codex says nothing at all when it does. **⚠ THIS IS THE FACT THAT SHOULD DECIDE ANY CODEX-HOOKS PROPOSAL, NOT THE FEASIBILITY.** A codex user who never grants trust gets dark panes beside working claude panes, with no diagnostic anywhere; the support burden lands on Chorus for a decision recorded in a file Chorus does not own. **Anything built on this needs its own "configured but untrusted" surface, because the CLI will not provide one.** | `_verify/spike-codex-hooks/` · `Investigations/Codex-Hooks-Spike-Result.md` · F70 · F66 | 2026-08-14 · **OPEN — informational; no adoption proposed** |
| **(F72, codex hooks spike, 2026-08-14) ⚠ `codex` HOOK TRUST IS CONTENT-HASHED, SO EMBEDDING THE PER-SESSION HOOK URL RE-PROMPTS THE USER FOREVER — THE OBVIOUS IMPLEMENTATION IS THE BROKEN ONE.** Trust is stored as `hooks.state.'<key>'.trusted_hash = sha256:…` **over the hook's command string**. Chorus's hook URL is `http://127.0.0.1:<ephemeral port>/hook/<per-session token>` and **both halves change every launch**: two such commands hashed to `sha256:262a4e19…` and `sha256:18461a92…`. A hook written that way is untrusted on every single pane launch, and by **F71** that means silently dead on every launch. **✅ THE FIX IS PROVEN AND CHEAP: keep the command string CONSTANT and pass the URL in the ENVIRONMENT.** A fixed command reading `%CHORUS_HOOK_URL%` printed the injected value, and Chorus already injects env into the PTY at launch — so the hash stays stable and one trust grant survives. **Trust still dies on any Chorus upgrade that changes the command string**, which is a release-process constraint, not a bug. | `_verify/spike-codex-hooks/` · `Investigations/Codex-Hooks-Spike-Result.md` · F71 · D49 | 2026-08-14 · **OPEN — the design constraint any future codex producer inherits** |
| **(F73, Task 6-5 G2 drive, 2026-08-14) ⚠ A claude TRUST PROMPT FIRED IN A PANE NOBODY CAN SEE BECOMES A SILENT *REJECTION* — AND A REJECTED `.mcp.json` SERVER VANISHES FROM `claude mcp list` ENTIRELY RATHER THAN DEGRADING TO `⏸ Pending approval`.** Hit while driving the Phase 6 milestone. `chorus-memory` was absent from both `claude mcp list` and `/mcp`, with `~/.claude.json` showing **neither** an approval nor a rejection — while the repo's `.claude/settings.local.json` carried `disabledMcpjsonServers: ["chorus-memory"]`. **⚠ THE CAUSE WAS THE DRIVING METHOD, AND IT IS THE HALF WORTH REMEMBERING: launching sessions through Chorus's IPC (`window.chorus.launch`) rather than the launch dialog creates REAL sessions with REAL PTYs and NO VISIBLE PANE**, because an IPC launch bypasses the renderer's pane bookkeeping. claude's trust prompt fired where nobody could answer it, and killing those sessions recorded a rejection. **⚠ AND THE OBSERVABLE STATE IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM "CHORUS NEVER WROTE THE FILE"** — which is exactly the wrong conclusion, and the file was on disk and correct throughout. **The cheapest way to separate the two is an A/B: copy the identical `.mcp.json` to a neutral directory and run `claude mcp list` there.** It listed as `⏸ Pending approval` immediately, isolating the fault to the project's recorded choice rather than the writer, the file or the CLI version. **✅ REMEDIATION WITHOUT CROSSING THE BRIGHT LINE: `claude mcp reset-project-choices`** — the CLI's own documented command, which clears recorded choices and **grants nothing**. **The settings file must NOT be hand-edited** (the handoff's explicit warning; D49), and the approval itself stays the human's. | `docs/Features/Foundation/Tasks/Task-6-5-Result.md` · `_verify/6-5/` · D49 · D126 Q6 | 2026-08-14 · **RESOLVED for this run; the IPC-launch hazard is OPEN and applies to any automated driving of the app** |
| **(F74, Task 6-5 G2 drive, 2026-08-14) `last_seeded_at` IS NOT STAMPED AFTER A SUCCESSFUL SCHEMA SEED.** `memory:seed` returned `{ok: true, from_version: 0, to_version: 1, applied: ['identity-constraints-and-indexes']}` and the row's `updated_at` moved, but the following `memory:get` still reported `last_seeded_at: null`. **Cosmetic today because nothing reads it** — recorded because it is precisely the field a settings surface would use to answer *"when was the schema last applied"*, and it would answer **"never"** forever. **✅ NOTED IN THE SAME BREATH, SO IT IS NOT MISREAD AS A SECOND DEFECT: `cache_was_stale: true` ON THE SAME CALL IS THE SYSTEM WORKING** — Chorus's cached `schema_version` said 1 while the freshly created container was at 0, and the seeder trusted the database over its own cache. | `src/main/services/memoryService.ts` · `Tasks/Task-6-5-Result.md` | 2026-08-14 · **OPEN — minor** |
| **(F75, Task 6-5 post-milestone drive, 2026-08-14) ⚠ `codex` NEVER RECEIVES THE MCP SERVER: `mcpLaunchArgs` IS DEAD ON THE LAUNCH PATH, AND A CODE COMMENT ASSERTS THE WIRING THAT DOES NOT EXIST.** Found by driving a codex pane at the memory graph after the claude milestone passed. Codex answered *"The current Codex session only exposes the node_repl MCP server; **chorus-memory is not registered**"*, and the **process command line confirms it** — Chorus's codex launches carry `-c tui.status_line=…` and `-c developer_instructions=…` and **no `mcp_servers.*` argv at all**. **⚠ THE MECHANISM IS BUILT, UNIT-TESTED, AND UNCALLED.** `codex.mcpLaunchArgs` (`codex.ts:114`) delegates to `renderMcpLaunchArgs`, which correctly emits `-c mcp_servers.<name>.command=…` / `.args=…`. **Nothing in `src/` calls it outside the adapters themselves**, `PtyLaunchSpec` has **no field to carry the servers**, and `codex.buildLaunch` never mentions MCP. **⚠ AND THE MISLEADING PART IS A COMMENT THAT READS AS A GUARANTEE:** `mcpConfigWrite.ts:179` returns `NOTHING_TO_DO` for `launch-args` adapters because *"the servers reach them through `mcpLaunchArgs` on every launch, **which `buildLaunch` composes**"* — **`buildLaunch` composes no such thing.** The comment is why the gap survived review: it states the missing half as though it were the shipped half. **⚠ A SECOND GAP SITS BEHIND THE FIRST, so fixing only the call site would still not work:** `renderMcpLaunchArgs` emits `env_vars` (passthrough **names**) but has **no path for env VALUES**, and local mode needs `NEO4J_URL=bolt://…` as a value. Wiring the call alone would hand codex a server it cannot reach. **✅ WHAT IS UNAFFECTED:** `~/.codex/config.toml` is byte-identical, so **D49 holds** — codex is argv-only in the sense that matters, because it is *nothing*-configured. The claude milestone is unaffected and was proven independently. | `src/main/adapters/codex.ts:114` · `src/main/adapters/mcpConfigWrite.ts:179` · `src/main/adapters/mcpConfigCore.ts` · `Tasks/Task-6-5-Result.md` | 2026-08-14 · **✅ CLOSED 2026-08-16 by Task 6a-3 (`b2b73df`)** — argv now carries all three `mcp_servers.chorus-memory.*` keys, and codex answered a graph question whose figure (468 `:File` nodes) matched the indexer's own `files_seen` in the same run |
| **(F77, established 2026-08-16 by the architect pass RE-MEASURING the Task 6a-3 drive, 2026-08-15) codex RENDERS THE MCP SERVER NAME BOTH WAYS — IT NORMALISES THE HYPHEN TO AN UNDERSCORE ONLY WHEN IT *LISTS* ITS SERVERS, AND USES THE EMITTED NAME VERBATIM WHEN IT *CALLS* ONE.** Chorus emits `-c mcp_servers.chorus-memory.*` — hyphenated, from the single constant `CHORUS_MEMORY_SERVER` (`memoryService.ts:200`), whose docblock records that the name is *"the key the merge replaces"*, so a rename strands the old entry in every `.mcp.json` Chorus has already written. **⚠ THE FINDING AS FIRST WRITTEN (in `_verify/6a-3/RESULTS.md`, and carried into this pass's input document) OVERSTATED ITSELF, AND THE CORRECTION IS THE USEFUL PART.** It concluded that codex *"exposes the server as `chorus_memory`"* and that 6a-1's instruction text therefore names *"a string codex never displays"* — reasoning from a single transcript, in which *"a `chorus-memory` search returns nothing and the underscore form returns a hit."* **That is true of that transcript and false of its sibling from the same drive.** Counted this pass across both codex transcripts: **`answer-list.txt` (asked to LIST its servers) — `chorus_memory` ×1, `chorus-memory` ×0**, rendered as the markdown heading `### chorus_memory`; **`answer-graph.txt` (asked to QUERY the graph) — `chorus-memory` ×8, `chorus_memory` ×0**, rendered as `• Calling chorus-memory.get_neo4j_schema({})` and `└ chorus-memory.read_neo4j_cypher(…)`. Same agent (`gpt-5.6-sol high`), same project, same session. **So the normalisation is a property of codex's server-INVENTORY prose, not of its tool namespace** — and 6a-1's instruction string matches what the agent sees at the only moment that could matter, the call itself. **⚠ THIS INVERTS THE DECISION IT WAS RAISED TO FORCE.** The original framing left a live choice — *emit an underscore name, or accept the mismatch knowingly*; the measurement removes it, because emitting `chorus_memory` would make Chorus's instruction text disagree with **eight** rendered tool-call lines in order to agree with **one** heading, while also stranding every `.mcp.json` key already written. **Accept the mismatch; do not rename.** The durable lesson is narrower than the finding and worth more: **a finding that rests on "grep returned nothing" has assumed the corpus, and this one had a sibling file in the same directory that said the opposite.** | `src/main/services/memoryService.ts:196-200` · `src/main/adapters/instructionsCore.ts:20-28` · `_verify/6a-3/answer-list.txt:793` vs `_verify/6a-3/answer-graph.txt:485` | 2026-08-16 · **CLOSED by measurement — no rename, recorded so it is not "fixed" later** |
| **(F78, attributed 2026-08-15 during Task 6a-3 by STASHING rather than by reasoning) THE SUITE'S 5 s PER-TEST DEFAULT vs 46 UNCACHED `where.exe` SPAWNS — A STRUCTURAL FRAGILITY WITH NO PRODUCT DEFECT BEHIND IT.** `adapters.test.ts` calls `buildLaunch` **46 times** (counted this pass), and **every call runs `resolveCli` → `execFileSync('where.exe', …)`** (`cliDetect.ts:51`) — a **synchronous process spawn**. Under the full parallel suite one of those spawns occasionally takes multiple seconds on Windows, and whichever test happens to be holding it then blows vitest's 5000 ms default. **Attributed, not guessed:** the failure moved between **different, unrelated** claude-argv tests run to run, always at **5.2–5.5 s**, **never** when `adapters.test.ts` ran alone, and **never** on a stashed clean tree (3/3 green). **⚠ THE OBVIOUS FIX IS THE WRONG ONE, AND THE CODE SAYS SO IN ADVANCE.** `resolveCli` is **deliberately NOT memoised** — `cliDetect.ts:176` documents that a launch resolves the binary **fresh on every spawn** so an agent upgraded mid-session is picked up, and that the memo *"can be confidently wrong, not merely stale."* Memoising it to speed the suite would trade a real product guarantee for test wall-clock. **So there is no product defect and the fix was the test budget:** `testTimeout: 20_000` in `vitest.config.ts`, landed in **`b2b73df`**, carrying the whole measurement in a comment at the constant. **Recorded anyway, because the fragility is structural rather than spent:** the budget absorbs today's 46 spawns, and the next task that adds `buildLaunch` calls is spending against a ceiling nobody will think to re-check. **A green suite here means "the spawns finished in time", not "the spawns are fast".** | `src/main/adapters/adapters.test.ts` · `src/main/services/cliDetect.ts:51`, `:176` · `vitest.config.ts` | 2026-08-16 · **CLOSED as a test budget; the structural cause is UNFIXED BY DESIGN** |
| **(F79, Phase 5 kickoff D4 pass, 2026-08-17) ⚠ ELECTRON 43.1.1 GRANTS THE MICROPHONE SILENTLY WITH NO PERMISSION HANDLER INSTALLED — THE FOLK ANSWER IS CORRECT, AND THAT IS THE PROBLEM.** `Phase-5-VoicePlan.md` §4.3 flagged Electron's unset-handler behaviour as **NOT VERIFIED**, noted that Electron 43.1.1's own typings *"describe how to SET the handlers and say nothing about the behavior when none is set"*, and warned that *"the folk answer is 'it approves'; that is training-data memory, and it is exactly what CLAUDE.md's D4 rule exists to stop."* **Measured in a real Electron 43.1.1 process (Chrome 150.0.7871.114) whose main script installs NEITHER handler — i.e. reproducing `src/main/` at `4369954` exactly: `navigator.mediaDevices.getUserMedia({audio: true})` RESOLVED in 147 ms**, returned one live audio track, and exposed the device's label (`"Default - Microphone (fifine Microphone) (3142:a010)"`), native sample rate 48000, channel count 1. The track was stopped immediately and `readyState` confirmed `ended`. **⚠ THE FINDING IS NOT "THE GUESS WAS RIGHT" — IT IS THAT THE BOUNDARY IS OPEN AT HEAD.** Chorus ships today with no stated microphone position, and any renderer content that asked would be granted without a prompt from Chorus. It has never mattered because nothing asks; **D162 closes it in Phase 5's first task, before the first line of capture code**, rather than as part of building the feature that would finally exercise it. **⚠ AND THE SECOND HALF IS THE ONE MOST LIKELY TO BE MISSED:** Electron's own `setPermissionRequestHandler` documentation states that `setPermissionCheckHandler` must also be implemented *"to get complete permission handling"*, because *"most web APIs do a permission check and then make a permission request if the check is denied"* — so installing only the request handler leaves a hole that **testing the happy path cannot reveal**, since the happy path is the one that goes through the handler you installed. | `src/main/index.ts` (neither handler present at `4369954`) · `Phase-5-VoicePlan.md` §4.3, §12 · D4 probe, 2026-08-17 | 2026-08-17 · **OPEN — closed by D162 in Task 5-1** |
| **(F80, Phase 5 kickoff D4 pass, 2026-08-17) ⚠ A `blob:` AUDIOWORKLET MODULE IS BLOCKED BY THE SHIPPED CSP, AND THE BLOCKED PATTERN IS THE ONE EVERY EXAMPLE USES — D1's LESSON IN A FOURTH REGISTRY.** Measured under the app's **own** CSP, copied verbatim from `src/renderer/index.html:8` into the probe page: **`audioWorklet.addModule('./worklet.js')` from a same-origin FILE → OK**, `AudioWorkletNode` constructed successfully; **`audioWorklet.addModule(URL.createObjectURL(new Blob([…])))` → `AbortError: Unable to load a worklet's module`**. The cause is `default-src 'self'` carrying **no `blob:`** — the same shape as Zod-in-preload throwing `EvalError` under D1, as the missing `connect-src` keeping STT vendors out of the renderer, and as the missing `wasm-unsafe-eval` keeping a whisper WASM build out of it. **⚠ IT MATTERS BECAUSE GENERATING THE PROCESSOR SOURCE AS A BLOB IS THE COMMON AUDIOWORKLET IDIOM** — it is what most examples and several libraries do, since it avoids a build-tool question — so an implementer following ordinary practice hits it, and the failure arrives as a generic `AbortError` that names neither the CSP nor the directive. **The worklet ships as a file that Vite emits as an asset**, which per the phase's purity contract is the standing answer: *the response to "the CSP blocks this library" is to move or change the code, never to widen the policy.* **✅ AND THE SAME PROBE RETIRED A DIFFERENT WORRY:** `new AudioContext({sampleRate: 16000})` is **honoured exactly** on this machine — device native rate **48000**, default context **48000** — so Chromium resamples for us and the plan's expectation that *"resampling from the device rate is the likely real work"* does **not** hold. | `src/renderer/index.html:8` · `Phase-5-VoicePlan.md` §4.2 · D4 probe, 2026-08-17 | 2026-08-17 · **RECORDED — binding on Task 5-1; no CSP change** |
| **(F81, Task 5-2 D4 pass, 2026-08-17) ⚠ SILENCE DOES NOT TRANSCRIBE AS `[BLANK_AUDIO]` — IT TRANSCRIBES AS THE WORD " you", AND NO ENGINE FLAG SUPPRESSES IT.** `ImplementationSpec-5-2.md` §3 requires the parser to map whisper's non-speech markers (`[BLANK_AUDIO]`, `(silence)`, `[SILENCE]`, `[MUSIC]`, the bare `[…]` shape) to an empty transcript, on reasoning that is exactly right: *"A user who says nothing must get nothing — surfacing `[BLANK_AUDIO]` into an agent's prompt would be absurd and is exactly what a naive passthrough does."* **Measured against whisper.cpp v1.9.2 + `ggml-base.en.bin`, both freshly downloaded and byte-verified: those markers appeared ZERO times in ELEVEN runs.** Given pure digital silence the engine emits a plausible English word — `" you"` — at **0.3 s, 1 s, 3 s, 10 s and 30 s**, and identically for quiet synthetic room tone. **⚠ FOUR FLAGS WERE TRIED AND NONE SUPPRESSES IT**, including the one whose name promises it: `-sns` / `--suppress-nst` ("suppress non-speech tokens"), `-nth 0.9`, `-nth 0.99`, and `-nf`. The threshold flags do not simply break decoding — the `jfk.wav` control still transcribed correctly at `-nth 0.9`. **⚠ THE FINDING IS NOT "THE MARKER LIST WAS INCOMPLETE" — IT IS THAT A MARKER FILTER IS THE WRONG CONTROL ENTIRELY.** A parser built to the spec's letter passes every test the spec asks for and then **injects the word "you" into an agent's prompt on every accidental hotkey tap** — worse than the marker case, because `[BLANK_AUDIO]` is obviously wrong on sight while "you" reads as something the user might have said. **The defence is an ENERGY GATE BEFORE transcription** (`whisperCore.ts` `hasSpeech`), so silent audio never reaches the engine and an accidental tap costs no process spawn. Anchors measured rather than tuned: real speech `jfk.wav` **0.14210** RMS whole-file / **0.38238** loudest 100 ms window; **threshold 0.01000**; live microphone ambient on this machine **0.00150** (Task 5-1 gate 4b); quiet synthetic noise 0.00053; digital silence 0. **Two orders of magnitude separate live ambient from real speech**, so the number is not delicate. **⚠ IT IS A PEAK-WINDOW TEST, NOT A WHOLE-FILE RMS**, and that is load-bearing: a real dictation is mostly pause, so someone who says one short word in a ten-second capture has a low whole-file RMS and a high peak window — averaging would discard exactly the shortest, most deliberate utterances, which are what a voice feature is most useful for. The marker filter is **retained as defence in depth** (D159's `small.en` upgrade may behave differently) but **must never be reported as the silence protection**. **⚠ BINDING ON EVERY FUTURE ENGINE OR MODEL CHANGE:** this is a property of engine + model, so Task 5-4's `small.en` path and any whisper.cpp bump must re-run the silence fixture — `resources/whisper/PROVENANCE.md` records the procedure. **A second, smaller measurement from the same pass:** a valid WAV with **zero samples** makes `whisper-cli` exit **0**, print nothing, and write **no `.json` at all** — so "JSON absent + exit 0" is a legal outcome meaning *empty transcript*, not a failure, and treating it as an error would turn an accidental tap into an error dialog. | `ImplementationSpec-5-2.md` §3 (corrected) · `src/main/services/whisperCore.ts` · `_verify/5-2/F-silence-hallucination.md` (11-run table) | 2026-08-17 · **RECORDED — spec §3 corrected; binding on Task 5-4 and on every engine/model upgrade** |
| **(F82, Task 5-2, 2026-08-17) ⚠ A VENDORED EXECUTABLE UNDER `resources/` SHIPS INSIDE `app.asar`, WHERE WINDOWS CANNOT SPAWN IT — AND THE BUILD CONFIG ALREADY SAID SO ABOUT A DIFFERENT PACKAGE.** `electron-builder.yml`'s `files:` list carries `resources/**`, which ships as **app content**, i.e. inside `app.asar`. The same file already states the rule for node-pty: *"NATIVE BINARIES CANNOT BE LOADED FROM INSIDE AN ASAR ARCHIVE … Windows cannot spawn an executable or load a DLL from inside an archive at all"* — which is why `asarUnpack` exists there. **Dropping Task 5-2's 13 whisper binaries into `resources/whisper/` therefore packaged cleanly and would have failed at the first dictation, on the PACKAGED PATH ONLY.** **⚠ THE FAILURE DISTRIBUTION IS THE WORST AVAILABLE:** every unit test, every dev drive and the whole build pipeline pass, because dev resolves the binary from the repo working tree. Only an installed build breaks, and only when a user first speaks. **The fix is `extraResources`, not `asarUnpack`** — it copies to `process.resourcesPath`, a real directory *beside* `app.asar` rather than an unpacked shadow inside it, which is also the path `ImplementationSpec-5-2.md` §1 specifies. `resources/whisper/**` is additionally **excluded from `files:`** so the 9.54 MB is not shipped twice. **Both paths are now driven**: dev, and a real `electron-builder --win --dir` build that transcribed from `process.resourcesPath` in 1321 ms. **The general rule this records:** `resources/` in this repo has, until now, held only *data* (`icon.ico`), for which asar membership is harmless. It is now a mixed directory, and **anything executable added to it must go through `extraResources`**. | `electron-builder.yml` (`files:` exclusion + `extraResources`) · `src/main/index.ts` (`whisperDir` resolver) · `resources/whisper/PROVENANCE.md` | 2026-08-17 · **RECORDED — fixed in Task 5-2; binding on any future vendored executable** |
| **(F83, Task 5-1, 2026-08-17) ⚠ A `blob:` URL INHERITS ITS INNER URL'S ORIGIN, SO AN ORIGIN COMPARISON ALONE ADMITS `blob:` AS THE APP — CAUGHT BY A TEST, NOT BY REVIEW.** D162's `isOwnOrigin` predicate compared `new URL(requestingUrl).origin` against the dev-server origin. Measured: **`new URL('blob:http://localhost:5173/x').origin` returns `'http://localhost:5173'`** — the WHATWG URL spec has a blob URL inherit the origin of the URL inside it — so the predicate returned **true** for a `blob:` requester and would have granted it the microphone. **⚠ THE PLATFORM IS NOT WRONG AND THAT IS WHY IT IS EASY TO MISS:** such a blob genuinely *is* same-origin, and the shipped CSP blocks `blob:` documents anyway (**F80**), so nothing could exploit it today. **But "the CSP would have stopped it" is defence in the wrong place for a permission boundary** — it makes the security property depend on a second, unrelated policy staying correct. **The fix is an explicit scheme gate**: only `http:`/`https:` reach the origin comparison, and only `file:` reaches the packaged-root prefix check; every other scheme (`blob:`, `data:`, `chrome-extension:`, `devtools:`) is refused outright. **⚠ THE PROVENANCE IS THE USEFUL PART: a unit test written to assert "this is not `() => true`" found it.** The case was authored as an obvious-looking negative alongside `data:` and `chrome-extension:`, both of which already failed correctly; only `blob:` passed. A boundary predicate whose whole purpose is to say *no* needs its negative cases enumerated and asserted, not reasoned about. | `src/main/services/voiceCore.ts` (`createOwnOriginCheck`) · `voiceCore.test.ts` ("is NOT () => true") · **F80** (the CSP that masked it) | 2026-08-17 · **RECORDED — fixed in Task 5-1 before landing** |
| **(F84, Task 5-3 runtime gates, 2026-08-18) ⚠ `session:write` INTO AN AGENT THAT IS STILL STARTING UP SUCCEEDS AND THE TEXT IS SILENTLY LOST — "THE TARGET EXISTS" IS NOT "THE TARGET IS READY".** Task 5-3 validates the dictation target at write time with `SessionManager.isRunning`, which is true the moment the PTY exists. Measured while driving gate 5: main reported `inserted`, logged *"transcript written to the dictation target"* with 107 characters, and **nothing appeared in the pane** — the app had just restarted, `sessions.restore()` had relaunched the session moments earlier, and the Codex TUI was not yet reading stdin. Re-driven against the same session once it was demonstrably accepting input (a plain `writeSession` marker landed first), the identical dictation appeared in the composer verbatim. **⚠ IT IS THE TARGET-DEATH PROBLEM AT THE OTHER END, AND THE EXISTING GUARD CANNOT SEE IT.** D162-era reasoning covers a target that has GONE; this is a target that has not ARRIVED. Both produce a live-looking session id, and `isRunning` answers "yes" to both — so the transcript is handed to a process that discards it, and the user is told it was inserted. **The failure is silent in the worst way: the state machine reports the happy path.** **⚠ AND IT IS NOT SPECIFIC TO VOICE.** Any caller of `session:write` immediately after a launch or a restore has the same hole; voice merely made it visible because it is the first feature that writes on a schedule the user did not choose. The restore path is staggered by design (D16), so the window is real rather than theoretical. **No fix shipped in Task 5-3, deliberately** — detecting TUI readiness needs a per-adapter signal (a prompt match, or the hook spine's first event) that no adapter currently declares, which is a decision with its own ruling rather than an edit. What Task 5-3 does instead is make the failure *recoverable*: the original transcript is held in main and never overwritten (D161), so a lost write is not a lost dictation. **Task 5-4 should either surface the held transcript for re-insertion or take the readiness decision.** | `src/main/services/voice.ts` (`deliver`, `targetExists`) · `sessionManager.ts:608` (`isRunning`) · `_verify/5-3/` gate 5 evidence | 2026-08-18 · **RECORDED — not fixed; Task 5-3 makes it recoverable, 5-4 inherits the decision** |
| **(F85, Task 5-3 runtime gates, 2026-08-18) ⚠ A HIDDEN SECOND WINDOW STOPS THE APP EVER QUITTING, AND IT LEFT A SYSTEM-WIDE KEYBOARD HOOK RUNNING ON A MACHINE WHERE THE USER HAD CLOSED CHORUS.** Task 5-3 added the dictation overlay as a second `BrowserWindow`. Electron fires **`window-all-closed` only when EVERY window is gone**, and a hidden, non-focusable, `skipTaskbar` indicator still counts as a window — so closing the main window left the overlay alive, `window-all-closed` never fired, `app.quit()` was never called, **`before-quit` never ran**, and `uIOhook.stop()` was never reached. **Measured exactly that way while driving Task 5-3's "no leaked hook" gate:** the window disappeared, the electron process stayed up, CDP kept answering with `overlay.html` as the only remaining page target, and the log showed `[voice] global hotkey installed` with **no matching `removed`**. **⚠ THE CONSEQUENCE IS THE SEVERE PART, NOT THE LIFECYCLE BUG.** What outlived the app is a **global low-level keyboard hook** — `ImplementationSpec-5-3.md` §2 states the standard plainly: *"a global low-level keyboard hook must not outlive the process … it observes every keystroke the user makes in every application."* The user sees a closed app; the machine has a live keystroke observer and an orphaned process holding it. Leaking it is worse than leaking a file handle, and worse still because nothing on screen suggests anything is running. **⚠ AND EVERY SYMPTOM POINTS AWAY FROM THE CAUSE.** The overlay is deliberately invisible, out of the taskbar and out of Alt-Tab (`focusable: false`, `skipTaskbar: true`) — which is correct for an indicator and is exactly what makes it undiscoverable as the thing holding the app open. Nothing in the voice code is wrong; the defect is that a window was created and never tied to the lifetime of the window it decorates. **Fixed in Task 5-3 before landing:** `mainWindow.on('closed')` destroys the overlay, so the last real window closing still empties the window set. Re-driven with the overlay confirmed alive (two page targets): the app quit, CDP stopped answering, zero processes remained, and the log shows **both** `installed` and `removed`. **⚠ BINDING ON EVERY FUTURE SECOND WINDOW.** **D156**'s Fleet Switcher inherits this overlay's always-on-top plumbing and would inherit this trap with it; so would any future palette, HUD or picker built as its own `BrowserWindow`. **The rule this records: an auxiliary window must be destroyed with the window it belongs to, and any app-lifetime resource — a hook, a port, a child process — must not depend on `window-all-closed` firing.** A gate that merely checks "the window closed" cannot see this; the check has to be that the PROCESS is gone. | `src/main/index.ts` (`mainWindow.on('closed')`, `before-quit`) · `src/main/services/voiceOverlay.ts` · `ImplementationSpec-5-3.md` §2 · **D156** (Fleet Switcher inherits the plumbing) | 2026-08-18 · **RECORDED and FIXED in Task 5-3; binding on any future auxiliary window** |

## 6. Global Decisions & Gates

### Decisions

All **RESOLVED 2026-07-18** unless noted.

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | All Zod validation lives in **main only** — CSP forbids `eval` in preload. | RESOLVED |
| D2 | **No electron-rebuild.** node-pty uses shipped prebuilds; better-sqlite3 via `.npmrc` electron runtime + `/Od` source build (temporary — see §5). | RESOLVED |

| D4 | Verify CLI flags against the tool's own `--help` at build time — never from training memory. | RESOLVED (CLAUDE.md, locked) |
| D5 | Child PTYs inherit env untouched; no credentials injected/logged anywhere. **Will be superseded by the Phase 3 vault work.** **⚠ SUPERSEDED 2026-07-24 by Task 3-6's `15a016e`, exactly as foreseen.** Child PTYs no longer inherit env untouched: `composeChildEnv` (`src/main/adapters/env.ts`) builds a **closed allow-list** env — `BASELINE_ENV_VARS` = `PATH, SystemRoot, TEMP, TMP, HOMEDRIVE, HOMEPATH, USERPROFILE` plus the one secret variable named by `resolveEnvVarName` — applied at `sessionManager.ts:361` for credential-bearing launches per D33(c). The second half of the original wording **still stands and is now enforced rather than assumed**: no credential ever reaches argv, logs, or transcripts (G4 clean across 6 patterns at `15a016e`). Kept here per supersede-never-delete; read D33, D46, D47 and D49 for the operative contract. | SUPERSEDED 2026-07-24 (`15a016e`) |
| D6 | Council Review runs in Cursor by Matthew; Claude flags, briefs, pauses, and records findings (§4). | RESOLVED |
| D7 | **Adopt Drizzle ORM now** — Phase 1 migrates the existing 4 tables' access code to Drizzle and defines all new Phase-1 schema in it. (Matthew's call at Phase 1 kickoff, against the deferral recommendation — typed queries from day one won.) | RESOLVED 2026-07-18 |
| D8 | **Adopt Tailwind CSS at sub-phase 1.1** — first real UI mass; existing scoped styles migrate as touched. | RESOLVED 2026-07-18 |
| D9 | Pane layout engine (CR-1.2): **Option C — owned binary split tree as persisted data model; splitpanes@~4.1.2 as dumb grid renderer behind a `LayoutRenderer.vue` adapter.** Council verdict unanimous 3-of-3 (Claude, Gemini, GPT); Gemini dissent-on-preference for full custom (B) recorded, conceded on implementation risk. **Escape hatch: if the xterm-in-splitpanes spike (timeboxed, go/no-go) fails, fall back to B — the tree model carries over unchanged.** Serialized schema: versioned binary tree, leaves bind `sessionId`, ratios clamped [0.05, 0.95], invariants Zod-enforced in main per D1. PTY resize: continuous `fit()`, debounced `pty.resize` (150 ms / drag-end). Brief: `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-1.2-LayoutEngine.md` · Findings: `docs/architecture/CR-1.2-pane-layout-council-findings.md`. splitpanes 4.1.2 existence/recency verified on npm 2026-07-18. **Spike result (Task 1-3, 2026-07-19): GO** — xterm-in-splitpanes passed all 12 checks at window widths 1024/1440/2560: canvases paint, splitter/canvas layer cleanly (screenshotted), ResizeObserver on our pane containers fires mid-drag (21 callbacks sampled with the mouse button still held; ~7 `@resize` emits per drag), `fit()` yields plausible cols/rows (e.g. 1200 px → 144 cols, 861 px → 53 rows). v4.1.2 API verified from shipped typings/source (D4): `@resize` payload is `{event, index, prevPane, nextPane, panes: [{min,max,size}]}` fired per frame during drag, `resized` fires at drag-end — ratio write-back reads `panes[0].size / 100`, NOT the spec sketch's `sizes[]`. splitpanes@~4.1.2 installed. | RESOLVED 2026-07-18 (council, unanimous) |

**Decisions from the Task 1-2 completion review** (`Tasks/Task-1-2-CompletionSummary.md`), ratified 2026-07-19:

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D10 | **Session store rekey (`AgentKind` → `sessionId`) belongs to Task 1-4, as its first step.** Task 1-3 leaves the store per-kind and keys only *pane components* by `sessionId` — with one session per kind the agent-keying is still correct, and rekeying inside a layout task is unverifiable until multi-session exists. Task 1-4 cannot skip it: two Codex sessions sharing one `sessions['codex']` slot would break the task's own headline criterion. Task-1-4.md amended 2026-07-19 (its "Initial Starting Point" had wrongly claimed the rekey was already done). | RESOLVED 2026-07-19 |
| D11 | **`storage.updateSessionStatus` is wired in Task 1-4**, at `watchSessionExits` in `src/main/index.ts` — persisted status only becomes meaningful once 1-4/1-5 restore sessions on boot. Task 1-3 confirmed the symptom (F4: closed panes leave rows stuck at `status='running'`). **Action closed 2026-07-19** — `src/main/index.ts` admitted to Task-1-4.md's scope table for this single purpose, with a step and an acceptance criterion. | RESOLVED 2026-07-19 |
| D12 | **Session rows are born `status='running'` before any PTY attaches.** Accepted as informational until D11 lands; no schema change. Nothing reads `sessions[].status` today. | RESOLVED 2026-07-19 |
| D14 | **Renderer→main IPC payloads must be plain objects.** Pinia state is a Vue reactive Proxy and Electron's structured clone refuses it — `Error: An object could not be cloned`, with **no compile-time signal**. Found at runtime in Task 1-3 (`setLayout(this.tree)`); fixed by sending `JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(tree))` from the store's persist path. Binds every task from here on: anything sourced from a store or `reactive()`/`ref()` gets snapshotted before crossing the bridge. **Promoted into `CLAUDE.md` 2026-07-19** (Matthew's approval at the 1-4 review). | RESOLVED 2026-07-19 |
| D13 | **`getPaneLayout` normalization is read-path, in-memory only** — a corrupted-but-parseable tree self-heals on the first `savePaneLayout`, not on read. Accepted: silent write-back on read would muddy the lazy-conversion semantics. | RESOLVED 2026-07-19 |

Also ratified from that review, no ID needed: `z.uuid()` over the Zod-4-deprecated `z.string().uuid()`; `layout:get` responses parsed outbound in main as well as inbound; first-run seeding of two default panes stands until Task 1-4 switches it to an empty layout (already carried in Task-1-4.md).

**Decision from the Task 1-4 completion review** (`Tasks/Task-1-4-CompletionSummary.md`), ratified 2026-07-19:

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D15 | **Task 1-4's five deviations ratified as the session-lifecycle contract.** (1) `attach` carries an optional **`respawn` flag** — plain view attach *never* spawns (F5 makes attach re-run on sibling-close remounts; an ungated attach resurrected killed sessions); only the Restart chrome sends `respawn: true`, after kill + awaited exit. (2) On a respawn attach, main flips the row to `running`/null — D11 in both directions. (3) An attach for a manager-unknown id reports `status: 'exited'` **even if the persisted row says `running`** — the row supplies only the exit code; the manager's map is the sole liveness authority within a run (F6). (4) Recent-cwds + project root travel over a dedicated `session:launch-context` channel. (5) Settings stay inline-Drizzle per key (`getWindowBounds` pattern); no generic get/setSetting pair. **Post-restart Restart is deliberately a no-op** (`respawn` on an unknown id spawns nothing) — whether that button relaunches after restart is 1-5's restore-contract decision; the `respawn` gate is the seam. **⚠ Items (1)–(2) superseded by D16 (CR-1.5 Q4, 2026-07-19):** the `respawn` flag is removed in Task 1-5; *all* respawn routes through the launch path gated on an existing row, and `attach` becomes a pure view binding with no spawn path at all. Items (3)–(5) stand. | RESOLVED 2026-07-19 · partially superseded by D16 |

**Decision from Council Review CR-1.5** (the session restore contract), findings filed and ratified 2026-07-19:

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D16 | **The session restore contract** (CR-1.5, 3-model council; brief `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-1.5-RestoreContract.md`, findings `CouncilBriefs/CouncilFindings-1.5-RestoreContract.md`). **Q1 — reconcile-on-boot (C), 2-of-3.** No schema change; `status='running'` read through a deterministic boot/activation reconcile pass; quit and crash converge by construction. *Gemini dissent preserved:* a `desired_state` intent column (B) is the cleaner model — adopt it when a user-facing "don't restore" toggle lands (Phase 2+). **Q2 — restore set, unanimous:** `{leaf.sessionId | leaf ∈ layout ∧ row.status='running'}`. Leaves∩exited → exited chrome + Restart; leaves-without-row → placeholder; **`running` rows without a leaf are healed to `exited` before any spawn** (the invisible-process guard). `session:delete` IPC ships (rejects live sessions). **Q3 — guarded-auto-relaunch, 2-of-3:** cwd `existsSync` before each spawn (missing → exited chrome, "Working directory not found"); 500 ms spawn stagger (250 ms if ConPTY tolerates); a transient "Session restarted — new conversation" badge (~5 s) on every restored pane; pane cap 16. *GPT dissent preserved:* affordance-driven ("Relaunch all") is more honest — the revert is renderer-only if auto proves confusing. **Q4 — unanimous:** Restart (in-run *and* post-restart) routes through a new `session:restart` channel → the launch path under the existing row id, with cwd re-validation; the D15 `respawn` attach flag is **removed** (see D15 supersession). **Coordinator resolutions (Matthew-approved 2026-07-19):** (a) `status='running'` is written **only after** spawn succeeds — supersedes findings Q4 step 3, per the findings' own Risk 1; (b) the "PID-prefix orphan scan" mitigation is dropped (contradicts the findings' own Q5 rejection of PID tracking); (c) the "Choose directory" re-homing action is trimmed to the not-found message — re-homing is Phase 2; (d) no context-menu/session-list UI — **pane close deletes the session row** after kill/exit completes (leafless rows are unreachable under the Q2 restore set, so close-flow deletion is the coherent cleanup); channels are singular (`session:delete`/`session:restart`); cwd-missing renders as its own chrome state, not a sentinel `exit_code=-1`; findings action item 7 (layout-tree migration) was already satisfied by `81e8a0b`. **Implemented verbatim in `fb384c5`** — the five-clause contract is in that commit's message; all 14 runtime steps individually verified (see `Tasks/Task-1-5-CompletionSummary.md`). **Note (F14, 1b-3):** `session:restart` emits no renderer event — restart drivers outside `TerminalPane` must flip the session store themselves; Gemini's `desired_state` dissent above remains the natural home for a future "don't restore" toggle. | RESOLVED 2026-07-19 · IMPLEMENTED `fb384c5` |
| D17 | **Task 1-5's six deviations ratified** (completion review, 2026-07-19). (1) **Consume-once restored badge** (F10) — a `restoredUnbadged` set consumed by first attach, replacing the sketched recency window: dev cold starts outlast any fixed timing window, and the boot event fires before any pane exists. (2) **`restore()` runs on every `project:select`**, not on tracked first-activation — `computeRestoreSet`'s live-guard makes re-runs idempotent, so the activation set was needless state. (3) `storage.getSessionById` added (restart reads a row without project context). (4) `src/main/db/schema.ts` touched **comment-only** ("respawns"→"re-creation") so the `grep -ri respawn src/` criterion returns literally nothing — no schema/migration change. (5) Stagger stays 500 ms (ConPTY never showed stress; the 250 ms condition never arose). (6) `restore()` is the one documented place the manager touches storage — the spec's own §4 sketch prescribed this; `launch`/`attach` keep the IPC-owns-rows division. | RESOLVED 2026-07-19 |

**Decisions at the Phase 1b kickoff** (Matthew, 2026-07-19):

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D18 | **Title source: OSC + first-line fallback.** Titles come from terminal-title escape sequences (xterm `Terminal.onTitleChange`, OSC 0/2 — API confirmed in the installed `@xterm/xterm` 6 typings, re-verified at execution per D4); fallback = the first Enter-terminated line typed into the pane, only while the title is still null. OSC keeps updating live. **No LLM summarization** (Phase 3+, when BYOK keys exist). Whether the CLIs actually emit OSC titles is unverified until execution — the fallback is the guaranteed path; the implementer reports which mechanism fired per CLI. **Outcome (`a00af48`, mechanism report in the commit message) — the "honest unknown" is resolved:** **both CLIs emit OSC 0/2 at spawn, before any keystroke**, so the first-line fallback is effectively unreachable in a live pane for these two. Claude Code 2.1.215 sets `✳ Claude Code` and **animates a spinner in the title** while working (`⠂`/`⠐ Claude Code`, ~1 Hz, settling back to `✳`); Codex 0.144.6 sets the **cwd basename** (`Chorus`). The fallback was still exercised genuinely on an **exited** pane (no OSC source): captured a typed line, sanitized in the DB (raw shown in-renderer until next attach), ellipsis-truncated with full-text tooltip, survived restart seeded purely from the DB row. Debounce on real OSC traffic: 5 changes in ~3.4 s → 4 writes (correct trailing, no per-redraw flood). D14: zero `An object could not be cloned` across the flow. | RESOLVED 2026-07-19 · VERIFIED `a00af48` |
| D19 | **Migration v3: nullable `title` TEXT on `sessions`**, via the hand-rolled MIGRATIONS runner + Drizzle schema. "DB schema growth" is technically a §4 CR trigger; **council waived** for one trivially-reversible nullable column (coordinator recommendation, Matthew's call). Matches PLAN §269's target schema. | RESOLVED 2026-07-19 |
| D20 | **View state per-project in `settings`** (key `view_state:<projectId>`, JSON `{mode, focusedSessionId}`) over a small Zod IPC. **Filmstrip is the default** (PLAN §183), including for existing DBs; grid is the alternate. `focusedSessionId` is never FK-checked — it legitimately goes stale (F4); views resolve staleness by falling back to the first leaf. **Implemented verbatim in 1b-2; all 10 runtime items individually verified** (filmstrip default on a no-row DB, byte-identical tree across focus clicks, F5 continued-output proof, per-project persistence across a tree-kill restart, stale-focus fallback with a hand-edited bogus id, cards never badge, multi-project isolation, zero structured-clone errors); the flagged `sessionInfoSchema` `exitCode` addition and the minimal grid-focus path are recorded in the commit message. | RESOLVED 2026-07-19 · IMPLEMENTED `3a28ec2` |
| D21 | **Palette skeleton = five commands** over an extensible registry: launch agent, switch project, focus pane (by title/agent), toggle filmstrip/grid, restart focused. In-repo fuzzy subsequence filter — **no new dependency**. Further commands, shortcuts (`Ctrl+T`/`Ctrl+1..9`/`Ctrl+Tab`), and MRU are later phases. **Outcome (`72ea471`):** five commands over the pure `buildCommands` registry shipped (focus/switch groups expand per leaf/project); the **capture-phase window hotkey won** (D4 report — verified over a focused xterm; `attachCustomKeyEventHandler` stays the unused fallback); **disabled commands are omitted** (`fuzzyFilter` returns `enabled()` only); the fuzzy subsequence filter lives in-repo with no new dependency; and the **F14 store-flip deviation** (palette restart flips the session store itself because `session:restart` emits no event) was ratified. | RESOLVED 2026-07-19 · IMPLEMENTED `72ea471` |

**Decisions at the Phase 2 kickoff** (Matthew, 2026-07-20):

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D22 | **Phase 2 workspace modes: current working tree + new isolated worktree + existing worktree.** The launch dialog ships three modes — current tree (default for a lone agent), new isolated worktree (default when a 2nd agent targets a repo another live session is already writing to), and an existing-worktree picker (re-attach to a retained worktree). **Read-only is deferred** to Phase 3+ — unenforceable for PTY agents until adapter permission modes exist (PLAN §12); shipping it as a label without enforcement misrepresents a guarantee. | RESOLVED 2026-07-20 |
| D23 | **Worktree location & branch convention.** Worktrees live OUTSIDE the repo in a sibling dir: `<repo-parent>\.chorus\<repo-name>\<short-session-id>`; branches are `chorus/<repo-name>/<short-session-id>`. Outside-the-repo keeps agents and repo-scanning tools from recursing into sibling checkouts; the PLAN §5 `role` segment is dropped until Phase 3 introduces roles. | RESOLVED 2026-07-20 |
| D24 | **F15 lands as a flagged chore commit at the start of the Task 2-1 execution session** — a separate micro-commit fixing the `main.css` unlayered reset (drop it or move it into a layer; Tailwind preflight already resets), then the task's own commit. G3 amended for this one chore (precedent: `de98679` standalone fix commit). The visual effect (app-wide margins/paddings return to designed values) is verified in that session's G2 runtime pass. | RESOLVED 2026-07-20 |
| D25 | **F14 deferred with a recorded note.** The restart-event asymmetry (`session:restart` emits no `session:restored`) stays as-is; the decision belongs to the phase that next ADDS a restart driver. Phase 2 as scoped adds none. F14's documentation in §5 remains the binding warning for any interim driver. | RESOLVED 2026-07-20 |

**Decision from Council Review CR-2.0** (worktree lifecycle & crash reconciliation), findings filed 2026-07-20:

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D26 | **The worktree lifecycle contract** (CR-2.0; 3-model council Kimi K2.7 / GLM 5.2 / Qwen 3.7 Max, arbiter GPT 5.5; brief `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-2.0-WorktreeLifecycle.md`, findings `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-2.0-Findings.md`). **Unanimous 3-of-3 on all four questions.** **Q1 — hybrid owned-with-retention (C):** a worktree is created by and attributed to a session but outlives it by design; session close offers removal only when clean; a dirty close always detaches (`status='detached'`, session link cleared, surfaced in a retained-worktree list, never silently destroyed); `session:delete` detaches rather than cascades. **Q2 — DB-first journaled creation:** states `creating → provisioning → active → detached → removing`; the row is inserted before any filesystem/git operation; the path derives deterministically from the worktree id; `active` only after full success. **Q3 — boot reconcile runs after storage init and BEFORE D16 restore (awaited),** classifying all five populations (row × git-entry × directory) with keep/promote/surface/delete rules; `git worktree prune` only ever after user confirmation, never automatic. **Q4 — destruction:** clean-enough-to-auto-remove = empty `git status --porcelain`; committed-but-unmerged work never blocks removal (object store keeps it; the branch ref stays); **`--force` is never used in any code path**; dirty removal requires typed confirmation showing the dirty file list; branches are never auto-deleted (explicit opt-in checkbox only). **Coordinator resolutions (a–h, flagged at findings review 2026-07-20):** (a) both pointer columns ship per PLAN §13, but `worktrees.session_id` is authoritative and both are written in one synchronous transaction; (b) reconcile classifies by EVIDENCE first (git entry × directory), journal status second — `creating`/`provisioning` rows with a valid entry + directory are promoted (to `active` when the owning session row stands, else `detached`), closing the creating-with-git-entry crash gap the findings' Q2 rules skipped; (c) population-4 adoption rows are born `detached`, not the findings' `active` (no owning session = detached under the council's own state model); (d) population 2's "session still alive" branch is vacuous (reconcile runs pre-restore, when nothing is alive) — the rule collapses to surface-as-prune-candidate; (e) `removing` crash rule (unspecified in findings): re-classify by evidence — git entry and directory both gone → delete row; anything remaining → revert to `detached` and surface; (f) the auto-worktree trigger is precisely: the launch dialog's mode DEFAULT flips to new-worktree when ≥1 other LIVE session's cwd resolves to the same repo root (computed in main, delivered via `session:launch-context`); the chosen mode always travels explicitly in the launch payload — main never silently overrides (aligns findings action 4 with D22); (g) the retained-worktree list ships as a minimal overlay dialog (LaunchDialog idiom) + palette command — NOT a settings panel (none exists; jumping ahead to settings screens is barred by CLAUDE.md); columns per findings risk 6 (path, branch, clean/dirty, Remove); (h) the D23 `<short-session-id>` path/branch segment becomes the WORKTREE row's short id — worktrees outlive sessions, so a session-derived name goes stale on detach/re-attach; D23's sibling-dir location and `chorus/` prefix stand. Findings risk 5's rename scheme is superseded by (h) + D23; retry-with-fresh-suffix on `git worktree add` collision is kept as cheap defense. **Amendments at the Phase-2 doc review (Matthew, 2026-07-20):** (i) **clause 7 amended** — `--force` is never used EXCEPT inside the single dirty-removal path, after main's typed-confirmation gate has passed: a targeted `git worktree remove --force <path>` on the confirmed worktree only. The drafting-stage no-force workaround (Node `fs.rm` + repo-wide `git worktree prune`) is REJECTED — `prune` is repo-wide and would silently resolve other surfaced prune-candidates without their own confirmation, and bespoke recursive deletion is a worse data-loss surface than git's own targeted, validated removal. (j) Branch deletion (the opt-in checkbox) runs `git branch -d`; an unmerged refusal is surfaced, and `-D` escalation requires the same typed-confirmation acknowledgment. | RESOLVED 2026-07-20 (council, unanimous) |


**Product decision (Matthew, 2026-07-20):**

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D27 | **Council Review becomes a native, first-class Chorus feature (new Phase 3b).** Matthew's Cursor/Kilo-Code multi-LLM council workflow (§4) is productized: 3–5 council members (highly capable, cheaper models) independently review a brief, discuss, and on disagreement a designated **frontier arbiter** rules on the better idea. OpenRouter-first configuration — per-member API key, base URL, model identifier, params — via a real configuration UI. **More than a skill:** invoked from a pane/window (palette command); input = the path to a brief `.md`; output = a findings `.md` (findings, risks, dissents, action items — the CR-x.y format already in §6 use), which a frontier model (e.g. Fable) then reviews as coordinator. **Placement rationale:** depends on Phase 3's DPAPI vault (member keys are credentials, never plaintext config), `provider_configs`/`credential_profiles`, and the api-mode adapter path — hence Phase **3b**. New scope relative to PLAN §14 (which lists council models only as an OpenRouter route); recorded here per the §2 authority split. Until 3b lands, §4 CR runs stay external. | RESOLVED 2026-07-20 (product decision, Matthew) |

**Decisions at the Phase 3 kickoff** (Matthew, 2026-07-21):

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D28 | **Phase 3 is scoped to its own stated milestone.** The provisional §7 line listed ten deliverables; the milestone ("agents launch with injected BYOK credentials, keys never touching args/logs/transcripts") needs four. **Ships in Phase 3:** safeStorage/DPAPI vault · `credential_profiles` + provider configs · `AgentAdapter` interface + capabilities · provider/credential settings UI · env-var injection into child PTYs (supersedes D5) · pino secret-redacting logging · a single-probe test-key. **Moves to a new provisional Phase 3a — Profiles & Catalog:** `model_catalog` + refresh, effort normalization (Fast/Balanced/Deep/Max), `launch_profiles`, `usage_records` capture. Rationale: roughly double the milestone's requirement in one phase, and the security-critical vault should not share execution sessions with UI polish. `usage_records` in particular has no honest producer until an api-mode adapter exists — PTY agents emit no token counts. | RESOLVED 2026-07-21 |
| D29 | **Phase 3 ships Chorus's first real Settings view.** `CLAUDE.md`'s "do not jump ahead to UI/settings screens" barred building settings *before their phase*; PLAN §14 places "provider/credential settings" in Phase 3, so this arrives on schedule rather than ahead of it. D26(g)'s overlay-not-settings-panel ruling was correct **for Phase 2** and is not a precedent against this. Consequences: `App.vue` gains a workspace ⇄ settings view switch — Chorus's first navigation concept — which Phase 3b's council configuration UI (D27) then inherits instead of inventing a second one. **The overlay idiom is not abandoned:** `LaunchDialog.vue` and `WorktreePanel.vue` stay overlays; only durable configuration moves into the view. | RESOLVED 2026-07-21 |
| D30 | **`pino` is added as a dependency** — PLAN §2 names it in the locked stack; `CLAUDE.md`'s list did not, hence the ask. It arrives with a **redacting serializer** (declared redact paths **plus** a key-shape regex scrub over free-text messages, since pino's `redact` covers object paths only) in **Task 3-1 — before any secret exists in the codebase**, so the vault is written into an already-safe logging environment rather than retrofitted. File rotation is **deferred to Phase 7** with the installer work. `pino` is the ONLY new runtime dependency approved for Phase 3; anything else needs a fresh ask. | RESOLVED 2026-07-21 |
| D31 | **Two council briefs, both issued before coding.** `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-3.0-Vault.md` (vault security — storage shape, decrypt lifetime, injection surface, failure modes, redaction coverage incl. the PTY-stream question) and `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-3.1-AdapterInterface.md` (interface surface, pty/api split, capability granularity, `detectState`, registry/identity). Task docs for 3-2 and 3-3 — and therefore 3-4/3-5, which consume their shapes — are authored **after** findings are filed and ratified as **D33** and **D34**. The two questions have little overlap, and the adapter is the interface Phases 3a/3b/4/6 all build on; a combined brief would shortchange whichever the council found less interesting. **Status update 2026-07-22:** CR-3.0 findings filed and recorded as **D33** (coordinator resolutions a–e); its interface-relevant outcomes were folded into the CR-3.1 brief's §3 before that council ran. CR-3.1 findings filed the same day and recorded as **D34** (coordinator resolutions a–f). **Both gates CLOSED — Tasks 3-2…3-5 are clear to author at the re-kickoff.** | RESOLVED 2026-07-21 · BOTH CRs CLOSED 2026-07-22 |
| D32 | **F21 and F23 land as a flagged chore commit at the start of Task 3-1** (precedent: D24's F15 chore in Task 2-1, and the standalone `de98679` fix commit). **G3 is amended for that one session: 3-1 makes two commits** — the chore, then the logging-spine task commit. Both are inherited defects that belong in neither the logging narrative nor a five-task wait; F23 is a live session-loss path. **F21's fix is a main-side gate only** — a distinct `branchForceConfirmation` naming the BRANCH, separate from the dirty-removal `confirmation` naming the path, because one token cannot honestly license two different destructions. No renderer affordance is added, leaving `-D` with zero callers (the dormant state `--force` sat in between Tasks 2-1 and 2-3). | RESOLVED 2026-07-21 |
| D33 | **The vault security contract** (CR-3.0; 3-model council Kimi K2.7-code / GLM 5.2 / Qwen 3.7 Max, arbiter GPT-5.5; brief `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-3.0-Vault.md`, findings `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-3.0-Vault-Findings.md`). Council verdict REVISE, resolved into a committed contract at filing. **Q1 — envelope blob (B), 3-of-3:** encrypt `{key, baseUrl?, extraHeaders?}` via safeStorage/DPAPI; plaintext metadata columns (`provider_id`, `label`, `fingerprint_hash`, `created_at`, `last_verified_at`); salted SHA-256 fingerprint in the clear (unanimous). **Q2:** decrypt-per-launch with variable isolation (C, 2-of-3); constructed allow-list env (unanimous — scope pinned by resolution (c)); **write-only inbound IPC** — no channel ever returns a key or fingerprint (unanimous); launch names a `credential_profile_id`, main resolves and decrypts (unanimous). **Q3 — refuse, never degrade (unanimous):** encryption unavailable → refuse creation; undecryptable/corrupt blob → mark `unavailable_since`, keep the row, refuse launch naming the profile by label only; `shouldReEncrypt` → re-encrypt on next write, throttled via `reencrypted_at`. **Q4:** full redaction coverage (structured paths + free-text scrub + Error messages/stacks + IPC-if-logged + crash artifacts + SQL); **PTY stream: SCRUB ON INGEST, 2-of-3** — a per-session exact-value streaming scrubber runs before ring-buffer append, renderer replay, and any future transcript write, replacing with `[REDACTED-CREDENTIAL]`, chunk-boundary carry-over required. *Qwen dissent preserved:* the PTY stream is an agent-controlled surface and accept-and-document would be honest; rejected by majority on Constraint-1 grounds (the ring buffer and transcripts are Chorus-controlled). **Q6:** no alternative shape load-bearing. **Coordinator resolutions (a)–(e), Matthew-ratified 2026-07-22:** (a) **clauses 4 and 7 of the filed contract contradict each other** — exact-match scrubbing REQUIRES retaining the injected plaintext for the session's lifetime; clause 4 gains that carve-out (the per-session scrubber match set: main memory only, never persisted, cleared on session end), and the crash-dump exposure window widening from milliseconds to session lifetime is a NAMED LIMIT — sound without a council round-trip because a heap-reading attacker can already read the child's environment block (same excluded threat class; longer duration, no new class); (b) **fingerprints are MAIN-SIDE ONLY** — the council's own clause 3 bars them from IPC, making clause 2's "UI can display" rationale impossible as written; their purpose becomes duplicate detection at creation ("already stored as profile X") and rotation detection; the mandatory label disambiguates in the UI; (c) **the allow-list env applies ONLY to credential-bearing launches** — a no-profile launch inherits `process.env` wholesale per clause 9/Constraint 2 (exactly today's behavior; ambient keys riding along on a no-profile launch is today's behavior and stays); the council's mandated allow-list test against both installed CLIs is a hard step of the injection task, with the findings' Q6 fallback if an agent breaks; (d) **the honest guarantee gains a Test-key carve-out** ("…except the explicit Test-key action, which sends an authentication probe to the provider's own API at your request") — the brief omitted the test-key feature entirely, a coordinator error flagged as such; the council could not have known; (e) **spec notes** (no contract change): scrubber carry-over bounded at maxSecretLen−1 bytes with a short timer flush (a TUI pausing mid-prefix must not stall rendering); `shouldReEncrypt` is reported only by `decryptStringAsync`, so rotation detection needs the async API (D4-verify against installed Electron typings); provider-level plaintext `base_url`/`extra_headers_json` vs the envelope's encrypted fields need an explicit precedence rule (envelope overrides provider default) and the plaintext provider-level values are documented non-secret. **Scope note:** the scrub ruling adds a streaming scrubber at SessionManager ingest that was not in the phase sketch — task placement (inside 3-5 vs its own task) is decided at the post-councils re-kickoff. Interface-relevant outcomes were folded into `CouncilBrief-3.1-AdapterInterface.md` §3 before CR-3.1 ran: allow-list + adapter-declared env vars, the `env_var_name` placement question, secret-distinguishable launch payloads (scrubber registration), optional credential in `buildLaunch`. | RESOLVED 2026-07-22 (council majority + coordinator resolutions, Matthew-ratified) |
| D34 | **The `AgentAdapter` interface contract** (CR-3.1; 3-model council Kimi K2.7-code / GLM 5.2 / Qwen 3.7 Max, arbiter GPT-5.5; brief `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-3.1-AdapterInterface.md` — run in its 2026-07-22 amended form carrying the D33 seam facts; findings `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-3.1-AdapterInterface-Findings.md`). Council verdict REVISE resolved into a committed shape at filing. **Q1 — narrow core + capability-gated extension interfaces (C), 3-of-3:** always-implemented `BaseAgentAdapter` (`id`, `displayName`, `executionMode`, `detectInstallation`, `getAuthMethods`, `getCapabilities`); domain concerns live on optional extension interfaces (`SupportsMcp`, `SupportsHooks`, `SupportsResume`) callers narrow to via type guards — "supported" and "implemented" become the same fact. **Q2 — discriminated union, 3-of-3:** `PtyAgentAdapter \| ApiAgentAdapter`; the session level eventually splits the same way, but **Phase 3 restructures only the adapter side** — `SessionManager` stays PTY-only. **Q3 — descriptors over booleans, 3-of-3:** `reasoningEffort: EffortDescriptor \| null`, `mcp: McpDescriptor \| null`, etc.; booleans only for truly binary facts; each descriptor carries `mode: 'static' \| 'dynamic'`, with dynamic ones populated by `detectInstallation()` through a `mergeCapabilities(base, detected)` utility. **Q4 — `detectState` OUT, 2-of-3:** removed from the interface entirely; Phase 4 designs its own buffered, stateful interpreter if hooks prove insufficient. *Qwen dissent preserved:* declaring a stateful interpreter seam now would cost little and give Phase 4 a concrete target. **Q5 — 3-of-3:** the adapter `id` IS the persisted `sessions.agent` value (no mapping layer); a static frozen registry for Phase 3 with a registration seam deferred to Phase 6; unknown persisted ids refuse cleanly, never crash. **Q6:** no alternative shape load-bearing. **Coordinator resolutions (a)–(f), Matthew-ratified 2026-07-22:** (a) **the findings' verbatim TS contradicted its own Q4 majority** by declaring `SupportsStateDetection`/`OutputInterpreter` (the outvoted Qwen position, even named inconsistently vs the synthesis text) — both are STRUCK from Phase 3's declaration set; the majority ruling stands; (b) **the `AgentKind` derivation flips to respect the process boundary** — the findings derived the type from the main-process registry (`keyof typeof staticRegistry`), but shared/renderer code can never import main-side modules, and the TS as written had the explicit `Record<string, …>` annotation widening `keyof` to `string` anyway; instead `agentKindSchema` in `src/shared/ipc.ts` remains the wire authority and the registry is typed **`Record<AgentKind, AgentAdapter>`** via a type-only import — same single source of truth, statically-forced exact coverage, no boundary violation; (c) **the unknown-agent rule is mapped onto the schema that exists** — there is no `'failed'` status (`running \| exited` only; PLAN §4's machine is Phase 4+) and no notification center until Phase 4; unknown-agent rows flow through the EXISTING D16 spawn-failure heal path (`getAdapterOrThrow` throws → caught → healed to `exited` → pino-logged → exited chrome), and `session:restart` swaps its `agentKindSchema.parse(row.agent)` throw for a registry lookup returning `{ok:false, reason}` inline; (d) **env policy has ONE owner: main** — the findings' `PtyLaunchRequest.env` carried the full environment (each adapter re-implementing the D33 allow-list) while `requiredEnvVars` separately existed so main could build the same list; instead `buildLaunch` contributes adapter-specific additions + `secretEnv`, and main's single launch path composes the child env per D33 (wholesale inherit for no-credential; baseline + `requiredEnvVars` + additions for BYOK) **[⚠ clause (d)'s parenthetical AMENDED 2026-07-27 by D89 — `envAdditions` applies on BOTH branches.
