Nobody accumulates hundreds of these by accident, and **a purge is irreversible and deletes something the user paid for** — 174 KB is a trivial price for keeping a $1.09 artifact. **Shipping the reader and an auto-reaper in the same task would also be self-defeating.** **⚠ `deleteCouncilRun` STAYS UNCALLED DELIBERATELY, AND IT IS NOT DEAD CODE:** it is the atomic transaction a future **user-initiated** delete would use (it already purges `council_messages`, which SQLite will not cascade — `schema.ts:501`). A background reaper nobody asked for is a different decision with a different blast radius, and **D97 declined to make it.** **⚠ FALSIFIABLE RATHER THAN PERMANENT — the revisit triggers are stated so this cannot become a standing excuse:** if `council_messages` passes **50 MB**, or a single transcript passes `council:transcript`'s **1,000,000-character** cap (8.9× the largest measured), the arithmetic above has stopped holding and the position must be re-taken. | ACCEPTED 2026-07-28 (Task 3e-4) |

| D103 | **⚠ THE COUNCIL'S INPUT IS A *CASE*, NOT A PROMPT AND NO LONGER A BRIEF.** Settled 2026-08-01 (Matthew, design session). The vocabulary is now closed and coherent with the metaphor the feature already ran on: a **Case** is filed, the council deliberates, it returns **Findings** carrying a **Verdict** per question and a **Consensus** measure, and the per-project history is the **Docket**. **THE REASON IS BEHAVIOURAL, NOT AESTHETIC** — "prompt" invites a sentence and "brief" reads as *short*, and the input this mechanism needs is the opposite: context, constraints, enumerated questions and now exhibits. **⚠ "BRIEF" IS RETIRED FOR NEW WORK AND KEPT FOR THE OLD** — the ~15 `CouncilBrief-*.md` under `CouncilBriefs/` are cited **by name** throughout this roadmap and in D98's re-measurement ruling, and renaming them would break citations to buy nothing. **⚠ AND THE COST OF THE WORD IS RECORDED RATHER THAN DISCOVERED:** `case` is a TS keyword and a generic noun ("test case", "use case"), so `caseId` / `casePath` will grep noisily. **Docket** was offered as the input document instead and declined — it inverts what a docket actually is. | ACCEPTED 2026-08-01 (Matthew) |
| D104 | **⚠ THE REPOSITORY REACHES THE COUNCIL AS AN IDENTICAL PRE-RESOLVED PACK — TOOL-USE RETRIEVAL IS REJECTED, AND THE ARGUMENT IS ABOUT MEASUREMENT, NOT COST.** Settled 2026-08-01. A case declares an `## Exhibits` section of paths and globs; **main resolves them BEFORE the blind round** into a numbered, path-labelled, line-numbered pack that **every member receives byte-identically**. **THE REASON THE OBVIOUS ALTERNATIVE IS WRONG:** give each member a `read_file` tool and members deliberate on **different evidence** — a disagreement then records what a model chose to fetch rather than what it thinks, and **`disagreementVector` (`councilCore.ts:584`) would be measuring retrieval noise while reporting it as dissent.** It also breaks the transcript as a complete record of what was seen, and tool-calling support across D71's roster (kimi · qwen3-coder · GLM) is uneven — a capability gap would silently become a quality gap. **⚠ REFERENCES ARE EXPLICIT, NEVER SNIFFED FROM PROSE.** Only the `## Exhibits` section is resolved: this roadmap's own council documents are full of file paths written as prose, and auto-detection would turn every one of them into a paid exhibit. **⚠ FULL TEXT IN ROUND 0 ONLY; LATER ROUNDS GET THE INDEX AND CITE BY NUMBER.** The case is re-sent on every round (`councilCore.ts:986` critique, `:1015` arbitration/synthesis), so an unbounded pack multiplies the phase's largest cost by the number of rounds — and the critique round argues about **positions**, not about source. **⚠ TRUNCATION IS RENDERED, NEVER SWALLOWED** — the 3e-4 / D55 idiom: a pack that hits its bound says so in the UI **and in the findings document**, because a council that silently saw half a file produces findings nobody can trust. **The bound itself and the cost-gate threshold are NOT set here** — both are referred to CR-3f.0. **A scout pass** (one cheap model reads the repo and writes a digest all members then see) was considered and **deferred, not rejected**: it preserves parity and compresses cost, but its summarization errors would poison every member identically and invisibly. | ACCEPTED 2026-08-01 (Matthew) · **bounds referred to CR-3f.0** |
| D105 | **⚠ CASES LIVE IN THE REPOSITORY, AND THEIR IDENTITY IS A UUID IN THEIR OWN FRONTMATTER RATHER THAN THEIR PATH.** Settled 2026-08-01. Layout: `docs/council/<slug>/case.md`, with each run under `runs/<timestamp>/findings.md`. **IN-REPO BEATS APP-DATA FOR ONE REASON THAT OUTWEIGHS THE REST:** the findings become context the project's own terminal agents can read, and they version with the code that prompted them — which is already how Matthew works (`CouncilBriefs/` is committed to this repo). **⚠ A CASE IS RUN MORE THAN ONCE, AND THIS PROJECT ALREADY PROVES IT — D98 ORDERED IT.** The 3e re-measurement ran `4c17069c` and `c06874ad` on the same document verbatim, and `nextFreeFindingsPath` (`councilService.ts:389`) already numbers the collision — `CouncilBrief-3b.0-ApiSessionProducer-Findings-2.md` exists on disk. So **Case → Run is 1:N**, the Docket is case-grouped, and "did the verdict change when we re-ran it?" is a first-class question rather than a diff two people do by eye. **⚠ THE `case_id` FRONTMATTER IS THE LOAD-BEARING PART AND IT IS EASY TO SKIP.** Once the case folder is git-tracked it gets renamed, cloned to another machine, and arrives on a teammate's disk with **no row in anyone's `council_runs`** — a path is not an identity across any of those. The DB indexes **runs**; the repo holds **case text and findings**; the Docket reconciles the two and **labels a case with no local run as never-run-here rather than hiding it**. **⚠ `transcript.md` IS WRITTEN ON DEMAND, NOT PER RUN** — the largest transcript measured is **112,531 characters** (D99), the DB already stores every turn, and `council:transcript` already reads them, so committing that to a repo on every run buys nothing an export button does not. | ACCEPTED 2026-08-01 (Matthew) |
| D106 | **⚠ THE VERDICT IS THE ARBITER'S; THE CONSENSUS IS THE MEMBERS'. TWO FACTS, TWO SOURCES, NEITHER FAKED.** Settled 2026-08-01. The **Verdict** is a structured per-question outcome the arbiter emits in the synthesis step, over a closed five-state vocabulary — **Approved · Approved with revisions · Revise · Rejected · Insufficient information** (*Approved with revisions* kept distinct from *Revise* at Matthew's explicit ruling: one proceeds, the other does not, and collapsing them loses the only distinction the reader acts on). The **Consensus** — `unanimous` / `majority` / `split` — is the mechanical tally already computed by `disagreementVector` from the members' own `AGREE`/`DISAGREE`/`QUALIFY` tokens. **⚠ COMPUTING THE OUTCOME FROM THE MEMBER TALLY ALONE WAS CONSIDERED AND REJECTED ON MEASURED EVIDENCE, NOT ON TASTE:** verdict-token compliance is **5 of 6 structural** (3e-2, after two prior measurements of 2 of 6 and 4 of 6), and the non-compliance is **concentrated in a MEMBER rather than a question type** — `qwen3-coder` emitted **no verdict token on any of the six**. A strip that majority-votes over that is reporting a number it does not have. **⚠ SO THE STRIP CARRIES ITS OWN DENOMINATOR — `n of m answered` — WHICH IS D55's RULE APPLIED TO A SECOND SURFACE**, and when the arbiter's structured block fails to parse it renders **unparsed**. It never guesses, and it never silently degrades to prose. **A `split` verdict offers a focused referral**: a new case pre-filled with that question, the dissenting positions verbatim, and the information the findings say would settle it — linked to its parent so the Docket shows the chain rather than two unrelated entries. | ACCEPTED 2026-08-01 (Matthew) |
| D107 | **⚠ THE TEMPLATE'S HEADINGS ARE MANDATORY AND A CASE MISSING ONE REFUSES THE RUN. WHAT GOES UNDER THEM IS FREE.** Matthew's ruling, 2026-08-01: *"All markdown headings are mandatory. Everything else in it is up for grabs."* **THIS IS AN EXTENSION OF A REFUSAL THAT ALREADY SHIPS** — `assembleRun` (`councilCore.ts:203`) already refuses a case whose questions cannot be found, and D67 Q3's argument is that without enumerable questions there is nothing to diff and every disagreement becomes a model's opinion about prose. Exhibits give that argument a second limb: without a delimited `## Exhibits` section there is nothing to resolve, bound, or audit. **⚠ AND THE ENFORCEMENT CLOSES A TRAP THIS CODEBASE HAS ALREADY FALLEN INTO ONCE.** `parseBriefQuestions` falls back to scanning the **whole document** when no questions heading is found — **D68(1), measured rather than reasoned about**: run over this project's own briefs it returned **21 and 23 "questions"**, none of the first twelve of which was a question. A numbered exhibit list in a case with no questions heading reproduces that failure exactly, and members would pay input tokens to render an `AGREE`/`DISAGREE` verdict on a file path. **The refusal must therefore also fire when an `## Exhibits` heading is present without a questions heading** — the fallback is safe only for the short synthetic case it was written for. | ACCEPTED 2026-08-01 (Matthew) |
| D108 | **⚠ THE EXISTING `council_runs` ROWS ARE ABANDONED, NOT MIGRATED — AND THE MECHANISM IS THAT THEY HAVE NO `case_id`.** Matthew's ruling, 2026-08-01: *"Ignore the legacy runs completely. They were test, or just for validation. We're starting fresh."* **THE PLEASANT CONSEQUENCE IS THAT THIS COSTS NOTHING TO IMPLEMENT:** a Docket keyed on `case_id` never selects a row whose `case_id` is NULL, so the historical runs become invisible **by construction** — **no purge, no backfill, no migration, and no data destroyed**. They remain readable through `council:transcript` by anyone who has a run id, which is the correct treatment for a record that is true but no longer indexed. **⚠ AND A PURGE WAS DECLINED FOR A REASON WORTH KEEPING:** D99 authorised indefinite retention on the arithmetic that these artifacts cost ~$1.09 each and total 220,648 characters — deleting them to tidy a view would spend an irreversible action on a problem the view does not have. **`CouncilBriefs/` IS NOT MIGRATED EITHER.** New cases use D105's layout; the existing documents stay where this roadmap links them. | ACCEPTED 2026-08-01 (Matthew) |
| D109 | **⚠ DELETE IS TWO ACTIONS, BECAUSE ONE WORD CANNOT HONESTLY COVER BOTH STORES.** Settled 2026-08-01, and it follows directly from D105's split: the DB indexes runs, the repo holds documents. **"Remove from Docket"** purges the `council_runs` and `council_messages` rows through the existing atomic `deleteCouncilRun` (`storage.ts:1883`, uncalled since 3b-2 and kept alive by D99 for exactly this) and **leaves the files** — the folder then reads as *never run here*, which is a true statement rather than a disappearance. **"Delete case…"** additionally removes the folder, **behind a confirm that names the full path and the file count**. **⚠ THE ONE-ACTION ALTERNATIVE WAS REJECTED FOR THE FAILURE IT HIDES:** purging rows while leaving files makes the case reappear as an un-run case, which reads as a bug and would be filed as one; deleting files silently on a menu click deletes from the user's repository, which is not a thing a Docket may do without saying so. **Both counts are stated in the menu before either is taken** — *"purges 2 runs, 16 transcript turns"* — the same no-number-without-its-denominator posture D55 fixed for attention and D106 applies to the Verdict. | ACCEPTED 2026-08-01 (Matthew) |
| D110 | **⚠ THE EXHIBIT ROOT IS SELECTABLE PER RUN, DEFAULTS TO THE PROJECT ROOT, AND IS STORED ON THE RUN.** Settled 2026-08-01. Exhibits resolve against `projects.root_path` unless the user picks one of the project's worktrees at launch, and **whichever root was read is recorded with the run and stated in the findings** — a case re-run months later (D98's whole practice) is worthless if nobody can tell what tree it read. **THE DEFAULT IS THE ROOT BECAUSE A COUNCIL DECIDES ARCHITECTURE**, and resolving against whatever pane happened to be focused would make the same case read differently on two consecutive runs for reasons invisible in the document. **THE SELECTOR EXISTS BECAUSE "REVIEW THIS BRANCH BEFORE I MERGE IT" IS A REAL COUNCIL QUESTION** and Phase 2 built the worktrees that make it answerable. **⚠ CONFINEMENT IS TO THE CHOSEN ROOT, AFTER `realpath`** — a glob that escapes via `..` or a symlink is a path-traversal bug wearing a config file's clothes, and the bytes it would read leave the machine. Deny-list and scrub behaviour are **CR-3f.0's** to rule on, not this decision's. **⚠ `git:`-DERIVED EXHIBITS (a diff, a branch's changed files) WERE OFFERED AND NOT TAKEN** — recorded so a later reader knows the option was seen and declined rather than missed; it remains available if the worktree selector proves clumsy in use. | ACCEPTED 2026-08-01 (Matthew) |

| D111 | **⚠ CR-3f.0 IS CLOSED — VERDICT `REVISE` (confidence 8/10), AND FIVE COORDINATOR RESOLUTIONS, TWO OF THEM LOAD-BEARING.** Findings: [`CouncilBriefs/CouncilRecord-3f.0-Exhibits.md`](CouncilBriefs/CouncilRecord-3f.0-Exhibits.md), run 2026-08-01 on [`CouncilCase-3f.0-Exhibits.md`](CouncilBriefs/CouncilCase-3f.0-Exhibits.md). **The council endorsed the design and refused to let it ship unbounded**, which is the outcome the gate existed to produce. **ADOPTED AS FILED:** Q2 — a secret-shape match **refuses the whole run** with the path named (ruling 3, and cleaner than drop-and-disclose, which would silently change what members saw); Q6 — **evidence parity is sound and is not a rationalisation**; Q1's *shape* — git-tracked eligibility is the right **primary** gate but **not a security boundary**, since a tracked file can carry a committed secret, so it layers with preflight scanning; and **P1, the preflight exfiltration manifest — genuinely additive, proposed by no one in the design session** (resolved files, byte totals, truncation status, destination APIs, retention warning, estimated cost, shown before any network call). **⚠ RESOLUTION (a) — Q5's PRIMARY REMEDY IS NOT IMPLEMENTABLE, AND IT WAS CHECKED IN THE CODE RATHER THAN REASONED ABOUT.** The council requires that *"round-0 exhibit text remains available and in context for all later rounds."* **There is no such context to retain.** `councilService.driveMember` calls `createApiSession` **per turn**, with the session id keyed `runId:memberId:phase:round` (`councilService.ts:866`) and `handle.dispose()` in a `finally` — **every turn is a fresh, disposed session**, which is also why the case text is re-sent on the rounds that need it. So the remedy reduces to one of three things, and the council's own **fallback is the only implementable one**: **deterministic re-injection of cited line ranges**, adopted; "retain in context" is **struck as resting on a false premise about the transport**; re-sending the whole pack is the cost the round-0 rule exists to avoid; refusing is the floor. **⚠ RESOLUTION (b) — THE 100 KiB CAP IS TOO SMALL, MEASURED AGAINST THIS REPOSITORY, AND IS PROBABLY AN ANCHORING ARTIFACT.** Real sizes, read 2026-08-01: `src/shared/ipc.ts` **99,689 bytes — 97.4 KiB, one file that nearly exhausts the entire proposed pack**; `councilCore.ts` **58,854** + `councilService.ts` **56,242** = **115,096**, so **the two files a council question about the council would obviously cite already exceed the cap**. At that size the binding constraint is **context window and attention, not dollars** — 100 KiB is ~25k tokens, ~$0.05–0.30 across four members on the input side, against a **measured $1.089 run whose cost is dominated by OUTPUT** (kimi alone contributed 23,991 output tokens ≈ $0.36). **⚠ AND THE PROVENANCE OF THE NUMBER IS SUSPECT: the record's own launcher configuration lists `Max code chars 100,000`** (§2 of the record), which is within 2.4% of the cap the council then recommended. **Flagged as possible anchoring on the harness's own limit rather than on the codebase.** **Coordinator recommendation, NOT ratified: 256 KiB** (~64k tokens per member, ~$0.13–0.79 input across the roster), which admits a realistic multi-file case and still refuses a `src/**` glob. **The number is Matthew's to set and the council should be asked again with the measured file sizes in the case.** **⚠ RESOLUTION (c) — $2.00 IS ACCEPTED AS THE THRESHOLD, BUT THE ESTIMATE IT GATES CANNOT BE A POINT ESTIMATE.** Pre-call, only the **input** side is knowable; output tokens dominate the bill and are unpredictable. **This project has already made exactly this error once**: the ~$0.83 figure was a **partial-run number used as a full-run number** and the real run came in at **$1.089, 31% over**. So the confirmation names an **input-side bound with the output assumption stated beside it** (D55's rule, at a third surface), never a single confident number. **⚠ AMENDED 2026-08-01 BY F42, AND THE AMENDMENT IS LARGER THAN THE ORIGINAL RESOLUTION: THE NUMBER THIS GATE WOULD READ IS ITSELF 37% LOW.** `council_runs.cost_usd` under-reports measured spend by a factor of **1.595** — a **$5.00** confirmation gate fed by it would not fire until roughly **$8.00** of real spend. **The $2.00-versus-$5.00 discussion was arguing about the wrong digit.** No cost gate may be built on `cost_usd` until F42 is fixed; the honest input is **stored tokens × published price**, which reproduces OpenRouter's bill to 0.05%. **⚠ RESOLUTION (d) — Q2's REFUSAL NEEDS AN ESCAPE HATCH OR IT IS A DENIAL OF SERVICE ON THE COUNCIL.** The scrub patterns are **shape-based**; a test fixture or a documentation example of a key shape would refuse **every** run citing that file, permanently, with no way through. Ruling 3 says refuse **with an actionable message**, and the council quoted it without operationalising it: the refusal must name **the path AND the matching pattern**, and removing the exhibit from the case must be the stated remedy. **The key-oracle objection does not apply** — it is the same-user-at-the-keyboard threat class D33 clause 5 already excludes, and it is stated here rather than left to be re-derived. **⚠ RESOLUTION (e) — AMENDED 2026-08-01, AND THE FIRST WORDING WAS WRONG: THERE IS NOTHING TO RECONCILE, BECAUSE THE RUN WAS NOT ON CHORUS.** (e) originally read *"the roster in the record is not D71's roster — reconcile the record or amend D71."* **Checked instead of assumed, and the answer is bigger than a roster drift: CR-3f.0 is a D6 EXTERNAL council run.** Four confirmations: **`council_runs` holds no row for 2026-08-01** (4 runs total, most recent 2026-07-28); the live `council_members` rows are **exactly D71's four**; the record's `Max code chars` / `Provider routing` / `Language hint` configuration fields return **zero grep hits across `src/`** — they are not Chorus concepts; and its `Request timeout 120 s` is the transport default **D71 explicitly overrode to 15 minutes** after measuring two reasoning members abort at 120 s. **An external run is legitimate under D6 — but it must be LABELLED, because F38's native-vs-external comparison is meaningless if nobody records which mechanism produced a document.** The record now carries a coordinator provenance header stating all four checks; **its own text is unaltered.** **⚠ AND THIS CONVERTS (b)'s ANCHORING SUSPICION INTO SOMETHING MUCH FIRMER:** the harness caps code input at **100,000 chars** and recommended a **~100 KiB** cap — within **2.4%** of the largest pack it could physically have been shown. **The real ceiling on Chorus's roster is ~840 KiB**, from `qwen/qwen3-coder`'s **262,144-token** context window, read live from OpenRouter's free `/models` on 2026-08-01 (the F32 instrument; every other member is ≥1M). **⚠ THE STANDING LESSON GAINS A SECOND HALF: a council's output is deliberation, not verified fact — AND A COUNCIL'S OWN RECORD OF ITSELF IS NOT PROVENANCE.** Both were caught by reading the database instead of the document. **⚠ THE STANDING LESSON HOLDS FOR A THIRD CONSECUTIVE COUNCIL, AND THIS TIME THE CASE PREDICTED IT: a council's output is deliberation, not verified fact.** The case declared up front that this council was reasoning about repository access **without having any**, and (a) is exactly what that limit produced — a remedy that sounds right and that the architecture cannot provide. **The re-ask on the byte budget should be the first case run WITH exhibits.** | **CLOSED 2026-08-01 as D111** — adopted with resolutions (a)–(e) |
| D112 | **⚠ THE DOCKET SHIPS ALONE, AHEAD OF CASES AND EXHIBITS — A DELIBERATE CUT OF A FEATURE THE ROADMAP CALLS "ONE FEATURE".** Phase 3f bundles three things that share a data model: exhibits, the Docket, and the structured Verdict. **They do not share a blast radius.** Exhibits are the first feature in the repo that exfiltrates project source to four third-party APIs by design, and **D111(b) left their byte budget explicitly OPEN** pending a re-ask *in a case run with exhibits* — a condition that does not exist yet. The Docket, by contrast, is **inert plumbing already paid for**: `council_runs.project_id` has been written on every run since 3b-3 (`councilService.ts:722`), `listCouncilRuns` and `deleteCouncilRun` have sat with **zero callers**, and `council:transcript` already reads a stored run back. Shipping the history half needs **no migration, no new table, no new column** — v13 stays head and Phase 6's v14 is untouched. **⚠ CONSEQUENCE FOR SEQUENCING: the collision recorded at §7's 3f entry no longer binds.** Nothing here changes the run mechanism, so **Phase 6's Task 6-1 `[CR]` gate can fire before or after** — the ordering question it posed ("build 3f first so 6-1 argues with exhibits") is untouched, because exhibits are precisely what this slice does not deliver. | **ACCEPTED 2026-08-04 (Matthew)** |
| D113 | **⚠ THE DOCKET KEYS ON `project_id` AND LISTS RUNS FLAT.** No join key is invented, so nothing pre-empts **D105**'s `case_id`, and **CR-3f.1's action A1** — no folder path may appear in a join predicate — is honoured for free by having no join at all. Grouping, re-run comparison, and the three-way *never-run-here / run-elsewhere / has-local-runs* state all arrive with Cases; a flat list is the honest thing to show without them. **⚠ AND D108 SIMPLY DOES NOT BIND A LIST KEYED THIS WAY.** That decision made the legacy runs invisible *by construction* — a Docket keyed on `case_id` never selects a row whose `case_id` is NULL. There is no such column here, so the pre-existing runs **do appear**. Matthew's ruling: *"I'm not worried about legacy runs. Keep them or ignore them. This is all test data so it's fine for now."* No special handling either way; when Cases land, D108 reasserts itself unchanged. **Measured on the dev DB 2026-08-04: 8 runs, 7 carrying a `project_id` (5 + 2 across two projects), 1 with NULL — and the NULL one correctly appears in no project's Docket**, which is D95's *"a run with no project id still credits `overhead`"* showing up at the read layer. | **ACCEPTED 2026-08-04 (Matthew)** |
| D114 | **⚠ THE DOCKET IS `CouncilView`'s LANDING SURFACE, NOT A FIFTH VIEW.** Opening the council answers *"what has this project already decided"* before it offers to spend ~$1.09 asking something new; **New council** is the primary action and the existing run surface becomes the detail page, reached by starting a run or opening a row. **No new `activeView` value**, so the attention-report enum (`src/shared/ipc.ts:1226`) and `attention/reporter.ts` are untouched — a fifth view would have widened a validated IPC boundary to render a list. The left rail keeps the roster: it is the live status display during a 14-minute run, and a second list in 208px would cost more than it buys. **⚠ ESC UNWINDS ONE STEP AT A TIME** — run → Docket → workspace — and the rail's back affordance is **labelled from the same state as the keycap beside it**, because a fixed "back to workspace" under an `esc` cap would advertise a journey the key no longer makes. The refusal to leave while `council.running` is unchanged. **The workspace gains exactly one new pixel: a fourth `.rail-action` row, "Council"**, disabled with no active project — the rule `palette/commands.ts` already applied to `council.run`, whose label moves to `Council…` while its **id stays `council.run`**. | **ACCEPTED 2026-08-04 (Matthew)** |
| D115 | **⚠ A STORED RUN'S COST RENDERS AS A FLOOR — "at least $1.09" — AND THIS SLICE SHIPS NO PROJECT-LEVEL TOTAL.** F42 measured `council_runs.cost_usd` at **1.540×–1.596× under** the real bill, and unlike a live `council:start` response a stored row carries **no `cost_is_provisional` flag** to qualify it with. Summing known-low figures into a headline is exactly how F42's error would be laundered into a total that looks authoritative. **⚠ AND THE PRECISE REASON THE DOCKET DOES NOT RECOMPUTE IT — STATED CAREFULLY, BECAUSE F42 PROVED RECOMPUTATION *IS* POSSIBLE.** Stored tokens × published price reproduces the bill to **0.05%**, twice, at different mixes. But doing it per historical run needs two things the read path cannot supply on its own: **live prices** — there is deliberately no price column, `storage.ts` refused to cache one and `glm-5.2` moved $0.67/$2.10 → $0.72/$2.25 in six days — and **per-turn model attribution**, which `council_messages` cannot give (no model column) and `council_members.model` cannot be trusted for, since **D56 never back-writes it**, so a member re-pointed at a new model silently reprices every run it ever sat on. **This is a DEFERRAL, not a prohibition.** Matthew: *"We can spend some focused time on cost totals after we get all of this completed."* What D115 fixes is that the number is never presented as settled in the meantime. **⚠ AND THE COUNCIL OVERRULED THE "NO TOTAL" HALF OF THIS ON 2026-08-04 (run `b534ed99`, Q3 — `APPROVED`, members split), WITH AN ARGUMENT THIS ENTRY MISSED:** a sum of lower bounds is itself a valid lower bound, so F42 forbids presenting the total as a bill, an estimate of one, or a forecast — **it does not forbid presenting the mathematical floor**, provided the "at least" language is exact and hard to miss. The arbiter also ruled that **repairing F42 itself should take priority** over discretionary presentation work. Not yet actioned; recorded here so the deferral is no longer resting on a premise the council has answered. **Token totals ARE honest and are summed from `council_messages`** — `council_runs.tokens_in/out` are dead columns (F42) and are never read — and **a total drawn from only some turns carries its denominator** (D55): the dev DB's run `4c17069c` reports 6 of 7 turns with usage and says so. | **ACCEPTED 2026-08-04 (Matthew)** |
| D116 | **⚠ THE VERDICT STRIP (D106) IS DERIVED FROM STORED TURNS, NOT STORED — SO IT NEEDED NO MIGRATION, AND v14 STAYS FREE FOR PHASE 6.** The arbiter's ruling has been sitting in its own `council_messages` row since 3b-3 like any other turn, and the members' verdict tokens in theirs. Deriving on read is also the house rule (CR-3f.1 **A12**: derive at render, never persist), and the alternative would have taken **migration v14 out from under `project_memory`**, which `Task-6-3.md:53` has already claimed — the mid-phase collision the 3f entry warns against, avoided rather than resolved. **⚠ AND IT RETRACTS A CLAIM THIS DOCUMENT MADE ONE COMMIT EARLIER.** D112's entry and the Docket's commit message both state that the per-question tally is *"computed on every paid council and then discarded"*, so runs predating the strip *"lose their tally permanently."* **The consensus half was never lost.** `parseVerdicts` reads the tokens back out of stored positions turns, `parseBriefQuestions` recovers the questions from the brief file, and `computeDisagreement` → `summariseQuestions` is the same chain the live strip runs. **Verified 2026-08-04 against run `c06874ad` (2026-07-28): `Q1 agreed · Q2 disagreed · Q3 qualified · Q4 agreed · Q5 qualified · Q6 not-measured`, recovered whole.** What IS unrecoverable is the ARBITER's half, and only because those arbiters were never asked for it. | **ACCEPTED 2026-08-04 (Matthew)** |
| D117 | **⚠ THE ARBITER'S RULING IS THREE-WAY, NOT TWO — `ruled` / `unparsed` / NEVER ASKED.** D106 specifies `unparsed` for a block that fails to parse. Implementation forced a third state out into the open: **every run recorded before this feature has no verdict block at all**, and rendering those as `unparsed` would report a council that ignored an instruction it was never given. `parseArbiterVerdicts` therefore returns `blockPresent` beside its map, the wire carries `arbiter_asked`, and the row's `verdict` is `null` for never-asked. **The view says "not asked" in words and tones it neutral — a missing ruling is not a bad ruling, and colouring it like `rejected` would make every historical run read as a condemnation.** The prompt change itself is strictly ADDITIVE (`councilCore.ts` `buildArbitrationPrompt`): the block is requested *before* the reasoning the arbiter already gives, nothing is removed, and a model that ignores it produces exactly the run it would have produced before. **✅ COMPLIANCE VALIDATED 2026-08-04 ON A REAL COUNCIL — run `b534ed99`, brief [`CouncilBrief-3f.2-VerdictStripFollowups.md`](CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-3f.2-VerdictStripFollowups.md), findings beside it.** The entry previously read *"compliance is unvalidated by design and this is the honest risk"*; it was, and then it was tested. `gpt-5.6-terra` emitted the block **exactly as requested and ruled 5 of 5** — `## Verdict` heading, one `Q<n>: TOKEN` line each, reasoning below it — and used **four of the five states meaningfully rather than rubber-stamping**: `REJECTED` for a cache proposal the brief's own 7 ms measurement undercut, and `INSUFFICIENT-INFORMATION` for a question deliberately unanswerable from the brief. **⚠ AND Q3 PRODUCED D106's THESIS CASE ON THE FIRST RUN: the members SPLIT and the arbiter APPROVED anyway** — the outcome that is only expressible because consensus and verdict are separate fields. The strip, the findings document's own verdict table and the Docket digest were confirmed to agree; the digest's counts-too-long fallback (`5 of 5 ruled`, four distinct ruling kinds) fired for the first time in production. **⚠ THE RUN ALSO CONFIRMED THE DEGRADATION PATH, UNPLANNED: `CR DeepSeek (v4-flash)` TIMED OUT** — 53 characters, *"The response exceeded its time limit and was stopped"*, after consuming 15.3 of the run's 17.5 minutes. The council continued on two members, the findings led with a partial-run banner naming it, and every question reported `silent: 1` **with its denominator** (D55). Cost `$0.138` stored, so higher in truth per F42. **The parser's 12 unit tests stand; they are no longer the only evidence.** | **VALIDATED 2026-08-04** |
| D118 | **⚠ THE MEMBERS' HALF KEEPS ITS EXISTING FIVE-STATE VOCABULARY RATHER THAN D106's THREE, AND THIS IS A CONSCIOUS DEVIATION.** D106 names the consensus badge `unanimous` / `majority` / `split`. Phase 3e had already shipped `agreed · qualified · split · disagreed · not-measured`, which is a strict REFINEMENT of those three: it distinguishes unanimous-for from unanimous-against from qualified-support, and `not-measured` carries D67 Q3's rule that too few parseable verdicts is not the same as agreement. That vocabulary is already rendered in the live glance strip and already written into every findings document, so replacing it would have re-worded shipped artefacts to carry LESS information. **⚠ ONE LEGIBILITY COST IS ACCEPTED AND RECORDED: `disagreed` MEANS UNANIMOUSLY AGAINST, NOT "THE MEMBERS DISAGREED WITH EACH OTHER" — that is `split`.** Beside `agreed` in a strip the two read ambiguously. The confusion is inherited from 3e rather than introduced here, and renaming it is a separate change to a shipped surface. | **ACCEPTED 2026-08-04 (Matthew)** |
| D119 | **⚠ THERE IS NO RUN-LEVEL VERDICT, ANYWHERE, AND THE DOCKET LINE COUNTS RATHER THAN ROLLING UP.** The arbiter rules per question and never issues one overall outcome, so reducing six rulings to a single word would attribute a judgement to the council it did not make. `digestFor` therefore emits counts in severity order — `1 revise · 2 approved · 3 of 3 ruled · members split on 1` — and falls back to the bare denominator when more than three kinds of ruling make the line unreadable. **It is TEXT, never a badge:** CR-3f.1's badge economy allows each Docket row exactly one status affordance and the run status already owns it, while counts and denominators are explicitly permitted as text. **⚠ AND IT IS DERIVED UNCACHED FOR EVERY ROW, WHICH IS CR-3f.1 Q5's RATIFIED ORDER — ship uncached, cache only behind a recorded measurement. The measurement: 7 ms for a five-run Docket** including brief reads, so no cache is justified. Costs are bounded by construction — the query pulls only `positions` and `arbitration` turns (critique and synthesis are the longest and contribute nothing), and briefs are read **once per path rather than once per run**, which matters because re-running one brief is the ordinary case. **Verified that one unreadable brief omits only its own line and leaves the other four rows intact** — CR-3f.1's R5, whole-Docket outage from one bad file, does not reproduce. | **ACCEPTED 2026-08-04 (Matthew)** |
| D120 | **⚠ THREE STATES, THREE DIFFERENT AMOUNTS OF "GONE" — ONE ORDERED VOCABULARY, NOT TWO BOOLEANS.** `projects.status` is `'active' \| 'hidden' \| 'archived'`, and the three differ in what they do to RUNNING WORK rather than merely in what they show. **`hidden` is cosmetic:** out of the main rail list, **sessions keep running and still restore at boot**, still offered by the command palette, instantly reversible. **`archived` is retired:** live PTYs are **stopped**, the project is skipped at boot restore and **cannot be selected, launched into, or councilled** — but every session row, council run, transcript and attention span is **kept and readable**. **`deleted` is not a status at all** — it is the removal of the row, and admitting it to the vocabulary would invite a read site to render a project the delete transaction has already removed. **⚠ `is_hidden` + `is_archived` WAS REJECTED BECAUSE IT EXPRESSES FOUR STATES, ONE OF WHICH (hidden-and-archived) IS NONSENSE**, and every read site would then have to decide for itself what it meant. Free-text column validated by a **Zod enum on the boundary** — the `sessions.status` / `worktrees.status` / `auth_mode` convention — and **no `CHECK` constraint**, for v13's reason: a limit belongs where it can be reported, not where it surfaces as a failed write. **⚠ WHICH MAKES THE ENUM IN `shared/ipc.ts` THE ACTUAL AUTHORITY rather than a copy of one**, so it lives there and `storage.ts` imports it. | **ACCEPTED 2026-08-07 (Matthew)** |
| D121 | **⚠ DELETE TOUCHES NOTHING ON DISK. CHORUS'S OWN ROWS ONLY.** No `fs.rm`, no `git worktree remove`, no folder deletion, in any task of Phase 3h. The user's project folder is never touched, and neither is any worktree directory or branch. **The confirmation says so IN ITS OWN SENTENCE rather than as a trailing clause** — "delete project" is a phrase a reasonable person reads as "delete my work", and a subordinate clause at the end of a sentence about deletion is the part that gets skimmed. **Verified at runtime:** the delete path reaches no filesystem call, and `project:delete`'s payload carries a project id with no path in it. | **ACCEPTED 2026-08-07 (Matthew)** |
| D122 | **⚠ HIDDEN AND ARCHIVED PROJECTS STAY REACHABLE — NOTHING SILENTLY VANISHES.** A collapsed disclosure at the foot of the rail — `Archived (3)`, `Hidden (1)`, or `Tucked away (5)` when both kinds are present — expanding **in place** with the rows **dimmed rather than restyled** (they are the same rows, set back; a different visual language would make them read as a different kind of thing). **THE COUNT IS ALWAYS IN THE HEADING**, and that is what makes the promise checkable: whatever is not in the list above is counted below. **A project you cannot find is a project you cannot un-hide, and the app has no other index of projects** — the palette lists hidden ones but deliberately NOT archived ones, so for an archived project **this row is the only way back**. **⚠ THE TUCKED ROWS CARRY ONLY `Unhide` / `Unarchive`. DELETE IS NEVER OFFERED FROM THE RAIL** — one destructive door, on the project settings screen, behind a typed name. A "remove" affordance two pixels from an "unhide" affordance, on rows the user is scanning quickly because they are looking for something they mislaid, is how the wrong one gets clicked. **The disclosure is hidden entirely when the rail is collapsed** (48px has no room for a labelled heading, and a bare count is a mystery button). | **ACCEPTED 2026-08-07 (Matthew)** |
| D123 | **⚠ DELETE REQUIRES TYPING THE PROJECT'S NAME, AND THE CONFIRMATION STATES THE COUNTS UP FRONT** — D109's rule at a new surface. The size of what is about to go is stated **before** the action, and the sentence names **what survives** as well as what goes. **The equality check is EXACT and lives in MAIN**, because that is the only side a mistaken or compromised renderer cannot skip; the renderer's own check merely arms the button. **Verified at runtime that four near-misses are all refused** — a shorter name, a case mismatch, a trailing space, and the empty string. **⚠ AND A SECOND, NON-NEGOTIABLE GUARD SITS BESIDE IT: A LIVE SESSION REFUSES THE DELETE.** No foreign key catches a running PTY — the rows delete cleanly and the process is orphaned, still holding a handle, with nothing left in the database that names it. Refusing is right rather than killing-then-deleting: the user asked to delete a project, not to stop four agents mid-thought, and **archive is the control that says so**. | **ACCEPTED 2026-08-07 (Matthew)** |
| D124 | **⚠ WORKTREE ROWS ARE PURGED; WORKTREE DIRECTORIES ARE NOT.** `worktrees.project_id` is `NOT NULL REFERENCES projects(id)` with FKs enforced (F16), so **a detached worktree row cannot outlive its project**, and the retained-worktree panel reads `getWorktreesForProject(projectId)` — so it could never surface one if it did. `deleteProject` therefore deletes those rows inside its transaction. **The directories and branches stay on disk**, and the confirmation **names the count and says Chorus stops tracking them**. This is D121 applied to the one case where "we deleted the row" and "we deleted your work" could be confused. **⚠ AND IT IS THE FIRST ORDERING CONSTRAINT IN THE PURGE: `worktrees` MUST BE DELETED BEFORE `sessions`**, because `worktrees.session_id` REFERENCES `sessions.id`. Any other order throws `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_FOREIGNKEY` and rolls the whole transaction back — **verified at runtime against a project holding a worktree that pointed at its own session.** | **ACCEPTED 2026-08-07 (coordinator)** |
| D125 | **⚠ THE CHANNEL EXCEPTION, DECLARED BEFORE THE CODE — FOUR CHANNELS, AND PHASE 3h ADDS NO OTHERS.** `project:set-status`, `project:reorder`, `project:delete`, `project:impact`. **`IpcChannel` 64 → 68**; **`ipcMain.handle(` in `src/main/ipc.ts` 58 → 62**, in `src/main/index.ts` **stays 0**; **`sqliteTable(` stays 16** (no new table); **`MIGRATIONS.length` 14 → 15** (exactly one migration, Task 1's). Task 1 held at 64/58, all four landed together in Task 2, Task 3 held at 68/62. The D74/D80 discipline, for the reason the `IpcChannel` map's own comment gives: *"a tally nobody maintains is worse than no tally: it reads as a check that has been passing."* Why each cannot ride an existing channel: **`set-status` cannot ride `project:update`**, which is a **total overwrite sent by a form**, while a status change **kills PTY processes** — folding a process-killing side effect into the identity-save path puts every future settings edit one typo away from stopping the user's agents. **`impact` cannot ride `project:list`** (its transcript-turn count scans `council_messages` through `council_runs`, and `project:list` runs at boot and on every `store.load()`) **nor be a `dry_run` flag on `project:delete`** (a dropped boolean on a destructive channel deletes data). **`reorder` takes `ordered_ids` — EVERY project id in the new order, not a moved pair** — and main validates a **full permutation against `listProjects()`'s ACTUAL ids**, never a uuid predicate, because a well-formed uuid that is not a project is still not a permutation. **`delete` is its own channel** because it is the only irreversible one, and a destructive verb sharing a handler with a reversible one is how the wrong branch gets taken. **⚠ ONE FIELD WAS ADDED OUTSIDE `projectSchema` AND IS RECORDED RATHER THAN ABSORBED: `project:add`'s response gains `reactivated_from`** — see F45, where it was later found to be unread, wired to a tested sentence, and narrowed to `TuckedProjectStatus`. | **ACCEPTED 2026-08-07 (coordinator)** |
| D126 | **⚠ CR-6.0 IS CLOSED — G5 DISCHARGED. SIX QUESTIONS, 4/4 MEMBERS, AND THE ARBITER APPROVED FIVE WHILE REFUSING ONE.** Findings: [`CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-6.0-MemorySchemaProvenance-Findings.md`](CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-6.0-MemorySchemaProvenance-Findings.md), run **`41c80955-9545-4078-be89-9f7365384803`**, 2026-08-08, on [`CouncilBrief-6.0-MemorySchemaProvenance.md`](CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-6.0-MemorySchemaProvenance.md). Verdicts: **Q1 · Q2 · Q4 · Q5 APPROVED WITH REVISIONS · Q6 APPROVED · Q3 REVISE.** Members split structurally on **Q2 only** (Qwen QUALIFY, other three AGREE); 25 dissents preserved, six per member — **read that as four members each objecting six times, not as twenty-five separate objections.** **ADOPTED AS FILED:** all six `Plan.md` §10 corrections (Q1, and D94/D102 are thereby ratified rather than merely asserted); **advisory-and-measured provenance** as the initial architecture (Q2) **on condition that the measurement is consequential** — numerator *and* denominator, ratio, trend, **new-unsourced split from historical backlog**, per-agent breakdown, an affected-node list and a repair action, because *"a bare count is decorative"*; **"names not values" retained but its claim NARROWED** (Q4) to durable configuration and credential lifecycle, with the explicit statement that it does **not** protect against runtime process inspection or agent self-disclosure — *"a secret-bearing pane is trusted code with runtime access to that secret"*; **write the config and disclose the approval step, never pre-approve** (Q6), behind a **`Configured → Pending approval → Connected → Failed`** state model where **`Connected` requires an observed probe read, not a written file.** **⚠ THREE THINGS THE COUNCIL FOUND THAT NO QUESTION ASKED, AND ONE OF THEM IS THE STRONGEST OUTPUT OF THE RUN:** *(1)* **arbitrary Cypher is a GRAPH-INTEGRITY risk, not only a provenance risk** — an agent can bulk-modify, poison or delete memories **and the provenance relationships that would have detected it**, which makes backup/export/restore a prerequisite rather than an operational afterthought (**opened as F49, and it GATES STAGE 5**); *(2)* **`(repo, path)` is itself insufficient** — a repository identity shared by several simultaneously-checked-out worktrees still collides, so identity must be a **stable worktree-instance id plus a normalized repository-relative path, with absolute paths demoted to fallback metadata**; *(3)* **a Chorus-written session node is not provenance** — if agents can ignore, relabel or delete it, counting it as a source manufactures a false denominator. **⚠ AND THE STANDING CAVEAT EARNED ITS PLACE AGAIN — SEE D128 resolution (b): the council asked for an allow-list addition this phase's own measurement had already refuted.** **⚠ THE COST OF THIS RUN IS DELIBERATELY NOT RECORDED, AND THE GAP IS DECLARED RATHER THAN LEFT TO LOOK LIKE AN OVERSIGHT.** Task 6-1's acceptance criterion *"cost stated against the ~$2.20 envelope, as a bound, saying which number is quoted"* is **WAIVED by Matthew, 2026-08-08** — *"I'm not too concerned about the reporting price right now. We can tighten up the accuracy of that later."* **The known defect is why the waiver is reasonable:** Chorus's `cost_usd` omits the synthesis turn because the key-usage read fires before OpenRouter settles it, so **the app's own figure is an under-report of unknown size** and quoting it would put a number in the roadmap that is wrong in a direction nobody has measured — the D55 failure in its purest form. **This is the FIRST phase-gate decision in this project recorded without its cost**, and it is a declared exception, not a new practice: the envelope (~$2.20, D101) still bound the run, and one run was spent. **Reconcile against OpenRouter's billing page when the reporting defect is fixed.** | **ACCEPTED 2026-08-08 (council + coordinator, see D128)** |
| D127 | **⚠ THE ACTIVE-CONTAINER CEILING IS TWO OR THREE, SET BY POLICY RATHER THAN BY PRESSURE — AND IT DELETES MOST OF WHAT CR-6.0 ASKED FOR.** Matthew's ruling, 2026-08-08, in his words: *"I can really only envision running 2 or 3 projects at a time (thus, only 2 or 3 containers at a time) so I can stay within the reasonable level for using this service."* **⚠ THIS IS DELIBERATELY TIGHTER THAN THE COUNCIL'S OWN NUMBER.** CR-6.0's Q5 ruling sets *"approximately three concurrently running containers on a 16 GB workstation, approximately five on a 32 GB workstation"* — **and this machine measured 31.24 GiB, so the council would have allowed five.** Matthew took three. **THE CONSEQUENCE THE COUNCIL DID NOT DRAW, AND IT IS THE POINT OF RECORDING THIS SEPARATELY:** the council's Q5 machinery — adaptive thresholds keyed on available memory, memory-pressure warnings, the *">5 triggers lifecycle management"* and *">10 is not reasonable"* escalation rules, and prompts to archive long-unused graphs — **is all machinery for a problem this ruling removes by policy.** A ceiling the user has already imposed on himself does not need a governor. **WHAT SURVIVES IS THE CHEAP HALF** of action items 17–21: **start on project focus, stop on idle, and show running/stopped state.** What is cut: adaptive memory-pressure control, the escalation bands, and the archive-prompting flow. **⚠ AND THE CEILING IS A DEFAULT, NOT AN ENFORCED CAP** — Chorus does not refuse a fourth container; D55 applies, so if it ever shows a count it shows *"3 of 3 running"* and never a bare number. Revisit only if the count is ever raised. | **ACCEPTED 2026-08-08 (Matthew)** |
| D128 | **⚠ THE COORDINATOR'S RESOLUTIONS ON CR-6.0, STATED SEPARATELY BECAUSE A COUNCIL DOES NOT RATIFY ITSELF** (the D33/D63 idiom). **(a) Q3's REVISE IS ADOPTED, AND ITS HONEST CONSEQUENCE IS THAT CREDENTIALED MODE LEAVES PHASE 6 ENTIRELY.** The council set **eight preconditions** before credentialed memory may ship — measured per-CLI environments including cold cache, executable-resolution verify-or-fail, an explicit confirmation gate, a persistent restricted-profile indicator, and proof that each CLI supports name-only indirection. **Local mode is MEASURED working (6-1 D4 pass) and needs none of them**, and it keeps `secretEnv` empty so **H3 never fires**. Building the eight inside this phase would roughly double it to satisfy a mode the default does not use. **Phase 6 therefore ships LOCAL-MODE ONLY**; credentialed mode moves out with its preconditions attached. **This is a scope CUT taken because the council refused something, which is what a gate is for.** **(b) ⚠ THE COUNCIL ASKED FOR A `BASELINE_ENV_VARS` ADDITION THAT THIS PHASE'S OWN MEASUREMENT REFUTES, AND THE MEASUREMENT WINS.** Deepseek's dissent requires the allow-list *"be corrected to include `APPDATA`, `LOCALAPPDATA`"*. **The D4 pass measured that `LOCALAPPDATA` IS NOT NEEDED** — `uv` derives its cache from `USERPROFILE`, which is already on the list, and **every MCP probe in the pass ran to completion under the unmodified seven-variable environment**. `APPDATA` matters only to **npm-distributed** servers; the Neo4j MCP server is **PyPI via `uvx`**, so nothing in this phase observes the need. **NEITHER VARIABLE IS ADDED.** D88's three-lists trap governs: a COPY-FROM entry is admitted **only on observed empirical necessity**, and reasoning from a brief is not observation. **This is the standing caveat — the council cannot see the repository — landing on a checkable fact, exactly as CR-3b.0 did.** The `npx` downgrade stays recorded as a real defect (**F48**) against whoever first ships an npm-distributed MCP server — **deferred, not dismissed.** **(c) THE 26 ACTION ITEMS ARE ROUGHLY TRIPLE THIS PHASE'S SCOPE AND ARE TRIAGED, NOT ABSORBED.** **In Phase 6:** items 1–5, 10, 11, 22–25 (identity model, APOC prohibition, provenance relationships and health calculation, enforcement-boundary statement, local-mode default, the four-state model). **Deferred with an owner:** 6, 7–9, 12–21, 26 — backup/restore, the repair workflow and context labelling, per-CLI probing, and container lifecycle. **⚠ ITEMS 7–9 ARE DEFERRED AS A UNIT AND THE REASON IS D55:** a validator that reports *"43 of 512"* is in scope; a trend, a repair workflow and context re-weighting are a **feature**, and the council itself said the measurement must be consequential or it is decoration — so shipping the number without the workflow would be shipping the decoration it warned against. **The honest statement is that Phase 6 ships the measurement and NOT yet the consequence, and the roadmap says so rather than letting `memory:validate` imply otherwise.** **(d) THE COUNCIL'S Q5 ARITHMETIC WAS SOUND AND IS SUPERSEDED ANYWAY BY D127.** It reasoned from the plan's 512 MB–1 GB heap figure; the D4 pass measured **496 MiB idle for an empty Neo4j**, so the band was right. Matthew's tighter ceiling governs. **(e) Q1's CORRECTION (b) IS NOW STRONGER THAN WHEN IT WAS WRITTEN.** *"One database per project is unachievable"* was recorded as a Neo4j Community limitation. **Measured 2026-08-08: Memgraph 3.12.0 Community refuses `CREATE DATABASE` with `Access to multi-tenancy requires an enterprise, ai_platform, or oem license`** — the same gate. It is a property of the free tier of the category, not a Neo4j quirk, and **no vendor swap relaxes it** (see the Phase 6 entry's storage-engine note). | **ACCEPTED 2026-08-08 (coordinator)** |

| D129 | **⚠ D78 IS DISCHARGED — THE FILMSTRIP HAS FOUR STATES, AND THE HOOK LISTENER IS BUILT.** Raised by Matthew 2026-08-07 from the running app: *"I'm not seeing the green/red/amber lights based on the status of the agent. They are just all green...all the time."* **TWO SEPARATE DEFECTS WERE BEHIND ONE SYMPTOM, AND ONLY ONE OF THEM WAS THE MISSING AMBER.** ***(1) THE LIGHTS WERE FROZEN, WHICH NO ROADMAP ENTRY PREDICTED.*** `App.vue` re-read its session rows on exactly two events — a project switch and a pane close — so a card's light was fixed at whatever the row said when it was last fetched. **Reproduced before the fix: a carded session was killed, main persisted `status:'exited', exitCode:1`, and the card kept rendering the green `running` circle indefinitely.** Grid mode hid it, because `TerminalPane` carries its own `onSessionExit` listener and **a card never attaches** — so the one surface built entirely out of lights was the one surface no lifecycle event reached. Fixed by patching the row **from the event payload rather than by re-fetching**: main persists the status in one `onExit` listener and broadcasts in another, and `ipc.ts` states outright that order within that Set *"is not contractual"*, so a `layout:get` fired by the broadcast can legitimately read the row before the write lands. Three relaunch paths (`TerminalPane` restart, `TerminalPane` relaunch, the palette's `restartFocused`) emitted **nothing** — `session:restored` is the restore ENGINE's alone — so a `chorus:session-relaunched` window event now carries them, on the `chorus:session-closed` precedent. ***(2) AMBER HAD NO SOURCE, AND NOW IT HAS ONE.*** D78 ruled three states because *"the renderer cannot know an agent is waiting for a human"*; **that premise is what this removes, not the reasoning.** A localhost hook listener (`services/agentEvents.ts`) receives Claude Code's own lifecycle events, so `Stop`, `Notification` and `PermissionRequest` mean **needs-you** as a REPORTED FACT. **This is D83's rule applied exactly as D83 states it: omit it OR GIVE IT A SOURCE, never fake it.** `chorusPulse` — shipped by 3c-3 with no caller — has its first one, on the card, where 3c-1 said it belonged. **⚠ THE DURABLE HALF OF D78 SURVIVES INTACT AND IS ENFORCED IN CODE: an unrecognised hook event returns `null` and leaves activity UNCHANGED, and an agent with no hook bus (codex, kimi, opencode) keeps exactly three states.** *"We don't know"* must never render as *"it needs you"* — amber is the only state allowed to interrupt. **⚠ VERIFIED ON THE RUNNING APP, ALL FOUR STATES, NOT REASONED:** `running` → `working` (on `UserPromptSubmit`, ~1 s) → `needs-you` (on `Stop`, ~3 s, amber diamond + `data-pulse` + *"needs you"*) → `error` (killed, red triangle, *"exit 1"*) → `done` (clean `/exit`, grey square). **⚠ AND THE HOOK VOCABULARY WAS READ, NOT RECALLED (D4):** the event names came off a shipping plugin's `hooks.json` and the lifecycle was then observed end to end against the installed claude **2.1.225**. | **ACCEPTED 2026-08-07 (Matthew — "Build the Phase 4 hook listener now")** |
| D130 | **⚠ THE [CR] SPOOFING QUESTION IS ANSWERED, WITH ITS LIMIT NAMED RATHER THAN PAPERED OVER.** Phase 4's open question was *"how do we secure the localhost hook listener against local processes spoofing agent events?"*. **The answer:** bound to **127.0.0.1 on an OS-assigned ephemeral port**; a **per-session capability token** (32 crypto-random bytes) minted at launch, mapped to exactly one sessionId and **revoked on every exit path**; **attribution by TOKEN, never by anything in the payload**, so a body claiming another `session_id` cannot cross sessions — which is also what makes attribution correct at all, since Claude's own `session_id` is not Chorus's and two sessions in one cwd are otherwise indistinguishable; **one route** (`POST /hook/<64 hex>`), with every other method, path and token shape getting an **identical empty 404** so the listener never confirms what exists; a **256 KB body cap** with the connection destroyed past it; and **only `hook_event_name` is read** — no prompt text, no transcript path, no tool input is extracted, stored or logged, because what is not taken cannot leak. **Probed at runtime: bogus token, wrong method, wrong path and malformed token all return 404, and the port is unreachable off loopback.** **⚠ NAMED LIMIT, NOT A CLAIM OF PROOF: this defends against a local process that does not already have the user's file access. It does NOT defend against a process running as the same user**, which can read the token out of the per-session settings file, the DB, or main's heap — the same excluded threat class `safeStorage` already assumes, recorded here so nobody later mistakes the token for a defence it is not. **⚠ TWO DELIVERY DECISIONS WORTH THE INK.** The config is injected with **`--settings <file>`, NOT written into `.claude/settings.json`** as the Phase 4 line proposed: that would mean Chorus writing into the user's REPOSITORY — a tracked file, in a worktree an agent is about to commit from — where `--settings` is additive, per-launch, and leaves nothing to clean up in git. And the token goes in a **FILE, never argv**: `PtyLaunchSpec.extraArgs` already carries the standing warning that argv is world-readable (`Get-CimInstance Win32_Process`), and `--settings` also accepts an inline JSON string that would have put a live capability there. **⚠ `curl -o NUL` IS LOAD-BEARING, NOT TIDINESS: a hook command's STDOUT is a control channel** — Claude Code parses JSON printed there as a hook decision object, which is how a `PreToolUse` hook denies a tool — so without it curl would write the listener's HTTP response body to stdout and hand the agent a decision it never made, **on every tool call**. | **ACCEPTED 2026-08-07 (coordinator)** |
| D131 | **⚠ THE MEMORY SURFACE LIVES IN `ProjectSettingsView.vue`, NOT IN A NEW SETTINGS ROUTE — AND THE SPEC IT OVERRIDES WAS STALE RATHER THAN WRONG.** `ImplementationSpec-6-3.md` §6 said *"`SettingsMemory.vue` is a new Settings route beside Credentials and Providers."* **That structure does not exist, and this was CHECKED rather than assumed at execution:** `SettingsView.vue` is **78 lines with exactly ONE nav entry** (`Providers & keys`), no router and no switching; **`SettingsCredentials.vue` is not a route at all** — it is a child component of `SettingsProviders.vue` (imported `:11`, used `:1004`). So "a new route beside Credentials" names a sibling relationship that has never existed. **Meanwhile `ProjectSettingsView.vue` arrived in Phase 3h** (`882dec3`, 1045 lines) as a per-project full-window view reached two ways — the rail's per-project gear, and immediately after `Add project` — and **`project_memory` is keyed by `project_id`.** **THE DECIDING ARGUMENT IS COST, NOT TASTE:** a global route would have to own **a project selector duplicating the rail**, and `SettingsView`'s nav has no switching to extend, so the spec's instruction buys a whole navigation mechanism in order to reach a **per-project** fact from a **global** screen. **The spec is not wrong; it PREDATES the file that is now the obvious host** — the D42/D94/D102 precedent, annotated rather than rewritten. **⚠ AND THE DECISION NUMBER ITSELF IS THE SECOND LESSON.** `Task-6-3.md` and its execution prompt both proposed **D129**, computed when D128 was the highest — **but D129 and D130 had been spent by Phase 4's hook-listener work in between**, so a doc-stated decision number decayed exactly the way a doc-stated migration number does. **The prompt's own instruction to re-check before using it is what caught it**, and the standing rule is now the same in both places: **a number recorded in a task doc is a claim about the past; confirm it at the moment of writing.** | **ACCEPTED 2026-08-08 (Matthew)** |
| D132 | **⚠ THE NAMING COLLISION D41(b) RAISED IS RATIFIED AND DISCHARGED: the scheduling panel keeps *Mission Control*; the Phase 5 always-on-top pane switcher is renamed *Fleet Switcher*.** D41 recorded the collision and recommended exactly this split "pending Matthew's ratification"; the ratification is now given, so the recommendation becomes the fact. **Renamed at all six sites carrying the OVERLAY sense**, none of which were about scheduling: D38's mock inventory (§ "5 (mission control, push-to-talk)" → "5 (Fleet Switcher, push-to-talk)"), the `Chorus Micro Surfaces.dc.html` ownership line, the Phase 3c kickoff-question list in **both** places it is quoted (the questions are asked once and echoed in the 3c retrospective — renaming one and not the other would have re-created the collision inside a single document), the Phase 5 feature line, and the `'mission-control'` hypothetical view id in the view-classification note, which becomes `'fleet-switcher'`. **⚠ NOTHING IN PHASE 8 OR D41 IS RENAMED** — every "Mission Control" from line 359 onward already meant the scheduler and is now unambiguous rather than merely correct-by-context. **Why the overlay was the one to move, stated so it is not relitigated:** the scheduler's name is load-bearing in a committed 518-line spec, its own feature directory, and a phase title; the overlay's name appears only in prose and one mock filename. The cheaper side moved. **⚠ THE DESIGN MOCKS DID CARRY THE NAME AS RENDERED LABEL TEXT, AND THEY ARE RENAMED TOO — 14 sites across 7 files.** `Chorus Micro Surfaces.dc.html` (*"A · MISSION CONTROL — always-on-top strip over your IDE"*), `Chorus Overview.dc.html` (the A/B legend and the orientation open question), `Chorus Workspace.dc.html` (the rail label and its tooltip), **each in both the v1 and v2 directories**, plus the `_ui/main-workspace.html` prototype's rail button and tooltip. **This is a D73 edit — "the mock is the authority" — and it is exactly why it was done rather than left:** an authority still reading MISSION CONTROL would have re-seeded the collision into whatever Phase 5 builds from it. **No file is renamed and no pixel moves** — only the label string changes, so the mocks stay diffable against their prior versions. **In shipped `src/`, one site changed:** `attentionCore.test.ts`'s hypothetical-future-view fixture, from `'mission-control'` to `'fleet-switcher'`, tracking the view-classification note; every other `src/` occurrence is a spec citation meaning the scheduler and is untouched. **`docs/Plan.md` is renamed too** (the §5 feature bullet and the Phase 5 line) because `CLAUDE.md` points every agent at it as the architecture source — a stale name there would outlive this roadmap entry. **⚠ COMPLETED-PHASE TASK DOCS ARE DELIBERATELY *NOT* REWRITTEN** — `Phase-3c-Overview.md`, `Task-3c-1-ExecutionPrompt.md` and `Task-3c-3.md` still say "mission-control overlay", and they should: they are the record of what was decided and shipped under the name in force at the time, and this roadmap's own header states the log "accumulates rather than being rewritten". **The line drawn, so it is not re-argued: rename what is FORWARD-LOOKING (roadmap, Plan, mocks, live code), preserve what is HISTORICAL (shipped task docs, D41's original text).** **⚠ NUMBERED D132, NOT D131 AS AUTHORED — see D134.** This decision was written on a branch as D131 while Task 6-3 was simultaneously taking D131 on another; renumbered at the merge rather than either side being trusted. | **RATIFIED 2026-08-08 (Matthew)** |
| D133 | **⚠ MISSION CONTROL'S TELEMETRY IS REPAIRED BEFORE ITS BOARD IS BUILT, AND THE REPAIR IS A NEW GRANULARITY RATHER THAN A BUG FIX.** Matthew's direction, 2026-08-08, on being shown the audit: fix the telemetry first. **(a) THE DIAGNOSIS THAT CHANGED THE PLAN — F52.** The capture layer works; the *unit* is wrong. A dispatch is one PTY lifetime and an interactive pane never completes, so 5 of 172 dispatches are `completed` and wall-clock measures terminal uptime, not work. **No repair to `classifyOutcome` helps** — it is already correct. **(b) THE RIGHT UNIT ALREADY ARRIVES AND IS THROWN AWAY, WHICH IS D50's ARGUMENT LANDING A SECOND TIME.** `agentEvents.ts` receives Claude Code's hooks over D130's authenticated listener, classifies them (`working` / `needs-you`), and holds the result in an **in-memory `Map`** for the filmstrip lights. A `needs-you → working` edge is `UserPromptSubmit`; `working → needs-you` is `Stop`. **That pair is one turn — an observed start and an observed end — and nothing persists it.** Every day unpersisted is calibration permanently lost. **(c) `Tasks/Task-8-0.md` + `ImplementationSpecs/ImplementationSpec-8-0.md` ARE AUTHORED AND DELIBERATELY HELD.** **⚠ AND THE COLLISION IS NOT HYPOTHETICAL — IT IS ALREADY BOOKED.** Phase 6's own entry reserves the next number outright (_"The next free version is `v16` and the assertion is `MIGRATIONS.length + 1 === 16`"_), and `MIGRATIONS.length` was **15** on `main` at `fd41f98`. **Task 8-0 is therefore NOT v16 — it is the next free number after Phase 6 lands, expected to be v17 and pinned to nothing.** Phase 6's entry also records its own version decaying **twice** while it waited, which is the standing rule's whole justification: confirm `MIGRATIONS.length + 1` at the moment of writing and stop on divergence. Racing it is a defect, not a shortcut. **Docs now, implementation when Phase 6's schema position is known** — Matthew's explicit choice over claiming v16 immediately. **⚠ THE TASK MAY NOT WIDEN D130's READ SURFACE BY ONE FIELD:** it consumes the already-classified `onActivity` callback and stamps a timestamp; it parses no hook body and stores no content. **⚠ AND IT MAY NOT INVENT TURNS FOR HOOKLESS AGENTS** — `codex`, `kimi` and `opencode` get no hook config, so they produce no turns, exactly as D129 already gives them three lights instead of four. **(d) THE INSTALLED APP IS THE SYSTEM OF RECORD, AND THE MERGE IS ADDITIVE ONLY.** Per F51 the history is spread over five stores. `_verify/mission-control/merge-telemetry.py` folds `dispatches` and `attention_spans` into `%APPDATA%\chorus-app\chorus.db` with `INSERT OR IGNORE` on the UUID primary key — **idempotent, target always wins a collision, and dry-run-verified at 0 content conflicts across all five sources.** It touches **no** `sessions`, `projects`, `credential_profiles`, `launch_profiles` or council row, because merging live app state would add phantom projects to the sidebar; this is a history merge, not a state merge. It **refuses to run while Chorus is up**, since the running app's WAL would replay over the result. **(e) ⚠ PROJECT IDS ARE PRESERVED, NOT RECONCILED, AND THE ROLLUPS ARE WRONG UNTIL THEY ARE.** Three ids mean "Chorus", three mean "TR-Integration" (F51). Remapping them would rewrite a recorded fact to make a report tidier — **the exact move the estimator-honesty rules exist to prevent** — so the script prints the mapping and applies nothing. A reconciliation table belongs with Task 8-0's migration, whatever number it lands on. **(f) WHAT WAS *NOT* DONE, NAMED SO IT IS NOT ASSUMED:** no seed authored (Phase 8 proper), no token coverage widened (**83 of 131 dispatches — every `codex`/`kimi`/`opencode` run — still have no token source at all**, since `subscriptionMeter.ts` parses only Claude Code's JSONL), and **no gates run** — `node_modules` is empty in both the worktree and the main checkout, so G1/G2 could not be executed against any of this. **⚠ NUMBERED D133, NOT D132 AS AUTHORED — see D134.** **⚠ (g) SUPERSEDED IN PART 2026-08-10: clause (c)'s hold is DISCHARGED.** Task 8-0 landed as **`28159dd`** at migration **v18**, and the gates it could not run then were run: typecheck 0, vitest 1783/52, secret-grep clean, plus six runtime drives. Clause (c)'s prediction that the number would not be v16 held — it decayed twice more, to v17 and then v18. The reconciliation table clause (e) defers **did not** ship with Task 8-0's migration and remains unwritten; clause (f)'s three omissions all still stand. | **ACCEPTED 2026-08-08 (Matthew)** · **(c) DISCHARGED 2026-08-10** |
| D134 | **⚠ THE DECISION LOG COLLIDED WITH ITSELF, AND THIS ROW IS THE EXPLANATION TWO OTHER ROWS HAVE BEEN POINTING AT SINCE 2026-08-08.** Task 6-3's memory ruling and the Fleet Switcher rename were **both authored as D131** on branches that could not see each other, and were renumbered to **D132 / D133** when they merged at `02abd95`. Both rows close with *"see D134"* — **and no D134 was ever written**, so for two days the log's own account of its only renumbering was a dangling reference. Authored here on **2026-08-10**, during the architect pass that followed Task 8-0. **⚠ THE POINT IS NOT THE TIDY-UP; IT IS THAT THIS IS F54's SHAPE IN A FOURTH REGISTRY.** The `IpcChannel` tally collided four times, migration versions twice, and the decision log once — and the *forward reference written to explain the collision* then went missing too, because whoever renumbered was working in the merge and the explanation belonged to neither branch. **The mitigation is unchanged and is G6:** shared counters are facts about the merged tree, so re-read them after merging — and if a renumbering promises a note, the note is part of the merge, not a follow-up. | **RECORDED 2026-08-10** |
| D135 | **⚠ PHASE 5's SCOPE IS ADOPTED FROM A REQUIREMENTS DOCUMENT WRITTEN OUTSIDE THIS PROGRAM, AND THE ADOPTION IS A TRANSLATION RATHER THAN AN IMPORT.** Matthew supplied `Voice-Input-Feature-Requirements.md` (authored 2026-08-12 with another model, in the TaxApp repo) and asked for it to be integrated. **It is adopted as the scope of Phase 5.** The document is preserved byte-for-byte at `Investigations/Voice-Input-Feature-Requirements-source.md` behind a provenance banner, and the translation is `Phase-5-VoicePlan.md`. **⚠ THE SOURCE IS A RECORD, NOT A SPEC, AND THE BANNER SAYS SO — nobody implements from it.** **WHY A TRANSLATION WAS NECESSARY RATHER THAN FUSSY:** it is written for a generic multi-tenant web application, and **eight of its assumptions are false here** — it assumes tenants (there are none), a browser matrix (one Chromium, Windows-only), a form field as the destination (a PTY running an autonomous agent), server-side keys (BYOK, keys never leave main), a vendor-by-default audio path (local-first), an analytics pipeline (no telemetry egress exists and none is being built), and it *recommends against* the system-wide shortcut that is this feature's entire point. **Pasting it in would have imported all eight.** **WHAT CARRIES OVER UNAMENDED, because it is right and is not re-argued at kickoff:** the original transcript is the source of truth and AI never silently replaces it; three refinement levels with Clean up as default and Verbatim always available; refinement never invents, preserves names/numbers/dates/amounts/identifiers, marks unclear passages rather than guessing, and falls back to the original on failure; the recording state is never ambiguous and never signalled by colour alone; the microphone is released the instant capture ends; raw audio is not retained by default; and its state machine, failure-mode list and accessibility requirements survive translation essentially intact. **⚠ THE PHASE HAS NOT BEEN RE-ESTIMATED — `Plan.md` §14's `wk 2` predates all of this and is dead.** | **ADOPTED 2026-08-12 (Matthew)** |
| D136 | **⚠ CLICK-TO-TALK SHIPS BESIDE PUSH-TO-TALK, AND IT IS THE ACCESSIBILITY PATH RATHER THAN A CONVENIENCE — WHICH IS WHY IT IS NOT CUTTABLE.** `Plan.md` §9 and the mock describe hold-to-talk only; the source document asks for both. Both ship. **The reasoning that makes it non-negotiable: a sustained key-hold is exactly the interaction a motor-impaired user cannot perform**, and the source's own accessibility section requires the feature be usable without one. A click-to-toggle mode that is "added later if there's time" is an accessibility requirement dressed as a nice-to-have. Optional stop-after-silence attaches to the toggle mode. Repeated activations must not open overlapping capture sessions — structural in the state machine, not a defensive check. **⚠ AND THE SOURCE'S ADVICE AGAINST A SYSTEM-WIDE SHORTCUT IS OVERRIDDEN, NARROWLY AND ON PURPOSE.** The whole value of PTT here is dictating at a pane **while the IDE owns the foreground**; a shortcut that only works when Chorus is focused solves a problem nobody has. `Plan.md`'s stack table already commits to `uiohook-napi` for the reason that survives: Electron's own `globalShortcut` fires on press and **cannot express hold-to-talk**. The spirit of the caution is honored instead — the hotkey is configurable, disable-able, visible in settings, and the recording indicator is global and unmissable while it is held. **⚠ THE TARGETING CONTRADICTION IS ALSO CLOSED HERE, IN THE MOCK'S FAVOUR (D73).** `Plan.md` §9 says the transcript goes to *"the focused pane's stdin"*; the phase line, this roadmap and the mock all describe an explicit **dictation target with a ring**, and the mock adds *"hold tab while dictating to cycle targets."* **These were never the same rule.** There is an explicit target; it **defaults** to the focused pane; its ring is visible **before the user speaks**; Tab cycles it while the key is held. `Plan.md` §9 is corrected rather than left contradicting its own phase line. **⚠ AND THE TARGET CAN DIE MID-FLIGHT** — closed, killed, exited, or its project archived — so **the transcript must survive the loss of its target**, never be silently discarded, and never be written into whichever pane inherited focus. | **RESOLVED 2026-08-12** |
| D137 | **⚠ THE THREE REFINEMENT MODES ARE ADOPTED, BUT THE SURFACE THAT COMPARES THEM CANNOT EXIST WHERE THE MOCK PUTS THE TEXT — AND THAT IS THE DEEPEST THING THE TRANSLATION FOUND.** Verbatim / Clean up / Organize ship, Clean up default, Verbatim always available, every refined version labeled and stored **beside** the original rather than over it. **THE STRUCTURAL PROBLEM:** text reaches an agent through `session:write`, and **once written those bytes belong to the child process's line editor — Chorus cannot un-write, re-render, or diff them.** So *"switch between Original / Cleaned / Organized"*, *"restore the original"*, *"re-run refinement in another mode"* and *"see which version was AI-refined"* — four of the source document's review requirements — are achievable **only before the write**. **THE MOCK DRAWS THE TRANSCRIPT LANDING STRAIGHT AT THE `❯` PROMPT AND DRAWS NO COMPOSER.** So a Chorus-owned composer is an extension **beyond** the D73 authority, not a reading of it, and it is **staged rather than assumed: build the direct-to-prompt path first, then the composer when the modes it compares actually exist** — which is D76's ordering rule applied to a surface instead of to data. **⚠ THE DIRECT PATH IS NOT A DEGRADED MODE:** for Verbatim it is complete, and for Clean up the agent's own line editor is a real review surface, because no-auto-Enter leaves the text sitting there editable. **⚠ REFINEMENT REUSES `apiSession.ts` / `createApiSession` — THE COUNCIL'S BYOK PATH — RATHER THAN GROWING A SECOND PROVIDER CLIENT**, and its spend rides `usage_records` so voice is not an unmetered hole in the per-credential rollups Phase 7 reports. **It inherits that path's known defect:** cost capture under-reports the final turn because the key-usage read fires before the vendor settles it, and a single short refinement turn hits that race proportionally harder than a long council run does. **⚠ AND AUTO-SEND IS RECLASSIFIED AS A SAFETY RULE.** The source says don't auto-submit a form; the Chorus analogue is that Enter in an agent pane **starts an autonomous process that edits files and runs commands**, on a possibly-misheard transcript. `Plan.md` §9's existing no-auto-Enter default stands and stops being a preference. Whether "Stop and send" is refused outright on agent-locked sessions is a **[CR]** question, not settled here. | **RESOLVED 2026-08-12** |
| D138 | **⚠ THE MICROPHONE POSTURE IS A PRECONDITION OF THE FIRST CAPTURE LINE, NOT A HARDENING PASS AFTERWARDS.** `grep -rn "setPermissionRequestHandler\|setPermissionCheckHandler" src/main/` returns **nothing** at `e1afe89` — the app has **no stated position** on what happens if something asks for the microphone, and the first `getUserMedia` would resolve against Electron's built-in default rather than a Chorus rule. **⚠ WHAT THAT DEFAULT IS WAS NOT VERIFIED, AND THIS DECISION DELIBERATELY DOES NOT REST ON IT.** An earlier draft of this row asserted "Electron approves by default"; that was training-data memory, Electron 43.1.1's shipped typings document only how to *set* the handlers and say nothing about the unset case, and **D4 exists to stop exactly that move.** It is on the plan's measure-it list. **The requirement is unchanged either way — a security boundary with an unverified default is a boundary to close.** **Phase 5 installs the policy allowing `media` for the app's own origin and denying every other permission outright, BEFORE it writes its first capture line.** **⚠ AND IT TAKES BOTH HANDLERS, WHICH IS THE PART MOST LIKELY TO BE MISSED — verified from the tool's own docs rather than assumed:** `setPermissionRequestHandler`'s documentation states *"you must also implement `setPermissionCheckHandler` to get complete permission handling. Most web APIs do a permission check and then make a permission request if the check is denied."* Installing only the request handler leaves a hole the happy path will not reveal. **⚠ THE PIPELINE'S SHAPE IS FORCED, NOT CHOSEN, AND IT IS WHY THIS PHASE CARRIES A `[CR]` MARKER.** `getUserMedia` is a Chromium API and **main has no microphone access at all**, so capture is renderer-only; CLAUDE.md forbids the renderer spawning processes, D33 keeps credentials out of it, and the CSP keeps it off the network, so transcription is main-only. **Voice is therefore the first feature in which the renderer is a bulk binary PRODUCER, and D14 meets a payload it has never been tested against.** The frame envelope is Zod-validated per D1; the sample block is **length- and type-checked rather than element-validated**, stated out loud so it is never read as a D1 exemption. **Backpressure is a named failure mode** — a stalled transcriber bounds or drops its queue and the UI says so. **⚠ THE CSP WOULD BREAK TWO OF THE THREE OBVIOUS IMPLEMENTATIONS, WHICH IS D1's LESSON IN A THIRD REGISTRY.** `src/renderer/index.html:7` carries **no `connect-src`** (inherits `default-src 'self'` — the renderer cannot reach OpenAI or Deepgram) and **no `wasm-unsafe-eval`** (a whisper WASM build in the renderer throws, exactly as Zod-in-preload did under D1). Both push the work into main, where D33 and CLAUDE.md already wanted it. **The generalisation, so it is not re-derived a fourth time: the answer to "the CSP blocks this library" is MOVE THE WORK TO MAIN, never widen the policy.** **⚠ RETENTION AND DISCLOSURE:** raw audio is **not retained by default** and dies when transcription completes or is cancelled; **original and refined transcripts are stored separately and one never overwrites the other**; **transcript text never reaches ordinary application logs** — G4's discipline applied to a new category of sensitive content, and worth its own grep at kickoff. **The offline floor is explicit and must stay one setting away: Verbatim + local whisper touches no network, no key, no vendor, no LLM.** Clean up and Organize send transcript text to an LLM; cloud STT sends the audio itself; both are off by default and **disclosed where the mode is chosen, not buried in settings**. **⚠ ANALYTICS ARE LOCAL-ONLY — Phase 5 introduces no telemetry egress**, and recording content is never used for metrics. **⚠ AND NO VOICE ERROR MAY DEPEND ON AN OS TOAST TO BE SEEN:** toasts are proven dead on this machine, and voice has more failure states than any feature so far. | **RESOLVED 2026-08-12** |
| D139 | **⚠ THE RESUME CONTRACT AS DECLARED IS TOO THIN TO IMPLEMENT, AND IMPLEMENTING IT VERBATIM WOULD FORK THE LAUNCH PATH IN TWO.** `ResumeSpec` (`src/main/adapters/types.ts:351`) and `SupportsResume` (`:574`) were declared ahead of a caller, and the caller has now arrived and does not fit. **`ResumeSpec` is `{sessionId, cwd}`** — and a resumed launch needs **everything a fresh launch needs**: the **credential**, the **route**, the **effort**, **`extraArgs`** and the **hooks** descriptor, **every one of which `buildLaunch` already receives today** (`sessionManager.ts:619`).
