A two-field spec cannot carry any of them, so an adapter implementing `resumeSession()` verbatim would have to reconstruct the launch from scratch beside `buildLaunch` — **two code paths that must agree forever about credential injection and hook wiring, which is a drift guarantee, not a risk.** D33's rule that keys are injected as env at launch would then live in two places. **PROPOSED SHAPE:** **`PtyLaunchSpec` gains an optional `resume` field**, so **`buildLaunch` stays the single launch path** and resumption becomes a *modifier* on a launch rather than a parallel kind of launch; **`SupportsResume`/`ResumeSpec` are REDEFINED, NOT DELETED**, because codex genuinely changes argv **shape** on resume (a subcommand, per D140) and something must declare that. **⚠ THIS IS NOT RATIFIED AND IS NOT BEING TREATED AS IF IT WERE.** It changes a shared adapter interface, which is the surface every agent in the fleet is built against, and it is exactly the class of question G5 exists for. **⚠ THE TWO LINE NUMBERS ABOVE ARE CORRECTED FROM THE KICKOFF DOCS, WHICH HAVE THEM WRONG:** `Tasks/Phase-4a-Overview.md` prints *"`SupportsResume` / `ResumeSpec` (`types.ts:351`, `:574`)"* with the pair **SWAPPED**, and cites `buildLaunch` at `sessionManager.ts:561`, which Task 4a-4's own edits pushed to `:619`. Measured at `a6fab79`. **⚠ RULED 2026-08-13 BY CR-4a.0 — THE PROPOSED SHAPE IS APPROVED, AND ONE THING THE BRIEF DID NOT ASK FOR IS ADDED TO IT.** `PtyLaunchSpec` gains an optional modifier and `buildLaunch` stays the only launch entry point; `ResumeSpec` and `SupportsResume.resumeSession()` are **deleted**. **The council's own amendment: the modifier must model ASSIGNED CREATION as well as resumption**, because Claude has to receive the Chorus-minted id on its *first* launch, not only on a restore — so the type is `AgentSessionLaunch = {strategy:'assigned', action:'create'|'resume', agentSessionId} | {strategy:'discovered', action:'resume', agentSessionId}`. It accepted the naming cost of a `create` action living in a field called `resume` as cheaper than a second launch API, and all three members raised that objection independently before accepting it — the strongest argument against the winner is recorded rather than smoothed over. Argv grammar stays **inside each adapter's `buildLaunch`** (Q2); `ResumeDescriptor` is discriminated `assigned` \| `discovered` with a required `discoverSessionId(context)` on discovered adapters (Q3); a failed resume clears the pointer, relaunches **once** fresh and shows a visible badge, while a codex **discovery miss is explicitly NOT a resume failure** (Q4); the D34 Q1 honesty pairing becomes a structural descriptor check plus exact-argv tests (Q5). Findings at `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-4a.0-ResumeContract-Findings.md`. **⚠ PARTIAL RUN, AND THE SIGNAL IS THINNER THAN IT LOOKS: 3 of 4 members completed (Qwen 3.8 refused at position), and GLM 5.2 returned NO VERDICT TOKEN ON ANY OF THE SEVEN QUESTIONS** — so every "members agreed" line is a **two-token** structural signal and the three-member convergence lives in the prose positions, not in the computed verdicts. Q3 and Q7 disagreed structurally and were adopted with the qualification folded in. **Nothing in the findings was compiled, executed or tested, and no member could see the repository** — which is why D143 exists. | **RESOLVED 2026-08-13 by CR-4a.0 — adopted with SIX coordinator amendments at D143; Tasks 4a-2 and 4a-3 are UNBLOCKED** |
| D140 | **⚠ CLAUDE ASSIGNS, codex DISCOVERS, AND THE CAPABILITY DECLARES WHICH — resumption is deliberately NOT a uniform mechanism, because the CLIs are not uniform and pretending otherwise would mean guessing.** Verified live 2026-08-12, not recalled: **`claude --session-id <uuid>` ASSIGNS the id at launch** and **`claude --resume <uuid>` reopens it** — so for Claude Code, Chorus mints the id, writes it down before a single byte of output exists, and the pointer is correct by construction. **codex has NO launch-time id option at all, and resume is a SUBCOMMAND — `codex resume [SESSION_ID]` — not a flag**, so the id can only be **discovered after the fact**, and per F57 it can only be discovered from the rollout file's `session_meta` header. **The discipline that makes discovery safe: a discovered id is written ONLY after a positive `cwd` match on that header.** No match, no write — an empty pointer is a session the user relaunches by hand, whereas a wrong pointer resumes **someone else's conversation into this pane**, and those two failures are not close in cost. **The capability declares which mechanism an adapter uses**, so a third agent joining the fleet states its shape rather than falling into whichever branch the code happens to reach first. | **RESOLVED 2026-08-12** |
| D141 | **⚠ SCROLLBACK GOES TO A FLAT FILE PER SESSION AND NEVER INTO SQLITE — and the reasons are properties of the DATA, not preferences about storage.** It is an **append-only byte stream written at a 50 ms cadence** (`SCRUB_FLUSH_MS`), not a set of queryable records: nothing ever asks it a question with a `WHERE` clause, the only read is "give me the tail", and the only write is "add these bytes to the end". **The cap is a HEAD TRUNCATION** — 4,000,000 chars, rewritten by temp-file-plus-rename with a 25% slack margin — which is one atomic file operation and would be a delete-and-vacuum campaign against a table. And the blast radius is the argument that settles it: **a corrupt scrollback file costs ONE PANE'S HISTORY; a corrupt SQLite page costs the PROJECTS LIST.** Putting a multi-megabyte churning blob inside the database that holds every project, session, credential profile and council run buys nothing and risks the one store the app cannot boot without. **⚠ CONTRAST THIS DELIBERATELY WITH TASK 4a-1, WHICH DID THE OPPOSITE IN THE SAME PHASE.** `agent_session_id` — a short, single-valued, per-row **pointer**, read transactionally alongside the row it belongs to and meaningless without it — went **INTO** SQLite, as a column on `sessions`. **Two stores, one principle: match the store to the SHAPE of the data.** A phase that used one store for both would have been consistent and wrong. `docs/PLAN.md:173` had specified the file since v2.1 and it had never been built. | **RESOLVED 2026-08-12, DELIVERED 2026-08-13 by `bbf6d32`** |
| D142 | **⚠ `session:restart` ROTATES THE AGENT POINTER; `restore()` PRESERVES IT — and this is why `clearAgentSessionId` exists as its own NAMED METHOD rather than as a nullable setter.** **D16 clause 4 makes restart a deliberate fresh conversation**, so a restarted pane must not resume the old agent session: restart **clears** the pointer, boot-restore **keeps** it. That asymmetry is the whole contract. **The API follows the contract rather than the convenience:** `StorageService` exposes **`setAgentSessionId`, `clearAgentSessionId` and `getAgentSessionId`** as three methods, and `clearAgentSessionId` is **NOT** expressible as `setAgentSessionId(id, null)` **on purpose — forgetting is an intentional act and it gets a name.** A nullable setter lets a caller that merely **had nothing to pass** erase a live pointer **silently and unrecoverably**, because `getAgentSessionId` returns `?? null` and a missing row is indistinguishable from a NULL column to everyone downstream. Two verbs, two intentions, no accidental amnesia. **⚠ AND AN OPEN SUB-QUESTION WAS FOUND DURING IMPLEMENTATION, RECORDED RATHER THAN DECIDED: restart goes through `spawn()`, so a restarted pane currently RE-SEEDS its scrollback from the mirror and shows its prior scrollback.** That is **terminal-like** and arguably right — restarting a shell does not wipe what is above the prompt — but **no document rules on the scrollback half of restart**, only on the pointer half, and "arguably right" is how an unratified behaviour becomes a contract nobody chose. **Task 4a-3 owns restart and must ratify it explicitly, in either direction.** | **RESOLVED 2026-08-12 for the pointer · the SCROLLBACK half CLOSED 2026-08-13 by CR-4a.0 Q7 · ⚠ BOTH HALVES NOW IMPLEMENTED AND MEASURED by `756066c`.** The ordering claim was proven rather than asserted: after a restart the relaunch argv carried **`--session-id <new uuid>`, NOT `--resume <old uuid>`** — which is only possible if `clearAgentSessionId` ran **before** `sessions.launch`, since `spawn()` reads the pointer out of storage itself. A kill, by contrast, **preserved** the pointer, as the split requires. |
| D143 | **⚠ THE COORDINATOR'S AMENDMENTS ON CR-4a.0, STATED SEPARATELY BECAUSE A COUNCIL DOES NOT RATIFY ITSELF** (the D33/D63/D128 idiom). The council's shape is adopted whole — none of these six changes it. **They close gaps a council that could not see the repository could not have closed**, and every one was measured against the tree at `a6fab79`. **(a) ⚠ `classifyResumeFailure(observation)` TAKES TERMINAL TEXT, AND THE CONTRACT DOES NOT SAY WHICH TERMINAL TEXT — IT MUST BE THE POST-SCRUB STRING FROM THE SINGLE EMIT PATH.** `ResumeExitObservation.output` is specified only as *"bounded terminal text"* that *"must not be logged"*. A classifier reading session output is a **new consumer of session text**, and D45(1) makes scrubbing a property of "a session emits text": the one `scrubber.push()` per chunk feeds the ring buffer, the broadcast and the disk mirror, and this must hang off that same computed string. **A tap on raw PTY bytes here would be F26's exact shape** — the live A/B that found unredacted output reaching a new destination — and Task 4a-2 may not ship a second wiring point. Vendor error strings are not credentials, but the *contract* is generic and the next adapter's are not the coordinator's to predict. **(b) ⚠ THE AUTOMATIC RELAUNCH IN Q4 FIRES EVERY EXIT LISTENER, AND THERE ARE MORE THAN FIVE.** Measured: `dispatches.closeOnExit`, `turns.closeOnSessionExit`, `notifications`' exit toast, `attention.onSessionExited`, `dispatchAttribution`, plus three further `sessions.onExit` registrations in `ipc.ts`. As the findings specify it, one classified resume failure closes a dispatch, closes a turn, fires an exit notification and lights the project rail red **for a session that came straight back**. **Task 4a-3 must suppress the intent-bearing ones**, and the mechanism already exists: `killRequested` is set on the session record BEFORE `pty.kill()` precisely so a deliberate end cannot misclassify as an agent failure (Task 3a-1). A resume-failure relaunch is a deliberate end. **(c) ⚠ THE POINTER IS MINTED BEFORE ARGV AND PERSISTED AFTER THE SPAWN SUCCEEDS, NOT BEFORE IT — action item 6's ordering contradicts a convention this app already runs on.** `restore()` writes `'running'` **only after** the spawn succeeds (D16 resolution a), so that a crash between the two leaves a row that is self-consistent at the next boot's reconcile. Persisting an assigned id first means a failed spawn leaves a pointer to a conversation that never existed; the next launch resumes it, fails, clears it, and shows a **spurious** "context not restored" badge on a session that never had context. The id must be in argv, so it is minted first — but `setAgentSessionId` runs after. **(d) ⚠ THIS PUTS `--session-id` ON EVERY CLAUDE LAUNCH, AND THE VERIFIED EVIDENCE IS PRINT-MODE ONLY.** D140's measurement was `claude --session-id <uuid> -p …` — `-p` is non-interactive, and every Chorus pane is an interactive TUI. `claude --help` documents the flag generically (*"Use a specific session ID for the conversation (must be a valid UUID)"*) with no `--print` restriction, so it very probably holds — **but this changes argv on the most-used path in the app, not merely the restore path**, and D4 does not accept "probably" for a CLI fact. **Task 4a-2 re-verifies it interactively before it ships.** **(e) ⚠ `--resume` WITH NO VALUE OPENS AN INTERACTIVE PICKER, SO THE FLAG MAY NEVER BE EMITTED BARE.** From `claude --help`, verbatim: *"-r, --resume [value] — Resume a conversation by session ID, or open interactive picker with optional search term."* The value is **optional to the CLI**, so an empty or undefined pointer does not error — it drops a session picker into the pane and waits for a human who is not looking. An explicit argv guard is required: no value, no flag. **(f) `ResumeDescriptor` IS ON THE IPC WIRE, so adding `kind` is a Zod schema change even though the channel count is not.** `resumeDescriptorSchema` (`src/shared/ipc.ts:2219`) feeds `agentCapabilitiesSchema` and `adapter:list`. The findings list *"renderer-facing IPC channels, `IpcChannel`, and renderer code"* as explicitly unchanged, and `IpcChannel` does stay **86** — but `z.object` **strips** unknown keys rather than rejecting them, so a `kind` added to the runtime object and not to the schema would vanish on the wire **silently**. The renderer never reads `sessionResume` (grep-verified), so nothing breaks either way; the schema is updated for honesty, per D1. **⚠ AND THIS IS WHY `mode: DescriptorMode` STAYS.** Three members flagged it as surplus beside `kind` and the council retained it for "compatibility" — the real reason is that it is a validated wire field, and the council's caution landed correctly for a reason it could not see. **(g) Q7 IS ADOPTED AND IT OVERRIDES WHAT SHIPPED.** Task 4a-4 currently re-seeds a restarted pane's scrollback **silently**; the ruling keeps the history but requires a visible boundary before any new PTY output. **The separator is emitted through the existing main-process emit path**, which means it is mirrored to disk like any other session text and therefore becomes a permanent, accurate record of when the session restarted — it must NOT be appended to the replay seed only, which would redraw it on every attach rather than once per restart. **Two smaller corrections, recorded so an implementer does not inherit them:** the findings' `supportsResume(adapter: unknown): adapter is SupportsResume` drops the `BaseAgentAdapter &` intersection the current guard carries (`types.ts:621`), and 4a-3's call sites need `buildLaunch` off the same value; and the file-by-file section's `src/main/sessionManager.ts` is `src/main/services/sessionManager.ts`. | **ADOPTED 2026-08-13 (coordinator) · ⚠ ALL SIX AMENDMENTS NOW DISCHARGED — (a)(d)(e)(f) by 4a-2 (`9e3c83d`), (b)(c)(g) by 4a-3 (`756066c`).** **(b) was measured as a BEFORE/AFTER on the same stale pointer**, which is the only way its four negatives could be shown: the run before the fix logged `[notify] toast shown: Claude Code exited (1)`, the run after it logged **no notify line at all**, left the row `'running'` with a null exit code throughout, closed **no** dispatch and **no** turn, and did not move the project error count. **⚠ AND (b)'s ARITHMETIC WAS WRONG WHILE ITS REASONING WAS RIGHT — the "nine listeners" are EIGHT (§5).** The conclusion is untouched: `wasKilledByChorus` is read by exactly one of them, so suppressing the fan-out remains the load-bearing half. **(c) was shown in both directions**, including the failure one — a forced spawn failure left the pointer **NULL** and produced no false notice on the next launch. |
| D144 | **⚠ CHORUS STAMPS EVERY codex LAUNCH WITH `CODEX_INTERNAL_ORIGINATOR_OVERRIDE=chorus-<sessionId>`, WHICH IS AN UNDOCUMENTED VENDOR SURFACE AND A NON-STANDARD CLIENT IDENTIFIER REACHING THE PROVIDER — TAKEN DELIBERATELY, WITH THE USER'S EXPLICIT APPROVAL, BECAUSE THE ALTERNATIVE IS A FEATURE THAT IS WRONG IN THE COMMON CASE.** Discovery without a stamp can only infer ownership from **cwd + launch time**, and `RESTORE_STAGGER_MS` (500 ms) means two codex panes in one project are **always inside each other's skew window**: both match the single rollout the user's one turn produced, both claim it, and both resume the **same conversation** — a silent data-crossing, which **D140** ranks as strictly worse than having no pointer at all. The stamp turns ownership from an inference into an **identity**. **⚠ MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED, AND THE NEGATIVE CONTROLS ARE PART OF THE EVIDENCE (D4):** on codex-cli 0.147.0, `-c originator="…"` is **IGNORED** and `CODEX_ORIGINATOR` is **IGNORED** (both left the header reading `codex-tui`), while `CODEX_INTERNAL_ORIGINATOR_OVERRIDE` is **honoured**. **And the session was verified to still WORK under the override** — the model answered normally with no error text — which was the precondition for adopting it at all. **⚠ THE RISK IS NAMED RATHER THAN HEDGED: it appears in no `--help`, so a future codex may drop it, and what the provider does with a non-standard originator is unknown.** Mitigation is structural rather than hopeful: discovery **falls back** to the cwd + two-sided-window rule whenever no stamped rollout matches, so losing the variable costs exactness and nothing else — and a rollout stamped by a **different** Chorus pane is **never** a fallback candidate, which is what stops the fallback quietly re-introducing the very cross-claim the stamp exists to prevent. | **ADOPTED 2026-08-13 (Matthew, presented with the measurement and the trade) — resolves F64** |
| D145 | **⚠ THE `agent_events` BUS IS NOT BUILT IN PHASE 4, AND `agent_turns` IS NOT THE REASON — NO SURFACE THIS PHASE SHIPS READS EVENT HISTORY, AND THE ONE SURFACE THAT WOULD IS BLOCKED BY D130 RATHER THAN BY THE MISSING TABLE.** The roadmap parked the bus *"until a consumer exists"*. **Four consumers of the hook spine have since arrived and NOT ONE OF THEM READS HISTORY** — measured at `00f0f0d`: the filmstrip broadcast (`ipc.ts:4000`), the project roll-up (`ipc.ts:4046`, via `recordFor`), the turn recorder (`turns.ts:81`, via `onActivity`) and the context ring (`index.ts:413`, via `onTranscriptPath`). Every one reads the CURRENT value. **⚠ AND THE CHOICE THE KICKOFF POSED — "an in-memory ring versus an append-only table" — WAS A FALSE ONE, BECAUSE THERE IS NO RING:** `agentEvents.ts` holds a `Map<sessionId, {activity, since}>` of **exactly one record per live session**, and `revoke()` **deletes** it on every exit path. Nothing anywhere in this app retains a second event. **⚠ NOR DID `agent_turns` "ANSWER THE HISTORY NEED" — IT HAS NO READER EITHER.** `readAgentTurns` (`storage.ts:2292`) is grep-proven to have **zero callers outside `storage.ts`**; v18 built the recorder and left the reader to Phase 8. So the honest position is not *"the bus was superseded"* but *"history has no consumer in EITHER store, and building a second empty one would double the commitment made for nobody."* **⚠ THE PART THAT SETTLES IT RATHER THAN MERELY DEFERRING IT: A BUS WOULD NOT SERVE THE PHASE'S HEADLINE SURFACE, BECAUSE THE BUS CARRIES EVENT *NAMES* AND THE MOCK DRAWS EVENT *BODIES*.** `docs/design/v2/Chorus Attention Inbox.dc.html` — the authority under D73 — renders the tool name (*"Bash command"*), the tool input (*"`dotnet ef database update`"*), the agent's own message (*"Migration compiles clean. I need to apply it…"*) and the prompt's numbered options. **Every one of those is a hook-body field `readHookEventName` deliberately refuses to take** (D130: *"no prompt text, no `last_assistant_message`, no tool input"*). An `agent_events` table of classified names would be built, migrated and still leave the Inbox undrawable — so the table is not the blocker and adding it would not unblock anything. Widening D130 is a **decision with a security argument**, and it is not this one. **✅ WHAT IS ACTUALLY MISSING IS NOT HISTORY AT ALL — IT IS THE *REASON* ON THE CURRENT STATE.** `docs/PLAN.md:184` names **two** states, `waiting-for-user` and `waiting-for-permission`, and `classifyHookEvent` collapses **six** events (`Stop`, `StopFailure`, `Notification`, `PermissionRequest`, `Elicitation`, `TeammateIdle`) into one undifferentiated `needs-you`. That is a **retention** change to a value derived from a field **already read** — not a widening of D130's read surface — and it belongs on the live record, not in a table. **✅ AND THE ONE POLICY RULE THAT DOES NEED HISTORY IS ALREADY SERVED:** PLAN.md:200's *"notify on completion only if runtime > 2 min"* is `agent_turns.started_at`/`ended_at`, on disk since v18. **⚠ THE LIMIT IS NAMED RATHER THAN HIDDEN: two Phase 4 items DO want per-event history — the per-session event timeline sidebar and D39's sub-agent awareness — and BOTH are deferred with the bus, for reasons a table does not fix.** The timeline is D130-blocked exactly as the Inbox is; D39's *"N sub-agents running / done"* is blocked by the edge trigger (see **F67**). **If either is later ruled in, the bus comes back with them, and this decision is what they reopen.** | **RESOLVED 2026-08-13 (Phase 4 kickoff) — the bus stays UNBUILT and is no longer "pending a consumer": it is pending a RULING on the timeline, D39, and D130's read surface** |
| D146 | **⚠ THE `needs-you` REASON VOCABULARY IS THREE VALUES, AND `Notification` BELONGS TO `permission` — AMENDING D145's OWN EXAMPLE ON EVIDENCE THAT ONLY EXISTED ONCE IT RAN.** D145 identified the missing thing as *"the REASON on the current state"* and listed the six `needs-you` events; Task 4-1 shipped it as `NeedsYouReason = 'permission' \| 'stopped' \| 'notice'`, derived from the event name at classification time and **never stored** — which is precisely why this is a one-line amendment and not a migration. **The grouping as shipped:** `permission` = `PermissionRequest`, `Elicitation`, **`Notification`**; `stopped` = `Stop`, `StopFailure`; `notice` = `TeammateIdle`. **⚠ `Notification` MOVED FROM `notice` DURING THE TASK, ON A MEASUREMENT RATHER THAN A SECOND OPINION** — it arrives ~6 s into a permission block and was downgrading the live reason (**F69**). **✅ THE VALUE OF THE FIELD IS THAT GETTING IT WRONG COSTS A LABEL, NOT DATA**, and this amendment is the proof: the fix was one map value, two tests and no schema change, three commits after the vocabulary was chosen. **⚠ TWO CONSTRAINTS THIS PUTS ON LATER TASKS.** (1) `notice` now has exactly **one** member, so a surface that renders three reason styles is rendering one that almost never appears — **4-2 should not build a case it cannot see**. (2) A `Notification` arriving mid-block is **suppressed, not delivered** (F69), so `onActivity` is not a nag counter. **If a later surface needs `permission` and `notice` distinguished on an idle pane, the answer is a precedence rule — never downgrade `permission` until `working` — and NOT a regrouping**, which would re-open exactly the failure F69 records. | **RESOLVED 2026-08-13 (Task 4-1, `f0cea8f` + `8cbd37c`) — amends D145's vocabulary; the field shipped, nothing renders it yet** |
| D147 | **⚠ PHASE 6 CLOSES AT ITS MILESTONE; STAGE 5 LEAVES THE PHASE AND BECOMES PHASE 6a, RE-SCOPED AROUND THE GOAL RATHER THAN THE ARCHITECTURE.** Proposal: [`Phase-6a-Proposal.md`](Phase-6a-Proposal.md), ratified by Matthew 2026-08-14. **(a) Phase 6 is COMPLETE** — the milestone was met and driven (a `claude` pane called `chorus-memory` and returned a hand-seeded canary), and Stage 5 does **not** remain inside it as silent unfinished scope. **(b) Stage 5 becomes provisional Phase 6a — Memory In Practice**, ordered **usage contract → `index-codebase` → codex argv (F75) → provisioner**. **⚠ THE REORDERING IS THE SUBSTANCE AND IT CAME OUT OF THE G2 DRIVE:** Stage 5 led with the Docker provisioner, and the drive proved a complete working memory path against a container started by hand with **one `docker run`** — while `memory:validate` returned **`0 of 0`** and nothing anywhere told either agent the graph existed. **The provisioner is convenience; the usage contract is the gap; `index-codebase` IS the stated goal** (*"models don't have to constantly re-discover a project"*). **(c) ⚠ ONE GRAPH PER PROJECT, TWO LABEL NAMESPACES — NOT TWO CONTAINERS**, answering Matthew's direct question. Decisive reason: `Plan.md` §10 already specifies `(AgentSession)-[:PRODUCED]->(Observation)-[:SUPPORTED_BY]->(File)` and `(Commit)-[:MODIFIED]->(File)` — **a memory that points at the code it is about — and two databases sever exactly that edge**, permanently, because **Community Edition cannot cross-query databases at all** (D128(e), measured, Memgraph checked and identical). Supporting: ~0.5–1 GB heap each, doubled per project, in a multi-project app. **The separation sought is a LABEL boundary** — `File`/`Class`/`Method` are machine-generated and wipeable, `Decision`/`Observation`/`Risk` are expensive and must never be. **(d) `dockerode` is NOT approved even though 6a-4 IS in scope** — a `git.ts`-style `docker` CLI adapter instead, per `Phase-6-MemoryPlan.md` §11's own standing recommendation (`docker` is already probed by `cliDetect`; `--format '{{json .}}'` gives structured output). **Phase 6's lineage therefore ends with no new dependency beyond `neo4j-driver`.** **(e) ⚠ THE USAGE CONTRACT IS DELIVERED BY SESSION-LEVEL INJECTION, NOT BY WRITING THE USER'S FILES — AND THIS IS THE CLAUSE THAT KEEPS D49 INTACT.** `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are hand-authored by the user; `.mcp.json` is a machine-owned config file, and **a clobbered `CLAUDE.md` is a different category of harm**. Injection uses claude's `--settings` (already the hook mechanism, D130) and codex's `-c developer_instructions` — **already in production use today**, as the F75 process dump incidentally proved. **Cost, stated rather than hidden:** it consumes context every launch and is invisible to an agent the user runs outside Chorus. A marked, idempotent block in `CLAUDE.md` stays available as an explicit **opt-in**, never a default. **(f) THREE OF THE FIVE LAUNCH SKILLS AND THE `skill.yaml` FRAMEWORK ARE DROPPED OUTRIGHT, NOT DEFERRED** — `backup-graph`, `repo-orientation`, `summarize-session`. **Building a provider-neutral skill format to ship two skills is premature**; `index-codebase` is an app capability first and is generalised when a third consumer exists. **Dropping beats carrying scope nobody intends to write.** **(g) 6a RUNS NEXT, ahead of Phase 4's three remaining tasks** — Matthew's priority call, 2026-08-14. Phase 4's `Task-4-2` execution prompt is already written and keeps; **its `IpcChannel` figure and line numbers must be re-measured when it is picked up** (G6/F54), since 6a will move the tree under it. | **RESOLVED 2026-08-14 (product decision, Matthew)** |
| D148 | **⚠ THE USAGE CONTRACT'S CLAUDE MECHANISM IS `--append-system-prompt-file`, NOT `--settings` — CORRECTING D147(e) ON A MEASUREMENT TAKEN AT THE PHASE 6a KICKOFF.** D147(e) named `--settings` because it is *already the hook mechanism* (D130) — but **`--settings` takes a settings JSON file and has no system-prompt field at all**; Chorus's own use of it writes `{hooks:{…}}` (`claude.ts:213`). Probed against the installed **claude 2.1.232** on 2026-08-14: `--append-system-prompt <prompt>` is documented in `--help`, and **`--append-system-prompt-file <file>` exists**, proven by omitting its argument and reading back `error: option '--append-system-prompt-file <file>' argument missing`. **The FILE variant is taken** (Matthew, 2026-08-14): it is the same shape `--settings` already gives hooks — main reserves the path under `userData\agent-instructions`, the adapter writes it, **every exit path deletes it** — and it keeps a multi-line snippet off a world-readable command line (the standing `extraArgs` warning in `types.ts`). **⚠ AND THE CODEX HALF IS NOT FREE EITHER:** `-c developer_instructions` is **already occupied** by the jade formatting rule (`CODEX_BASELINE_ARGS`, `codex.ts:742`), **`-c` REPLACES rather than appends** (`codex.ts:708`), and codex ignores a duplicated or unknown `-c` path **silently** (`codex.ts:680`) — so a second token would destroy one of the two values with no error anywhere. **6a-1 gives that key exactly one emitter**, composing both parts into one physical line (a raw newline is illegal inside a TOML basic string and `tomlBasicString` does not escape one). **⚠ SCOPE: TWO ADAPTERS, NOT FOUR.** `kimi`, `opencode` and `noHarness` declare the capability **null** — honest, and it makes the capability-honesty loops cover all five adapters across three descriptors. kimi's only vehicle (`--agent-file`) replaces an agent profile wholesale; opencode's `instructions` config key is unmeasured and its schema is `additionalProperties:false`. **⚠ AND THE CONTRACT IS EMITTED ONLY WHEN THE PROJECT HAS MEMORY CONFIGURED** (`mcpLaunchInput !== null` — already the early return at `ipc.ts:647`, so one gate rather than two): telling an agent about a graph that does not exist is worse than saying nothing. | **RESOLVED 2026-08-14 (Phase 6a kickoff; Matthew chose the vehicle)** |
| D149 | **⚠ `index-codebase` v1 INDEXES FILES, DIRECTORIES AND COMMITS — NO PARSER, NO SYMBOLS, NO `CALLS` — AND THE CUT IS STATED IN THE UI, NOT ONLY IN A DOC.** The roadmap's own list (`File`/`Class`/`Method`/`Namespace` + `CONTAINS`/`DECLARES`/`HAS_METHOD`/`CALLS`) is reduced to `:File` / `:Directory` / `:Commit` + `CONTAINS` / `MODIFIED`, walked from `git ls-files` and `git log`. **Why:** declarations would need the TypeScript compiler API, i.e. **promoting `typescript` from a devDependency to a runtime one** — a dependency ask CLAUDE.md requires be made explicitly — and a cross-file **call graph needs a full type-checked program build on every index**, which is a phase rather than a task. **The honest value is FINDING code, not READING it**, and the Memory section must say so in plain words: *it cannot say what a function does or what calls it*. **⚠ (b) THE INDEXER NEVER DELETES A NODE OR AN EDGE, AND THIS IS THE CLAUSE WITH TEETH.** `memory:validate` counts a `:Memory` as sourced only while its `SUPPORTED_BY` target **exists in the graph** (identity model §6), so the obvious wipe-and-rebuild refresh would **delete provenance targets and drop the trust ratio because a refresh ran** — a corrupted measurement wearing a measurement's clothes. A vanished file is **marked** (`missingSince`, cleared automatically when it returns) and never removed; the marking pass keys on the run stamp rather than shipping the whole path list back as a parameter. A grep for `DELETE`/`DETACH`/`REMOVE` over the indexer's Cypher is **a unit test**, not a review habit. **(c) REFRESH IS MANUAL ONLY** — one button, one IPC channel, no watcher, no post-commit hook, no boot hook, no timer (D58's user-initiated rule, and *"a watcher would fight the agents for the database"*). **(d) THE PROJECT'S OWN CHECKOUT ONLY** — `workspaceInstanceId = 'pj:' + projects.id`; indexing every live worktree would multiply every node and make `MODIFIED` ambiguous. **(e) THE COMMIT WINDOW IS BOUNDED AT 200 AND THE SKIPPED COUNT IS REPORTED** — a cap nobody is told about reads as "we covered everything". | **RESOLVED 2026-08-14 (Phase 6a kickoff; Matthew chose the depth)** |
| D150 | **⚠ codex's MCP ENV VALUES TRAVEL THROUGH `envAdditions`; ONLY NAMES REACH ARGV.** F75's second gap is that `renderMcpLaunchArgs` emits `env_vars=[…]` — a list of **names** — and has no path for a value, while `mcp-neo4j-cypher` needs `NEO4J_URL` as a value (measured: it reads `NEO4J_URL` first and `NEO4J_URI` only as a fallback). The ref `memoryService.mcpLaunchInput` assembles carries **literal, non-secret values** (`memoryService.ts:265`), and **`wireMcpForLaunch` — the one function that knows which mechanism an adapter uses — converts it**: the env map's KEYS become `envPassthrough` (argv), the map itself becomes `envAdditions` (the child environment `composeChildEnv` builds). **This is the shape D47/D49 already ratified for `model_providers.<key>.env_key`**: a name in argv, a value in the environment. **⚠ THE REJECTED ALTERNATIVE IS NAMED SO IT IS NOT RE-PROPOSED:** `mcp_servers.<name>.env=` is accepted by codex and carries name→VALUE pairs; `mcpConfigCore.ts:78` forbids it in its own docblock, and taking it would put configuration values on a world-readable command line for no gain. **⚠ AND THE GUARD RUNS ON THIS PATH TOO.** A file mechanism guards its BYTES; the argv mechanism has no bytes, so `assertNoSecretInRendered` runs over the rendered argv **and** the env values, and a hit costs the memory server rather than the launch (`mcpConfigWrite.ts:166`). `renderMcpLaunchArgs` is therefore **not edited**, and its docblock stays literally true. | **RESOLVED 2026-08-14 (Phase 6a kickoff)** |
| D151 | **⚠ THE PROVISIONER REMOVES CONTAINERS AND NEVER VOLUMES — F49 IS WHY, AND IT IS A GATE RATHER THAN A PREFERENCE.** 6a-4 ships create / start / stop / status / **remove-container**, the last behind main's typed-confirmation gate (the `project:delete` precedent, D123 — enforced in main, never by a disabled button). **There is no `docker volume rm`, no `docker rm -v` and no `--rm` anywhere in the diff, and a grep for them is a test:** F49 gates durability on an export/restore path **this phase does not build**, so no code path in Chorus may destroy a graph. A re-provision re-attaches the existing volume, which makes the data's survival a driveable round trip rather than a promise. **⚠ (b) THE CONTAINER IS PUBLISHED ON LOOPBACK EXPLICITLY — `-p 127.0.0.1:<port>:7687` — AND THIS IS A SECURITY CHANGE, NOT A DETAIL.** Every Phase 6 drive used `-p 7688:7687`, which binds `0.0.0.0`; combined with `NEO4J_AUTH=none` that publishes an **unauthenticated graph database to the local network**. Acceptable for a by-hand probe on a dev box; **not** acceptable for something Chorus starts on a user's machine — and **D93's whole no-secret argument rests on the `127.0.0.1` half of its own sentence**, which until now has been the operator's habit rather than the app's code. The Neo4j HTTP port is **not published at all**; `http_port` stays NULL. **(c) NOTHING RUNS UNATTENDED:** every docker call is user-initiated (D58 applied to a second kind of connection), there is no boot reconciliation and no timer, and a stale `container_id` is healed by the status read when a person opens the screen. **(d) A RUNNING CONTAINER IS NOT A CONNECTION** — the chip stays governed by D126: `Connected` is earned by an observed read, never by a process existing. **(e) NO MIGRATION IS NEEDED** — `project_memory`'s five container columns were created by v16 and have sat NULL ever since, deliberately. | **RESOLVED 2026-08-14 (Phase 6a kickoff; Matthew chose the lifecycle)** |
| D152 | **⚠ AN ADAPTER MAY DECLARE WHERE ITS LEVELLED CONTROLS START, AND CLAUDE STARTS AT `xhigh` + `auto` — WHICH REVERSES TWO STANDING 3a-4 RULINGS ON A PRODUCT ARGUMENT RATHER THAN A TECHNICAL ONE.** Matthew, 2026-08-14: *"when I start an agent and select Claude I want the agent to be in auto mode on and effort x-high by default… map Deep to x-high since I rarely if almost never choose a low effort."* **(a) THE CLAUDE EFFORT LADDER MOVES UP ONE RUNG** — `fast→medium`, `balanced→high`, `deep→xhigh`, `max→max`. 3a-4 held `xhigh` *"deliberately NOT reachable from the four-level slider"* because stretching four normalized positions across five vendor values *"would make Deep mean a different distance here than on codex"*. **That symmetry loses to a measured fact about the only user this app has:** the bottom rung was never picked, so a quarter of the control was dead. `low` is now the unreachable value and is still reachable through `extra_args` (rank 1) — **the same escape hatch `xhigh` used to have, so nothing was removed; the default shifted.** codex's ladder is UNCHANGED, and claude's *Deep* is now one notch hotter than codex's: recorded here rather than in a commit message. **(b) PERMISSION MODE BECOMES A REAL CAPABILITY** (`AgentCapabilities.permissionMode`), built as the exact twin of `reasoningEffort` — PLAN principle 009 (*"CLI-native permission modes"*) as an object: Chorus picks the word, the CLI enforces it. D4-verified against the installed **claude 2.1.232** by running it: `--permission-mode <acceptEdits|auto|bypassPermissions|manual|dontAsk|plan>`, corroborated by claude's own `auto-mode` subcommand (*"Inspect or reset auto mode classifier configuration"*). Every emitted value was then made to ACCEPT at the binary. **⚠ FOUR OF THE SIX POSITIONS SHIP AND THE TWO OMISSIONS ARE THE DECISION:** `bypassPermissions` is *"Recommended only for sandboxes with no internet access"* by its own help and must not sit one click from *Auto* in a dialog whose every other control is reversible; `dontAsk` is unmeasured against `acceptEdits` and spec §4.2 forbids a control position Chorus is guessing at. Both stay reachable via `extra_args`. **codex/kimi/opencode declare `null` — UNMEASURED, NOT ABSENT** (all three plainly have approval controls); permission is the one capability where a wrong mapping does not misconfigure a session, **it hands an agent authority the user did not grant.** **(c) ⚠ `defaultLevelId` IS APPLIED IN `buildLaunch`, NOT IN THE LAUNCH DIALOG, AND THAT IS THE LOAD-BEARING HALF.** The dialog is one of FOUR launch paths — restore (`sessionManager.ts` `restore()` passes **no options at all**), `session:restart` and profile relaunch are the others — and it is the only one a person is looking at. A default that lived in the renderer would have evaporated on the next app restart and handed a restored agent back its permission prompts, silently. Rank 3 of the resolver carries it instead, so **every** claude launch says `--effort xhigh --permission-mode auto` unless something overrode it. **Rank 1 still suppresses the DEFAULT, not merely an explicit choice** — two `--permission-mode` flags on one command line would leave the outcome to unverified last-wins parsing. **(d) `effort.ts` BECAME `argLevels.ts`** (`resolveEffortArgs`→`resolveLevelArgs`, `overridesEffort`→`overridesLevel`). Nothing in it was ever effort-specific; copying 100 lines of near-miss matching rules (`--effortless`, `model_reasoning_effort_summary`) into a second file would have meant only one copy getting fixed next time. **(e) `launch_profiles.permission_mode` STOPS BEING INERT** — 3a-5 created it as free text because it was *"stored, consumed by nothing"*; it is now narrowed to the app vocabulary by `resolveLaunchProfile` (out-of-vocabulary rows degrade to the adapter default, exactly as `effort` already did) and the wire schema tightened to match. **(f) A LEAK FOUND BY RUNNING IT, NOT BY REASONING:** the dialog opens on claude, claude's default puts *Deep* in the effort control, the user switches to codex — and `deep` is in codex's vocabulary too, so codex silently gained `-c model_reasoning_effort="high"` that nobody chose. Fixed with per-control provenance: **a user's choice survives an agent switch; an inherited default does not.** | **RESOLVED 2026-08-14 (product decision, Matthew) — amends 3a-4's ladder and its "emit nothing when nobody chose" rule; verified end-to-end by driving the real dialog and reading the spawned PTY's command line** **⚠ RENUMBERED FROM D148 TO D152 ON THE 2026-08-15 MERGE** — the Phase 6a kickoff claimed D148–D151 on the same day, in a branch that had not yet been pushed. This row was cited by nothing but itself; D148 was cited in ~25 places across the 6a task docs, `adapters.test.ts` and `claude.ts`, so the cheap and reference-safe move was to renumber this one. |
| D153 | **⚠ "WHAT DID I WORK ON TODAY" IS ANSWERED FROM GIT, NOT FROM AGENT TELEMETRY — AND THAT SINGLE CHOICE IS WHAT MAKES IT WORK FOR CODEX.** Matthew, 2026-08-15: *"I just need some summary of 'this is what we worked on today' so that I can use it for project notes and timesheets… I want the end of day summary to be agent agnostic."* **(a) THE OBVIOUS SOURCE CANNOT ANSWER THE QUESTION.** `agent_turns` (v18, Task 8-0) is the natural home and it is Claude-only: `bindHooks` is claude-only by **D129**, codex cannot be discovered at all (**F64**), and `readAgentTurns` has never had a reader. A telemetry-derived report would silently omit every codex session — worse than no report, because the omission is invisible. Git observes **ARTIFACTS**, so a commit reads identically whichever agent, or which pair of hands, produced it: **agent-agnosticism is not implemented here, it is the source's default state.** It also sidesteps **F51** entirely — git is on disk, one copy per repo, identical to whichever Chorus instance asks — and needs no consolidation of the five scattered stores. **(b) THREE TRAPS, EACH FOUND BY RUNNING THE SWEEP AGAINST THE REAL SIX-PROJECT RAIL, EACH NOW A NAMED TEST.** **`--all` INCLUDES `refs/stash`** — the first live run reported *"On main: Auto stash before merge"* and *"index on main: 3f364cd Phase 6 docs"* as a morning's work; the fix is `--branches --tags --remotes`. **TWO PROJECTS CAN BE ONE REPOSITORY** — `TR-Integration` and `CCH-integration` both resolve to `C:/Projects/TaxApp/TaxApp/.git`, so a sweep keyed on the working tree counts every TaxApp commit twice under two headings; deduplication is on `rev-parse --git-common-dir`. **DIRTY ≠ TODAY, AND THIS ONE FALSIFIES A TIMESHEET** — `git status` carries no timestamp, and `InboxRail` holds 31 dirty paths / 809 insertions last written **August 9–10**; in-flight evidence is therefore bounded by file **mtime**. **(c) A FOURTH TRAP FOUND ON THE FIRST REAL RENDER: THE HARVEST FABRICATED AN API.** The `Phase 6 docs` commit added ten markdown specs containing TypeScript and Cypher samples, and `INDEX_BATCH_SIZE`, `UPSERT_COMMITS`, `ContainerState` and `IndexReport` were reported as the day's new API surface. **None was written as code.** Symbol harvesting is now restricted to an extension allowlist — **a timesheet naming work that never happened is the worst failure this feature has, worse than naming nothing.** **(d) THE PROSE IS A CONVENIENCE OVER THE EVIDENCE, NEVER A GATE ON IT.** The bullets are produced with no model at all; the 1–3 sentence tie-together is one call to a user-chosen credential+model, and **every** failure path returns a stated reason beside a null summary while the report still renders. Only commit subjects, file PATHS and harvested symbol NAMES are sent — **no file content and no diff text ever leaves the machine.** **(e) THE SNAPSHOT IS STORED (migration v20) BECAUSE HALF THE EVIDENCE IS IRREPLACEABLE.** Commits re-derive from git forever; in-flight work does not — once those files are committed, the fact that they were being worked on *on that date* is gone. Regeneration therefore **merges**: commits are replaced, dirty paths are unioned. Without that rule, re-running Monday's report on Wednesday would silently empty the one half nothing can rebuild. **(f) NO PER-AGENT BREAKDOWN, BY REQUEST AND BY CONSTRUCTION** — *"I don't need a breakdown of 'this was in Claude' and 'that was in Codex'."* Nothing in the table, the wire schema or the render records it, and the UI says so on the page. **(g) SCOPE: manual generation with a date picker; no scheduler.** **(h) ⚠ AMENDED 2026-08-15, HOURS AFTER SHIPPING, BY F76 — THE COMMIT LIST IS FILTERED TO THE USER'S OWN GIT IDENTITIES.** Matthew asked whether his two git profiles (`mwilson@taxapp.com` for one project, `mwilson29072@gmail.com` for the others) would both be seen. Both were — there was no author filter at all — **and answering the question exposed the opposite and far worse defect: every OTHER contributor's commits were being reported as his.** `--branches --tags --remotes` deliberately includes remote-tracking refs, so a `git fetch` drags a whole team's work into the window under their own committer dates. **Measured: a report for 2026-08-06 listed TWELVE commits under `Trupanion` — *"Merged PR 4612: Fixed tickets WEB-12951, WEB-12925…"* and eleven more — of which ZERO were his**; they belong to four colleagues across two companies. The fix filters on `--author`, OR'd across every identity `git config --get-all user.email` reports (**`--get-all`, not the effective value: TaxApp holds 136 commits under the work address AND 21 under the global personal one, in one repository**), with `-F` because `--author` is a regex and an email is full of dots. **The identities used are RENDERED in the report**, because that is the only way to tell a genuinely quiet day from a mis-filtered one — and when none can be determined the report says outright, above the work, that it contains everyone's commits. | **RESOLVED 2026-08-15 (product decision, Matthew; three forks chosen at kickoff: cheap model via OpenRouter · store the snapshot · manual button)** — verified by driving the real app on an isolated `--user-data-dir` seeded from the installed profile: 6 projects → 4 repositories in **1.3 s**, migration v19→v20 applied on boot, markdown byte-identical across the SQLite round-trip |
| D154 | **⚠ PHASE 5 — VOICE INPUT RUNS NEXT, AHEAD OF PHASE 4's REMAINING THREE TASKS — SUPERSEDING D147(g) ON THE USER'S RULING RATHER THAN ON AN ARGUMENT.** Matthew, 2026-08-16, at the Phase 6a close. **D147(g) queued Phase 4's remainder** (4-2 Attention Inbox · 4-3 notification policy · 4-4 OS delivery) **immediately after 6a**; **Phase 5 takes that slot instead.** Phase 4 is **deferred, not dropped** — it keeps its `[CR]` closure (D130), its landed spine, its landed Task 4-1, and its three authored task docs. **⚠ THIS IS RECORDED AS A NUMBERED DECISION BECAUSE THAT IS THE ONLY WAY THIS ROADMAP LETS A PHASE MOVE.** The practice is established by every prior reordering — **D52** moved the two PTY adapters out of 3a into 3d, **D91** staged Phase 6 connect-first with the provisioner last, **D147(g)** put 6a ahead of Phase 4 — and §7's own queue ruling states the principle for the neighbouring case: *"Neither may be dropped without a numbered decision saying so"* (the 2026-07-26 3c→3d→3e ordering). **A phase that moves in a hallway conversation and not in this table is a phase whose order nobody can reconstruct.** **⚠ AND THE CARRY-FORWARD IS NOW MANDATORY RATHER THAN ADVISORY.** D147(g) already warned that `Task-4-2`'s execution prompt must have its `IpcChannel` figure and line numbers re-measured when picked up. That prompt was authored when `IpcChannel` was **86**; it is **97** today and **Phase 5 will move it again before Phase 4 is reached**, so the figure will be stale by **two** phases rather than one. Under **G6** the prompt's *Initial Starting Point* table is re-measured wholesale at pickup — not deltaed, not patched. | 2026-08-16 · **RESOLVED — Matthew's priority call** |
| D155 | **⚠ PHASE 5 v1 SHIPS THE OFFLINE FLOOR *PLUS* THE THREE REFINEMENT MODES; CLOUD STT IS OUT.** Matthew, 2026-08-16. **IN:** Verbatim + **local whisper** — no network, no key, no vendor, no LLM — **and** the three refinement modes (Verbatim / Clean up / Organize, **D137**) over the **existing BYOK path** (`apiSession.ts`, D45(2)). **OUT of v1: cloud STT**, i.e. sending the **audio itself** to a vendor. **⚠ THE CUT IS NOT A SIZE CUT AND SHOULD NOT BE RESTORED AS ONE — IT SEPARATES TWO DIFFERENT DISCLOSURES THAT PHASE 5's OWN ENTRY HAD ALREADY NAMED AS DIFFERENT.** Clean up and Organize send **transcript text** to an LLM on a credential the user configured; cloud STT sends **raw microphone audio** to a third party. The first is the same egress the council and the day summary already make; the second is a new class of egress this app has never made, and it is the one a user is most likely to be surprised by. **v1 keeps the class it already reasons about and defers the class it does not.** **⚠ ONE CONSEQUENCE IS IMMEDIATE AND MECHANICAL: the kickoff's D4 pass DROPS the "current cloud STT vendor API shapes" row** — three unverified facts remain (the `uiohook-napi` Windows prebuild for this Electron ABI, whisper.cpp's Windows distribution shape and model size, and `AudioWorklet` at 16 kHz mono under Electron 43.1.1), and the **`uiohook-napi` probe is marked *do this first*** because a missing prebuild changes the phase's shape rather than its schedule. | 2026-08-16 · **RESOLVED — scope set at the close, sized at kickoff** |
| D156 | **⚠ THE FLEET SWITCHER OVERLAY IS SPLIT OUT OF PHASE 5 — THE PLUMBING ARGUMENT THAT BUNDLED IT IS TRUE AND TOO SMALL TO CARRY THE COUPLING.** Matthew, 2026-08-16. It was folded in (renamed from "mission control" by **D132**) because it shares **always-on-top window plumbing** with the mic overlay. **That is real and it is a handful of `BrowserWindow` options — it is not a feature.** Against it: **D135 already expanded Phase 5 without re-estimation** (the requirements document added a refinement model, a permission/retention posture and a second activation mode to what had been a four-line dictation pipe), and `Plan.md` §14's `wk 2` sizing predates all of it. **Bundling an unrelated overlay into a phase that has already grown twice is how a phase stops being finishable.** **The Fleet Switcher is deferred with its D132 name and its shared-plumbing note intact**, to be placed by a later pass; **it is NOT dropped**, and per the standing rule it cannot be without a numbered decision saying so. **The mic overlay builds the always-on-top plumbing; the Fleet Switcher inherits it** — which is the same debt in the other direction, and cheaper. | 2026-08-16 · **RESOLVED — deferred, not dropped; placement owed by a later pass** |
| D157 | **⚠ VOICE REFINEMENT SPEND IS METERED — AND THIS IS A DECISION PRECISELY BECAUSE THE NEWEST PRECEDENT IN THE CODEBASE RECORDS NOTHING.** Matthew, 2026-08-16: `onUsage` wired and persisted; **where** the numbers land is settled at kickoff, not here. **⚠ THE TABLE BOTH GOVERNING DOCUMENTS NAME AS THE DESTINATION DOES NOT EXIST AND NEVER DID.** `Plan.md:233` says refinement spend rides **`usage_records`**; `Plan.md:313` lists `usage_records (id, session_id, provider_id, tokens_in, tokens_out, cost_est, ts)` in the schema; `Plan.md:331` has Phase 3 *"begin per-credential usage capture (`usage_records`)"*; and Phase 5's own roadmap entry repeated it. **The code says outright that the name was superseded** — `schema.ts:238` (*"The token/cost columns live on THIS row, not in a separate usage_records table… one home, not two (D48)"*) and `storage.ts:264-268` (*"The roadmap's `usage_records` name is superseded by this table"*). **Spend lives on `dispatches`, `council_messages` and `council_runs`.** Corrected in **both** documents this pass. **⚠ AND THE NEWEST CONSUMER OF THE SHARED BYOK PATH METERS NOTHING AT ALL, WHICH IS WHY "IT WILL FOLLOW THE PRECEDENT" WOULD HAVE BEEN THE WRONG DEFAULT.** The day-report summarizer (D153) calls `createApiSession` with **`{baseUrl, maxOutputTokens}` and no `onUsage`** — measured at `ipc.ts:4274`, inside `resolveDaySummarizer` (`:4263–4299`); the **only** `onUsage` producer in main is `councilService.ts:1129`. So the live precedent for "a small BYOK call" is **unmetered**, and voice would have inherited that silently. **⚠ IT ALSO INHERITS F42's RACE, PROPORTIONALLY HARDER:** the council's `cost_usd` under-reports because the minted-key spend counter is read before the vendor settles the final turn — and **a refinement turn is a single short turn, i.e. all of it is the final turn.** Per-token derivation from stored counts is the method F42 established (twice, inside 0.05%); the minted-key scalar is not. | 2026-08-16 · **RESOLVED to meter; the destination table and the F42-safe derivation are settled at kickoff** |
| D158 | **⚠ PHASE 5's CR GATE RUNS ON CHORUS'S OWN IN-APP COUNCIL, ON THE *INSTALLED* APP — AND THE BUILD IS PART OF THE RULING, NOT AN OPERATIONAL DETAIL.** Matthew, 2026-08-16. Phase 3b's native council (**D27** delivered, 2026-07-26) is the venue; §4 is corrected this pass to stop claiming *"Claude cannot run the council"* and *"CR runs stay in Cursor"*. **⚠ THE VENUE IS LOAD-BEARING BECAUSE MEMBERS AND CREDENTIALS LIVE IN THE DATABASE, NOT IN THE CODE, AND THE TWO DATABASES ON THIS MACHINE ARE OPPOSITES.** Measured this pass, read-only, no credential blob touched: **installed** (`%APPDATA%\chorus-app`, Chorus **0.6.0**) — **5 council members, 2 OpenRouter credential profiles**; **dev** (`%APPDATA%\chorus`) — **0 and 0, and no provider configs at all.** **Firing the gate against a dev build finds no council and reads as a broken feature rather than an empty fixture.** **⚠ ONE FURTHER FACT, MEASURED AND NOT ASSUMED, BECAUSE IT WILL LOOK LIKE A BUG THE FIRST TIME SOMEONE NOTICES IT:** the installed app's DB is at **`MAX(version)` 19** while the dev tree is at **20** — the installed build simply predates the Day Summary migration. It is a **version lag, not a divergence**, and it does not affect the council, whose tables have been stable since v11. **Phase 5's [CR] questions are already listed** in its entry and in `Phase-5-VoicePlan.md` §11; **§4's four obligations bind unchanged** — flag, brief, **pause**, record in §6 — and the brief must state **which build ran it**. | 2026-08-16 · **RESOLVED — installed app, in-app council; §4 corrected to match** |
| D159 | **⚠ `base.en` IS PHASE 5's DEFAULT WHISPER MODEL, NOT `small.en` — CORRECTING `Plan.md` ON A NUMBER NOBODY HAD MEASURED.** Settled 2026-08-17 at the Phase 5 kickoff. `Plan.md:224` commits to *"Local default: whisper.cpp `small.en`, models downloaded on first use to `%APPDATA%`"*. **Measured live this session** (HTTP HEAD against HuggingFace, all four returning 200 with a `content-length`): `tiny.en` **74.1 MB** · **`base.en` 141.1 MB** · **`small.en` 465.0 MB** · `medium.en` 1462.7 MB. **⚠ AT 465 MB THE FIRST DICTATION A USER EVER ATTEMPTS IS ALSO A 465 MB DOWNLOAD** — on the path `Phase-5-VoicePlan.md` §10 itself calls *"the one most likely to be tested least"*, which can fail, be slow, or meet an offline machine. **`base.en` is 3.3× smaller and adequate for close-mic English dictation, which is the whole of v1's use case** (D155 put cloud STT out, so there is no higher-accuracy fallback to lean on — but there is also no multi-speaker or far-field case to serve). **`small.en` remains available as an opt-in upgrade in settings**, with **both sizes shown at the point of choice**, so the accuracy/size trade is the user's and is made with the numbers visible. **⚠ THE DECISION IS RECORDED RATHER THAN JUST APPLIED BECAUSE IT OVERRIDES `Plan.md`**, which §2 makes authoritative on intent — the override is narrow (a default, not the engine) and `Plan.md:224` is corrected in Task 5-2's own commit rather than left to contradict the shipped behaviour. | 2026-08-17 · **RESOLVED — Matthew, at the kickoff, from the measured sizes** |
| D160 | **⚠ PHASE 5 v1 IS DIRECT-TO-PROMPT ONLY. NO COMPOSER — AND THE MODE-COMPARISON FEATURES ARE OUT BY CONSTRUCTION RATHER THAN BY OMISSION.** Settled 2026-08-17. The transcript lands at the agent's `❯` prompt and **the agent's own line editor is the review surface**; the user presses Enter, edits, or clears. **Three standing rulings already pointed here and none had to be overturned:** the mock draws the direct path and **D73** makes the mock the authority; **D137** explicitly staged the composer *after* the direct path works; and **D76**'s precedent is to build a thing before the surface that compares it. **⚠ THE CONSEQUENCE MUST BE STATED PLAINLY BECAUSE THE SOURCE REQUIREMENTS DOCUMENT ASKS FOR THE OPPOSITE:** *"switch between Original / Cleaned / Organized"*, *"restore the original"*, *"re-run in another mode"* and *"see which version was AI-refined"* are **not deferred features, they are unbuildable after the write** — `session:write` hands bytes to a child process's line editor and **Chorus cannot un-write, re-render or diff them** (`Phase-5-VoicePlan.md` §6.1). They are possible only *before* the write, in a surface Chorus owns, which is precisely what a composer is. **v1 therefore chooses the mode BEFORE dictating.** | 2026-08-17 · **RESOLVED — Matthew, at the kickoff** |
| D161 | **⚠ PHASE 5 v1 PERSISTS NO TRANSCRIPTS: SPEND METERING ONLY — AND THE PHASE THEREFORE TAKES NO MIGRATION AT ALL.** Settled 2026-08-17. **D157** required `onUsage` wired and persisted and left the destination to this kickoff; the destination is a **`dispatches`** row, which already carries `tokens_in` / `tokens_out` / `tokens_cached` / `cost_usd` / `tokens_source` and which `schema.ts:238` declares to be spend's **one home** (*"not in a separate `usage_records` table … one home, not two"*, **D48**). **`MIGRATIONS.length` stays 20 and `v21` stays FREE.** **⚠ THIS RETIRES BOTH SCHEMA TRAPS `Phase-5-VoicePlan.md` §8.1 PRE-RECORDED, WHICH IS THE MOST USEFUL THING ABOUT IT:** the next-free-version hazard (**G6** — this log records it going wrong twice, once shipping a silent runtime failure) **cannot fire because no version is claimed**, and the `deleteProject` FK trap — whose precedent is Task 6-3 adding purge step **7b** *"in the same commit as the table"* to stop `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_FOREIGNKEY` rolling back the whole purge — **cannot fire because no new table references `projects(id)` or `sessions(id)`**. `dispatches` is additionally safe here because it carries **zero `REFERENCES` on every column** by deliberate design, so a refinement outliving its pane stores cleanly. **⚠ THE SOURCE DOCUMENT'S CLEAREST RULE IS UNCHANGED AND ONLY ITS STORAGE MOVES: the original transcript is the source of truth and is never overwritten** — enforced in memory for the life of a dictation rather than in SQLite. A durable voice-session record (refined versions, user edits, error category, retention state) returns when a surface actually reads one. | 2026-08-17 · **RESOLVED — Matthew, at the kickoff** |
| D162 | **⚠ THE MICROPHONE PERMISSION POLICY IS INSTALLED BEFORE THE FIRST CAPTURE LINE, AND IT TAKES BOTH HANDLERS — AND F79 TURNS THIS FROM TIDINESS INTO A LIVE GAP.** Settled 2026-08-17. `setPermissionRequestHandler` **and** `setPermissionCheckHandler` on `session.defaultSession`, allowing `'media'` **only** for the app's own origin and denying every other permission outright, installed at the top of `whenReady` (`index.ts:321`) **hundreds of lines before `createWindow` (`:822`)**. **⚠ THE ORDERING IS THE CONTENT OF THE RULING:** `createWindow` is what makes a renderer capable of asking, so a policy installed after it has a window of exposure in it — and per **F79**, measured this session, **Electron 43.1.1 grants `getUserMedia({audio:true})` SILENTLY in 147 ms with no handler installed**, which is `src/main/` at `4369954` exactly. **The boundary is open at HEAD; this is closing it, not pre-hardening a future feature.** **⚠ AND ONE HANDLER IS A POLICY WITH A HOLE IN IT**, on Electron's own documented authority: *"you must also implement `setPermissionCheckHandler` to get complete permission handling. Most web APIs do a permission check and then make a permission request if the check is denied."* Testing the happy path will not reveal the gap, because the happy path goes through the request handler. **Task 5-1 must be revertible to a still-hardened app**: delete its renderer half and the boundary survives. **⚠ AMENDED 2026-08-17 AT IMPLEMENTATION, ON A MEASUREMENT: THE SHIPPED ALLOW-LIST IS NOT `media`-ONLY — IT IS `media` + `clipboard-read` + `clipboard-sanitized-write`, ALL STILL ORIGIN-CHECKED.** The media-only policy this ruling specifies was built first and DRIVEN before anything else was written. Under it, `navigator.clipboard.readText()` and `writeText()` both failed with `NotAllowedError: Read/Write permission denied` — Chromium routes both through Electron's permission handlers, and the app already depends on them at `TerminalPane.vue:913` (Ctrl+V into a PTY) and `:868` (Ctrl+C), plus the council and day-report copy buttons. **A media-only allow-list would therefore have shipped a HARDENING commit that silently broke copy and paste in every terminal pane** — the precise class of regression a happy-path test cannot catch, since the happy path is the microphone. A probe taken *before* the policy existed recorded both clipboard permissions passing, so the two entries **restore existing behaviour and grant nothing new**. Everything else the ruling says stands unchanged: both handlers, installed at the top of `whenReady` hundreds of lines before `createWindow`, origin-checked, main-frame-only, permission NAME logged and never the URL, and **`geolocation`, `notifications`, `midi`, `usb`, `hid`, `serial`, `display-capture`, `idle-detection` and `window-management` all refused outright** — `geolocation` and `notifications` verified DENIED at runtime in both dev and a packaged build. **⚠ AND ONE MEASURED BOOT BEHAVIOUR IS RECORDED SO IT IS NOT CHASED AS A BUG:** the check handler is called with an **empty origin** a handful of times during startup — Chromium checks `media`, `geolocation` and `web-app-installation` before the renderer commits a URL. Those are refused, correctly, and appear as refusals in the log on a perfectly healthy boot; once the page commits, every `media` check arrives with the real origin and is allowed. | 2026-08-17 · **RESOLVED at kickoff; AMENDED 2026-08-17 at implementation — allow-list widened to the two clipboard permissions on a measurement, origin check unchanged** |
| D163 | **⚠ THE nodeterm COMPARISON IS RECORDED, AND EXACTLY TWO OF ITS ELEVEN RECOMMENDATIONS ARE ADMITTED AS *CANDIDATE FEATURES* — UNSCHEDULED, UNNUMBERED AS PHASES, AND NOT AUTHORITATIVE UNTIL A LATER ARCHITECT PASS PLACES THEM.** Source: [`Investigations/Nodeterm-Comparison.md`](Investigations/Nodeterm-Comparison.md) (2026-08-17) — a feature-by-feature read of nodeterm (macOS/Linux, tmux-backed, React Flow canvas) against Chorus, its two Reddit threads (~290 comments), and Claude Code's own docs. **nodeterm is BUSL-1.1: every idea admitted here is to be re-implemented from scratch — no code, no generated shell text, no SKILL.md prose may be copied.** **ADMITTED AS CANDIDATES: R1 "Peer context & messaging" (→ C1) and R3 "Sub-agent visibility via transcript tail" (→ C2)**, written up in §7's new *Candidate features* section. **DOCUMENTED FOR LATER CONSIDERATION, NEITHER ADOPTED NOR REJECTED: R2, R4–R11** — recorded in the same section's table so the analysis is not re-run from memory. **TIER 3 IS RECORDED AS CONSIDERED-AND-NOT-ADOPTED WITH REASONS** (infinite canvas; agents driving layout/`spawn-team`; tmux/SSH/mobile/server/team presence; notch HUD; the ADHD marketing framing) — *not deleted, superseded in place*, so a future pass sees what was weighed rather than an absence. **⚠ THE TWO FACTS THAT MADE THIS WORTH ADMITTING AT ALL, AND NEITHER IS A FEATURE-LIST OBSERVATION. (a) CLAUDE CODE SHIPPED NATIVE CROSS-SESSION MESSAGING AND IT DOES NOT EXIST ON WINDOWS.** v2.1.224 (2026-08-08) gives `ListAgents`/`SendMessage` between independent sessions via a per-session **Unix** inbox socket, text only; the docs state plainly *"Claude Code doesn't offer cross-session messaging on native Windows"* (macOS/Linux/WSL2 only), and agent view (`claude agents`) is the same macOS-first research preview. Chorus is Windows-only — so this is not a me-too, and because Chorus already owns MCP config injection for claude/codex/opencode (`mcpConfigCore.ts` / `mcpConfigWrite.ts` / `wireMcpForLaunch`) it can be **cross-vendor**, which Anthropic's cannot. **(b) THE SHARING MODEL IS *PULL*, NOT PUSH, AND THE COMMUNITY ALREADY FOUND BOTH FAILURE MODES** — *"why does linking two fresh CLIs use so many tokens?"* (unbounded eager reads; pull only pays when it is **bounded and lazy**) and *"context … poisoned after a few turns; a spec, plan, handoff document works better"* (raw transcript ≠ good context — **which Chorus already answered** with the durable `chorus-memory` graph, Phase 6/6a). What is missing is only the **ephemeral** tier. **⚠ TWO SCOPE FENCES THAT ARE PART OF THE RULING, NOT COMMENTARY. (1) C1 IS NOT THE ORCHESTRATION §8 BARS** — no agent spawns, stops or steers another; it is **read + notify between sibling sessions the human launched**, the same boundary Anthropic itself drew for `SendMessage`. §8 gains a clarifying bullet rather than an amendment. **(2) C2 IS A CANDIDATE *VEHICLE* FOR D39's "N sub-agents running / done" CHIP THAT REOPENS NEITHER D145 NOR D130.** **F67** killed the bus route (`SubagentStart`/`SubagentStop` both classify to `working`, and the bus's edge trigger sits upstream of every listener, so the count is unobservable there); **D145** deferred the bus and named D39 as one of the two things that would reopen it. A read-only tail of files **under the `transcript_path` directory the context ring already opens** (`contextUsage.ts`, itself a documented D130 widening) is upstream of nothing and adds **no new hook-body field** — so the bus stays unbuilt and D130's read surface stays where it is. **⚠ AND THE LAYOUT IS UNVERIFIED: `<sessionId>/subagents/agent-<id>.jsonl` + `.meta.json` is nodeterm's observation on macOS/Linux and MUST be verified against Claude Code on Windows before anything relies on it — D4-style, never asserted from another codebase.** **NOTHING HERE IS SCHEDULED.** No phase number is assigned, no phase is reordered, and no task doc may be authored against C1 or C2 without a kickoff that places them first. | **RESOLVED 2026-08-18 (architect pass, Matthew) — comparison recorded; R1/R3 admitted as CANDIDATES only; R2/R4–R11 documented; Tier 3 not adopted; no scheduling, no phase number, no authority until placed** |
| D164 | **⚠ `grok` JOINS THE REGISTRY — FOUR ENTRIES BECOME FIVE, `agentKindSchema` AND `staticRegistry` TOGETHER, UNDER D86/D90's WIDEN-TOGETHER RULE.** Settled 2026-08-18 at Matthew's request: *"I just installed the Grok CLI so I want to have Grok be an option when adding an agent like Claude, Codex, or OpenCode … a button on the 'Add Agent' screen to start a Grok session in the terminal."* The compiler walked the `Record<AgentKind, …>` sites for the third time (`notifications.ts`, `palette/commands.ts`, `LaunchDialog.vue`, `FilmstripRenderer.vue`, `TerminalPane.vue`); tile code **`gk`**, label **`Grok`**; `DETECTED_TOOLS` gains `'grok'` LAST among the agents so on screen the cards read claude · codex · opencode · grok (kimi's card stays withheld by `HIDDEN_AGENTS`). No migration — `sessions.agent` is unconstrained TEXT (D120). **⚠ D4-VERIFIED BY RUNNING THE INSTALLED `grok 1.0.5 (5115b46bc9) [stable]` (`~/.grok/bin/grok.exe`, a real .exe on the USER PATH — a Chorus started before the install will not see it until it restarts), and its bundled user guide (`~/.grok/docs/user-guide/`).** **The `-c` trap for the THIRD time (kimi D87, opencode D90): `-c` is `--continue` on grok** and is never emitted. **What makes grok a peer of CLAUDE rather than of kimi: every levelled capability has a top-level TUI flag, and resume is claude's ASSIGNED shape.** (1) `--reasoning-effort <EFFORT>` — per-model menus (`grok models` / `models_cache.json`: **grok-4.6 low·medium·high·xhigh, grok-4.5 low·medium·high, both default `high`**), and an unlisted value is **rejected at launch** (measured: `--model grok-4.5 --reasoning-effort xhigh` → exit 1 *"unknown effort level 'xhigh'; use one of: high, medium, low"*). Mapped **ONE-TO-ONE** — Fast→low, Balanced→medium, Deep→high, Max→xhigh — the first adapter whose ladder needs no unreachable rung; `defaultLevelId: 'deep'` = grok's own default, safe on both models. **The one documented trap: `Max` on a grok-4.5 route fails at launch;** Chorus does not probe per-model menus (an explicit non-goal since 3a-4), so it is recorded, not special-cased. (2) `--permission-mode [default, acceptEdits, auto, dontAsk, bypassPermissions, plan]` — **three of six declared**: Auto→`auto`, Accept edits→`acceptEdits`, Manual→`default` (ask); `bypassPermissions` excluded per `permissionModeSchema`'s standing ruling, `plan` because the guide calls it *"accepted for compatibility"* (meaning unmeasured), `dontAsk` because it is a CI posture. `defaultLevelId: 'auto'` follows the one line claude's descriptor exists for (Matthew, 2026-08-14). Verified in the running TUI: its status line reads **`Grok 4.6 (high) · auto`**. (3) `-m <MODEL>` on the top-level invocation, emitted only when a route names a model; **a route's `base_url` is NOT applied** — grok has no per-launch endpoint flag (custom endpoints are `[model.<name>]` tables in config.toml). (4) **`XAI_API_KEY` reaches it through the ENVIRONMENT** — measured in an isolated home with a bogus key: `grok models` → *"You are using XAI_API_KEY."*, `--debug` log `resolved credentials … auth_type=ApiKey`, server *"Incorrect API key provided"*; the guide ranks it below an active session token. So `apiKey: true`, and `requiredEnvVars: []` was measured under a CLEARED env of exactly `BASELINE_ENV_VARS` + `PINNED_ENV_VARS` + the key (exit 0, both models listed) — the D90 probe re-run. (5) **RESUME, ASSIGNED (D140):** `-s/--session-id <UUID>` names a NEW conversation, `-r/--resume [<id>]` reopens it (value optional → **empty-pointer guard, D143(e)**: a bare `--resume` adopts the cwd's most recent session). Measured strings: `--session-id <live>` → exit 1 *"Error: Error: Session ID <uuid> is already in use."* (→ `in-use`); `--resume <unknown>` → grok tries a **REMOTE** restore first, exit 1 *"Failed to restore session from remote: fetching session record: session get failed: 404 Not Found"* — **matched on the 404, not the prefix**, so a network outage keeps a healthy pointer. `classifyResumeFailure` earns the descriptor; `RESUME_SUPPORT.grok = true`. **Declared NULL, each a decision:** `mcp` (a `grok mcp` subcommand exists but its dialect/location is unmeasured and Chorus knows two dialects), `hooks` (10-hooks.md documents a Claude-compatible bus but there is no per-launch `--settings`-style load flag; the file route means writing into the repo), `instructions` (`--rules <RULES>` is a real APPEND vehicle but argv-only and unmeasured against the TUI — the seam for a later task). **⚠ AND ONE THING THE RUNTIME DRIVE CLARIFIED, NOT A DEFECT:** a MANUAL restart of a grok pane minted a NEW `--session-id` rather than `--resume` — that is **D142 working** (a restart is a deliberate fresh conversation; only the RESTORE engine resumes). **Verified end-to-end on an ISOLATED instance** (port 9333, copied user-data-dir with `Local State` per F31, worktree `node_modules` junctioned per house practice): the card renders fourth as `gk / Grok / grok 1.0.5 (5115b46bc9) [stable]`; selecting it anchors Effort=Deep (`--reasoning-effort high`) and Permission=Auto; Launch spawned **exactly** `grok.exe --reasoning-effort high --permission-mode auto --session-id 93104dad-…` and the row carried that pointer; the trust prompt, a real turn and a cold app restart all behaved — the restore engine relaunched with `--resume 83f31e2d-…` and the pane came back showing the prior turn. `npm run typecheck` 0 errors; `npx vitest run` **2505/2505** (adapters.test.ts 150 → 182: three named tables widened, D164 block, grok resume/classifier fixtures; the launch-behaviour `describe.each` gains grok because it carries BOTH levelled descriptors). The one non-passing suite, `codeIndexCore.test.ts`, is a pre-existing environmental miss (reads the gitignored `_verify/6a-2/` fixture, absent from worktrees). **Two Grok test sessions (`PINEAPPLE`) exist under Matthew's own `~/.grok/sessions/` from the drive; nothing was written into either repository checkout.** | **RESOLVED 2026-08-18 — Matthew's request; adapter shipped with effort, permission, XAI_API_KEY and assigned resume, all D4-measured; mcp/hooks/instructions null pending measurement** |

### Gates

| ID | Gate |
|---|---|
| G1 | Typecheck: zero errors. |
| G2 | **Run, don't just compile** — drive the real app window, observe both TUIs; screenshots when headless. |
| G3 | **One** intentional narrated commit per execution session (style of `80e69c3`). |
| G4 | **Secret-grep gate** before any phase touching credentials — no keys in args, logs, or transcripts. |
| G5 | Council Review checkpoints per D6 on all **[CR]** phases. |
| G6 | **Re-count every shared counter AFTER merging, never before — `IpcChannel`, `MIGRATIONS.length`, and the next free D/F/G number.** Take the value from the merged tree; never add your own delta to what the file said when you branched, and never trust a number a branch was green on. **Per F54 this has already gone wrong four times on the channel tally, once in the decision log, and twice on migration versions — the last of which shipped a silent runtime failure.** For migrations specifically, check `main`'s highest version as well as this file's `MIGRATIONS.length`, and **stop on divergence rather than renumbering**. |

### Phase 1 — Grid + Projects — ✅ COMPLETE (2026-07-19)

_Five serial tasks, each planned (`Task-1-#.md` + `ImplementationSpec-1-#.md`), executed in its own session, and coordinator-reviewed (`Task-1-#-CompletionSummary.md`). Two council reviews ran: CR-1.2 (layout engine → D9) and CR-1.5 (restore contract → D16). Decisions D7–D17 and findings F2–F11 all trace to this phase._

| Task | Scope | Status |
|---|---|---|
| **1-1** Tailwind + session lifecycle UI | Status dots driven by exit codes; restart/kill per pane, incl. clean process-tree kill; exit banner removed. | ✅ `185f972` |
| **1-2** Data layer | Drizzle typed queries; migration v2 (`sessions`); stable session ids; flat layout → versioned binary tree (lazy conversion); Vitest (24 tests). Zero visual change. | ✅ `81e8a0b` |
| **1-3** Layout view | `LayoutRenderer.vue` over the persisted tree via splitpanes; `layout:set` IPC; debounced PTY resize; close-kills-session. **Spike gate returned GO** (12/12 checks, ~45 min of a 4h box). Split buttons disabled as planned. Filmstrip spike ran and was deleted; notes kept. Produced D14 + findings F2/F3/F4. | ✅ `e7d6e60` |
| **1-4** Launch dialog + multi-session | D10 rekey landed first as a verified standalone refactor; explicit launch flow (`session:launch` + dialog + empty state); N sessions per agent kind proved (3 panes, 2 independent Codex TUIs); both 1-3 guards cleared; D11 wired. One runtime bug (F5 attach-resurrection) found, fixed via the `respawn` gate (D15), re-verified. | ✅ `c91aea1` |
| **1-5** Project tabs + restore | Project tabs (native picker, persisted active project, window title); `registerIpc` freed of its project closure (per-request FK-checked `project_id`); the **D16 restore contract implemented verbatim** — pure `computeRestoreSet`, heal-before-spawn, guarded staggered auto-relaunch, unified `session:restart`, close-flow row deletion, `respawn` removed end-to-end. One runtime-found flaw (badge timing) fixed as F10/D17. | ✅ `fb384c5` |

**Milestone — MET:** many sessions across multiple projects, restart-safe and killable. Runtime-proven at `fb384c5`: sessions keep running while their project's tab is hidden; quit leaves zero orphans; relaunch auto-restores the active project's sessions (staggered, badged) in the persisted layout shape with lazy restore on tab activation; and no code path can spawn a PTY that no pane can reach.

**[CR] checkpoint (1.2):** _splitpanes library vs. custom split-tree vs. hybrid_ — **CLOSED** as **D9**, unanimous 3-of-3. Brief: `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-1.2-LayoutEngine.md`; findings: `docs/architecture/CR-1.2-pane-layout-council-findings.md`. The remaining open question is empirical, not architectural: Task 1-3's spike gate decides splitpanes vs. custom renderer behind the same adapter contract.

**Milestone:** many sessions across multiple projects — restart-safe and killable.

| **1b-3** `Ctrl+K` palette skeleton | `CommandPalette.vue` + extensible registry (`palette/commands.ts`) + in-repo fuzzy filter; five D21 commands over existing plumbing; capture-phase hotkey interception (xterm swallows keys). Renderer-only. Closes Phase 1b. **Delivered:** pure `buildCommands` registry + `fuzzyFilter` (subsequence, disabled omitted) with 14 new tests (84/84 across 6 files); `CommandPalette.vue` on the LaunchDialog trap idiom; App.vue capture-phase Ctrl+K listener, `paletteCommands` computed, `restartFocused`; the **capture-phase hotkey won** over a focused xterm; **F14 store-flip deviation** ratified (restart flips the session store — `session:restart` emits no event); all **9 runtime script items** individually CDP-verified (Ctrl+K over a live TUI, `'cod'` ranks "Focus Codex" first, wrap-around arrows, Enter focus-swap with byte-identical `pane_layouts` + persisted `focusedSessionId`, launch dialog, project switch both directions, filmstrip⇄grid persistence, live-session restart with fresh TUI and no still-running refusal, empty-project palette, Esc/click-close, no double-mount on rapid Ctrl+K; zero console errors / unhandled rejections / structured-clone errors). Surfaced **F15** (pre-existing app-wide CSS reset defect, not fixed here). | ✅ `72ea471` |

**Milestone — MET:** the workspace is glanceable and drivable — sessions name themselves, the default view is one focused pane plus a status strip, and `Ctrl+K` reaches launch/switch/focus/toggle/restart without the mouse. Runtime-proven at `72ea471`: Ctrl+K opens over a running TUI, fuzzy-ranks and runs all five commands, and returns keyboard control to the terminal on close.

### Phase 3 — BYOK Vault + Adapters — ✅ COMPLETE (kickoff 2026-07-21, re-kickoff 2026-07-22, closed 2026-07-24 by `e3d72c5` + `15a016e`) **[CR-3.0 CLOSED as D33 · CR-3.1 CLOSED as D34]**

_Kicked off 2026-07-21 (**D28–D32**), **re-kicked-off 2026-07-22** after both council gates closed (**D33** vault, **D34** adapter interface) and **D35** split the phase into **six** serial tasks. Scoped tight to its own milestone per D28 — model catalog, effort normalization, launch profiles, and `usage_records` moved to the new **Phase 3a** below. See [`Tasks/Phase-3-Overview.md`](Tasks/Phase-3-Overview.md), which supersedes the original kickoff's five-task table. **Task 3-1 is COMPLETE** (chore `bc9b403` + task `0e0640a`); **Tasks 3-2 … 3-6 are AUTHORED** against the two ratified contracts, each with a paired ImplementationSpec. **Prime directive: a key never reaches a command line, a log file, a transcript, the renderer, or disk in plaintext.**_

| Task | Scope | Status |
|---|---|---|
