**⚠ THOSE NUMBERS ARE NOT THE ONES THE PHASE WAS SCOPED AGAINST, AND THE REASON IS THE PHASE'S
LAST LESSON: PHASE 3d LANDED IN THE MIDDLE OF IT.** Tasks 3d-1 … 3d-4 shipped **between 3c-4 and
3c-5**, out of order at Matthew's request because they blocked his daily use of the app. The
kickoff froze `IpcChannel` **56** · `handle` **51** · `sqliteTable` **15** · `MIGRATIONS` **11** ·
vitest **941/29**; every one of those was correct when written and stale by the time 3c-5 ran.
**The purity contract itself held — 3c-5 moved nothing** (its diff is four `views/` files plus one
new stylesheet, with zero `stores/`, `main/`, `shared/`, `components/` or `palette/`). **The
lesson is that a frozen number is only frozen relative to a phase that runs uninterrupted**, and a
prompt authored against the code catches this while a prompt authored against the task docs does
not. Full correction table in [`Tasks/Phase-3c-Overview.md`](Tasks/Phase-3c-Overview.md).

**⚠ THE MILESTONE IS MET WITH ONE DELIBERATE DEVIATION AND FOUR UNMOCKED REGIONS, AND BOTH ARE
STATED HERE RATHER THAN LEFT IN A TASK REPORT** — "visually indistinguishable" is the milestone's
own wording, so a reader deserves to know where it is knowingly false.

- **The deviation: the settings mock draws MASKED KEY PREVIEWS** (`sk-ant-…Xq4F`, `sk-proj-…9dKm`,
  `sk-proj-…T2wa`, `sk-or-…v81A`) and **Chorus does not draw them.** **D33 clause 3 admits no
  exception**; even four characters narrow the search space for anyone who sees a screenshot or a
  screen share. The refusal is **structural, not incidental**: `settings.css` has **no
  `.set-row-hint` class**, so there is nowhere to put one. **A mock is the authority on appearance
  and is never an authority on what the app may disclose** — the same precedence
  `ImplementationSpec-3c-5.md` §3 sets for verification chrome in the council view. Two smaller
  D76 omissions in the same file: the mock's **six** nav entries (one is live) and its
  **`neo4j :7688`** chip (Phase 6; no source).
- **The unmocked count is FOUR REGIONS ACROSS THREE FILES, not the two surfaces D83 left it at.**
  `WorktreePanel.vue` and `EmptyState.vue` (declared), plus **two found by authoring 3c-5's prompt
  against the code**: the **council-member surface** (3b-2 — `grep -ci "council"` over the mock
  returns **0**) and the **model shortlist section** (3d/D85 — `grep -ciE "shortlist|favourite|
  star|pin"` returns **0**). Both post-date the mock. **`SettingsProviders.vue` is 1,334 lines and
  the mock describes roughly half of it**; any later claim that this file "matches the mock" is
  false without that qualification.

**⚠ D76 BOUND HARDER IN 3c-5 THAN ANYWHERE ELSE IN THE PHASE, AND ONE OMISSION IS A REASONED
EXCEPTION TO D55.** The council mock's phase header draws `elapsed 4:38`, `est. remaining ~9m`,
`round 1 **of 2**` and `$0.31 so far`; **none has a source** — no run start time in the store
(adding one is store logic the task may not touch), an estimate is the dishonest number the
five-stop track exists to refuse, the renderer is never told how many rounds are planned, and
`costUsd` arrives only with the end-of-run accounting. **What ships is the round ordinal alone.**
⚠ That is a **deliberate exception to D55's "no number without its denominator"**: an ordinal
position is not a measured quantity out of a total, and inventing "of 2" to satisfy D55's letter
would breach D76. Recorded so the next reader does not "fix" it.

**Two defects the RUNNING APP exposed that reading the code did not** — both found in the G2 pass,
both fixed in the task commit. **A route whose credential had never been verified rendered "0 of 1
verified" in the same healthy green as "1 of 1"** — D55 inverted: the number was right and the
*rendering* of it lied; zero-verified now takes the neutral tone. And **a two-word provider name
wrapped and pushed the status chip onto a second line.**

**⚠ THE COUNCIL BEHAVIOUR RE-CHECK WAS NOT PERFORMED, AND IT IS RECORDED AS UNPROVEN RATHER THAN
REASONED AWAY.** `Task-3c-5.md` asks for a real run streaming into the restyled view.
**`council_members` is EMPTY — zero rows**; the D71 frontier roster is gone (the DB was rebuilt
2026-07-27, though the three OpenRouter credential profiles survive and verify). Rebuilding that
roster belongs to **Phase 3e**, which has the authorised budget and the measurement questions that
need it; doing it here would spend frontier money on the wrong question. So **streaming,
Esc-during-run, and the findings-file write are UNPROVEN by this phase.** They are unchanged code
paths — `stores/council.ts`, `councilCore.ts`, `councilService.ts` all have an empty diff — but
**"unchanged code" is an argument, not evidence, and this phase's whole rule is that the two are
different.** 3e's first real run closes it.

**G2 covered 13 of 14 surfaces fully; surface 4 (a pane in each of four states) is PARTIAL** —
`running` observed on three live panes, `exited`/`error` **not induced because that means killing
sessions Matthew has real work in**, and `needs-you` still has **no data source** (D78, owed by
Phase 4). **The six council states were driven SYNTHETICALLY** by writing the renderer store over
CDP in the exact shape main's broadcast produces — **`$0.00`, no IPC, no API call, no database
write, reverted by a reload** — which is *better* evidence than a real run for a styling task
(`refused` and `error` are states a live run may not produce on demand), and is stated plainly so
nobody later mistakes them for photographs of a live deliberation.

**⚠ The phase now has TWO intentional behavioural changes, not one.** `frame: false` (D74) was
declared at kickoff. The second, `98191ec`, was landed at Matthew's explicit request **after**
3c-3's task commit and deliberately kept out of it: 3c-3 put two session **counts** on screen for
the first time (the rail's and the status bar's) and nothing told them a pane had closed, so the
counts went stale until a project switch or restart. The fix is a refresh cadence — a
`chorus:session-closed` window event and a re-read from main — which is behaviour, not styling.
**It is a defect this phase created by displaying facts that were previously undisplayed**, and it
sits in its own commit precisely so the "no behavioural change" claim over the task commit stays
literally true. No channel, no handler, no store logic, no test edited.

**Kickoff decisions.** **D72 — RAISED AND DISCHARGED THE SAME DAY.** The council view had no mock,
so the ruling was **design it, do not defer it**: a detailed prompt was authored
([`docs/design/CouncilView-DesignPrompt.md`](../../design/CouncilView-DesignPrompt.md)), Matthew
produced the design in Claude Design, and delivered `docs/design/v2/Chorus Council.dc.html`
(**69,011 B**, all six states). **Coordinator-reviewed at delivery: all five invariants pass** —
the F27 redaction wording and the standing caveat both **verbatim**, the caveat above the
synthesis, unavailable members shown *and* explained, every accounting figure carrying its
denominator, and **zero** verification chrome. **⚠ AND IT EXCEEDS THE SHIPPED VIEW IN TWO PLACES
THAT ARE NOT COSMETIC:** refused turns render as transcript **rows rather than gaps**, and the
cost line states ***"true total is at least this"*** — **F39's under-reporting made visible in the
UI**, which the shipped view does not say. Both are adopted into 3c-5's scope. **⚠ The whole
`v2/` folder is now the cited authority and it is NOT a fork: all seven pre-existing screens are
BYTE-IDENTICAL to their originals** (verified by `cmp`), so v2 adds one mock and changes nothing
else — citing root for six files and v2 for one would be a two-homes hazard for no benefit. **D73** — **faithful** extraction of all ~45 hex values,
near-duplicates included, so the screenshot diff stays literal; collapsing them later is a design
change, not a refactor. **D74** — `frame: false` with **fully custom** window controls. **D75** —
**`@fontsource` packages**, which are **two dependencies not in `CLAUDE.md`'s locked stack and were
explicitly approved by Matthew at kickoff**; the CDN link must be gone, proven by an offline cold
boot. **D76 (coordinator)** — **the mocks draw data that does not exist** (per-project cost, a
daily rollup, a Neo4j chip): **render what the data supports, omit the rest, and never render a
placeholder or a zero** — the pixel-level form of the D55 rule that a number without its
denominator is worse than no number.

**Decisions settled DURING the phase (D77–D82), each found by authoring a task's execution prompt
against the code.** Full text in [`Tasks/Phase-3c-Overview.md`](Tasks/Phase-3c-Overview.md);
recorded here at roadmap level because three of them change what later phases owe.

| # | Ruling | Consequence beyond its own task |
|---|---|---|
| **D77** | **No component-test harness** — the repo has none (`vitest` is `environment: 'node'`, no `@vue/test-utils`, no jsdom), and building one for a single component would need two off-stack dependencies plus a changed test environment. Shape-distinctness is proven on the **running app via CDP grayscale screenshot** instead. | 3c-1 mounts `StateMarker` **nowhere**, so the runtime proof was **owed by 3c-3** — ✅ **performed and passed** for the three states that exist. `vitest.config.ts` stays byte-identical through the phase. |
| **D78** | **⚠ DISCHARGED 2026-08-07 BY D129 — SEE THERE BEFORE ACTING ON THIS ROW.** The hook listener gave `needs-you` a source, so the filmstrip now renders four. **The half that survives permanently:** an agent with no hook bus still gets three, and an unrecognised event changes nothing — a false amber is worse than none. Original text follows. **The renderer can derive exactly THREE session states, not four.** `sessionStatusSchema` is `running \| exited`; the `attention:*` machinery is write-only outbound and measures the **human's** attention, not the agent's. Nothing reads the PTY stream for an agent prompt. | **No attention badge, no "waiting" tally, no pulsing card in 3c-3.** `chorusPulse` ships with **no first caller**. **`docs/design/v2/Chorus Needs Attention.html` becomes PHASE 4's normative spec** — it specifies a *capability*, not a skin. The fourth marker geometry is **UNPROVEN at runtime and owed by Phase 4**. |
| **D79** | The `needs-you` marker is **8px canonical** (`Chorus Needs Attention.html`), not the 7px 3c-1 read from the Workspace mock; the mock's 6px rail badge stays an intentional density variant. | Source-only edit in 3c-3 with **no visible effect this phase** — it hands Phase 4 the right value. |
| **D80** | **`project:list` gains `sessionCount`**, computed in main by one `GROUP BY project_id`. A **declared exception to the purity contract's "no channel is RESHAPED" clause**, admitted on D74's terms — before the task, bounded to one field on one response. | **No channel or handler added** (56 / 51 hold). Forced edits to **two** pre-existing tests, both IPC-fixture-only: `ipc.test.ts` (predicted) and `palette/commands.test.ts` (**not** predicted — it stopped compiling). **No other task in this phase may reshape a payload.** |
| **D81** | **`LaunchDialog` has NO model input.** `ImplementationSpec-3c-4.md` §3/§6.3 require verifying it is "an `<input>` with a `<datalist>`, not a `<select>`" — there is no such element; 3a-4 moved model resolution into main and the dialog merely **displays** `resolvedModel`. | **The check is struck and must not be ticked. ⚠ The hazard inverts the spec's intent:** an implementer who "restores" a model input creates exactly the second home for "which model" that **D48** forbids. **3c-4 adds no model input.** |
| **D82** | **The 3c-4 spec's shared overlay anatomy contradicts BOTH mocks.** §1 names `--color-surface-card` / `--color-border-inset` / `--radius-card`; the Workspace and Launch Dialog mocks **agree** on `#10141A` / `#262D35` / `8px` — which 3c-1 already tokenised as `--color-surface-overlay` / `--color-border-badge` / `--radius-overlay`. | **D73 applies: the mock wins.** Because §1 is the *extract-once* shared shell, the error would have propagated into all three mocked overlays at once. The scrim base `#050608` has **no token** and its two alphas (`.62` palette, `.55` dialog) are reproduced **as drawn, not unified**. |
| **D83** | **`Chorus Startup.dc.html` is an animated 2.75 s LAUNCH SPLASH over the workspace — not an empty state**, and `grep -rniE "splash\|startup"` across `src/main/` and `src/renderer/src/` returns **nothing**: the app has no splash. `EmptyState.vue` (App.vue `:434`, rendered when `layout.tree` is null) is a different surface, and **no mock draws it**. | **⚠ AMENDS THE MILESTONE: there are TWO unmocked surfaces, not one.** `EmptyState.vue` joins `WorktreePanel.vue` at token-and-primitive conformance. **The splash is a NEW FEATURE and is not built in 3c** — its boot line *"waking 7 voices · restoring 3 sessions"* would need live restore progress the renderer is never told, which is **D76** again. Unbuilt and unscheduled, recorded so it is not silently dropped. **⚠ SUPERSEDED 2026-07-27 BY `2ace8bb` — THE SPLASH WAS BUILT, AND THIS CLAUSE IS NO LONGER TRUE.** `components/StartupSplash.vue` + `boot/bootInfo.ts` ship. **It was not built in defiance of this ruling; it was built by REMOVING THE RULING'S PREMISE.** D83's objection was **D76** — the boot line needs restore progress the renderer is never told — and `bootInfo.ts` gives it a source: main **stamps the write-once boot constants onto the renderer URL** before the window exists, parses them as **untrusted input** (clamped, length-capped, rendered as text), and enforces D76 **in the parser rather than the template**, so an absent, malformed or zero value resolves to the same *"nothing to say"* state and the splash **cannot render a placeholder or a zero even if main sends a broken one**. **The D76 lesson survives intact and is the durable half of this row: the answer to "the mock draws data that does not exist" is omit it OR GIVE IT A SOURCE — never fake it.** See Phase 3d. |

**⚠ Three things the kickoff found that the roadmap's own text did not say:**

- **The phase's purity contract needed an exception before it was written down.** "No IPC change"
  is impossible with `frame: false` — a frameless window's buttons **cannot** minimize, maximize
  or close without asking main. **3c-2 adds exactly four channels** (`window:minimize`,
  `window:toggle-maximize`, `window:close`, and a `window:maximized-changed` event **required**
  because the state also changes via double-click and `Win+↑`), taking `IpcChannel` **52 → 56**
  and `ipcMain.handle(` **48 → 51**. Every other task in the phase holds at those numbers.
- **⚠ THERE ARE NO COMPONENT TESTS IN THIS REPO — ZERO.** All 6 renderer test files are
  stores/logic. **No visual claim in this phase can be discharged by vitest**, which makes the
  F15-in-reverse warning literal: verification is a **per-surface CDP screenshot pass over a
  named 14-surface inventory**, re-run in full at 3c-5 after the last change lands.
- **`Chorus Micro Surfaces.dc.html` is the Fleet Switcher + push-to-talk mock — it is PHASE 5's,
  not 3c's**, and `Chorus Attention Inbox.dc.html` is Phase 4's. Of the seven files in
  `docs/design/`, **four are 3c's** (Workspace, Launch Dialog, Settings Providers, Startup), one
  is an index, and **no CSS custom property exists in any of them** — every value is an inline
  literal, so "extract tokens" had no existing naming to inherit.

**Milestone — ⚠ AMENDED AT KICKOFF, because "every surface that exists" now includes surfaces with
no mock.** Mocked surfaces (Workspace and its parts, Launch Dialog, Settings, Startup, **and
Council once D72's mock lands**) are held to **visually indistinguishable, screenshot-diffed**,
which D73 makes literal. **`WorktreePanel.vue` has no mock and no plan for one** and is held to
**token-and-primitive conformance only** — explicitly not redesigned, and **recorded as the
phase's one known visual gap** rather than quietly satisfied by a screenshot of something nobody
drew. No behavioral change, with `frame: false` as the single declared exception.

---

#### Original scope note _(D38, retained)_

The `docs/design/` mockups become the app: design tokens extracted into the Tailwind v4 theme (the five-surface ladder, jade accent, state colors); **Archivo + JetBrains Mono bundled locally**; the frameless custom titlebar (`BrowserWindow` `frame:false` + drag regions — a main-process change); the left project rail replacing `ProjectTabs`; the filmstrip as the right rail; the bottom status bar; the colorblind-safe state components (dot/diamond/triangle/square); pane-header enrichment to the design's anatomy where the data already exists. Restyles every existing surface incl. 3-4's settings shell — which was built to the design's skeleton precisely so this phase recolors rather than rearranges.

- **Placement rationale:** before Phase 4, so the notification center, inbox, and tray chrome are built *in* the design language rather than retrofitted into it — the same land-the-frame-first principle as D30's logger and D35's scrubber, applied to pixels. Sequencing relative to 3a/3b is Matthew's call at the next `/architect`.
- **Kickoff must settle:** the mock's own open-questions list (filmstrip right-rail vs bottom strip at 16+ sessions; amber pulse strength; inbox mode vs overlay; Fleet Switcher orientation; card width), plus the F15 lesson applied in reverse — an app-wide CSS/token change needs a per-surface visual regression pass, not a compile check.
- **Milestone:** the running app is visually indistinguishable from the Workspace and Settings mocks for every surface that exists, with no behavioral change — screenshot-diffed against the mocks.
### Phase 3d — Provider Routes & New Adapters — ✅ **COMPLETE 2026-07-28** _(created by D52 2026-07-24 as "Two New PTY Adapters"; **RENAMED and reconciled by the architect pass of 2026-07-28**; closed by `35a592f`)_

**⚠ RENAMED, AND THE RENAME IS THE POINT — Matthew's ruling, 2026-07-28.** The phase was created to hold two PTY adapters. It ended up holding **two different kinds of work**: *(1)* **provider/route semantics** — what `provider_configs.adapter_type` actually means, which has nothing to do with a PTY agent — and *(2)* **the two adapters**. The prior entry carried a flag asking whether that should be one phase or two. **It is one phase, renamed**, because all of it landed in a single uninterrupted run of commits over two days and splitting it after the fact would invent a phase number for work already done. **The flag is discharged and removed.**

**⚠ THIS PHASE RAN OUT OF ORDER, AT MATTHEW'S REQUEST, BECAUSE IT WAS BLOCKING HIS DAILY USE OF THE APP** — it interleaved with Phase 3c, landing entirely between **3c-4** (`070f381`) and **3c-5** (`c4f82fb`). That is why 3c's frozen numbers went stale mid-phase; see the Phase 3c entry.

**⚠ AND IT SHIPPED WITH NO PHASE OVERVIEW, NO TASK DOCS AND NO IMPLEMENTATION SPECS — only `Tasks/Task-3d-1-ExecutionPrompt.md` exists.** Verified 2026-07-28. That is the mechanical reason this entry sat stale for four days while seven commits landed under it: **there was no task doc whose close would have prompted an update.** Recorded as a process fact, not a reproach — the work is sound and its decisions (D84–D90) were all recorded properly in §6 as they were settled. **The lesson is that the decision log stayed honest while the phase entry did not, because only one of the two had a ritual attached to it.**

| Commit | Decision | What shipped |
|---|---|---|
| `717d312` | **D84** | `adapter_type` names **the harness that runs the traffic**, and `NO_HARNESS_ADAPTER_TYPE = 'none'` is a legal answer. New `adapters/noHarness.ts`. **No schema change, no migration** — the column was already `TEXT NOT NULL`. |
| `a62aad7` | **D85** | The **model shortlist** — `model_shortlist(provider_id, model_id, added_at)`, migration **v12**, `sqliteTable` 15 → 16. Rejected the `favourite`-column alternative on a property: the catalog is a **cache** (D56) and a curation column would make one table mean two things. **No FK onto `model_catalog`** — a user must be able to shortlist an id no refresh has returned. |
| `e87037a` | **D86 · D87** | **Kimi CLI.** `agentKindSchema` + `staticRegistry` **2 → 3, together** — the D34 Q5 freeze lifted as a numbered decision. D87 is the D4 pass: **`-c` is `--continue` on kimi, NOT `--config`**, so kimi has **no per-launch configuration door** and declares `apiKey: false` / `reasoningEffort: null` honestly. |
| `0d1c93f` | **D88** | **A real user-facing defect, found in use:** every Claude pane was printing *"Transcript saving is off — inherited CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION marker"*. **The cause was Chorus**, inheriting the launcher's session markers wholesale. `stripHostAgentSessionVars` removes four names from `parentEnv` **once, before either branch reads it**. |
| `7a738d5` | **D89** | **A launch profile's `env_json` was silently dropped on every SUBSCRIPTION launch** — the common case. `envAdditions` now applies on **both** branches of `composeChildEnv`. Recorded as an **amendment to D34(d)**, not a bug fix, because the shipped behaviour matched its own governing clause exactly. |
| `2ace8bb` | *(see D83 note below)* | The **startup splash**, `renderer/src/boot/bootInfo.ts`, and docs. **Mixed commit** — it also carries 3c doc updates. |
| `35a592f` | **D90** | **`opencode`** as the harness behind the launch dialog's OpenRouter card — registry **3 → 4**, same widen-together rule. Plus the launch dialog's **model picker**, a **closed `<select>`** over `model_shortlist`. D4-verified by running the binary: the key reaches opencode **through the environment**, so **nothing is written to its auth file** — the only reason this adapter was adoptable at all. |

**Ground facts, verified 2026-07-28 at `331267b`:** `agentKindSchema` = `['claude','codex','kimi','opencode']` · `staticRegistry` holds the same four, typed `Readonly<Record<AgentKind, AgentAdapter>>` so coverage is **compiler-enforced in both directions** · `adapters/` holds `claude.ts`, `codex.ts`, `kimi.ts`, `opencode.ts`, `noHarness.ts` · `NO_HARNESS_ADAPTER_TYPE = 'none'` at `shared/ipc.ts:258` and is **still not in the enum** · `MIGRATIONS.length` **12** · `sqliteTable(` **16** · `IpcChannel` **57** · `ipcMain.handle(` **52 / 0**.

**⚠ THE MILESTONE AS ORIGINALLY WORDED IS NOT MET, AND SAYING SO IS THE MAIN VALUE OF THIS PASS.** It read: *"a launch profile can name an agent CLI Chorus did not ship with, and it launches on the route and model the profile specifies."* **Kimi and opencode are adapters Chorus DID ship with.** The registry is still `Object.freeze`d and still static; `registry.ts` says in its own comment that **`register()` behind a Map-backed registry is Phase 6's**. So the phase delivered **reach — four harnesses instead of two — and not extensibility.** A reader who took the old wording at face value would believe a user-registration path exists. It does not.

- **Milestone, as actually met:** four agent harnesses ship (`claude`, `codex`, `kimi`, `opencode`) — **five since D164 (`grok`, 2026-08-18)** —, a provider may honestly declare that **no** harness runs it (`'none'`), and a launch profile launches on the route and model it specifies — with the model chosen from a user-curated shortlist rather than a 341-entry catalog. **The registry-freeze lift (D34 Q5) is spent** (D86, D90).
- **⚠ What is therefore still owed, and by whom:** **runtime adapter registration is Phase 6's**, unchanged and now the *only* remaining part of the original wording. Phase 6's own entry already says the D34 Q5 seam is spent; that stays correct — what it inherits is `register()`, not the freeze.

**⚠ D83's PROHIBITION WAS OVERRIDDEN, THE SPLASH WAS BUILT, AND THE ROADMAP'S OWN D83 ROW IS NOW FALSE UNTIL CORRECTED.** D83 (Phase 3c) ruled `Chorus Startup.dc.html` a **new feature**, said **"DO NOT BUILD IT"**, and recorded it as *"unbuilt and unscheduled… so it is not silently dropped"*. It was built in `2ace8bb` (2026-07-27) — `components/StartupSplash.vue` (7,326 B) and `boot/bootInfo.ts` (4,379 B), both verified present 2026-07-28.

**⚠ AND IT WAS BUILT LEGITIMATELY RATHER THAN IN DEFIANCE, WHICH IS THE PART WORTH RECORDING.** D83's objection was **D76**: the mock's boot line *"waking 7 voices · restoring 3 sessions"* needs live restore progress **the renderer is never told**. `bootInfo.ts` answers exactly that objection — main **stamps the boot constants onto the renderer URL** before the window exists, so the splash has a real source on its first frame. Its header records why this is a query string and not an IPC channel (write-once boot constants that can never change while the window lives), that it is **parsed as untrusted input** (clamped, length-capped, rendered as text), and that **D76 is enforced in the parser rather than the template** — an absent, malformed or zero value resolves to the same *"nothing to say"* state, so the splash **cannot render a placeholder or a zero even if main sends a broken one**.

**So D83 is SUPERSEDED, not violated:** the ruling was correct on the facts it had, and the facts changed when someone built the source it said was missing. **The standing lesson is D76's, and it survives intact — the answer to "the mock draws data that does not exist" is either omit it or GIVE IT A SOURCE, and never fake it.**

#### ✅ Task 3e-2 — the fixes, and the corrections the run forced

Full record: [`Investigations/3e-2-Proving-Run.md`](Investigations/3e-2-Proving-Run.md). Run

  only after normalising to a shared unit.** Read in the raw BYTES the table uses, 3e-1's figures
  look like row 1 "pathological", **which is the reading 3e-1 retracted.** `RESPONSE_CAP_BYTES`
  **4_000_000 → 8_000_000**.
- **⚠ THE RAISE IS LOAD-BEARING ON A REAL TURN, NOT ONLY ON ARITHMETIC.** Kimi's critique streamed
  **4,168,377 bytes — 104% of the old cap — and completed.** Under 4 MB that turn refuses and the
  run is partial again. Its *positions* turn in the same run streamed **2,446,913**, which the old
  cap would have passed: **kimi's streams straddle the old line**, so no single observation could
  have set this bound.
- **KIMI'S REAL RATIO, MEASURED AT LAST: 177.9 – 407.2 bytes/token.** ⚠ **3e-1's bound of "> 250"
  was TOO STRONG** — that was a property of one capped turn. Kimi varies **2.3× within one run**.
- **⚠ MY OWN FIRST DERIVATION OF THE NEW CAP WAS THE SAME UNIT ERROR 3e-1 RETRACTED, AND IT IS

  the **arbiter's** 32,000 allowance — a cross-model product — and landed within 1% of the right
  answer **by luck**. Corrected: each model's own ratio × its **own** allowance, worst case **kimi
  407.2 × 16,000 = 6,515,200**, so 8 MB is **1.23×** it and tolerates **500 bytes/token** at a
  16,000-token allowance.

  participating fully. **The missing `usage` block was a property of the ABORT — an aborted stream

#### ✅ Task 3e-4 — the transcript reader, and the retention position (D97)

**`council_messages` had been written on every run since 3b-3 and read by nothing. It has a door
now.** One channel, `council:transcript`, **read-only** — `IpcChannel` **57 → 58**,
`ipcMain.handle(` **52 → 53**, `index.ts` still **0**, `sqliteTable(` **16**, `MIGRATIONS.length`
**12**. The declared exception D97 stated in advance, spent exactly once.

- **It calls the read function that already existed** (`storage.ts:1819`, zero callers since 3b-2)
  rather than writing a second query over the same table.
- **⚠ BOUNDED AT THE BOUNDARY, AND THE PAYLOAD ADMITS IT WHEN IT BIT.** Cap **1,000,000
  characters** — **8.9× the largest transcript measured** (112,531 over 8 turns). Over it, the
  response returns what fits and sets `truncated`; `total_turns` travels with every response so a
  truncated read can never read as a short deliberation (D55).
- **⚠ THE UNIT IS CHARACTERS AND THE FIELD NAMES SAY SO.** `content` is a JS string; `.length` is
  UTF-16 code units, not bytes. **Calling that "bytes" is the exact class of error F39's retraction
  cost a run to learn**, so `chars` / `cap_chars` it is.
- **The historical transcript is state of its own, never `messages`.** Loading it into the live
  array would push finished rows through the delta-append path whose `(member, phase, round)`
  identity is **F37's fix** — 291 fragments where 8 turns belonged. **F37's regression test is
  green and unedited**, and a new store test asserts the independence **in both directions**.
- **The toggle is the mock's:** `FINDINGS [ findings | transcript · N turns ]`, active segment
  raised, count carrying its noun, all 3c-1 tokens and no stock palette utility.
- **⚠ Task-3c-5's invariant 2 holds:** switching panes does **not** move the standing caveat out of
  view — it is rendered above and outside the toggled region, so no state of the toggle can hide it.
- **Runtime (G2), twice over.** The channel was proven end-to-end against 3e-1's run **before** the
  paid run — 7 of 7 turns, 93,868 chars, `truncated: false`, plain objects (D14) — and the toggle
  was then driven on **3e-2's own run**: 8 stored turns, rendered as turns with member, phase and
  round, live blocks untouched. **Cost $0.00; it reads runs already paid for.**
- **Retention: answered as D99** — kept indefinitely, with the growth arithmetic (**~38 MB/year** at
  a pessimistic 4 runs/week), the reason a purge is the wrong trade, and **stated revisit triggers**
  so the position is falsifiable rather than permanent.

#### ✅ Task 3e-3 — council time is task work (D95)

**One branch in `classify()`, one nullable field in `AttentionInputs`, one field on an existing
payload. No channel, no schema, no migration.**

- **⚠ A RESHAPE OF `attention:report`, NOT A NEW CHANNEL — DECLARED THE WAY D80 DECLARED ITS OWN.**
  `attentionReportSchema` gains `councilProjectId: z.uuid().nullable()`, **required rather than
  optional** (an absent field would let a pre-D95 renderer look valid while contributing no
  attribution). `IpcChannel` stays where 3e-4 left it at **58**, `ipcMain.handle(` **53 / 0**,
  `sqliteTable(` **16**, `MIGRATIONS.length` **12**, and **`attention_spans` gained no column** —
  D70's checkable fact, re-verified by dumping `pragma_table_info` before reasoning about it, which
  is the one thing D70's own flagged raise failed to do.
- **The branch sits AFTER every guard that describes whether the user is present at all and BEFORE
  the generic `overhead` fallback.** `locked`, `idle` and `blurred` still win: those describe the
  **human**, not the view, and a 21-minute run continues while its owner is at lunch. Placing it
  higher would credit a walk away. **Asserted by a test, not left to the comment.**
- **⚠ D70 IS AMENDED, NOT REVERTED, AND ITS PROPERTY IS NOW ASSERTED BY A TEST SO A LATER READER
  CANNOT QUIETLY LOSE IT.** A hypothetical future view (`'fleet-switcher'`) classifies as
  `overhead` **by construction** — and still does even when a project id is reported alongside it,
  because the branch requires **both** halves. A future view earns its way out of overhead by naming
  the project it works for; it does not get there by being added.
- **⚠ NO PROJECT → `overhead`, and the row still names the storage-level active project rather than
  inventing a council attribution.** This is the D76-shaped hazard the task warned about, and the
  test asserts the whole slot, not just the class.
- **⚠ THE HAZARD THAT WAS NOT IN THE SPEC, FOUND WHILE WIRING IT: A STALE SESSION ID.**
  `activeSessionId` is renderer-latched from `focusin`, and switching to the council view unmounts
  every `TerminalPane` **without necessarily firing another one** — so the last-focused session can
  still be reported while the council runs. Since the branch now returns `pane`, `slotFor`'s
  `cls === 'pane'` test would have attached that session and credited council time **to whichever
  terminal the user last touched**. Excluded explicitly, and asserted: **the most plausible-looking
  wrong number this function could have produced.**
- **The rule is enforced in `buildReport`, not trusted from the caller.** `App.vue` passes
  `projectStore.activeId` unconditionally — one primitive, no branch at the call site, D14-safe —
  and the reporter nulls it outside the council view. `shouldReport` compares it too, or a project
  switch **while sitting in the council view** would change no reported field, send nothing, and
  leave main crediting the previous project for the rest of the run.
- **RUNTIME (G2), BOTH DIRECTIONS, read from `attention_spans` in the real DB** with the window
  foregrounded and **`keybd_event` SHIFT pulses** (CDP keystrokes are invisible to
  `GetLastInputInfo`, so a CDP-only proof would have classified as `idle` and proved nothing):

  | Window | Class | Seconds | project | session |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | council + project selected | **`pane`** | 105 | `985d547b` | **NULL** |
  | council + no project | **`overhead`** | 90 | `985d547b` | NULL |

  **The `pane` row with a NULL session is the signature of council work** — a workspace pane row
  names its session. Under the old rule the first row would have been `overhead`. The bracketing
  `idle` and `blurred` 15-second rows are the pre-input tick and one instance of **focus theft**,
  which is itself evidence the presence guards still win.

#### ✅ Task 3e-1 — the two numbers this phase exists to obtain

Full record: [`Investigations/3e-1-Measurement.md`](Investigations/3e-1-Measurement.md). Run
`4c17069c` on `CouncilBrief-3b.0-ApiSessionProducer.md` **verbatim** (D98), frontier roster rebuilt
through the app's own channel with **all four ids re-checked against OpenRouter's live `/models`**
(339 returned, 2026-07-28).

- **VERDICT-TOKEN COMPLIANCE: 4 of 6 questions resolved STRUCTURALLY, 2 fell to `model-judged`.**
  **F38 measured the inverse** (4 of 6 falling through) on the cheap roster at the 700-token cap.
  **⚠ THE IMPROVEMENT IS NOT ATTRIBUTABLE TO ANY SINGLE DIAL, AND READING IT AS "THE TUNING FIXED
  IT" IS THE EXACT OVER-READ D67 FILED F38 TO PREVENT** — the roster, the ceiling, the mint cap and
  the timeout all moved between the two, and **this run had only 2 of 3 members answering**, so the
  structural arm had fewer verdicts to reconcile. **F38's compliance half is discharged as
  MEASURED, not as FIXED.**
- **⚠ F39 — THE FIRST READING WAS "PATHOLOGICAL" AND IT WAS WRONG. RETRACTED AND CORRECTED
  2026-07-28, BEFORE ANY CODE ACTED ON IT.** Kimi streamed **4,000,372** bytes against a largest
  completed turn of **692,858**, and that 5.8× was read as "unbounded". **⚠ THE COMPARISON WAS
  INVALID: IT COMPARED BYTES ACROSS MODELS WHOSE BYTES-PER-TOKEN DIFFER BY 20×.** Measured on this
  run's own rows — stream bytes ÷ `tokens_out` per turn:

  | Member | bytes / output token |
  |---|---|
  | CR Arbiter (opus-5) | **9.9 – 13.2** |
  | CR GLM (5.2) | **61.4 – 66.1** |
  | CR Qwen (3-coder) | **201.2 – 205.1** |

  **SSE framing overhead per token is a property of the MODEL's chunking, not of how much it
  said.** At Qwen's observed ratio a **16,000-token allowance is ~3.3 MB** — so kimi reaching 4 MB
  is fully consistent with it **spending the allowance Chorus gave it**, not with being unbounded.
  **The 4 MB byte cap and the 16,000-token allowance are mutually inconsistent for a verbosely
  framed reasoning model, and THAT is the defect** — a bound on bytes and a bound on tokens that
  were never reconciled with each other.
- **⚠ WHAT IS ACTUALLY ESTABLISHED, STATED NARROWLY:** kimi's bytes-per-token is **> 250** (it
  exceeded 4 MB inside a 16,000-token budget) and is **otherwise UNKNOWN**, because the capped turn
  reported no `usage` at all. **F39 is NOT resolved.** It is better understood and still open.
- **⚠ MATTHEW'S RULING, 2026-07-28: KIMI STAYS.** The roster is unchanged and no replacement member
  is needed — which also means D67 Q6's two-member floor is not being approached deliberately.
  `ImplementationSpec-3e-2.md` §3 already offers **"bound the member, or drop it"**; 3e-2 takes the
  bound. **The two candidate bounds, both to be decided on evidence rather than taste:** lower
  kimi's own `max_tokens` until its worst-case stream fits the existing cap (**touches no global
  constant**), or raise `RESPONSE_CAP_BYTES` on a **computed** basis — worst observed ratio (205)
  × the largest allowance (32,000, the arbiter) = **6.6 MB**, which would make the existing 8 MB
  `modelCatalog` cap the natural value and replace the current "half of 8 MB" relationship, which
  the constant's own comment shows was never computed either.
- **F39's second half REPRODUCED: the capped turn contributed NO `usage` block**, so **every cost
  figure from a run kimi participates in is a FLOOR.** Observed now, not inferred.
- **⚠ A DECISION FOR MATTHEW BEFORE 3e-2 CAN CLOSE F39:** dropping kimi leaves **two** deliberating
  members — **D67 Q6's floor with zero margin**. This run already demonstrates the exposure: it
  survived on 2 only because neither survivor failed. **If kimi is dropped the roster needs a
  replacement member, and choosing one is Matthew's call.**
- **Three of the four proofs Phase 3c-5 left UNPROVEN are discharged**: a real run streams into the
  restyled view · the findings `.md` landed beside the brief (**39,832 B**, ending cleanly) · **F37
  grouping holds — 7 blocks for 7 turns.** ⚠ **Esc-refuses-to-leave-mid-run stays UNPROVEN** and is
  owed by 3e-2's run.
- **⚠ F40 DID NOT REPRODUCE, AND THAT IS NOT EVIDENCE IT IS ABSENT.** The document carries exactly
  one `## Dissents preserved` — but this was a **partial** run whose arbiter may simply not have
  written the section, leaving the core's unconditional append nothing to duplicate. **3e-2 must
  re-check on a full run and must not read this as a fix.**

**⚠ THE ABORTED FIRST ATTEMPT COST $0.037 AND WAS WORTH MORE THAN THAT.** It died on D67 Q6's
two-member floor because `params_json` was written with **`max_completion_tokens`** — OpenRouter's
own API field name — while `resolveMaxOutputTokens` reads **`max_tokens`** and falls back
**silently** to `MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS_DEFAULT = 1200`. Two reasoning members spent the whole shrunken
budget on reasoning and returned **empty** content. **⚠ D71's row records the VALUES and not the
KEY, which is what made this mis-settable**, and `ImplementationSpec-3e-1.md` §2 carried the same
wrong key until a run disproved it. **Also learned: `params_json` is settable at CREATE ONLY** —
`updateCouncilMember` takes `{id, label}` — so fixing it meant deleting and recreating all four
members.

**Spend: `$0.037` + `$0.658` = `$0.695` of the ~$4.00 authorised.** ⚠ Both figures are Chorus's
own and are **floors**; neither was checked against OpenRouter's billing page.

#### Original scope note _(retained — see the Overview for how D95 and D97 widen it)_


**⚠ THIS PHASE EXISTS BECAUSE THE WORK HAD NO OWNER, AND THAT IS THE ENTIRE JUSTIFICATION FOR NUMBERING IT.** Phase 3b closed with a residual that neither 3c (design) nor 3d (adapters) touches, and the close record said in as many words that it "should be assigned deliberately rather than rediscovered the next time someone runs a real CR natively." Post-close tuning (**D71**) then discharged one half of it and **added two new findings**, which is evidence that the residual is live work rather than a filing artefact. **A named phase is the difference between a queue item and a thing that gets found again by accident.**

Scope is **tuning and measurement over a shipped, working feature** — no new milestone capability, no schema, and (with one exception below) no new surface:

- **⚠ THE HEADLINE ITEM IS A MEASUREMENT, NOT A FIX, AND IT MUST BE DONE FIRST: re-measure verdict-token compliance on the frontier roster.** F38 measured **4 of 6 questions falling to `model-judged`** rather than structurally measured — on the **cheap** roster, at the 700-token cap. **Nobody has re-measured it on D71's roster.** The document got dramatically better (F38's other half is discharged), and **reading that as evidence the structural arm now works is exactly the over-read D67 filed it as a not-checkable prediction to prevent.** The honest status is **unmeasured**. If it still fails, the fix is a prompt/detection question and belongs here; if it passes, this bullet closes on evidence rather than on hope.
- **F39 — one member refuses every run, and the fix depends on a fact nobody has established.** `moonshotai/kimi-k3` hit `RESPONSE_CAP_BYTES = 4_000_000` on **three consecutive runs**, contributes **no `usage` block** (so Chorus's own reported run cost **under-reports the truth** whenever it participates — narrowing F35), and leaves the council sitting on **exactly D67 Q6's two-member floor with zero margin**, where one more failure aborts the run. **⚠ Whether kimi is pathological or the 4 MB cap is simply too small for a model that streams its chain of thought is NOT ESTABLISHED, and the two answers have opposite fixes** (raise the cap vs drop the member). **Measure before touching the constant — a cap raised on a guess is how an unbounded stream gets re-authorised**, and D63(e) put that bound there on purpose.
- **F40 — the duplicated `## Dissents preserved` heading, whose obvious fix is forbidden.** The arbiter writes one section and the pure core appends one unconditionally. **The core's unconditional append is the whole enforceability argument** (D67 Q5 ruling 5C as corrected) and **must not be the thing removed** — deleting it hands dissent preservation back to the arbiter's goodwill, which is 5A, explicitly rejected. The fix lands on the **core's heading** or the **synthesis prompt**, never on the append.
- **The dissent matcher is noisy** — on the dogfood run **two of nine "dissents" say nothing challenges the synthesis**, because mechanical extraction from a `DISAGREE:` label cannot distinguish a non-objection from an objection; and **one talkative member produced six of the nine**. 3b-3 predicted both and left them rather than tuning them away.
- **F41 — decide whether council time is task work or overhead**, and record the answer as a numbered decision either way. **⚠ TIME-SENSITIVE for the same D50 reason as everything else in the telemetry story: actuals cannot be backfilled**, so this one is cheap now and unrecoverable later. If the answer is "task work", it is the only item here that touches a surface outside the council files.
- **⚠ `council_messages` IS A WRITE-ONLY STORE WITH NO READER AND NO RETENTION POLICY.** `deleteCouncilRun` has no caller and **`getCouncilMessagesForRun` has none either** — the view renders from the live broadcast, so 3b-2's prediction that the view would become the first reader **did not come true**. Runs now cost **~$0.83 and ~14 minutes** each (D71), which makes a transcript nobody can re-read a materially worse deal than it was at $0.0012. **Give it a reader or justify the absence in writing** — a store with no delete path is a decision, not an oversight, and it has now been inherited across three tasks.
- **Not in scope:** the deliberation protocol itself. D67 ruled it, CR-3b.1 is closed, and re-opening the protocol is a new CR (§4), not a tuning pass.

- **Sequencing:** after 3c, and **its position is deliberate rather than a default** — every item here is measurement or a constant, none of it blocks daily use of the app, and the one genuinely decaying item (**F41**) is a one-line classification decision that does not need a phase's worth of runway if it becomes urgent. **⚠ But the decay is real and should not be allowed to justify indefinite deferral twice** — that is precisely how it became a residual the first time.
- **⚠ Cost is a first-class constraint here, unlike every earlier phase.** At D71's dials a single full run is **~$0.83 pre-authorized against a $10 minted cap** and takes **~14 minutes**, so a measurement pass that re-runs the same brief four times is a **~$3.30, one-hour** exercise. **State the envelope before the first run and measure against it** (3b-1's standing lesson), and note that **F39 means the reported figure under-reports the truth whenever kimi participates** — so the envelope must be checked against OpenRouter's own billing page, not only against Chorus's number.
- **Milestone:** verdict-token compliance is **re-measured on the frontier roster and the number is written down** whatever it says; F39 is resolved by measurement rather than by guess; F40 and F41 are closed as numbered decisions; and the transcript store has either a reader or a written justification for not having one.

### Phase 3f — Council Cases, Exhibits & Docket — 🟡 **PARTIALLY DELIVERED — the DOCKET and the VERDICT STRIP landed 2026-08-04 (D112–D119); Cases and Exhibits remain DESIGNED AND NOT DECOMPOSED** **[CR-3f.0 CLOSED as D111 — verdict `REVISE`, adopted with five coordinator resolutions]**

> **⚠ THE HISTORY THIRD SHIPPED ALONE, AND THE SPLIT IS RECORDED AT D112 RATHER THAN LEFT AS DRIFT.** The entry below still describes the whole phase as designed on 2026-08-01; what follows is what is now TRUE of the code, so a reader does not take the scope section as a statement of what exists.
>
> **DELIVERED (D112–D115).** A per-project Docket, keyed on `project_id` and listing runs flat, as `CouncilView`'s landing surface. Three channels — `council:docket`, `council:findings`, `council:forget-run` — taking the map **60 → 63** (declared before the code landed per D74/D80, and **the running prose tally in `ipc.ts` was found stale at "58": 3c-2's four `window:*` channels landed after 3e-4 wrote that line and nobody moved it; `ipc.test.ts` had the right number all along**). `listCouncilRunsForProject` + a one-`GROUP BY` `getCouncilRunStats` (`countSessionsByProject`'s D80 precedent); a pure `councilDocketCore.ts`; **`deleteCouncilRun` finally has its caller** — the one D99 kept alive uncalled for exactly this — behind D109's count-stating confirm that leaves the findings document on disk. **No migration: v13 stays head and Phase 6's v14 is unaffected.**
>
> **THE VERDICT STRIP LANDED 2026-08-04 (D116–D119), AND IT RETRACTS A CLAIM MADE HERE ONE COMMIT EARLIER.** This entry previously said the per-question tally was discarded and that runs before the work *"lose their tally permanently."* **The members' half was never lost** — it re-derives from stored positions turns plus the brief, and run `c06874ad` from 2026-07-28 was recovered whole (D116). What genuinely cannot be recovered is the ARBITER's ruling for old runs, because those arbiters were never asked; the strip renders that as **not asked**, a third state distinct from `unparsed` (D117). Delivered: `parseArbiterVerdicts` + the five-state vocabulary, an additive arbitration-prompt change, `assembleVerdictStrip`/`digestFor`, `council:verdict` (**63 → 64**), the per-question strip in the run detail and a counts-not-rollup text line on Docket rows. **No migration — derived, not stored, which is what kept v14 free for `project_memory`.** **⚠ THE PROMPT CHANGE IS UNVALIDATED AGAINST A REAL COUNCIL and that was the accepted trade** (D117): the next natural run validates it, and non-compliance degrades to `unparsed` rather than costing a run.
>
> **NOT DELIVERED, AND STILL BOUND BY D103–D111.** Exhibits (D104) and everything CR-3f.0 ruled about bounding them, **including the byte budget D111(b) left open**. Cases in the repository, `case_id` identity, case grouping, the disk scan and watcher, re-run comparison, and the three-way *never-run-here / run-elsewhere / has-local-runs* states (D105, CR-3f.1's A1–A18). The mandatory template (D107). D106's **split-verdict referral** — a new case pre-filled with the disputed question and linked to its parent — which is blocked on Cases existing at all. D109's second action, "Delete case…", which has no folders to delete yet.
>
> **⚠ THE SEQUENCING COLLISION BELOW NO LONGER BINDS** — see D112. Nothing in the Docket changes the run mechanism, so Task 6-1's `[CR]` gate may fire whenever; the open question it framed (should 6-1 argue *with exhibits*) is untouched, because exhibits are exactly what this slice did not build.

Design settled in a working session with Matthew on **2026-08-01**, against the codebase at `5ba3b98` **plus an uncommitted working tree** (see §5's pass of the same date). **This entry records WHAT and WHY. The phase is NOT decomposed, no task doc exists, and nothing here is authoritative for execution** until its own `/phase-kickoff`. The settled design is carried by **D103–D110**; two questions were deliberately left open for the council rather than guessed at.

**The problem, in three parts.** The council can only reason about text pasted into a brief — **it cannot read the repository the decision is about**. Its runs are invisible once the window closes: `council_runs` has been accumulating rows since 3b-3 and nothing lists them. And its output is a prose document a reader must parse to learn what was actually decided. One feature, because the three share a data model.

#### ⚠ Most of the plumbing already exists and is inert — verified 2026-08-01, and it materially lowers this phase's cost

| Existing | State |
|---|---|
| `council_runs.project_id` | `schema.ts:477` — nullable column, **and `council:start` already carries it on the wire** |
| `listCouncilRuns()` | `storage.ts:1861` — newest-first, **no project filter and NO IPC channel**; the history is stored and unreachable |
| `deleteCouncilRun(id)` | `storage.ts:1883` — atomic, purges `council_messages` explicitly because SQLite will not cascade a soft pointer; **zero callers**, which **D99 made deliberate** |
| `disagreementVector` | `councilCore.ts:584` — per-question, per-member verdicts plus `disagrees` and `nonCompliant`, **computed on EVERY run to build the arbitration prompt and then thrown away** |
| `parseBriefQuestions` | `councilCore.ts:427` — enumerated questions are **already mandatory**; `assembleRun` refuses a case that has none |

**⚠ THE CONSENSUS HALF OF THE VERDICT STRIP IS THEREFORE A PLUMBING JOB, NOT A NEW CAPABILITY** — the split detector has been running on every paid council since 3b-3 and has never been shown to anyone. What is genuinely new is **exhibits**, the **Docket**, and a **structured outcome from the arbiter**.

#### Scope

- **Vocabulary (D103): Case · Docket · Findings · Verdict · Consensus.** "Brief" is retired for new work and kept for the ~15 existing `CouncilBrief-*.md` the roadmap links by name.
- **Exhibits (D104): the repository reaches the council as an identical, pre-resolved pack** — never by giving members retrieval tools. **Evidence parity is the point**, not a convenience: members that fetch different files produce disagreements that are artifacts of retrieval, and `disagreementVector` would then be measuring noise.
- **In-repo storage (D105):** `docs/council/<slug>/case.md` with `runs/<timestamp>/findings.md` beneath it, and a `case_id` UUID in the case's own frontmatter as the identity that survives a rename or a clone.
- **The Verdict strip (D106):** the arbiter emits a structured five-state outcome per question; the member tally supplies the consensus badge. Two facts, two sources, **and the strip must render `n of m answered` rather than compute a majority from partial data** — `qwen3-coder` emitted no verdict token on any of six questions in 3e-2's run.
- **A mandatory template (D107):** headings enforced, content inside them free-form. A case missing a required heading **refuses the run**.
- **The Docket:** per-project history, case-grouped, with the re-run comparison first-class (D98's re-measurement is the use case). Two delete actions, stated plainly (D109).
- **Not in scope:** the deliberation protocol itself (D67 ruled it; re-opening it is a new CR), an in-app markdown editor (D108's authoring flow deliberately avoids the dependency question), and any change to what a council member *is* — the roster, the vault, and the transport are untouched.

#### ✅ CR-3f.0 — fired and closed 2026-08-01 as **D111**, verdict `REVISE`

**The council endorsed the design and refused to let it ship unbounded, which is what the gate was for.** Evidence parity (Q6) and whole-run refusal on a secret match (Q2) are adopted as filed; git-tracked eligibility survives as the **primary gate but not a security boundary**; and **P1, a preflight exfiltration manifest, is adopted — an item nobody in the design session proposed.** Five coordinator resolutions are recorded at D111, and **two of them change work**: the council's primary Q5 remedy ("retain round-0 text in context") **rests on a false premise about the transport** — every turn is a fresh disposed session (`councilService.ts:866`), so its own fallback, deterministic re-injection of cited ranges, is what is adopted; and the **100 KiB pack cap is too small against measured file sizes** in this repository (`src/shared/ipc.ts` alone is **97.4 KiB**) and is **suspected of anchoring on the harness's own `Max code chars 100,000` setting**. **⚠ THE BYTE BUDGET IS THEREFORE STILL OPEN and is the first thing that should be re-asked — in a case run WITH exhibits**, which is the one condition this council could not have.

#### ⚠ Why the gate was not a formality

**CR-3f.0 asks how the exhibit pack is bounded and confined.** Exhibits send repository bytes to four third-party APIs, deliberately, in an app whose vault contract (**D33**) is scrub-on-ingest and whose bright line (**D49**, restated as **D93**) is that no secret value reaches a file Chorus does not own. **This is the first feature in the repo that exfiltrates project source by design**, and `npm run grep:secrets` does not cover a prompt in flight. The two numbers left open above — **the exhibit byte budget and the cost-gate threshold** — are put to the council rather than picked, because both are bounds on spend and blast radius and neither has an obvious right answer.

- **[CR] question — ANSWERED as D111:** what deny-list, confinement rule and byte bound make an exhibit pack safe to send, and what must happen when a scrub matches? **Answered: layered eligibility (git-tracked primary + preflight scanning), whole-run refusal on a match, and a preflight manifest. The byte bound is the one part that came back unsatisfactory** — see D111 resolution (b).
- **⚠ AND THE RECURSION WAS REAL, WAS DECLARED IN THE CASE, AND IT IS EXACTLY WHERE THE COUNCIL WENT WRONG.** The case stated up front that this council reasoned about repository access **without having any**. Resolution (a) is the consequence: a remedy that reads as obviously correct and that the architecture cannot provide. **The limit was predicted in writing and then observed — which is the most useful thing this run produced.**

#### Sequencing — and it collides with Phase 6

**⚠ PHASE 6 IS KICKED OFF AND ITS OWN `[CR]` GATE IS UNFIRED AND BLOCKING (Task 6-1, G5).** Two orderings are defensible and **the choice is Matthew's, not this document's**: fire 6-1's council on the current mechanism and keep Phase 6 moving, or build 3f first so that 6-1's memory-schema gate — a question that is *about the codebase* — is argued with exhibits rather than with pasted excerpts. **What is NOT defensible is discovering the collision mid-phase**, which is why it is written here.

- **Cost is a first-class constraint, per Phase 3e's standing lesson.** A full four-member run measured **$1.089 and 21 minutes** (3e-2), and **exhibits multiply the input side of that** — the case text is re-sent on every round (`councilCore.ts:986`, `:1015`), so an unbounded pack is a multiplier on the largest cost in the phase. D104's round-0-only rule and the pre-run estimate exist for that reason.
- **Milestone:** a council convened from a project reads that project's files as exhibits every member saw identically; its Docket lists every run for that project with a per-question Verdict and Consensus at a glance; a split question offers a focused referral; and a case deleted from the Docket says exactly what it deleted.

### Phase 3g — Reasoning-Token Spend — ⚠ **NOT A PHASE. THE LETTER IS CLAIMED BY A COUNCIL BRIEF THAT HAS NO ROADMAP ENTRY.**

**This heading exists so the gap between 3f and 3h is not read as a lost phase.** [`CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-3g.0-ReasoningSpend.md`](CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-3g.0-ReasoningSpend.md) and its [`-Findings.md`](CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-3g.0-ReasoningSpend-Findings.md) were issued **2026-08-07** and numbered `3g.0`, which took the letter; they were **untracked until `663ad35` (2026-08-10)**, so for three days the only record of the claim lived on one machine. `Tasks/Phase-3h-Overview.md` documents the same reasoning from the other side — it took `3h` precisely because `3g` was already spoken for.

**Status: deliberated, never decomposed, never scheduled.** A council of three members plus an arbiter answered two questions — whether Chorus should cap reasoning tokens when dispatching council members, and how reasoning spend should be surfaced. **All three members returned `QUALIFY` on Q1** (cap via `reasoning.max_tokens`, preserve a visible-output reserve, per-member settings inheriting a run default; **do not assume `exclude` saves cost without verifying route semantics**) and agreed on Q2 that reasoning tokens must be shown **separately** from visible output and only when honestly sourced — D55's rule arriving independently. Two members also flagged that **empty visible output with positive usage is a truncation, not a refusal**, which is a live misclassification in the council code if true.

**⚠ NOTHING HERE IS VERIFIED FACT** — the findings carry the standing warning that no member could see the repository, and this project has already shipped four compile errors behind a unanimous council. **Whoever picks this up owes it a grounding pass first.** If it becomes real work it needs a proper entry, a decomposition, and its own gate; until then the letter is reserved and the deliberation is on disk.

### Phase 3h — Project Lifecycle & Rail Order — ✅ **COMPLETE (2026-08-07)** — three tasks, three commits, **$0.00**

_Kicked off and executed 2026-08-07 against `78ef546`. **No `[CR]` gate: G5 was assessed and deliberately not triggered** — the phase makes no security and no protocol decision; the schema change is three columns on an existing table under a settled nullable ruling, and the destructive path is bounded by D121/D124 to rows Chorus itself wrote. Recorded so the absence reads as a judgement rather than an omission._

> **⚠ WHY `3h` AND NOT `3g`.** There is no Phase 3g in this roadmap and this entry does not create one. The letter was taken by `CouncilBriefs/CouncilBrief-3g.0-ReasoningSpend.md` and its `-Findings.md` (issued 2026-08-07, another workstream's, untracked at the time). **`3h` was the next free letter** — noted so a later reader does not read the gap as a lost phase.
>
> **⚠ AND THE COUNTS THIS DOCUMENT CARRIED WHEN THE PHASE STARTED WERE TEN COMMITS STALE.** §5's last measurement was 2026-08-01 at `5ba3b98` against a dirty working tree, and every number in it had moved by `78ef546`: `IpcChannel` **59 → 64**, `ipcMain.handle(` **54/0 → 58/0**, `MIGRATIONS.length` **13 → 14** (`sqliteTable(` was unchanged at 16). The phase was authored against the CODE and re-measured every figure before its first edit. **The corrected baseline is written into the table below** so the next reader inherits a true one.

**What the rail could do before this phase: add a project, and select one.** Every project was always shown, ordered by `created_at ASC`, with no way to retire a finished one, tuck one out of sight, remove one added by mistake, or change the order.

| Task | Delivered | Commit |
|---|---|---|
| **3h-1** Schema v15, the status vocabulary, the colour decoupling | Migration **v15** (`status`, `sort_order`, `color_seed`, back-fill, `projects_sort`) + the Drizzle mirror; `ProjectStatus` in `shared/ipc.ts`; `projectSchema` gains `status` + `color_seed`; **both** `projectChip.ts` functions re-parameterised to `seed`; `projectIndex` deleted; `getProjectByRootPath` + the boot-seed fix; `projectChip.test.ts` (the module had none) | `9ac5059` |
| **3h-2** The lifecycle, end to end | The four D125 channels + both tripwire edits; `deleteProject`'s ordered purge; archive semantics; `requireLaunchableProject` on four call sites; `projectLifecycleCore.ts`; `shared/projectLifecycle.ts`; `renderer/projectRail.ts`; the rail disclosure, the settings section, the typed-name delete | `882dec3` |
| **3h-3** Drag and Alt+Arrow reorder | Pointer-event drag on a 14px sibling handle (4px threshold, transforms only, 24px auto-scroll, Escape/`pointercancel` abort, `prefers-reduced-motion` on the displaced rows only, disabled collapsed and in the disclosure); Alt+↑/↓ with focus survival; `project:reorder`'s first caller | _(this commit)_ |

**The tally, at the end of each task — the D125 declaration, met exactly:**

| Gate | Baseline `78ef546` | 3h-1 | 3h-2 | 3h-3 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| vitest | 1219 / 34 files | **1229 / 35** | **1275 / 38** | **1275 / 38** |
| `IpcChannel` keys | 64 | **64** | **68** | **68** |
| `ipcMain.handle(` — `ipc.ts` / `index.ts` | 58 / 0 | **58 / 0** | **62 / 0** | **62 / 0** |
| `MIGRATIONS.length` | 14 | **15** | **15** | **15** |
| `sqliteTable(` | 16 | **16** | **16** | **16** |
| Runtime dependencies | 7 | **7** | **7** | **7** |

**⚠ THE COLOUR DECOUPLING IS WHY TASK 1 HAD TO LAND ALONE, AND IT IS THE PHASE'S ONE INVISIBLE DELIVERABLE.** A project with `color === null` (every project older than v13) was drawn from its **position in the rail** — and `resolveChipHex`, which the settings picker opens on, cycled the same array the same way. **The first time the rail partitions or reorders, that index is a position in a SUB-ARRAY and every legacy project repaints.** `color_seed` freezes each project's migration-time index onto its row. **Verified as strongly as it can be: the rail was photographed before the migration and after it, and 142,800 pixels of the rail region differ by ZERO** — plus every project's `color_seed` matched its pre-migration DOM index exactly, so the same-millisecond tie-break the migration comment warns about did not fire on this database. Then proven under load: hiding one project moved `Bryk` from rail position 2 to position 1 **and it stayed blue**, where the old cycle would have repainted it sand; after two drags reversed the rail, `sort_order` read 0,1,2,3 across `color_seed` 3,1,2,0.

**⚠ EVERY RUNTIME OBLIGATION WAS RUN, AND THE DESTRUCTIVE ONES RAN AGAINST A THROWAWAY `--user-data-dir`** seeded from the dev database — vitest is `environment: 'node'`, **cannot import `storage.ts`** (better-sqlite3 is built for the Electron ABI; a plain `node` `new Database(':memory:')` still fails to load the binding at `78ef546`), and there are **no `.vue` tests in this repo at all**, so everything this phase does to the database and to the rail is reachable **only** through the running app. What was actually observed: archive stopped a live PTY and healed its row, and unarchive + select did **not** relaunch it; deleting a project whose worktree pointed at its own session threw **no** `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_FOREIGNKEY` and left **0 rows in all seven tables**; `SELECT key FROM settings WHERE key LIKE '%<deleted-id>%'` returned **zero rows**; re-adding an archived folder returned `reactivated_from='archived'` **on the same row**, while a restart with that project archived did **not** reactivate it; an archived project refused `project:select`, `session:launch`, `session:launch-context` **and `council:start`** while a hidden one passed all four; the confirmation's counts matched SQL exactly (1 session, 2 worktrees, 6 council runs, 41 transcript turns); and the drag was exercised end to end — sub-threshold move wrote nothing, Escape-cancel wrote nothing, auto-scroll ran to each edge and stopped, the order survived a restart, and focus survived every Alt+Arrow.

**Three defects were found by running it and fixed in the same phase**, none of which any test could have caught: the sticky Save row covered the **Delete permanently** button at full scroll; the store's `projects[0]` fallback made an **archived** project locally active when every project was archived; and an unclamped drag transform auto-scrolled without bound (**F44**).

**Findings opened: F43** (the attention tracker kept crediting a deleted project on every tick — deliberately left open by the phase, then **fixed the same day at Matthew's request**, with 9 mutation-checked unit tests), **F44** (the transform/overflow auto-scroll runaway, fixed), **F45** (`reactivated_from` is one payload field beyond the phase's stated budget — a declared deviation, and the field was also DEAD; **resolved the same day at Matthew's request** by wiring it to a tested sentence, keeping a reactivated project out of the create flow, and narrowing its type to the two states it can actually carry), **F46** (`getOrCreateProject` could duplicate a `sort_order` after a delete — **fixed the same day at Matthew's request**, `MAX(sort_order) + 1`), **F47** (pre-existing: session rows naming projects that do not exist — **22, not 19, plus a third orphaned id in a soft-pointer table; resolved the same day at Matthew's request** by adopting the recoverable history into the project that shares its `root_path`, deleting the rest, and adding a boot-time orphan check that covers the soft pointers `foreign_key_check` cannot see).

**Milestone (met):** a project can be **hidden, archived, unhidden, unarchived, deleted and reordered**; an archived project **cannot be selected, launched into, or made the subject of a council**; the order **survives a restart**; a delete **states its counts before it happens and names what survives**; and **not one chip changed colour**.
