### Phase 4 — Notifications — 🟡 **IN PROGRESS: THE SPINE LANDED EARLY (2026-08-07, D129/D130); DECOMPOSED INTO FOUR TASKS 2026-08-13; TASK 4-1 OF 4 LANDED 2026-08-13** **[CR: localhost hook listener security — ✅ CLOSED as D130]**

- **✅ TASK 4-1 — THE REASON ON THE LIVE RECORD — LANDED 2026-08-13 (`f0cea8f`, then `8cbd37c`).** The live activity record, its listener, `snapshot()` and `sessionActivityEventSchema` all carry a `reason` (`NeedsYouReason | null`); `NEEDS_YOU_EVENTS` became a name→reason map; **`record()`'s edge trigger was WIDENED to fire on either fact and `since` is re-stamped ONLY when the activity changes** — a session that stopped and then asked a question has been waiting since it *stopped*. **No channel (`IpcChannel` still 86), no migration, no renderer file, and nothing renders the field yet** — that is 4-2's job. **⚠ THE TASK TOUCHED THE HOOK SPINE, WHICH TWO PHASES HAD KEPT BYTE-IDENTICAL ON PURPOSE**, which is why it shipped alone: a revert costs one commit. Verified by driving a **real** `claude` pane to a genuine permission prompt in a throwaway `--user-data-dir` — `permission` → `working` → `stopped` all observed on the wire, the cold read agreeing with the live stream, and a **negative control** (guard inverted) confirming the wait's age resets by ~6 s without it. **Two findings came out of running it: F68** (an unattributed single-test failure) and **F69** (`Notification` fires mid-block), the latter resolved the same day by **D146**.
- **⏸ DEFERRED BEHIND PHASE 5 (D154, 2026-08-16) — NOT DROPPED.** ~~**▶ NEXT: TASK 4-2**~~ D147(g) queued this phase's remaining three tasks immediately after Phase 6a; **Matthew ruled that Phase 5 — Voice Input goes next instead.** 4-2 / 4-3 / 4-4 keep their authored task docs, their specs, and 4-2's execution prompt.
- **⚠ WHEN 4-2 IS PICKED UP, ITS *Initial Starting Point* TABLE IS RE-MEASURED WHOLESALE — G6 IN FULL, AND THIS IS NOW MANDATORY RATHER THAN ADVISORY.** The prompt was authored when `IpcChannel` was **86** and states 86 → 88; it is **97 at `4369954`**, and **Phase 5 will move it again before this task is reached**, so by then the figure will be stale by **two phases**. Every line number in it is likewise a snapshot: the `ipc.test.ts` assertions have already walked **3438/3816 → 3442/3820 → 3462/3840**, three positions for a value that changed once. **Re-count from the merged tree; never delta, never seek to a remembered line.**

- **✅ DECOMPOSED 2026-08-13 against `00f0f0d`, and the phase's one open scope question was SETTLED FIRST rather than carried into the tasks.** `Tasks/Phase-4-Overview.md` plus `Task-4-1..4-4.md` and their four implementation specs. **The question was whether the `agent_events` bus is still wanted; the answer is D145 — it stays unbuilt, and `agent_turns` is NOT the reason.** Four consumers of the spine have arrived and **not one reads history**; `agent_turns`' own reader does not exist either (`readAgentTurns` has zero callers outside `storage.ts`); and the bus would carry event **names** while the Attention Inbox mock draws event **bodies** that D130 refuses to take — so the table would have been built, migrated, and still left the headline surface undrawable. **What is actually missing is the REASON on the current state**, which `docs/PLAN.md:184` has named as two states since v2 and which `classifyHookEvent` collapses six events into one. **⚠ AND A NEW FINDING CAME OUT OF ASKING: F67** — D39's sub-agent count is unobservable for F56's exact structural reason, and a bus fed from `onActivity` would inherit the blindness, so **the vehicle D39 names cannot carry it.** The timeline sidebar and D39 are deferred **with** the bus and are what would reopen it.
- **The four tasks, and the ordering is the answer to `ToastEnabled=0`:** **4-1** the `reason` on the live record (no channel, no migration — it lands alone so a revert costs one commit); **4-2** the Attention Inbox, cross-project, oldest-first, `Enter` focuses the real pane (`IpcChannel` 86 → 88); **4-3** the notification policy — PLAN.md:200's defaults as a pure module, **no settings UI** — plus the in-app centre it feeds (88 → 91); **4-4** OS delivery last, because **half of it cannot be seen working on this machine.** **⚠ THE INBOX SHIPS WITHOUT THE MOCK'S PREVIEW PANEL, AND THAT IS D83 APPLIED RATHER THAN A SHORTFALL:** the preview is not omitted for lack of a source — the pane's own post-scrub ring buffer holds the text and needs **no D130 widening at all** — but **F58 measured those buffers to be TUI repaint streams rather than transcripts** (44,958 non-blank lines, **14 unique**, 3,211× duplication), so a preview built on one is unproven, and answering a prompt you cannot see is worse than not offering to. A later phase owes it a measured spike, not a table.
- **Two rules the decomposition found in the code and wrote into the tasks rather than leaving to be rediscovered:** the policy's *"never for the focused pane"* must go through **`attentionCore.classify()`**, never `viewStore.focusedSessionId` — `reporter.ts:11` lists three verified reasons the latter is *"CONFIDENTLY WRONG and therefore worse than missing"*, chiefly that **grid mode never updates it**; and the notification policy must **refuse to treat a NULL exit code as a failure**, which is `attentionRollup.ts:143`'s bug verbatim — all five of `restore()`'s heal paths write `('exited', row.exitCode ?? null)`, and reading `null !== 0` as failure once lit three of four projects red on a cold start with nothing crashed. **Here it would fire a burst of false failure notifications on every boot.**

Hook listener + hook injection into `.claude/settings.json`; append-only `agent_events` bus; notification policies; toast → focus-pane; tray badge; notification center; Attention Inbox; per-session event timeline sidebar; sub-agent lifecycle surfaced as read-only awareness (D39).

- **⚠ WHY PART OF THIS PHASE RAN EARLY, AND OUT OF ORDER.** Matthew reported the filmstrip's activity lights *"all green...all the time"* — which turned out to be **two defects, not one** (D129): the lights were **frozen** (a real bug, no phase gate needed), and amber had **no source** (D78, parked here). Asked which way to take it, he chose *"Build the Phase 4 hook listener now"* over deriving amber from PTY-output silence. So the **spine** was built for its first consumer, the lights — deliberately, and no further.
- **✅ BUILT:** the localhost listener (`services/agentEvents.ts`), its pure classification core (`agentEventsCore.ts`, unit-tested), per-session capability tokens, hook injection via **`--settings`** (NOT `.claude/settings.json` — see D130), the `claude` adapter's `hooks` descriptor and `writeHooksConfig` (**the first non-null extension descriptor any adapter carries**), and two channels — `session:activity` + `session:activity-list` (**`IpcChannel` 68 → 70**).
- **✅ THE SPINE GREW A SECOND CONSUMER, 2026-08-09 (`adbf0e9`): the PROJECT RAIL now carries one light per project, rolled up in main.** The filmstrip lights D129 built answer *"which agent needs me"* only for the project you are already looking at; the renderer holds session rows for the **active project only** and structurally cannot derive the rest. `services/attentionRollup.ts` is the join — pure, inputs passed in, unit-tested without an Electron host — over three sources that each know something the others do not: the persisted rows (the only source surviving a restart), main's in-memory hook activity (the only source that can say `needs-you`), and in-memory exit instants. **Amber escalates on a four-rung ladder and red deliberately does not** — quiet under 30s because a stop may resolve itself, pulsing to 20m, then quiet again because a light ignored for twenty minutes has become noise; an error appears, holds, and never competes with the pulse. **⚠ NO MIGRATION WAS TAKEN FOR IT, DELIBERATELY:** `sessions` records THAT a session exited and never WHEN, and the honest cost of not adding an `exited_at` column is that an error predating this run reports a null age and renders calm — which is true, since nobody was watching. **⚠ AND THE PUSH IS DEFERRED ONE TURN OF THE EVENT LOOP, WHICH IS A CORRECTNESS FIX RATHER THAN A THROTTLE** — `SessionManager`'s exit fan-out is a Set whose order its own source says is *"not contractual"*, and the listener persisting `status='exited'` is registered after `registerIpc`, so rolling up inline reads a table that has not caught up, produces an empty list, matches the last-sent value and pushes nothing. **Verified on a running build against a copy of the dev database (G2), not reasoned:** all four rungs render as specified, red never climbs, absence clears the marker, the marker survives rail collapse at the same size, and a real `session:delete` produced exactly one coalesced push with the error count going 12 → 11. Channels `project:attention` + `project:attention-list` (the tally's 84 → 86).
- **❌ NOT BUILT, AND NOT STARTED:** notification policies, toast → focus-pane, tray badge, notification center, Attention Inbox, the per-session event timeline, and D39's sub-agent awareness. **⚠ THERE IS ALSO NO `agent_events` TABLE.** Events are held **in memory only**, because the lights need the CURRENT state and nothing yet reads history — an append-only bus with a migration behind it is a schema commitment made for nobody. **The bus stays this phase's to add when a consumer exists.** _(⚠ SUPERSEDED 2026-08-13 by **D145**, and the sentence above is kept because its reasoning was right and its prediction was wrong: consumers did arrive — four of them — and **none of them reads history**, so the trigger this sentence set was never actually pulled. The bus is now deferred on a **ruling**, not on a consumer count: it returns only with the per-session event timeline, D39 (**F67**), or a widening of D130.)_
- **⚠ ONE PRODUCER, NOT FOUR.** Only `claude` has a hook bus this app has verified. **codex, kimi and opencode keep three states**, asserted per adapter in `adapters.test.ts` so a second producer is a decision someone makes rather than a matcher quietly widening.
- **Note:** OS toast delivery is already **proven blocked** by the dev machine's `ToastEnabled=0` — the **in-app notification center must be first-class**, not an afterthought. *(Re-confirmed 2026-08-07: the exit toasts fired during D129's verification and every one failed with `HRESULT: -2143420140`.)*
- **Sub-agent awareness (D39):** the `agent_events` bus + per-session event timeline surface sub-agent lifecycle (e.g. Claude Code's Task tool) as **read-only** awareness — an "N sub-agents running / done" indicator on the parent pane and per-child entries in the timeline. In-process children are **not** separate sessions and get **no** per-child stop/interact; real per-agent isolation stays the launch-a-sibling-session path. Not a new process model — it rides the hook bus this phase already builds. Exact CLI hook event names verified at build time (D4).
- **[CR] question:** how do we secure the localhost hook listener against local processes spoofing agent events?
- **Milestone:** agent attention events reliably surface in-app regardless of OS toast state.

### Phase 4a — Session Continuity — ✅ **COMPLETE 2026-08-13** _(kicked off 2026-08-12 against `82e16d7`; all four tasks landed: 4a-1 + 4a-4 `bbf6d32`, 4a-4's visibility half `a6fab79`, 4a-2 `9e3c83d`, 4a-3 `756066c`)_ **[CR-4a.0 CLOSED as D139 — all seven questions APPROVED, adopted with six coordinator amendments at D143, ALL SIX now DISCHARGED]**

_Kicked off 2026-08-12 against `82e16d7`, from a sentence of Matthew's: **"If my PC reboots at night, or I have to shut Chorus down to install a new version, then I lose all my sessions."**_

- **⚠ HALF OF THAT SENTENCE WAS ALREADY FALSE, AND ESTABLISHING WHICH HALF IS WHAT MADE THE PHASE SMALL ENOUGH TO BUILD.** Session **rows** persist, the **layout** persists, and **`restore()` already relaunches** — the D16 contract has been working since Task 1-5 and was re-read rather than re-assumed. What comes back is a **pane in the right place, running the right agent, in the right directory**. **What does NOT come back is the CONVERSATION**, and that is the expensive half — it is the half the user paid tokens for. The roadmap already said so in its own words, in the Phase 8 note: *"a restored session is a genuinely **fresh conversation**, not a resumed one — the id survives, the context does not."* This phase exists to delete that sentence.
- **⚠ TWO DISTINCT LOSSES, TWO DIFFERENT MECHANISMS, AND CONFLATING THEM IS HOW THIS BECOMES AN UNBUILDABLE PHASE.** **(1) The AGENT'S OWN MEMORY** — Claude Code's and codex's own on-disk conversation — recoverable only by handing the CLI back its own session id, which is a **pointer** problem (Tasks 4a-1, 4a-2, 4a-3). **(2) WHAT THE USER COULD SEE** — the terminal scrollback, which no CLI will ever give back and which Chorus must therefore have kept for itself, a **mirror** problem (Task 4a-4). They share a phase and nothing else. **The organising decision is to store the POINTER, not the conversation:** both CLIs already write their own transcripts, crash-safely, in formats Chorus does not own and cannot replay into them anyway.
- **✅ LANDED — 4a-1: the pointer's HOME, deliberately DORMANT.** Migration **v19**, `ALTER TABLE sessions ADD COLUMN agent_session_id TEXT` — **nullable, no default, no FK, no index**, mirrored in Drizzle at `schema.ts:129`, with three accessors on `StorageService` (`setAgentSessionId` / `clearAgentSessionId` / `getAgentSessionId`, see **D142**). **⚠ THE COLUMN HAS ZERO READERS OUTSIDE `schema.ts`, `storage.ts` AND ONE TEST — grep-proven, and reverting the commit breaks nothing.** That is the deliverable, not a shortfall: the schema position is claimed while the contract above it (**D139**) is still in council, so the blocked half does not also block the migration. **G6 was run as a procedure, both halves** — the `MIGRATIONS` array **parsed** to 18 on `main` and `origin/main` (parsed, **not** grepped: a backtick grep miscounts SQL containing backticks), every sibling branch and the live sibling worktree parsed to **18/17/15/15/12/12/4**, and the dev DB's own `SELECT MAX(version)` read **18** with versions **1..18 contiguous**. Both agreed; `v19` was taken.
- **✅ AND IT WAS PROVEN ON A COPY, NEVER ON THE REAL STORE.** A three-dump protocol against a copy of the dev database in a throwaway `--user-data-dir`: **pre** (13 columns, MAX 18, 17 rows) → **post-boot** (14 columns, `agent_session_id TEXT notnull=0 dflt=NULL`, MAX 19, 0 rows non-NULL) → **post-restart** (**byte-identical** to post apart from its header line, i.e. the migration is idempotent in fact rather than by inspection). Acceptance was then driven through the **real `StorageService`** — esbuild-bundled and run under Electron's ABI, since vitest is `environment: 'node'` and cannot import it: `getSessionsForProject` rows carry `agentSessionId` (all null), and set → read-back → clear → unknown-id-is-null all round-trip. **The installed app's database (`%APPDATA%\chorus-app`) was never opened.**
- **✅ LANDED — 4a-4: the scrollback mirror, riding ONE existing string.** `scrollbackCore.ts` (pure: `capTail`, `planReplay`, `exceedsSlack`, plus `stripAltScreen`/`replayEpilogue` from the F58 fix) and `scrollbackStore.ts` (impure: `append`/`readTail`/`remove`/`pruneOrphans`/`whenIdle`), writing `${sessionId}.log` under `join(app.getPath('userData'), 'scrollback')`. **⚠ THE DISK WRITE IS THE THIRD CONSUMER OF THE ONE STRING `emit` HAS ALREADY COMPUTED** (invariant 1 / D45(1) / F26) — **one** optional `onPersist` option, **one** call site inside `emit`, no second `scrubber.push()` and no tap on raw PTY bytes. It runs **BEFORE** the broadcast, and the ordering is a contract rather than a preference: **the store is contractually non-throwing and the listener set is not.** Cap **4,000,000 chars == `BUFFER_MAX_CHARS`**, asserted equal by a test that reads `sessionManager.ts`'s own literal so the two cannot drift apart silently. Path traversal is refused by a **positive allow-list** — `/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,128}$/`, which makes `..` **unrepresentable rather than filtered** — and it is **currently unreachable** because ids are `randomUUID()`, **which is exactly why it is written down**. History is composed in **`snapshot()`**, not seeded into the ring buffer, so **it never re-enters the emit path**: not re-broadcast, not re-persisted.
- **✅ THE RUNTIME EVIDENCE, ALL DRIVEN ON THE REAL APP AGAINST A THROWAWAY DB COPY — this is the half no test in this repo can reach.** **Redaction A/B:** a codex session launched on a **genuinely stored** credential (safeStorage/DPAPI), the key typed **one character at a time** via `session:write`; the pane showed `[REDACTED-CREDENTIAL]` (control passed) and the mirror showed **0 hits for the full value and 0 for a 16-character fragment, across every file in the directory**. **Restart:** marker present after a full quit/reopen, buffer **30,024 chars**. **No duplication:** marker count **5 → 5 → 5** across three boots while the file grew **14,280 → 32,590 → 44,696 bytes** by new output only. **Cap driven past 4M live** via roughly **11,700 terminal resizes**: **7 rewrites** observed, **max 4,984,070 chars**, never over the 5,000,000 slack threshold, **min 4,009,886**, tail preserved, **no `.tmp` debris**. **Pane close:** mirror deleted, confirmed by path. **Orphan sweep:** a row deleted with the app closed, and the next boot logged `[scrollback] swept 1 orphan mirror file(s)`. **Responsiveness under the worst load the feature can create** — mirror at ~4.8M chars, 4 MB rewrites firing, ~100 KB/s of output — **typing round-trip p50 0.2 ms · p90 0.4 ms · p99 2.3 ms · max 3.3 ms, zero console exceptions.**
- **✅ AND THE FEATURE WAS NOT DONE WHEN THE BYTES WERE ON DISK — F58 IS THE REST OF THE PHASE'S LANDED HALF.** After `bbf6d32` the mirror was correct and **a restored pane still looked empty**, for two different reasons: codex emits `ESC[2J` and never uses the alternate screen (**4 × `ESC[2J`, ZERO `?1049h`/`?1047`/`?47`**), and Claude Code **does** (`?1049h` ×2). **The obvious fix was measured and fails twice: the escapes ARE the layout** (stripping them produced `Claude Codev2.1.229`), **and a TUI mirror is a REPAINT STREAM, NOT A TRANSCRIPT** — **44,958 non-blank lines, 14 unique, 3,211× duplication** on one file, **21 / 13, 100% chrome** on another. `a6fab79` keeps **every** escape, drops **only** the alternate-screen switches from the seed, and appends `ESC[r ESC[m ESC[999;1H` + 40 CRLFs so the painted screen is **scrolled up into scrollback before the fresh TUI's `ESC[2J` arrives** — `ESC[2J` clears the viewport, never the scrollback. **Result, per agent: codex history is VISIBLE by scrolling up, including text typed before the quit; Claude Code's is preserved on the normal buffer** (`seedEpiloguePresent=true`, `altEnterBeforeEpilogue=0`, `altEnterTotal=1` — the one entry is the **new** pty's own) **and unreachable only while the TUI holds the alternate screen, exactly as a terminal behaves while you are in vim.** A session with no mirror seeds nothing, so a brand-new pane is **byte-identical to before**.
- **✅ UNBLOCKED 2026-08-13 — CR-4a.0 ruled, and the contract is now written down.** The blocker was never a bug: the declared `ResumeSpec` is `{sessionId, cwd}` (`adapters/types.ts:351`; the `SupportsResume` capability at `:574`) and cannot carry the **credential, route, effort, `extraArgs` or hooks** that `buildLaunch` receives today (`sessionManager.ts:619`), so implementing it as declared would have **forked the launch path in two and guaranteed drift** — including about credential injection, which D33 owns. **The council approved the single-launch-path shape** (`PtyLaunchSpec.resume`, `ResumeSpec` and `resumeSession()` deleted, argv grammar kept inside each adapter's own `buildLaunch`), **and added one thing the brief had missed: the modifier must model ASSIGNED CREATION as well as resumption**, because Claude needs the minted id on its first launch and not only on a restore. See **D139** for the ruling and **D143** for the six coordinator amendments that condition it — chiefly that the failure classifier must read **post-scrub** text (D45(1)/F26), that the automatic relaunch must not fire the five-plus exit listeners as a real failure, and that the assigned pointer is persisted **after** the spawn succeeds rather than before (D16 resolution a). **Of the six amendments, (a) the post-scrub classifier input, (d) the interactive `--session-id` re-verification, (e) the never-bare `--resume` guard and (f) the wire-schema `kind` are DISCHARGED by 4a-2. (b) the exit-listener storm, (c) persist-the-pointer-AFTER-the-spawn, and (g) D142's restart half remain OPEN and belong to Task 4a-3.**

- **✅ LANDED — 4a-2: the resume CONTRACT, and it is deliberately INERT.** `PtyLaunchSpec` gains a **`resume` modifier** — resumption is a modifier **on the one launch path**, never a second one — alongside a discriminated **`ResumeDescriptor` (`kind`)**, `AgentSessionLaunch`, `DiscoverSessionContext`, `ResumeFailureReason` and `ResumeExitObservation`. **`ResumeSpec` and `SupportsResume.resumeSession()` are DELETED, not deprecated**, so the forked launch path D139 refused is now **unrepresentable rather than merely discouraged**. **claude** gains an **`assigned`** descriptor plus `classifyResumeFailure`; **codex** gains a **`discovered`** descriptor plus `discoverSessionId` and its own `classifyResumeFailure`; `resumeDescriptorSchema` in `shared/ipc.ts` gains `kind`, because `z.object` **strips** unknown keys rather than rejecting them and the discriminant would otherwise vanish on the wire in silence. **⚠ NOTHING CALLS ANY OF IT — no call site, no channel, no migration — and that is the deliverable**, the same discipline 4a-1 used to claim its schema position while the contract above it was still in council. Six files, all under `adapters/` plus `shared/ipc.ts`.
- **✅ THE PHASE'S MOST LOAD-BEARING NEW FACT IS THAT `--session-id` SURVIVES *INTERACTIVE* CLAUDE — D143(d) ASKED FOR IT, AND IT WAS MEASURED RATHER THAN REASONED.** D140's original evidence was **print-mode only** (`-p`), and every Chorus pane is an interactive TUI — so a flag that now lands on **every** claude launch rested on an untested generalisation. Re-verified on **claude 2.1.229** through a real pty with **no `-p`**: the transcript landed at `~/.claude/projects/<munged>/1cf4b139-….jsonl` **under the exact minted id**, **15 records all carrying that `sessionId`**, the distinctive word present **4×**; `claude --resume <uuid>` then brought it back. **The assigned strategy works for interactive claude, and D139 did NOT need re-opening.** **⚠ ONE ENVIRONMENTAL TRAP IS PRESERVED BECAUSE IT WOULD HAVE FAILED THE CHECK FOR A REASON HAVING NOTHING TO DO WITH THE FLAG:** launched from inside Claude Code the child **inherits `CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION`**, prints *"Transcript saving is off"* and writes **no transcript at all**. The harness therefore **clears every `CLAUDE*` variable**. A false **FAILURE** rather than a false green — the opposite direction from G1's, and the same class.
- **✅ TWO GENUINELY OPEN QUESTIONS WERE CLOSED BY MEASUREMENT RATHER THAN BY PREFERENCE.** **(1) codex's `-c` POSITION**, which the spec named as the one fact it did not know. **Both** orderings parse, dispatch and preserve their overrides; root-position `codex -c … resume <id>` **genuinely reaches the subcommand**, proven by **the subcommand's own error** — *"No saved session found with ID …"* — rather than by an exit code. **The shipped ordering appends `resume <id>` LAST so `CODEX_BASELINE_ARGS` stays a genuine argv PREFIX**, which is what keeps `adapters.test.ts`'s exact-equality assertions **pins** instead of tail-reasoning. **(2) BOTH CLASSIFIERS GATE ON A NON-ZERO EXIT BEFORE MATCHING ANY STRING**, and both CLIs were measured exiting **1** on a real failed resume. **The reason is specific to this app, which is why the gate is a contract rather than a nicety: `observation.output` is agent conversation, and Chorus is a tool whose users read and write about Chorus.** An agent quoting these exact error strings **while discussing this code** exits **0** like any other turn — and without the gate that would **clear a HEALTHY pointer and relaunch the pane**. **Found by self-review, not by a test.**
- **✅ CAPABILITY HONESTY GOT STRONGER, AND TWO SPEC DEPARTURES WERE DECLARED RATHER THAN ABSORBED.** The `['sessionResume','resumeSession']` row **left** `EXTENSION_METHODS` — the opposite of a relaxation: the check moved from **name-pairing to a structural one** (CR-4a.0 Q5), and `supportsResume()` now verifies that the **descriptor's own kind matches the methods present** — `assigned` **forbids** `discoverSessionId`, `discovered` **requires** it. **The two departures from the ruled TypeScript:** the guard **KEEPS** its `BaseAgentAdapter &` intersection although the ruled signature dropped it — both sibling guards carry it, and **4a-3 needs `buildLaunch` off the same value it just narrowed**; and the support interfaces carry **METHODS ONLY**, because the ruled shape declared `sessionResume` as an adapter **property** while in this tree the descriptor lives on **`getCapabilities()`'s return value**, so declaring it would **bypass `capabilities.ts`'s detected-override merge** and let the guard read a stale descriptor. **Recorded rather than silently absorbed, because a spec that quietly diverges stops being a spec.**
- **⚠ ONE SCOPE DEVIATION, DECLARED RATHER THAN DISCOVERED.** Task 4a-4's own file table listed **six** files and its acceptance criteria **could not be met from inside them**: `index.ts` (**+29 lines**) constructs the store from `app.getPath('userData')` and runs the boot sweep, and `ipc.ts` (**+4 lines**) calls `removeScrollback` at both `deleteSession` sites. **33 lines across two files outside the declared scope** — recorded here because a file list that quietly grows is how a scope stops meaning anything.
- _Superseded by the phase-close entries below — kept for the trail, and **three of its claims did not survive**: **F61** is settled as ship-with (unreachable only because F64 makes codex resume never fire), **F62** is downgraded (the real cwd compared byte-identical; F64 is the actual cause of every miss), and **D142's scrollback half is implemented** by `756066c`. **F63 is the one item that carried forward unchanged** — 4a-3 neither produced nor reserved the two orphan members._ **⚠ SIX FINDINGS OPEN AND ONE SUB-QUESTION STILL UNSETTLED, ALL OF THEM LEFT OPEN ON PURPOSE.** **⚠ NEW THIS PASS, ALL THREE FROM 4a-2 AND ALL THREE OWNED BY 4a-3: F61** — codex does **not re-apply `tui.status_line` on resume** (**0 occurrences across all three `-c` positions**, while `-c model=` **does** apply and draws codex's own model-mismatch warning), so a resumed pane's context ring stays **blank for the whole session with nothing logged** — **free today because nothing resumes, and 4a-3 is exactly what makes it bite**. **F62** — discovery's `cwd` compare is **exact and therefore case-sensitive on a case-insensitive filesystem**, so a casing mismatch misses **100% of the time** and looks identical to *"not discovered yet"*. **F63** — `ResumeFailureReason` declares `transcript-unavailable` and `unusable-pointer` with **no producer**, the first proven unreachable **by planting a corrupt transcript** rather than by assuming. **And carried forward: F59** — history replays **only** for sessions `restore()` actually relaunches, so a session healed to `exited` (credentialed under D33/F26, missing cwd, or beyond `RESTORE_CAP`) never spawns, never reads its seed, and shows an empty pane **beside a complete mirror on disk** — arguably the case where history matters most, since that is the session the user must relaunch by hand. **F60** — `deleteProject` (D121) purges session rows in one transaction and leaves their mirrors for the next boot's sweep; bounded and not a leak, but "the file dies with its row" holds **only** at the two session-delete sites in `ipc.ts`. **F57** — codex's `session_index.jsonl` has no `cwd`, so discovery must read the rollout header. **And D142's scrollback half:** ruled **on paper** by CR-4a.0 Q7 — scrollback retained behind a **mandatory visible separator**, per D143(g) — and **implemented by nothing**, so restart still goes through `spawn()` and a restarted pane **re-seeds its prior scrollback with no boundary**. **Task 4a-3 still owns settling it in code.**
- **✅ LANDED — 4a-3: the WIRING, and the commit that makes the phase's sentence true.** `resumeCore.ts` (pure: `planResume`, `toLaunchModifier`, `planExitDisposition`, `isResumeAction` — no `fs`, no clock, **the minter injected**) plus the call sites. **⚠ THE PLAN IS COMPUTED INSIDE `spawn()`, THE ONE FUNNEL ALL THREE CALL SITES PASS THROUGH, AND THAT IS WHAT KEPT THE DIFF HONEST:** `restore()` needed **no edit at all**, so its credentialed heal, `RESTORE_CAP`, the stagger and the `existsSync(row.cwd)` guard are provably untouched — the file shows they were not touched. Ordering is **mint → `buildLaunch` → `pty.spawn` → only then `setAgentSessionId`** (D143(c)), grep-provable by line number. `session:restart` clears the pointer **before** relaunching and asks for the boundary; the boundary is **emitted through `output.ingest`**, so it takes the one emit path (D45(1)), lands in the mirror, and is still in the right place at the next boot. Q4's *"visible pane badge"* ships as **that same emitted line** rather than renderer chrome — **no renderer file, no schema field, no channel** — and the existing restart chip is untouched.
- **✅ THE HEADLINE CLAIM IS NOW A MEASUREMENT AND NOT A DESIGN.** A claude pane was told *"Remember the number 4917"*, Chorus was **quit until zero electron processes remained**, reopened, and the pane — asked what number it had been given — **answered 4917**, with the whole earlier exchange replayed above it. The relaunch argv carried **`--resume 4a695944-…`**, the exact stored pointer. **Corroborated by claude itself:** on a later kill it printed its own farewell — *"Resume this session with: `claude --resume 4a695944-…`"* — i.e. **the id Chorus minted IS the conversation id claude kept.** Eight runtime gates were driven on the real app against a throwaway DB copy; evidence under `_verify/4a-3/`. **⚠ ONE GATE WAS NOT RUN AND IS NAMED RATHER THAN QUIETLY DROPPED: the repeat across a GENUINE MACHINE REBOOT.** Rebooting the user's machine was not something to do unasked; the full process-lifecycle equivalent was run **four times** and passed each time.
- **⚠ TWO SPECIFICATION DEFECTS WERE FOUND BY RUNNING THE APP, AND BOTH WOULD HAVE SHIPPED BROKEN — THIS IS G2 EARNING ITS PLACE FOR THE SECOND PHASE RUNNING.** **(1) The failure classifiers could not match real terminal bytes (F66).** claude renders the spaces in *"No conversation found with session ID"* as **`ESC[1C` cursor-forwards**, so 4a-2's prose regexes — and their prose fixtures — matched **nothing**, and the entire Q4 recovery path was **dead code**. Left unfixed this commit would have been a **REGRESSION**: a stale pointer would leave the pane **dead** where the pre-4a-3 app always came back **fresh**. Fixed by `flattenTerminalText` in **main**, not per-adapter, so terminal decoding has one home. **(2) The boundary's specified bytes printed THROUGH the history they separate.** `ImplementationSpec-4a-3.md` §6's frame never positions the cursor, and the replayed screen is painted with **absolute** cursor moves — so the line landed wherever that screen happened to leave the cursor, **overwriting the retained history**. Photographed, then fixed with a `ESC[999;1H` park. **Both are recorded as departures from the spec rather than absorbed into it**, because a spec that quietly diverges stops being a spec.
- **⚠ THE PHASE SHIPS HALF ITS AGENTS, STATED IN THE COMMIT RATHER THAN DISCOVERED LATER — claude RESUMES, codex DOES NOT (F64).** codex writes its rollout file **on the first user turn, not at launch** — measured at **3 minutes 19 seconds** after the spawn, with the filename and header **backdated** to the spawn instant — so **any** bounded post-spawn discovery window misses, and widening it is **worse** rather than better (the header bound has no upper edge, so a long window lets one pane claim a **later** launch's rollout in the same cwd). The orchestration ships in place, is correct when it fires, is **silent** when it does not, and is abandoned on quit. `Task-4a-3.md` authorised exactly this outcome in advance — *"half the feature working correctly beats all of it working sometimes"* — which is why it is a recorded finding and not a failure.
- **Gate tally at `756066c` (phase close):** vitest **1964 / 1964 across 58 files** (**1924 / 57** at `9e3c83d`; **1888 / 56** at `a6fab79`; **1837 / 53** at the phase's `82e16d7` baseline — **+127 tests and +5 files across the phase**), typecheck **0** (node + web), secret-grep clean, `IpcChannel` **86 — UNCHANGED across all four tasks**, `MIGRATIONS.length` **19 — unchanged since 4a-1**, `sqliteTable(` **18 — unchanged**. `agentEvents.ts`, `agentEventsCore.ts` and **every file under `adapters/`** are byte-identical at 4a-3 (D130's read surface did not widen; 4a-2's contract needed no repair to be consumed). **⚠ HEAD MOVED MID-TASK:** `7c91fc8`, a third party's `contextUsageCore` fix, landed between 4a-2 and 4a-3 and carries **+3** of the tests above.
- _Superseded — the tally at `9e3c83d`, kept for the trail:_ vitest **1924 / 1924 across 57 files** (**1888 / 56** at `a6fab79`; **1837 / 53** at the phase's `82e16d7` baseline), typecheck **0** (node + web), secret-grep clean, `IpcChannel` **86 — unchanged across every landed task in this phase**, `MIGRATIONS.length` **19 — unchanged, because 4a-2 authored no migration**, `sqliteTable(` **18 — unchanged**. **⚠ A run in the DIRTY tree reports 1927, not 1924** — the tree carries an **unrelated uncommitted** `contextUsageCore.test.ts` change worth **+3 tests**; the figure above is **HEAD's**, because that is the one a fresh clone reproduces.

> **▶ THIS PHASE RUNS NEXT, AHEAD OF PHASE 4's REMAINING THREE TASKS — Matthew's ruling, 2026-08-16, recorded as D154.** It supersedes D147(g), which had queued 4-2 / 4-3 / 4-4 immediately after Phase 6a. Phase 4 is **deferred, not dropped**, and keeps everything it has landed.
>
> **✅ v1 SCOPE, SET AT THE PHASE 6a CLOSE (D155):** the **offline floor** — Verbatim + local whisper, no network, no key, no vendor, no LLM — **PLUS** the three refinement modes (Verbatim / Clean up / Organize, D137) over the existing BYOK path. **❌ CLOUD STT IS OUT OF v1.** The line is drawn between two *different disclosures*, not by size: refinement sends **transcript text** to an LLM on a credential the user configured — the egress the council and the day summary already make — while cloud STT sends **raw microphone audio** to a third party, which is a class of egress this app has never made. v1 keeps the class it already reasons about.

It was bundled for shared always-on-top window plumbing; the plumbing is real and is a handful of `BrowserWindow` options rather than a feature, and D135 had already expanded this phase once without re-estimation. **The mic overlay builds that plumbing and the Fleet Switcher inherits it later** — the same debt, in the cheaper direction. Placement is owed by a later architect pass.

Design plan: [`Phase-5-VoicePlan.md`](Phase-5-VoicePlan.md) (2026-08-12, authored against `e1afe89`) — **authoritative on design**; this entry records placement, scope and status only. The requirements it translates are preserved verbatim at [`Investigations/Voice-Input-Feature-Requirements-source.md`](Investigations/Voice-Input-Feature-Requirements-source.md).

> **⚠ THE PHASE GREW ON 2026-08-12 AND HAS NOT BEEN RE-ESTIMATED.** Matthew supplied a requirements document written with another model, and it is adopted as scope by **D135**. The four-line phase this replaced described a dictation pipe; the phase now also owns a **transcript refinement model** (Verbatim / Clean up / Organize, D137), a **permission and retention posture** (D138), and a **second activation mode** (D136). **The `wk 2` sizing in `Plan.md` §14 predates all of it and is no longer meaningful.** Re-sized at kickoff, not here.

- **✅ WHAT WAS ALREADY TRUE AND SURVIVES INTACT.** `Plan.md` §9's core pipeline — hold key → capture 16 kHz mono → release → transcribe → insert, **no auto-Enter by default** — is unchanged and is *strengthened* by the new scope rather than replaced. The stack table's two locked rows (whisper.cpp local default / cloud toggle; `uiohook-napi` for true keydown-keyup) stand. The mock keeps its authority under D73.
- **⚠ ZERO CODE EXISTS, AND THERE IS NO MICROPHONE POLICY AT ALL.** Verified 2026-08-12 at `e1afe89`: no `uiohook-napi`, no whisper binding, no `getUserMedia`, no `services/voice.ts`, and **neither `setPermissionRequestHandler` nor `setPermissionCheckHandler` anywhere in `src/main/`**. Nothing asks today so nothing is granted, but the app has **no stated position on what happens if something does** — the first `getUserMedia` would resolve against Electron's built-in default rather than a Chorus rule. **⚠ WHAT THAT DEFAULT IS WAS NOT VERIFIED AND IS NOT ASSUMED HERE:** Electron 43.1.1's shipped typings document how to *set* the handlers and say nothing about the unset case, so "it approves by default" is training-data memory of exactly the kind D4 exists to stop. It is on the plan's measure-it list. **The requirement does not depend on the answer** — D138 makes the policy a precondition of the first capture line either way.
- **⚠ THE PIPELINE IS SPLIT ACROSS THE BRIDGE BY FORCE, NOT BY CHOICE, AND THIS IS THE PHASE'S REAL ARCHITECTURAL SHAPE.** `getUserMedia` is a Chromium API and **main has no microphone access at all**, so capture can only be in the renderer. CLAUDE.md forbids the renderer spawning processes, D33 keeps keys out of it, and the CSP (below) keeps it off the network — so transcription can only be in main. **Voice is therefore the first feature in which the renderer is a bulk binary PRODUCER**, and D14's plain-object rule meets a payload it was never tested against. The frame envelope is Zod-validated per D1; the sample block is length- and type-checked rather than element-validated, and the plan says so out loud so it is not read as a D1 exemption. Backpressure is a named failure mode, not an afterthought.
- **⚠ THE SHIPPED CSP WOULD BREAK TWO OF THE THREE OBVIOUS IMPLEMENTATIONS — D1's LESSON IN A THIRD REGISTRY.** `src/renderer/index.html:7` carries **no `connect-src`** (so it inherits `default-src 'self'` and the renderer cannot reach OpenAI or Deepgram) and **no `wasm-unsafe-eval`** (so a whisper WASM build in the renderer would throw exactly as Zod-in-preload did). Both push the work into main, where D33 and CLAUDE.md already wanted it. **The rule generalises: the answer to "the CSP blocks this library" is move the work to main, never widen the policy.**
- **⚠ THE DESTINATION IS A PTY, WHICH DELETES HALF THE SOURCE DOCUMENT'S REVIEW MODEL.** Text reaches an agent through `session:write`, and **once written those bytes belong to the child's line editor — Chorus cannot un-write, re-render or diff them.** So "switch between Original / Cleaned / Organized", "restore the original" and "re-run in another mode" are possible **only before the write**, in a surface Chorus owns. **The mock draws text landing straight at the `❯` prompt and draws no composer**, so a composer is an extension *beyond* the D73 authority rather than a reading of it — see D137, which stages it.
- **⚠ AND THE AUTO-SEND QUESTION IS A SAFETY QUESTION HERE, NOT A UX ONE.** The source document's rule is "don't auto-submit a form." The Chorus analogue is that Enter in an agent pane **starts an autonomous process that edits files and runs commands**, on a transcript that may have been misheard. `Plan.md` §9's existing no-auto-Enter default is therefore promoted from a preference to a safety rule.
- **✅ REFINEMENT REUSES THE COUNCIL'S BYOK PATH RATHER THAN GROWING A SECOND CLIENT** — `apiSession.ts` / `createApiSession`, ~~with spend riding `usage_records`~~ **⚠ CORRECTED 2026-08-16 (D157): `usage_records` DOES NOT EXIST AND NEVER DID.** The code says so outright — `schema.ts:238` (*"The token/cost columns live on THIS row, not in a separate usage_records table… one home, not two (D48)"*) and `storage.ts:264-268` (*"The roadmap's `usage_records` name is superseded by this table"*). **Spend lives on `dispatches`, `council_messages` and `council_runs`.** `Plan.md` carried the same phantom in three places (`:233`, `:313`, `:331`) and is corrected this pass. **⚠ AND METERING VOICE IS THEREFORE A DECISION RATHER THAN A DEFAULT — TAKEN: METER IT (D157)** — because the newest consumer of this very path **records nothing**: the day-report summarizer calls `createApiSession` with `{baseUrl, maxOutputTokens}` and **no `onUsage`** (`ipc.ts:4274`, in `resolveDaySummarizer` `:4263–4299`), leaving `councilService.ts:1129` as main's **only** `onUsage` producer. "It will follow the precedent" would have shipped voice unmetered. **⚠ It also inherits a known defect (F42):** the council's cost capture under-reports its final turn (the minted-key spend read fires before the vendor settles it), and **a refinement turn is a single short turn — so all of it is the final turn.** Derive from stored token counts, the method F42 proved twice to within 0.05%; never from the minted-key scalar. Where the numbers land is settled at kickoff.
- **✅ THE OFFLINE FLOOR IS EXPLICIT: Verbatim + local whisper touches no network, no key, no vendor and no LLM**, and must stay reachable in one setting change. Clean up and Organize send transcript text to an LLM; cloud STT sends the audio itself. Both are disclosures, both are off by default, and the disclosure belongs where the mode is chosen rather than buried in settings.
- **✅ IT FINALLY GIVES THE "VOICE & DICTATION" SETTINGS SECTION SOMETHING TO BE.** The mock draws six nav entries; `SettingsView.vue` renders two, because D76 forbids a nav row with nothing behind it. **That withholding is the precedent being followed, not overturned** — the entry appears when the settings behind it exist, exactly as "Agent lock" earned its own.
- **⚠ TWO SCHEMA TRAPS ARE PRE-RECORDED SO THEY ARE HANDLED RATHER THAN DISCOVERED.** (1) `MIGRATIONS.length` was **18** at `e1afe89`, so the next free version *looked like* v19 — **⚠ AND v19 HAS SINCE BEEN SPENT BY TASK 4a-1 (`bbf6d32`, 2026-08-13): `MIGRATIONS.length` is now 19 and the next free is `v20`.** ~~next free is `v20`~~ **⚠ AND IT HAS MOVED AGAIN SINCE THAT AMENDMENT — `MIGRATIONS.length` is 20 at `4369954` (v20 = `day_reports`, D153) and the next free is `v21`, re-measured 2026-08-16 by AST parse and confirmed against the dev DB's own `SELECT MAX(version)`.** **⚠ THIS BULLET HAS NOW BEEN WRONG TWICE AND IS *STILL* NOT CORRECTED IN PLACE, WHICH IS THE POINT OF IT:** three different numbers have been "the next free version" between this plan being written and the phase being reached, and the third will be stale too if Phase 5 is not kicked off promptly. This is the trap firing exactly as the bullet predicted, between the plan being written and the phase being kicked off, and it is left in place rather than silently corrected because the *instruction* is the durable part — **per G6 it must be recomputed against `main` at the moment of writing and stopped on divergence**; this log records that going wrong twice already, once shipping a silent runtime failure. (2) **Any enforced FK to `projects(id)` or `sessions(id)` is a change to `deleteProject`** — Phase 6 learned this the expensive way, and the purge must grow its step **in the same commit as the table**.
- **⚠ NO ERROR MAY DEPEND ON AN OS TOAST TO BE SEEN.** Toasts are proven dead on this machine (`ToastEnabled=0`, every exit toast failing `HRESULT: -2143420140`). Voice has more failure states than any feature so far — permission denied, device busy, model missing on first run, native hook failed to load, network gone mid-transcription — and the in-app surface is the only one that reliably exists.
- **⚠ WHAT IS EXPLICITLY STILL OUT.** TTS and wake word stay in §8 (unchanged). Voice *commands* ("switch to TaxApp", "restart pane 2") remain deferred — dictation only. Speaker separation, meeting capture and voice identification stay non-goals, as they already were in the source document. **Analytics stay LOCAL-ONLY: Phase 5 introduces no telemetry egress**, and recording content is never used for metrics.
- **[CR] questions carried to the gate** (full list in the plan, §11): direct-to-prompt only for v1 or composer-first from the start; whether "Stop and send" is refused outright on agent-locked sessions; streaming interim text vs finalize-on-release; where a transcript goes when its target pane dies mid-flight; whether the audio frame channel gets its own preload surface; and whether a voice session row references `sessions(id)` at all.
- **⚠ THE FIRST THING THE KICKOFF DOES IS A D4 PASS, AND IT IS NOT OPTIONAL.** ~~Four~~ **THREE** load-bearing facts are **unverified** — **the "current cloud STT vendor API shapes" row is DROPPED by D155, since cloud STT is out of v1**: **(1) whether `uiohook-napi` ships usable Windows prebuilds for this Electron ABI — ⚠ DO THIS ONE FIRST** (**this program has been burned in both directions — node-pty needed no rebuild, better-sqlite3 needed a `/Od` source build — so assume neither**), because a missing prebuild changes the phase's **shape**, not its schedule, and every other estimate is downstream of the answer; **(2)** whisper.cpp's Windows distribution shape and model size; **(3)** `AudioWorklet`'s behaviour at 16 kHz mono under Electron **43.1.1**.
- **Milestone (unchanged in words, larger in meaning):** voice dictation injects into the targeted session — **with the original transcript never lost, the microphone released the moment capture ends, and nothing sent to an agent without explicit confirmation.**

The old milestone wording — *"claude and opencode receive a Chorus-written MCP config"* — would have been declared met days earlier by a feature that did not work. **Full evidence and the honest gaps: [`Tasks/Task-6-5-Result.md`](Tasks/Task-6-5-Result.md).**
>
> **✅ THE SECURITY CONTRACT HELD, MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSERTED.** `~/.codex/config.toml` is **byte-identical** across the whole drive (`ec0fde13…`, re-baselined immediately before launch because codex writes that file itself and the handoff's four-day-old hash would have reported a change Chorus did not make). The written-file secret grep — the one `npm run grep:secrets` structurally cannot reach — returned **no matches** over `.mcp.json` and `%APPDATA%\chorus\mcp\`. **The approval was performed by the operator; Chorus wrote configuration only, never a trust record** (D49, CR-6.0 Q6).
>

The **four-state model is still only partly driven** — `Configured` and `Connected`/`Failed` describe **Chorus's own** connection, and there is deliberately **no per-agent light**, because one would need an IPC channel 6-5's non-goals forbid and a dot meaning "we wrote a file" is the dishonesty CR-6.0 was convened to prevent.
>

It was caused by driving the app through `window.chorus.launch` instead of the launch dialog, which creates real PTYs with **no visible pane**.

> **✅ AND THE RULING THIS NOTE ASKED FOR EXISTS: D147, 2026-08-14.** Stage 5 does **not** stay here. > **✅ STAGE 2 IS DONE — CHORUS HAS OPENED ITS FIRST BOLT CONNECTION.** `5486928`, 2026-08-08. Migration **v16** created `project_memory` (`sqliteTable(` 16 → 17), five `memory:*` channels landed (`IpcChannel` **71 → 76** — five, not the six an early draft claimed), and the **`● neo4j :7688` chip D76 omitted from Phase 3c came back because `memory:status` finally gave it a data source.** Gates: typecheck 0 · vitest **1599 / 44 files** (from 1479 / 41) · `grep:secrets` clean. **G2 was run against a real, disposable, auth-disabled `neo4j:5-community` on `127.0.0.1:7688`, started and removed BY HAND — Chorus starts no container in this phase:** `memory:test` returned **`probe: 1`**, a wrong port returned an authored refusal carrying **no URI, no driver stack trace and no Neo4j error code**, the chip rendered for the configured project and was **absent — not empty —** for the other, and disposal was observed **closing the socket** (`Established` → `TimeWait`) rather than merely inferred from the process exiting.
>
> **⚠ THREE THINGS THE TASK DOCS DID NOT ANTICIPATE, ALL FOUND BY CHECKING RATHER THAN BY FAILING.**
> **(1) `deleteProject` WOULD HAVE THROWN, AND THE FIX SHIPPED IN THE SAME COMMIT AS THE TABLE.** `project_memory.project_id` is `PRIMARY KEY REFERENCES projects(id)` — enforced, and **never null** — so Phase 3h's nine-table purge would have hit `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_FOREIGNKEY` on any project with memory configured and rolled the whole delete back. **This is the same D62 trap the migration's own comment describes for `credential_profile_id`, except that one is unreachable this phase and this one is LIVE from the first configured project.** The purge gained a tenth step and `project:delete`'s response gained `project_memory`. **The lesson generalises: an enforced FK to `projects(id)` is a change to `deleteProject`, always, and the credential-shaped trap the docs warned about was not the one that would have fired.**
> **(2) `neo4j.auth.none()` IS GONE FROM THE 6.x TYPES.** It still exists at runtime in **6.2.0** but `types/index.d.ts:133` declares only `basic`/`kerberos`/`bearer`/`custom`, so reaching it needs a cast. `authToken` is **optional** in the declared signature, so **omitting it** is the supported way to say "no authentication" — which is what shipped and what G2 proved against **Neo4j Kernel 5.26.29 community**. D100 approved the package, not a version; the `latest` line was taken and no `electron-rebuild` was needed, so **D100's "pure JS, no native build" premise holds on measurement.**
> **(3) `RETURN 1` COMES BACK AS A NEO4J `Integer` OBJECT, NOT A NUMBER.** A naive `=== 1` would have reported failure against a database that answered correctly — so the normalisation in `neo4jClient.ts` is load-bearing rather than defensive, and **6-4's seeder and validator inherit the same obligation for every count they read back.**
>
> **⚠ AND ONE CUT WAS TAKEN WITH ITS DEBT WRITTEN INTO THE SQL RATHER THAN A DOC.** `countProjectMemoryForCredential` was **not written** (D128(a) makes `credential_profile_id` permanently NULL this phase, so it would have had no caller and its refusal could never be read). The instruction for whoever ships credentialed memory — add it to `credential:delete`'s existing guard at `ipc.ts:1779`, **beside** its two siblings, **before** the first credentialed row exists — lives in the **v16 migration comment**, where a reader of the schema meets it at the column it concerns.

>
> **⚠ THE STORAGE ENGINE WAS RE-OPENED ON 2026-08-08 AND CLOSED ON MEASUREMENT — NEO4J STANDS.** Matthew asked whether Memgraph escapes the one-database-per-container limit. **It does not: Memgraph 3.12.0 Community refuses `CREATE DATABASE` with `Access to multi-tenancy requires an enterprise, ai_platform, or oem license`** — measured on this machine, the same Enterprise gate Neo4j has (D128(e)). Measured side by side: **idle RAM 496 MiB Neo4j vs 184 MiB Memgraph**, images 968 MB vs 931 MB, and **both speak bolt through the same driver**. Memgraph's **BSL 1.1** carries a distribution question a v1 does not need; **the embedded option that would have deleted the container entirely — Kuzu — was archived in October 2025** after Apple acquired the company, and its MIT successor **LadybugDB** is months old. **Apache AGE is the only permissively-licensed option in the field and is heavier, not lighter.** Recorded so the question is not re-derived: **the swap seam is `memoryConfigCore.ts` plus the MCP descriptor, not the architecture.**

#### ⚠ The kickoff's grounding pass found two things the design plan had wrong

Both are recorded here because **a task doc built on a stale fact wastes an implementation session**, and both change work:

- **The capability-honesty test covers 2 of 5 adapters, not 5.** `adapters.test.ts:41` is `const adapters = [claudeAdapter, codexAdapter]`. **`kimiAdapter` and `opencodeAdapter` were never added** — they arrived in Phase 3d (D86 lifted the registry freeze 2 → 3, D90 widened it 3 → 4), both are imported and individually tested in the same file, and **neither has ever been through capability honesty**; `noHarness` is absent too. So the plan §3 instruction to *"replace the mcp arm with an explicit table `{claude, codex, opencode, kimi, none}`"* **cannot be followed as written — three of its five keys name adapters the loops do not iterate.** Task 6-2 **widens the list 2 → 5 first**, and the generic declared-iff-implemented case at `:515` then covers kimi, opencode and none **for the first time**.
- **`Plan.md` §10 is wrong in six places, not four** — recorded as **D102**. D94's four, plus the `generated password → vault` step (**which D93 rejects**) and the stale `agentdesk-neo4j-<slug>` container prefix (**D92 says `chorus-`**).

**Three of the plan's line citations had also drifted** (`writeMcpConfig` is `types.ts:344` not `:343`; the argv note is `:174` not `:170`; `headersContainSecret` is `src/main/ipc.ts:252` not `:1350`) and **one names the wrong directory** — the `composeChildEnv` policy flip is `src/main/adapters/env.ts:142`, not `services/`. **The Overview's fact table supersedes the plan's line numbers.**

**⚠ THE D4 VERSIONS DRIFTED IN THE TEN DAYS THE PHASE SAT WAITING — THREE OF SEVEN MOVED, AND THE PREDICTION THAT THEY WOULD IS IN THIS PARAGRAPH'S OWN PREVIOUS TEXT.** Re-probed 2026-08-08 at `84dcf54`, method `<tool> --version`: **codex 0.145.0 → 0.147.0 · claude 2.1.218 → 2.1.224 · opencode 1.18.8 → 1.18.15**; kimi 0.29.1, docker 28.0.4, uvx/uv 0.11.19, npx 11.12.1 unchanged. **⚠ THE LINCHPIN WAS RE-PROVEN ON THE NEW CODEX, NOT ASSUMED:** per-invocation `-c mcp_servers.…` is still accepted and **still writes nothing** — `~/.codex/config.toml` byte-identical by hash and mtime before and after — so **Stage 1's zero-write premise survives the bump.** And the pass found a mechanism the kickoff did not know about: **`env_vars` is a distinct field from `env`, taking a list of variable NAMES**, which is D93's "names not values" existing natively in codex's own vocabulary. Full evidence, with the three-way live-probed / binary-inspected / unverified split intact, in [`Investigations/6-1-D4-Pass.md`](Investigations/6-1-D4-Pass.md).

dockerode provisioner; per-project containers; schema templates + provenance; MCP wiring into CLIs; index-codebase skill; lifecycle UI; provider-neutral skill format (`skill.yaml`).

- **⚠ TWO OF THIS PHASE'S LISTED DELIVERABLES ARE ALREADY SPENT, and the bullets that assigned them are DELETED rather than left to be inherited as false scope.** ~~D34 Q5's adapter-registration seam lands here~~ — **D86 lifted the freeze in Phase 3d (registry 2 → 3) and D90 widened it again (3 → 4)**, so the seam and its widen-together rule are **shipped, enforced code**, not a Phase 6 obligation. ~~Kimi CLI and an OpenAI-compatible agent CLI may arrive before this phase~~ — **they did**: `e87037a` (kimi) and `35a592f` (opencode). What remains of the original scope is the provisioner, schema + provenance, the MCP *config-writing implementation*, `index-codebase`, the lifecycle UI, the D76 status chip, and `skill.yaml`.
- **⚠ THE `SupportsMcp` SEAM EXISTS BUT IS WRONG-SHAPED, AND ITS VOCABULARY POINTS AT THE FILE D49 FORBIDS.** `McpDescriptor` (`types.ts:88`) can only describe adapters that write a **file** — the only shape fitting codex is `{format:'toml', location:'home', configPath:'.codex/config.toml'}` — and `McpServerRef` (`:337`) has no `env` field, which makes env-var indirection **untypeable**. Both are fixed in Stage 1. See the plan §3.
- **⚠ THE BRIGHT LINE IS THE PHASE'S CENTRAL DESIGN CONSTRAINT, not a footnote — see D93.** Wiring a Neo4j MCP server normally means putting a bolt password into a CLI's own config file, which is exactly what D49 and the AUTH-PRECEDENCE FINDING forbid. It is avoidable in every mode, but **only by design**: local Docker runs `NEO4J_AUTH=none` on `127.0.0.1` (no secret exists), and credentialed modes pass a variable **name**, never a value. **Note `npm run grep:secrets` reaches neither `~/.codex/` nor a project's `.mcp.json`** — G4 does not cover this phase's riskiest write, and a structural guard is what does.
- **Staged connect-first per D91:** Stage 0 CR + D4 · Stage 1 MCP honesty + codex (**zero new deps, writes nothing**) · Stage 2 connect to an existing Neo4j (`neo4j-driver`, migration **v13**) · Stage 3 graph schema + provenance + validator · Stage 4 `writeMcpConfig` for claude + opencode (**milestone met**) · Stage 5 Docker provisioner, then skills.
- **⚠ `Plan.md` §10 IS WRONG IN FOUR CHECKABLE PLACES (D94)** — `File.path` uniqueness breaks on worktrees; "one database per project" is unachievable on Community Edition; `confidence: number` is uncalibrated self-report; and three provenance fields must be edges rather than string properties. The spec is annotated, not rewritten (the D42/LiteLLM precedent).
- **✅ THE `[CR]` GATE FIRED AND CLOSED — D126.** Run `41c80955`, 2026-08-08, 4/4 members. **⚠ ITS QUESTION LIST NEARLY SHIPPED WRONG AND THE CHECK THAT CAUGHT IT COSTS NOTHING:** the case's first draft **parsed as TWELVE questions, not six**, because `parseBriefQuestions` (`councilCore.ts:434`) counts every numbered line in the questions section and Q1's elaboration carried a six-item sub-list. **Nothing refuses this** — `assembleRun` only refuses a case with *zero* questions — so the run would have proceeded and the verdict machinery would have keyed on `Q7`…`Q12` that no member was asked. **Fixed structurally** (flat list alone under the heading, elaboration moved to its own same-level section) and re-checked before spending. **⚠ VERIFY THE PARSE BEFORE EVERY PAID RUN, and D107's mandatory-heading template must state that the questions section holds the questions and nothing else numbered.**
- **[CR] question — ANSWERED as D126:** what memory schema and provenance model keeps agent-written knowledge trustworthy and attributable? **Answered: advisory-and-measured, NOT app-mediated writes — but only on condition the measurement is consequential** (numerator and denominator, ratio, trend, new-unsourced split from backlog, per-agent breakdown, affected-node list, repair action), because *"a bare count is decorative"*. **⚠ AND D128(c) STATES THE HONEST LIMIT: PHASE 6 SHIPS THE MEASUREMENT AND NOT YET THE CONSEQUENCE.** Items 7–9 are deferred as a unit; the roadmap says so rather than letting `memory:validate` imply the workflow exists.
- **⚠ Milestone — RE-WORDED BY Q6, AND THE OLD WORDING WAS FALSE.** It read *"agents read/write a per-project memory graph via MCP."* **Measured: a Chorus-written `.mcp.json` is reported by claude 2.1.224 as `⏸ Pending approval` and unapproved servers are "not connected to."** Writing the file is **necessary and not sufficient.** The milestone is now: **agents read and write a per-project memory graph via MCP, for a given agent and project, once approval has occurred and a probe read has confirmed the connection** — surfaced through the **`Configured → Pending approval → Connected → Failed`** state model, where **`Connected` is earned by an observed read, never by a written file.** **Met at Stage 4; Stage 5 is the tail.**

### Day Summary — ✅ **COMPLETE (2026-08-15)** _(not a numbered phase — a user-directed task taken ahead of Phase 6a at Matthew's request, "focus on that for a bit today and then we'll get back to Phase 6")_ **[D153]**

**"What was worked on today", across every project, agent-agnostic — for timesheets and project notes.**

The design decision is **D153(a)** and everything else follows from it: the source is **git**, not the hook spine. `agent_turns` could never answer this for a fleet containing codex (**D129**, **F64**), whereas git observes artifacts and so is agent-blind by nature. No per-agent breakdown exists anywhere in the feature, by request.

| Piece | Where |
|---|---|
| Rules (window, log/patch args, parsing, harvest, mtime filter, merge, render, prompt) — **pure, no `fs`, no `electron`** | `services/dayReportCore.ts` (+ 42 tests) |
| The sweep (git spawn, stat, grouping, degradation) — **every effect injected** | `services/dayReport.ts` (+ 9 tests) |
| The prose call — **total, never throws** | `services/dayReportSummarizer.ts` (+ 9 tests) |
| Read-only git additions: `gitCommonDir`, `readOnlyHistory`, `statusPorcelain(-uall)` | `services/git.ts` |
| Snapshot | migration **v20**, `day_reports`, one row per local calendar day |
| Surface | `views/DayReportView.vue`, palette command `day.summary`, five `day:*` channels |

- **⚠ FOUR TRAPS, ALL FOUND BY RUNNING THE SWEEP AGAINST THE REAL RAIL RATHER THAN BY REASONING** — the stash in `--all`, two projects sharing one `.git`, week-old dirt billed to today, and a symbol harvest that invented an API out of markdown code samples. Each is now a named test carrying the measurement that produced it. See D153(b)–(c).
- **⚠ `readAgentTurns` REMAINS WITHOUT A READER, DELIBERATELY.** This feature did not adopt it. F55 (turns count agent activity, not prompts) and F56 (tool counts unobservable) are untouched and still belong to the first task that reads turns.
- **⚠ F76 — SHIPPED WITH A FALSE-TIMESHEET DEFECT AND FIXED THE SAME DAY, AND THE ROUTE TO IT IS THE LESSON.** It was not found by testing; it was found because Matthew asked a *different* question — whether his two git profiles were both visible. They were. What the check revealed is that **nothing was filtering by author at all**, so on a fetched team repository the report attributed colleagues' commits to him: twelve of them under `Trupanion` on 2026-08-06, none his. **The four traps found before shipping were all found by running the sweep; this fifth one needed a user's question, because on this machine's own repositories the output looked entirely plausible.** Now filtered to the identities `git config --get-all user.email` reports, OR'd, with the identity list rendered in the report so the filter is auditable rather than invisible. Regression tests: five, in `dayReportCore.test.ts`.
- **⚠ THE FEATURE STATES ITS OWN LIMITS ON THE PAGE**: work written and then discarded never reaches git and is invisible; in-flight work is recognised by mtime, which a checkout disturbs. A timesheet source that quietly overstated a day would be worse than none.
- **Gates:** typecheck **0** (node + web) · vitest **2122 / 2122 across 63 files** (from 2012 / 59) · `grep:secrets` clean · **G2 driven** on an isolated `--user-data-dir` seeded from the installed profile — 6 projects → 4 repositories in **1.3 s**, v19→v20 applied on boot, palette route reached, markdown byte-identical across the round-trip. Evidence under `_verify/day-report/`.
- **✅ THE LIVE MODEL CALL IS VERIFIED** — `anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5` on the `OpenRouter-Chorus` credential (Matthew's choice, 2026-08-15). Two real generations: **4.9 s** end to end, the choice survived an app restart, and `summaryError` was null both times.
- **⚠ AND THE FIRST LIVE RESPONSE EXPOSED A FIFTH TRAP THAT ONLY A REAL CALL COULD HAVE SHOWN — THE SUMMARY RESTATED ITS OWN BULLETS.** It came back as two 75-word sentences walking every file in the evidence (`ICchTokenStore` via `EfCchTokenStore` and `InMemoryCchTokenStore`, then `CchAccessTokenProvider`, then the registration extension…) — *precisely* the list rendered directly beneath it, so the reader had to read everything twice to discover it was the same thing. **"1 to 3 sentences" alone permits one enormous sentence, and that is what arrived.** The prompt now says CHARACTERISE, DO NOT ENUMERATE, carries a **60-word ceiling** to make that enforceable, and names the rule's origin at the constant. Re-measured after the fix: **44 words, two sentences, both projects named.** A stubbed transport could never have caught this — the unit tests were green through both versions.

**Gate tally at `4369954` (phase close), every figure re-measured against `main` this pass rather than deltaed:** vitest **2230 / 2230 across 66 files** (**2012 / 59** at the `47f633c` kickoff baseline — **+218 tests, +7 files across the phase**) · typecheck **0** (node + web) · `grep:secrets` **clean, 6 patterns** · `IpcChannel` **97** (86 at kickoff — **+11 across the phase, and +5 of those were the Day Summary task landing mid-phase**, not 6a's) · `ipcMain.handle(` **87** · `sqliteTable(` **19** · **`MIGRATIONS.length` 20 — UNCHANGED, exactly as the phase predicted: no task here authored a SQLite migration** · runtime dependencies **8 — unchanged across all four tasks, and the provisioner is why that is worth stating** (D147(d) took the `docker` **CLI** over `dockerode`, so a container lifecycle shipped without a dependency) · `LATEST_GRAPH_VERSION` **1 → 2** (6a-2's `File`/`Directory`/`Commit` migration — **the phase's one schema move, and it is in the graph's ledger rather than SQLite's**).

**✅ THE PHASE'S MILESTONE WAS MET AT 6a-2 AND HELD THROUGH THE REMAINING TWO TASKS** — see the drive recorded below. **⚠ AND THE PHASE'S REAL RESULT IS NARROWER THAN "MEMORY WORKS": it is that BOTH agents can now reach the graph, and that a project can get a graph without the user running a `docker` command.** Phase 6 proved an agent *can* query the graph when told to; 6a-1 made the telling automatic, 6a-2 gave the graph something structural to say, 6a-3 closed the gap that meant codex was being told about a server it could not see (**F75**), and 6a-4 removed the manual container step.

**⚠ THE KICKOFF'S GROUNDING PASS FOUND THAT ONE OF D147's OWN MECHANISMS DOES NOT EXIST, AND IT CHANGES 6a-1's WHOLE VEHICLE.** D147(e) says injection *"uses claude's `--settings`"*. **`--settings` takes a settings JSON file and has no system-prompt field** — Chorus's own use of it writes `{hooks:{…}}` (`claude.ts:213`). Measured against the installed **claude 2.1.232** this session: `--append-system-prompt <prompt>` is documented, and **`--append-system-prompt-file <file>` exists** (probed by omitting its argument). **D148 takes the file variant**, which is the same shape `--settings` already gives hooks: main reserves the path, the adapter writes it, every exit path deletes it. **A task doc built on the `--settings` sentence would have wasted a session discovering this.**

**⚠ AND THE SECOND MECHANISM IS ALREADY OCCUPIED.** codex's `-c developer_instructions` is not free: `CODEX_BASELINE_ARGS` (`codex.ts:742`) has been setting it to the jade formatting rule since v17, **`-c` REPLACES rather than appends** (`codex.ts:708`), and codex ignores a duplicated or unknown `-c` path **silently** (`codex.ts:680`). A second token would destroy one of the two values with no error anywhere. **6a-1 gives that key exactly one emitter.**

**⚠ AMENDED 2026-08-15 — 6a-2's OWN STARTING NUMBERS WERE STALE BY THE TIME IT RAN, AND THREE OF THEM WERE LOAD-BEARING.** The task doc was authored against `47f633c`; between then and execution, 6a-1 landed and the Day Summary task (D153) shipped. Re-measured at execution: `IpcChannel` **91 → 92** (the doc said 86 → 87), `MIGRATIONS.length` stays at **20** (the doc said 19 — still true that this phase authors none), vitest baseline **2122** (the doc said 2012), tracked files **466** (the doc said 447). **And the doc's runtime-drive command would have failed outright:** `docker run --name chorus-6a2 -p 7688:7687` collides with `chorus-g2-neo4j`, which has held 7688 since the Phase 6 G2 drive; 6a-2 used **7689** on a fresh container instead. **A task doc's "Initial Starting Point" table is a measurement with a date on it, not a constant** — G6 exists for exactly this and it earned its keep here.

**⚠ AND THE TASK'S OWN VERIFICATION COMMAND GIVES A FALSE POSITIVE.** `grep -nE "DETACH|DELETE|REMOVE " codeIndexCore.ts` prints **five** hits — every one inside a comment *explaining* the no-deletion rule. The real guard is `codeIndexCore.test.ts`'s assertion over `ALL_INDEX_STATEMENTS`, which is why that list is exported: a statement added without being listed is one the test does not cover.

**⚠ NO TASK IN THIS PHASE AUTHORS A SQLITE MIGRATION — MEASURED, NOT PLANNED, AND ✅ CONFIRMED AT THE CLOSE.** ~~`MIGRATIONS.length` is **19** and stays there~~ **⚠ THE PREDICTION HELD AND THE NUMBER MOVED ANYWAY, WHICH IS WORTH MORE THAN EITHER FACT ALONE: `MIGRATIONS.length` was 19 when this was written and is 20 at the close — moved by the Day Summary task (D153, `day_reports`) landing mid-phase, not by any task in Phase 6a.** The claim was about *this phase's* authorship and it is true; the *counter* was never this phase's to hold still, and reading it as a guarantee is precisely the mistake `Task-6a-3.md` and `Task-6a-4.md` both made. **Next free is `v21`.** 6a-4's container columns (`container_id`, `container_name`, `volume_name`, `bolt_port`, `http_port`) were created by **v16** and have sat NULL ever since (`schema.ts:686`), deliberately, *"because `MIGRATIONS.length` moves EXACTLY ONCE in this phase"*. **Phase 6 paid for Phase 6a's schema in advance.** The graph's own ledger does move: 6a-2 adds **graph migration v2**.

**Baseline at decomposition (`47f633c`, 2026-08-14):** typecheck **0** · vitest **2012 / 2012 across 59 files** · `grep:secrets` **clean** · `IpcChannel` **86** · `ipcMain.handle(` **76** · `sqliteTable(` **18** · `MIGRATIONS.length` **19** · runtime deps **8** · `LATEST_GRAPH_VERSION` **1**. D4 versions probed the same day: claude **2.1.232** · codex **0.147.0** · opencode **1.18.18** · kimi **0.29.1** · git **2.50.0.windows.1** · **docker 29.7.2 (a whole major above the Phase 6 pass's 28.0.4)**.

- **⚠ 6a-1 IS THE ONE THAT TOUCHES THE BRIGHT LINE, AND D147(e) IS WHY IT DOES NOT.** The natural home for *"query before assuming; write Decisions after milestones"* is `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` — **the user's own hand-authored files**. Injection via ~~claude's `--settings`~~ **`claude --append-system-prompt-file` (corrected by D148 — `--settings` cannot carry an instruction)** and codex's `-c developer_instructions` touches **no user file at all**, and Chorus **already does exactly this for codex today** (the F75 process dump proved it incidentally). **Named cost:** context on every launch, and invisibility outside Chorus.
- **⚠ 6a-2 MUST SET ITS OWN EXPECTATION, IN THE TASK DOC AND IN THE UI: it replaces FINDING code, not READING it.** ~~*"What calls this method"*~~ **⚠ CUT BY D149 — a call graph needs a type checker and is a phase, not a task; `Class`, `Method` and `CALLS` go with it, and the UI says so.** *"Which files did this commit touch"* and *"where does this live"* are the wins that remain. It is **not** a semantic index and must not be sold as one. **Two traps pre-recorded:** **D94** — `File.path` uniqueness **breaks on worktrees**, and this is a worktree-first app, so identity must be repo-relative; and the **refresh policy is a real decision** (`Plan.md`: *"manual + optional post-commit"*) — a save-triggered watcher would fight the agents for the database. **D149 takes manual only.**
- **⚠ AND THE KICKOFF FOUND A THIRD TRAP THAT NEITHER THE PLAN NOR THE PROPOSAL SAW: A RE-INDEX CAN LOWER THE PROVENANCE RATIO WITHOUT ANYONE TOUCHING A MEMORY.** `memory:validate` counts a `:Memory` as sourced only while its `SUPPORTED_BY` target **exists in the graph** (identity model §6). The obvious refresh — wipe the structural namespace and rebuild it — **deletes provenance targets**, so the trust number would fall *because a refresh ran*, and a corrupted measurement would look like a measurement. **D149 forbids deletion outright:** the indexer only ever `MERGE`s, a vanished file is **marked** (`missingSince`) rather than removed, and a grep for `DELETE`/`DETACH`/`REMOVE` over the indexer's Cypher is a test rather than a review habit.
- **⚠ 6a-3 HAS TWO GAPS AND FIXING ONLY THE FIRST SHIPS A SERVER codex CANNOT REACH** (F75): `mcpLaunchArgs` is never called **and** `renderMcpLaunchArgs` emits only passthrough **names**, no env **values** — while local mode needs `NEO4J_URL=bolt://…` as a value. **The misleading comment at `mcpConfigWrite.ts:179` dies with the fix.**
- **✅ ONE GRAPH, TWO LABEL NAMESPACES — NOT TWO CONTAINERS (D147(c)).** The edges between memory and structure are the most valuable thing in the schema, and Community Edition cannot cross-query databases at all.
- **❌ DROPPED OUTRIGHT, NOT DEFERRED (D147(f)):** the `skill.yaml` framework, `backup-graph`, `repo-orientation`, `summarize-session`.
- **Milestone:** an agent that has **not** been told about the graph in its prompt consults it anyway, and a structural question about this codebase is answered from the index rather than by re-reading the tree.
- **✅ THE MILESTONE WAS MET AND DRIVEN, 2026-08-15 — BOTH HALVES.** A `claude` pane was launched in this project and asked *"which files did the most recent commit touch? Answer from this project memory rather than by running git or reading the filesystem"* — **the prompt never named `chorus-memory`**, which is the entire test, because Phase 6's G2 drive only ever worked because the server WAS named (D147(b)). The transcript records `● Calling chorus-memory…`, then `CALL db.labels()`, `db.relationshipTypes()`, a `:Commit` probe, and a `MODIFIED`→`:File` walk; it resolved `6a4fe19a0a0f47743df358a1007d105efc8a3ba5` — the correct newest commit — and named `git.ts`, both `ipc.ts` files, `roadmap.md` and *"the dayReport* core plus its tests"*, which is all nine of that commit's files, grouped rather than listed. **38 seconds, no filesystem access.** It also volunteered two accurate caveats nobody asked for: that this was the indexer's structural view as of the last index, and that **`MODIFIED` edges carry no properties so no add/modify/delete distinction is recorded** — a true observation about 6a-2's own schema, and the most useful review comment of the phase. Evidence: `_verify/6a-2/milestone-transcript.txt`.
- **⚠ AND A HARNESS LESSON WORTH MORE THAN THE TASK IT CAME FROM.** Driving a TUI agent over `session:write` failed twice before it worked, both times *looking like the feature was broken*: a combined `"text"` in ONE write is read by Claude Code's input box as a **paste** and never submits, and polling "until output stops changing" **exits during the agent's first think**, before a single token. The text and the Enter must be separate writes, and idle only counts once output has actually started. Recorded here because the next task that drives an agent will hit both.
- **✅ LANDED — 6a-3: codex's argv, and F75 IS CLOSED BY A QUESTION THE AGENT ANSWERED RATHER THAN BY A DIFF.** The token-by-token delta against the pre-fix argv is **exactly three keys and nothing else** — `mcp_servers.chorus-memory.command="uvx"`, `.args=["mcp-neo4j-cypher"]`, `.env_vars=["NEO4J_URL","NEO4J_DATABASE"]` — and **`bolt://` appears nowhere in argv**, so **D150 holds as shipped: names travel argv, values travel the environment.** **⚠ THE DECISIVE EVIDENCE IS THE ONE THAT SEPARATES *SEE* FROM *REACH*, WHICH THE SPEC NAMED AS THE MOST LIKELY WAY TO SHIP THIS BROKEN.** Listing a server proves configuration; it does not prove connection. codex was asked a graph question and answered *"The memory graph holds 468 `:File` nodes"* — and the indexer reported **`files_seen: 468` in the same run**. **The count matches exactly**, so the server was queried rather than merely enumerated. Controls were driven too: codex in a project with **no** memory gets no `mcp_servers` and keeps its jade `developer_instructions` intact; **claude's argv is byte-unchanged by this task**. **⚠ AND THE DRIVE PRODUCED F77, WHOSE FIRST WRITE-UP THIS PASS HAD TO CORRECT** — codex normalises the server name to `chorus_memory` when it *lists* servers and uses `chorus-memory` when it *calls* one; see the finding, where the correction inverts the decision it was raised to force.
- **✅ LANDED — 6a-4: the provisioner, and TWO OF ITS BUGS WERE FOUND BY CLICKING RATHER THAN BY READING — THE PHASE'S G2 LESSON, AND IT IS A SHARP ONE BECAUSE THE WIRE-LEVEL DRIVE PASSED BOTH.** (1) A container removed **outside** Chorus left the user with **no way back** — the UI offered no path to recreate what it could see was gone; fixed with an explicit *"Create it again"*. (2) The state line claimed *"removed outside Chorus"* **after Chorus itself removed it** — because **`docker` reports absence and never a cause**, so the copy was inferring intent from a null; now *"no longer exists"*. **⚠ NEITHER IS VISIBLE FROM THE IPC LAYER, WHICH IS EXACTLY WHY G2 SAYS *RUN, DON'T JUST COMPILE*:** both channels returned correct values throughout, and a test asserting those values would have been green through both defects. Evidence: eight UI screenshots plus nine drive transcripts under `_verify/6a-4/`.
- **⚠ THE F49 ACCEPTANCE GREP WAS NARROWED, AND THE REASON IS A CONFLICT BETWEEN TWO OF 6a-4's OWN REQUIREMENTS RATHER THAN A RELAXATION.** The task doc's command globs `docker*.ts` and greps for `--rm` / `rm -v` / `volume rm` expecting silence — but that glob matches **`dockerCore.test.ts`, which must contain those exact strings in order to assert their absence** (`it('no builder emits --rm, rm -v, or a volume+rm pair')`). Meanwhile **Spec §6 requires UI copy telling the user to run `docker volume rm` themselves**, so the string must also exist in the renderer. **Resolution: the destruction copy lives with the UI where §6 puts it** (`ProjectSettingsView.vue:1064`), **the refusal constants stay shared, and the grep runs over the two implementation files** — `docker.ts` and `dockerCore.ts`, where it is **genuinely clean, re-verified this pass**, so any future hit there is a real finding rather than noise. **⚠ A GREP THAT MATCHES ITS OWN TEST IS NOT A GATE, IT IS A TRIPWIRE THAT WILL BE DISABLED** — and the version that survives is the one narrow enough to stay armed.
- **⚠ TWO ACCEPTANCE ITEMS WERE NOT DRIVEN, AND WERE NOT CLAIMED.** **(1) Indexing into the provisioned database:** the drive's project **is not a git repository**, so the indexer **honestly refused** — schema-apply and a live query both worked against the provisioned container, but the File/Commit walk had no repository to walk. **(2) The "Docker is not available" refusal:** driving it live means stopping Docker Desktop and killing `chorus-g2-neo4j`, which was judged too destructive for an unattended session; **unit-tested only**. Both are named rather than rounded up, per the practice this document has kept since Phase 2.
- **⚠ BOTH TASK DOCS CARRIED STALE COUNTERS — G6 FIRING FOR THE THIRD AND FOURTH TIME IN ONE PHASE, EXACTLY AS PREDICTED.** `Task-6a-3.md` pins `IpcChannel` **87** / `MIGRATIONS.length` **19** (`:95`, `:179`); `Task-6a-4.md` pins **92** / **19** / `sqliteTable(` **18** (`:25`, `:35`, `:196`). **Actual at close: 97 / 20 / 19.** Cause, and it is the same one every time: **the Day Summary feature (D152/D153) landed between the 6a docs being authored at `47f633c` and the tasks being run**, taking five `day:*` channels, migration v20 and a nineteenth table with it. **A task doc's *Initial Starting Point* table is a measurement with a date on it, not a constant** — and this phase now has four instances of that sentence being true.
