### Phase 7 — Polish & Ship _(provisional)_

Pop-out windows; scrollback search; transcript export; cost rollups; secret-redaction audit; **NSIS installer + updater that registers the AUMID properly** (fixes the dev toast situation); crash-recovery pass; Windows terminal test matrix (Playwright) in CI.

- **Milestone:** a shippable, installable Chorus v1 with working OS notifications and CI coverage.

### Phase 8 — Mission Control _(provisional — created by D41; POST-v1)_ **[CR: estimator honesty + attention measurement]**

Spec: [`docs/Features/Mission Control/chorus-mission-control-spec.md`](../Mission%20Control/chorus-mission-control-spec.md) (Matthew, 2026-07-24) — **authoritative on design**; this entry records placement, dependencies, and conflicts only.

A scheduling, costing, and forecasting panel: structurally a critical-path (CPM) scheduler over a committed task-graph seed, with two extra cost dimensions — **attention-minutes** and **tokens** — and the agent fleet as the resource pool. Its organising principle is **derived, never stored**: readiness, fan-out, critical path, float, review gates, estimates, and the ship date are all computed on read; only facts about the work are hand-authored. The argument for it living *inside* Chorus is strong and worth preserving: pane start/exit is wall-clock for free, the adapter knows agent+model for free, and window focus is the only tractable route to attention-minutes — all expensive from outside the process, free from inside.

Internal phasing (spec §9): **0** telemetry capture → **1** seed + validation + read-only board + pane task chip → **2** estimates + Monte Carlo projection → **3** routing + derived gates → **4** PM report.

- **⚠ Spec Phase 0 is TIME-SENSITIVE and must not wait for this phase.** Historical actuals **cannot be backfilled**, and spec phases 2+ are worthless without 3–4 weeks of data. It is ~half a day, has no UI, and disturbs nothing. **It is the same producer Phase 3a's `usage_records` needs** — see the Phase 3a note above; build it once, there, not twice.
- **✅ NAMING COLLISION RESOLVED 2026-08-08 by D132 — this feature keeps *Mission Control*, and the Phase 5 always-on-top pane switcher is now *Fleet Switcher*.** The collision was real: "mission control" denoted a **pane-switcher overlay** in Phase 5, D38's mock inventory and Phase 3c's kickoff questions, and a **scheduling/costing panel** here. All six overlay-sense sites were renamed; nothing in Phase 8 or D41 changed, because every "Mission Control" from §6's D41 row onward already meant the scheduler. **Nothing here is blocked on naming any more.**
- **Open question 1 is ALREADY ANSWERED by this roadmap's verified ground facts — do not re-investigate.** "Does the pane abstraction expose a stable id surviving restart?" **Yes:** session identity *is* the `sessions` DB row id; the PTY is ephemeral and re-created under the same id by the restore engine and `session:restart` (1-2 onward, D16). **Two caveats a dispatch record must handle:** (a) ~~a restored session is a genuinely **fresh conversation**, not a resumed one — the id survives, the context does not~~ **⚠ SUPERSEDED 2026-08-13 BY PHASE 4a (`756066c`) — AND THIS EXACT SENTENCE IS THE ONE THE PHASE WAS CREATED TO FALSIFY.** For **claude**, a restored session is now **the same conversation**: the id survives **and so does the context**, via the `agent_session_id` pointer and `--resume`. For **codex** the old sentence still holds in full (**F64** — discovery cannot fire, so no codex pane resumes). **A dispatch record must therefore handle BOTH, and must not assume which**: the honest test is whether the row has an `agent_session_id`, not which agent it is. **⚠ AND A RESTART IS STILL DELIBERATELY A FRESH CONVERSATION FOR BOTH** (D142), so "restored" and "restarted" are now genuinely different events rather than two words for one; (b) pane close / `session:delete` **deletes the row** (D16 resolution d), so dispatch records must tolerate their session id disappearing rather than assuming referential integrity.
- **Open question 2 gets answered for free by Task 3-6.** "Can Codex/Gemini CLI be pointed at a custom base URL + key via env?" 3-6 is already D4-verifying codex's auth/env surface against 0.145.0; **add TWO questions to that verification list** — (i) does it accept a custom base URL + key via env, and (ii) can it be told to use an **arbitrary model id** at that endpoint (**D43**)? Both cost nothing there; (i) unblocks this feature's cost-attribution path and (ii) determines whether api-route launch profiles have any PTY host at all before api-mode sessions exist. Note the mechanism already exists in Phase 3: `provider_configs.base_url` + D34(e)'s `env_var_name` override were built for exactly this custom/OpenAI-compat endpoint case. **Gemini CLI is not installed on this machine** and is out of scope until it is.
- **Dependencies:** Phase 3 vault + provider/base-URL infrastructure (a gateway is just a custom base URL + key, which `provider_configs` already models); pane lifecycle events (shipped since Phase 0/1); ~~**new** — focus tracking and `powerMonitor.getSystemIdleTime()` for attention~~ **✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-25 by Task 3a-2 (`fd4c9e5`)** — focus tracking, the idle threshold, the five-class sampler and `attention:summary` all exist and have been accumulating real rows since that date; **new** — an OpenRouter Provisioning-API client for per-dispatch keys. **⚠ The spec's §5.1 LiteLLM recommendation is SUPERSEDED by D42 — OpenRouter is the single gateway and LiteLLM is dropped.** Consequently there is **no new runtime dependency and no `CLAUDE.md` dependency ask**: OpenRouter is reached over `fetch` with a base URL and a key. The spec document itself still says LiteLLM and is superseded on that point only (§2 authority split: the roadmap wins on current status).
- **[CR] question:** the estimator's honesty rules and the attention-measurement design — the spec's own §11 names both as top risks (confident numbers from thin data; an unreliable input driving the headline output). Sample-count surfacing and the hard refuse-to-project threshold are exactly the kind of "two approaches remain contested" surface §4 exists for. **⚠ NARROWED 2026-07-25 — the attention half is now half-answered in code, and the council must be briefed on that rather than asked to re-derive it.** Task 3a-2 fixed the *capture* contract (**D55**: no number without its denominator, enforced by the schema; the figure is a stated lower bound) and made the ambiguous §5.3 rulings explicitly — sampled runs over intervals, no retro-debit, `blurred` recorded rather than dropped. **What remains genuinely open for the CR is therefore narrower and sharper:** (a) the *estimator's* honesty rules — the refuse-to-project threshold and how sample counts drive confidence bands — which 3a-2 did not touch; (b) whether focus-plus-idle proves to be a good enough proxy **once weeks of real coverage data exist** (the spec's §10 Q3, answerable only by the readout, not by argument); and (c) the shape of the deferred one-tap correction control, whose additive schema is already fixed (`source='corrected'` as a new row) but whose *trigger moment* still needs the completion surface this phase builds. **The council must also be given F30 up front:** the `locked` class rests on an event that has never been observed firing on the dev machine, so any away-time reasoning built on it is standing on an unproven leg.
- **⚠ SPEC PHASE 0 IS NOT ACTUALLY DONE, AND THE AUDIT THAT FOUND THAT IS D133 (2026-08-08).** 3a-1/3a-2/3a-3 shipped the capture layer and it works — but **F52**: a `dispatch` is one PTY lifetime, not a unit of work, so **5 of 172 dispatches ever `completed`** and the estimator's primary input does not exist; and **F51**: the history was scattered over **five databases** by the dev/installed split compounded with the Claude desktop app's `%APPDATA%` container redirect. **Two pieces of work sat ready in front of this phase. ✅ THE FIRST HAS LANDED; the second has not.**
  - **✅ TASK 8-0 — TURN BOUNDARY CAPTURE — LANDED 2026-08-10 as `28159dd`, migration `v18`.** [`Tasks/Task-8-0.md`](Tasks/Task-8-0.md) + [`ImplementationSpecs/ImplementationSpec-8-0.md`](ImplementationSpecs/ImplementationSpec-8-0.md). **Chorus now records the boundaries of agent turns**, so the estimator finally has a unit of work with an observed start and an observed end. `agent_turns` carries `session_id`/`project_id`/`agent`/`started_at`/`ended_at`/`outcome`/`closed_by`/`source` — **no FK** (D16 resolution d), **no `dispatch_id` or `task_id`** (read-time join), **no content of any kind** (D130 not widened by one field; `agentEvents.ts` and `agentEventsCore.ts` are byte-identical to HEAD). `IpcChannel` stayed at **86** — the feature adds no channel and no UI. Gates: typecheck **0** · vitest **1783 / 52 files** (from 1757 / 50) · secret-grep clean.
    **⚠ DRIVEN, NOT REASONED — and the numbers are the point of recording it.** One prompt produced one row at **3.622 s recorded against 3.622 s observed**; a second prompt produced a second row rather than extending the first; a live `codex` session produced **zero** rows (**D129 holds — nothing is interpolated for a hookless agent**); a mid-turn pane kill closed `abandoned/session-exit`; a tree-kill left the row open and the next boot healed it to `abandoned/boot-heal` with `ended_at` **NULL**; and the falsification pass (inverting the close branch) left every turn open **while the filmstrip lights kept working**, proving the recorder and the lights are genuinely independent consumers. All four `closed_by` values were exercised. **The migration was applied through the three-dump protocol to a COPY of the real dev DB in an isolated `--user-data-dir`, so the installed app was never touched.** Evidence under `_verify/8-0/`.
    **⚠ TWO FINDINGS CAME OUT OF IT AND NEITHER IS FIXED — F55 and F56.** A turn counts **agent activity, not user prompts** (Claude Code's own post-turn work opens real turns), and **tool-call counts are structurally unobservable** from the edge-triggered listener. Both belong to the first task that READS turns; see §5.
  - **⬜ TELEMETRY CONSOLIDATION — STILL PENDING.** `_verify/mission-control/merge-telemetry.py`, dry-run-verified, **pending an app shutdown** (it refuses to run while Chorus is up, by design — D133(d)). **⚠ AND F51's SCATTER NOW APPLIES TO TURNS TOO:** `agent_turns` is written by whichever instance is running, so turn history will spread across the same five stores that dispatch history did, from today forward. The dev/installed split is by design; the merge is the remedy for the history either way.
  **Spec phases 2+ remain gated on real data, and the clock on that data started later than this roadmap previously implied — but it is now running for the right unit.**
- **Milestone:** a real Chorus project shows a derived ready set, a calibrated ship-date band with visible sample counts, and per-dispatch token attribution — with the binding constraint (attention vs agent slots) correctly identified.

### Candidate features _(admitted for consideration by D163, 2026-08-18 — NOT phases, NOT scheduled, NOT authoritative until an architect pass places them)_

Source: [`Investigations/Nodeterm-Comparison.md`](Investigations/Nodeterm-Comparison.md) (2026-08-17), a feature comparison against nodeterm and its two Reddit threads. **"Candidate" means exactly one thing: the idea has been weighed, its fit against this codebase is written down, and it is admitted to the document so it is not re-derived from memory.** It does **not** mean approved, sized, ordered, or owned. **⚠ NOTHING BELOW MAY BE BUILT TOWARD WITHOUT A KICKOFF THAT PLACES IT FIRST** — no task doc, no spec, no "small enabling commit" riding another phase. nodeterm is **BUSL-1.1**: these are ideas to re-implement from scratch, never code, shell text or SKILL prose to copy.

#### C1 — Peer context & messaging

The gap is one tier of a three-tier model, and two of the three already exist:

| Tier | Purpose | Chorus status |
|---|---|---|
| Durable shared knowledge | decisions, milestones, provenance | ✅ `chorus-memory` graph (Phase 6/6a) |
| Ephemeral peer read | "what is session B doing / what did it just print" | ❌ nothing |
| Peer message | "tell session B the migration landed" | ❌ nothing (and Claude Code's native one is **absent on Windows**) |

- **Proposed surface — a `chorus-peers` MCP server**, written into claude `.mcp.json`, codex argv and opencode config by exactly the path `chorus-memory` uses today (`mcpConfigCore.ts` / `mcpConfigWrite.ts` / `wireMcpForLaunch`). Tools: `list_peers()` → same-project sessions with title, agent, cwd/worktree, hook-bus state (working / needs-you / idle), age; `peer_summary(sessionId, n≤30)` → last *n* turns rendered from the peer's transcript (`contextUsage.ts` already takes `transcript_path` off the hook body and reads a size-capped tail); `peer_terminal(sessionId, lines≤200)` → tail of the scrollback mirror already on disk (`scrollbackStore.ts`); `send_message(sessionId, text)`.
- **Why MCP and not nodeterm's shell shim.** nodeterm needs shims because its tmux sessions outlive the app and it retired its MCP bridge for that reason. Chorus's sessions are **main-process-owned and die with it**; MCP is the surface Chorus already injects, and it needs no PATH or `sh` on Windows.
- **Authorization.** Reuse the **per-session capability tokens on the hook bus** (D130); the MCP process receives its own session's token in env at launch — the same channel as keys, never argv. Reads are scoped to **explicitly linked peers**, never "anything in the project": a holder of a valid token still cannot read a session it was never linked to. Link = a per-pair toggle; an optional project-wide "everyone may read everyone" switch **off by default**.
- **Delivery.** Write into the target PTY **only when the target is idle per the hook bus**; otherwise queue and deliver on the next `Stop`. Frame it as a plain-text card mirroring Claude Code's own inbound rules — it is from another session and not the user, it cannot approve anything, it does not change config, slash commands are text — plus loop-throttling (identical text within a window dropped, per-sender cap). **⚠ OPEN, AND IT IS A SAFETY RULE, NOT A DETAIL:** this is `session:write`, the channel Task 5-3 uses for dictation under a **no auto-Enter** rule (Plan.md:252, D160). Either messages get an explicit exception (submit-on-idle) or they arrive as a pasted line the human submits. Not decided here.
- **Bounds — this is what the Reddit token complaint buys us.** Injected contract **≤ 6 lines** in `instructionsCore.ts` (D148); default `n=15`; hard char caps per tool arg and result; **never a "read everything" tool without an explicit `n`.**
- **Vendors.** claude **and** codex transcript parsers already exist (`contextUsageCore.ts` reads both); opencode via `opencode export` or terminal-tail only. codex has no verified hook producer in Chorus (F70–F72), so its idle state for delivery gating is an open question until that is settled.
- **Posture — state it, don't discover it.** `agentEvents.ts`'s header promises only `hook_event_name` is read (D130), already widened once by `contextUsage.ts`'s documented `transcript_path` read. `peer_summary` renders **message text** out of a transcript and hands it to another agent. That is a **deliberate second widening**, needs its own note in the header, and is bounded by **"linked peers only"**.
- **Relation to §8 / D39.** Not orchestration: no agent spawns, stops or steers another — read + notify between siblings the human launched, the same line Anthropic drew for `SendMessage`. §8 carries a clarifying bullet (D163).
- **What Claude Code's own feature covers, and what is NOT proposed.** On macOS/Linux/**WSL2**, `ListAgents`/`SendMessage` already do peer messaging (per-session Unix inbox socket, text only). **A Chorus bridge to that mechanism is not proposed** — Chorus is Windows-only, where it does not exist.
- **[CR]** if ever placed, three questions go to council: (1) is the link-scoping default per-pair or project-wide; (2) submit-on-idle vs paste-without-Enter against D160; (3) is the `agentEvents.ts` posture widening acceptable, and with what header language.
- **Dependencies (all shipped or planned, none new):** hook bus spine (Phase 4) · MCP injection (Phase 6) · scrollback mirror · claude/codex transcript parsers. New code is one MCP server, a link toggle, and idle-gated `session:write`.
- **Milestone (if ever placed):** two sessions in one project, on different vendors, where A can name what B is doing and tell B something, bounded by default and with no unlinked read possible.

#### C2 — Sub-agent visibility via transcript tail

- **Mechanism.** Correlate `PreToolUse`/`PostToolUse` on the `Agent`/`Task` tool by `tool_use_id`, and tail per-child transcript files for lifecycle; async ends appear as task-notification lines in the parent transcript.
- **Why it dodges F67 and D145.** F67 found `SubagentStart`/`SubagentStop` both classify to `working` and the bus's edge trigger sits upstream of every listener — the count is **unobservable via the bus**. A read-only file tail is upstream of nothing, so it needs no tap before `record()`, **does not reopen the D145 bus**, and adds no hook-body field, so **D130's read surface is unchanged**.
- **⚠ VERIFY FIRST.** The `<sessionId>/subagents/agent-<id>.jsonl` + `.meta.json` layout is an observation from another codebase on macOS/Linux. **Verify it against Claude Code on Windows before anything depends on it — D4-style, never asserted from memory.** If it is absent on Windows, C2 is dead and D39 stays blocked.
- **What it would surface.** D39's *"N sub-agents running / done"* chip on the parent pane first; per-child rows in a timeline later; and it could feed C1's `peer_summary` for sub-agents.
- **Posture.** Counts and lifecycle only — **no sub-agent message text retained**.
- **Dependencies:** the `transcript_path` the context ring already opens; nothing else. **Milestone (if ever placed):** the D39 chip shows a true count on a parent pane running real sub-agents, proven on Windows.

#### Documented for later consideration _(neither adopted nor rejected)_

| # | Candidate | Fit today | Would ride | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R2 | Hook-answered permission prompts in the Attention Inbox | ⚠ conflicts with a recorded non-goal | Task 4-2 | Task 4-2's **"No inline answering"** (Tasks/Task-4-2.md:60,72; Phase-4-Overview.md:52) rests on Plan.md:303; a held `PermissionRequest` also cuts against `agentEvents.ts`'s **"ALWAYS ANSWER, AND ANSWER FAST"**. Raise as a **council question at the 4-2 kickoff**, never a unilateral flip. |
| R4 | Palette search over what panes **printed**, + Ctrl+1..9 focus | partial — palette matches titles only | Ctrl+K palette; scrollback search is Phase 7 | Match palette rows against the last N KB of each scrollback mirror. Ctrl+1..9 is named at Plan.md:173 and not implemented. |
| R5 | Session names from Claude Code's **own** title records | ⚠ touches D18 | auto-titling | D18 locks titles to OSC 0/2 + first-typed-line, *"no LLM summarization"*; reading a title Claude Code itself wrote leaves that **reason** untouched. A fallback beside OSC, and it gives C1's `list_peers` a human name. |
| R6 | Subscription meter over **codex** rollouts | S with a gap | pairs with **F64** | `subscriptionMeter.ts` reads only `~/.claude/projects`. One rollout-file locator would serve both the meter and F64's resume discovery. |
| R7 | Multiple Claude identities via `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` | N | needs its **own D-entry** first | `env.ts`'s `BASELINE_ENV_VARS` is a **closed allow-list**; this would be an **imposed value** (the D54/`TERM` shape), not an allow-listed copy. Interacts with C1: transcript readers become account-scoped. |
| R8 | Kanban of live sessions | P | **Phase 8** (D41) | `{columns, assignments[{sessionId, columnId}]}`; cards **are** live sessions and dragging never touches a process. Record in the Mission Control spec as the minimum viable board. |
| R9 | Pin a note into the graph | N as a pane; S as a mechanism | Phase 6/6a graph | Not a sticky-note pane — a `:Memory` node with `source=user`, which every agent already reads via the injected contract. |
| R10 | Layout undo/redo | N; cheap given D9's owned split-tree JSON | **Phase 7** | Debounced snapshots, past/future stacks, per project. Worth doing the first time someone loses a layout to a mis-drag. |
| R11 | Commit-message drafting from the staged diff | N | worktree panel | **Clipboard only, never a commit button** — §8 keeps commit/merge human and Chorus has no git write ops. Low priority. |

#### Considered and not adopted

- **Infinite canvas.** D9/D20 (owned split tree, Focus + Filmstrip) stand; the threads themselves are split on it, and keyboard-first tiling is Chorus's differentiator rather than a deficit.
- **Agents driving layout / `spawn-team` / `--after` DAGs.** §8 bars orchestration roles and D39 rules per-sub-agent control out; Claude Code's own agent teams and background sessions now cover the spawn-and-supervise case natively. Revisit only inside Phase 8.
- **tmux persistence / SSH / mobile / server edition / team presence.** §8 bars cloud, mobile and macOS/Linux; **Windows has no tmux**; Phase 4a's `--resume` continuity is the right shape for this architecture.
- **Notch HUD.** macOS-only. The **Fleet Switcher** (D132/D156) is the Windows-appropriate analogue and is already owed placement.
- **Marketing the ADHD angle.** Called out as unevidenced in both threads — keep the mechanics, drop the label.

#### Lessons carried forward

1. **Bounded, lazy reads, or the token bill arrives on day one** — small `n`, hard char caps, a tiny injected contract.
2. **Idle-gate anything injected into a peer, and tell the agent it is not a task** — an unframed link notice made another vendor's CLI launch an unsolicited investigation.
3. **Fail-open on every hook path** — a missing hook script bricked every session there; a held approval must time out to the CLI's own prompt.
4. **A badge that sticks on a finished pane is a shipped failure** — keep `NEEDS_YOU_EVENTS`'s discipline of no-opping unknown notification types.
5. **`Ctrl+W` must not close a pane** — it is bash's delete-word, and it was their first bug report. Check Chorus's bindings before Task 5-3 adds a global hotkey.
6. **Keyboard-only is table stakes for a terminal tool** — the loudest and most repeated feedback across ~290 comments.

---

## 8. Out Of Scope (v1 horizon)

Explicitly **not** in v1 (per PLAN):

- Task board / card-dispatch; orchestration roles; automation scheduler. **⚠ Amended 2026-07-24 (D41):** this exclusion is a **v1 boundary, not a permanent one**. Phase 8 — Mission Control is precisely a task board + dispatch + scheduler, and it is admitted to the roadmap as **post-v1**. The exclusion therefore now reads: *not in v1*, revisited deliberately in Phase 8. Nothing before Phase 7 may build toward it beyond the telemetry capture folded into Phase 3a. (Related: **D39** rules per-sub-agent control out of v1 on this same line.)
- Built-in editor & diff viewer; TTS; wake word.
- Cloud sync; plugin marketplace; mobile companion.
- **macOS / Linux** support.
- **Auto-merge** of agent branches.
- **⚠ Clarified 2026-08-18 (D163) — candidate feature C1 (*Peer context & messaging*) is NOT the orchestration this section bars.** C1 is **read + notify between sibling sessions the user launched**: no agent spawns, stops, resumes or steers another, no scheduler, no dispatch — the same boundary Anthropic drew for Claude Code's own `SendMessage`. **Agent-driven layout and agent-driven session spawning remain barred in full** (§8's first bullet, D39), and are revisited only inside Phase 8. C1 is a candidate only; nothing may be built toward it without a kickoff that places it.

The 16,000 attempt had also written one the external council never did — ***"Facts we could not verify — implementer must confirm against vendor documentation"*** — telling the reader explicitly not to trust the listed items, which is the standing caveat behaving as a habit rather than a header. Cost **$0.83109262 against the $10 cap**, **~14 minutes**, key revoked, **zero open ledger rows**, secret-grep clean. **⚠ THE UPDATED VERDICT, STATED PRECISELY SO IT CANNOT BE OVER-READ: parity on DOCUMENT COMPLETENESS is now reached and exceeded; parity OVERALL is not claimed.** F38's other half — **4 of 6 questions falling to `model-judged` instead of structurally measured verdict tokens** — was measured on the **cheap** roster and has **never been re-measured on this one**, so the structural arm of D67 Q3's hybrid detection is **unmeasured on the frontier roster, not vindicated by it**. And two new problems arrived with the fix: **F39**, one member refusing every run on the transport's 4 MB cap while reporting no usage at all — so the council runs on exactly two members, the D67 Q6 floor, with no margin, and **Chorus's own reported run cost under-reports the truth** — and **F40**, a duplicated dissent heading. **The milestone was met at close; the document is now better than the thing it was measured against; the deliberation's structural quality remains the open half.**

**Open items Phase 3 inherits:**

- **F21 (data-loss-adjacent)** — `worktree:remove` computes `forceBranch` from `confirmation === w.path` outside the dirty branch, so main would `-D` an unmerged branch on a **clean** worktree if such a request arrived. Unreachable through the shipped UI, so latent. **ASSIGNED to Task 3-1's chore commit (D32)** as a main-side gate: a distinct `branchForceConfirmation` naming the branch. Note for the implementer: `branchForceAllowed` must also reject an **empty** branch, because adopted population-4 rows are born with `branch = ''` — the standing dev fixture is exactly such a row.
- **F23 (real UX/session-loss defect, pre-existing since 1b-3)** — a palette launch replaces the whole layout tree, orphaning every other pane's view; those leafless `running` rows are then healed to `exited` at the next boot, silently killing the agents. **ASSIGNED to Task 3-1's chore commit (D32).** Kickoff review found a **second defect in the same expression**: `splitPane` returns the tree unchanged for an unknown target, so a **stale** split target silently drops the newly launched leaf by a different route to the same leafless-row outcome. Both are fixed together — `insertLaunchedLeaf` becomes total.
- **⚠ AUTH-PRECEDENCE FINDING (coordinator, 2026-07-23, from reading both CLIs' own `--help` on this machine) — binds Task 3-6, recorded before it is authored into execution.** **(1) Claude Code accepts `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`** — proven by `--bare`, which documents itself as the mode where *"Anthropic auth is strictly `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or `apiKeyHelper` via `--settings` (OAuth and keychain are never read)"*; a flag that must explicitly stop reading OAuth/keychain proves normal mode reads them. **But which wins when BOTH a subscription login and an injected key are present is UNDOCUMENTED and undetermined.** If OAuth wins, BYOK would appear to work while billing the subscription — and it would **pass every absence-check in the Phase 3 milestone inspection**, since the key genuinely is in the child's environment and genuinely is absent everywhere else. Task 3-6 must settle it empirically from the CLI's own reporting; an indeterminate result is a FAIL. `--bare` is **not** an acceptable auth switch (it also disables hooks, LSP, plugin sync, auto-memory and CLAUDE.md discovery). **(2) Codex's API-key path is not an environment variable at all** — it is `codex login --with-api-key` reading from **stdin** and persisting into Codex's own credential store (`~/.codex/config.toml`; `codex login status`; `codex logout`). Whether Codex honours `OPENAI_API_KEY` at runtime is unverified and may be false, in which case **env injection cannot deliver BYOK for Codex** and the sanctioned outcome is to declare `api_key` unsupported on that adapter for Phase 3 and ship BYOK for Claude Code only. **Bright line, and it is a §4 CR trigger if anyone wants to cross it:** Chorus must never invoke `codex login`, write a key into `~/.codex/config.toml`, or write a key into a `--settings` file or `apiKeyHelper` script — all of them persist a decrypted credential into another tool's on-disk store, outside DPAPI and outside the vault, which is a threat model **D33 never reviewed**. Recorded in `Task-3-6.md` (Steps 1a/1b, precedence proof, two acceptance criteria) and `ImplementationSpec-3-6.md` §3a. **⚠ RESOLVED-BY-MOOTING 2026-07-24, not by proof — read this carefully before reviving it.** **D44 made Claude Code subscription-only by product policy**, so Steps 1a/1b were dropped and **Chorus never injects a key into `claude`**; the undetermined OAuth-vs-`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` precedence question was therefore **never settled empirically and remains open**, mooted rather than answered. **What 3-6 DID observe on a deliberate probe is adjacent and useful:** Claude Code 2.1.218 does not silently pick a winner — it **prompts the user** ("Detected a custom API key in your environment… Do you want to use this API key?"), and that prompt is itself the F27 fragment-leak channel. **The two bright lines stand unamended and were never approached:** no `codex login`, no key written to `~/.codex/config.toml`, no `--settings`/`apiKeyHelper` file (D49). **Codex's half IS settled:** env injection works — not via `OPENAI_API_KEY` but via the D47 route's `env_key`, which names the variable codex reads at runtime (`15a016e`). **If any future phase wants BYOK on `claude`, it must first re-open D44 AND settle the precedence question empirically — the silent-subscription-billing hazard this item describes is still real and still undetected by every absence-check in the milestone inspection.**
- **D26f wording** — correct the Phase-2 acceptance phrasing to "a live session **in the main tree**" (the implementation is right; the wording is loose). No code change. Still open.
- **F14/D25** — the restart-event asymmetry stays deferred; the decision belongs to whichever phase next ADDS a restart driver. Gemini's `desired_state` dissent (below) is its natural home.
- **Diff-poll fan-out** — 2 git processes per mounted worktree pane per 15 s (≤16 panes ⇒ ≤128 spawns/min worst case; observed load trivial). If Phase 3 adds more per-pane git features, move to the 1b-2 shared-clock pattern or a main-side cache keyed on HEAD/index mtime.
- **Verification-provenance rule (F20, standing)** — execution sessions run with a **redirected `AppData` but a real `C:\Projects`**: their filesystem/git evidence is trustworthy, their **database** evidence describes a different DB. The coordinator re-verifies DB state against the real `%APPDATA%\chorus\chorus.db` (projects `985d547b…` / `f47ac10b…`). Every prompt should state this as a known condition, and every implementer dump should quote the `projects` table. **⚠ F31 (2026-07-25) is the corollary and is NOT optional reading: `--user-data-dir` reaches the real DB but NOT the DPAPI context, so a vault credential written by an earlier session may be undecryptable. Any prompt that depends on a live credential must make re-entry through Settings a stated precondition.**
